\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{p}_{c}\,{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\tau_{k}\leq r_{0}\}\cap E_{k}},

where
\widetilde{p}_{c}=c^{-1/2}\vee c^{-3/2}
. Therefore, taking
c_{0}\in[1,\infty)
and

\delta\in(0,1)
as in Lemma
4.8
and applying
(
4.18
) and Lemma
4.8
,
for any
n\geq 1
and any
c\geq c_{0}
we get

\displaystyle\prob[N_{r}\geq n,\,E_{r}]=\prob[\tau_{n}\leq r_{0},\,E_{r}]

\displaystyle\leq\prob[\tau_{n}\leq r_{0},\,E_{n}]\leq\widetilde{p}_{c}^{n-1}\prob[\tau_{1}\leq r_{0},\,E_{1}]

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{p}_{c}^{n-1}\prob[\tau_{1}\leq r_{0}]\leq\widetilde{p}_{c}^{n-1}(1-\delta),

completing the proof.
∎

Lemma 4.10
.

Suppose we have the same setup as in Lemma
4.9
. Let
A_{r}
denote the sum of the areas of the components which are cut off by the exploration within exploration time
r
and have area at most
cr^{4}(\log r^{-1})^{6+8u}
each.
The probability of the event
E_{r}\cap\{A_{r}>r^{4}(\log r^{-1})^{7+12u}\}
decays to
0
as
r\to 0
with a decay rate independent of
\ell
and faster than any polynomial of
r
.

Proof.

Set

R=r^{3}(\log r^{-1})^{3+4u}\quad\text{and}\quad R^{\prime}=r^{24}(\log r^{-1})^{36+54u}.

Step 1.
Let
Z
be a
3/2
-stable Lévy process with only upward jumps and let
\Lambda=\{(t,u)\}
be the set of pairs consisting of the jump times and sizes for
Z
. That is,
(t,u)\in\Lambda
if and only if
u=Z_{t}-Z_{t-}>0
. Then
\Lambda
is a Poisson point process with intensity measure given by a constant times
u^{-5/2}\,du\,dt
where
du,dt
both denote Lebesgue measure on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
. Let
f
denote the density function for the area of a sample from
\mu_{\mathrm{BD}}^{L=1}
as given in (
3.7
). We associate with each upward jump of
Z
an independent random variable
a
with density given by
f
. Then
\Lambda_{0}=\{(t,u,a)\}
is a Poisson point process with intensity measure given by a constant times
dt\otimes u^{-5/2}du\otimes f(a)da
where
dt
,
du
, and
da
all denote Lebesgue measure on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
. Thus
[
35
, Lemma 4.19]
implies that
\widetilde{\Lambda}=\{(t,u^{2}a)\}=\{(t,v)\}
is a Poisson point process with intensity measure given by a constant times
dt\otimes v^{-7/4}dv
where again
dt
and
dv
denote Lebesgue measure on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
. Note that
u^{2}a
is equal in distribution to the area of a Brownian disk with boundary length
u
.

Fix
S\in(0,R^{\prime})
. For
k\in\mathbb{Z}
, the number of elements
(t,v)\in\widetilde{\Lambda}
with
t\in[1-S,1+S]
and
v\in(2^{-k-1},2^{-k}]
is distributed as a Poisson random variable with mean
m_{k}
given by a constant times
S2^{3k/4}
. Let
k_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}
be the smallest
k\in\mathbb{Z}
so that
S^{-1/2}m_{k}\geq 1/2
.
For
k\geq k_{0}
, (
4.2
) with
\alpha=(\log\tfrac{1}{r})^{1+u}S^{-1}
implies that the probability that there are more than
(\log\tfrac{1}{r})^{1+u}S^{-1}m_{k}
such elements is at most
\exp(-(\log\tfrac{1}{r})^{1+u}S^{-1}m_{k})
, which decays to
0
as
S\to 0
faster than any power of
S
when
r>0
is small enough and fixed.
It likewise decays to
0
faster than any power of
r
as
r\to 0
provided
S\in(0,R^{\prime})
. Let
k_{1}
be the smallest
k
so that
2^{-k}\leq r^{4}(\log\frac{1}{r})^{6+9u}
.
By (
4.2
) with
\alpha=(\log\frac{1}{r})^{1+u}/m_{k}
, for each
p>0
there exists
r_{0}>0
so that for all
r\in(0,r_{0})
and for each
k\in[k_{1},k_{0}]\cap\mathbb{Z}
, the probability that we have more than
(\log\tfrac{1}{r})^{1+u}
elements
(t,v)\in\widetilde{\Lambda}
with
t\in[1-S,1+S]
and
v\in(2^{-k-1},2^{-k}]
is
O(S^{p})
as
S\to 0
. Likewise, it tends to
0
as
r\to 0
faster than any power of
r
provided
S\in(0,R^{\prime})
.
Altogether, we see that for each
p>0
there exists
r_{0}>0
so that for all
r\in(0,r_{0})
the probability that
\sum_{(t,v)\in\widetilde{\Lambda}:t\in[1-S,1+S],\,v\leq cr^{4}(\log r^{-1})^{6+8u}}v
exceeds
r^{4}(\log\tfrac{1}{r})^{7+11u}
is
O(S^{p})
as
S\to 0
. Likewise, it tends to
0
as
r\to 0
faster than any power of
r
provided
S\in(0,R^{\prime})
.
We also see that for each
q\in[0,\infty)
, the probability that
\sum_{(t,v)\in\widetilde{\Lambda}:t\in[(q-4R)\vee 0,q+4R],\,v\leq cr^{4}(\log r^{-1})^{6+8u}}v
exceeds
r^{4}(\log\tfrac{1}{r})^{7+11u}
tends to
0
as
r\to 0
faster than any power of
r
,
by the argument in this paragraph with
m_{k}
a constant times
R2^{3k/4}
,
k_{0}=\min\{k\in\mathbb{Z}:m_{k}\geq 1/2\}
and (
4.2
) used with
\alpha=(\log\frac{1}{r})^{1+u}
for
k\geq k_{0}
.

Step 2.

We are now going to transfer the result of Step 1 about
3/2
-stable Lévy processes to the setting of
3/2
-stable Lévy excursions. Let
Z
be a
3/2
-stable Lévy process and set
S_{t}=\sup\{0\vee Z_{s}:0\leq s\leq t\}
for each
t\geq 0
. Let also
L=(L_{t})_{t\geq 0}
be a local time of
S-Z
at
0
in the sense of
[
20
, Chapter IV, Sections 2-4]
and let
L^{-1}
be the right-continuous inverse of
L
. Note that we can choose
L
by setting
L_{t}=-\inf\{0\wedge Z_{s}:0\leq s\leq t\}
for each
t\geq 0
(see
[
20
, Chapter VI]
).
By
[
20
, Chapter VIII, Lemma 1]
, we have that
L^{-1}
is a stable subordinator of index
1/3
. Let
A=\sup\{t\leq 1:Z_{t}-\inf_{0\leq s\leq t}Z_{s}=0\}
and
B=\inf\{t\geq 1:Z_{t}-\inf_{0\leq s\leq t}Z_{s}=0\}
. Then
B-A
is equal to the length of the interval in the complement of the range of
L^{-1}
which contains
1
.
In particular, by
[
20
, Chapter III, Proposition 2 (i)]
, the probability that
B-A\in[s,2s]
is of order
s^{1/3}
as
s\to 0
and is of order
s^{-1/3}
as
s\to\infty
. By
[
20
, Chapter VIII, Proposition 15]
, the conditional law of the process

(B-A)^{-2/3}(Z_{A+(B-A)t}-\inf_{0\leq s\leq 1}Z_{s})
is that of a unit length
3/2
-stable Lévy excursion. By Step 1, the probability that
\sum_{(t,v)\in\widetilde{\Lambda}:t\in[1-S,1+S],\,v\leq cr^{4}(\log r^{-1})^{6+8u}}v
exceeds
r^{4}(\log\tfrac{1}{r})^{7+11u}
decays to
0
as
S\to 0
faster than any fixed power of
S
provided
r>0
is sufficiently small and fixed.
Therefore, combining with the above description of the unit length
3/2
-stable Lévy excursion with the scaling property of a
3/2
-stable Lévy process, we obtain that the same is true if
\widetilde{Z}
is a
3/2
-stable Lévy excursion of time length
S\in(0,R^{\prime})
and we sum
v
over all of the associated jumps.
Similarly, for a
3/2
-stable Lévy excursion
\widetilde{Z}
of time length
S\in[R^{\prime},\infty)
and for each
p>0
, the probability that the sum of
v
over all the jumps within some time interval

I\subset[0,S]
of length at most
R
exceeds
r^{4}(\log\tfrac{1}{r})^{7+11u}
decays to
0
as
r\to 0
with a decay rate determined solely by
u
and
p
and faster than any power of
r
provided
R^{\prime}\leq S\leq r^{-p}
.

Step 3.
We are now going to deduce the result in the setting of
\mu_{\mathrm{BM}}
from the above estimates. We will subsequently explain how to transfer the result to the setting of the Brownian disk in Steps 4 and 5 below.
Suppose that
(\mathcal{S},d,\nu,x,y)
has distribution
\mu_{\mathrm{BM}}
and let
Y_{t}
be the time-reversal of the boundary length process so that
Y_{t}
is a
3/2
-stable CSBP excursion.
By the Lamperti transform (
3.3
), if we let
s(t)=\inf\{r^{\prime}\geq 0:\int_{0}^{r^{\prime}}Y_{s^{\prime}}ds^{\prime}\geq t\}
then we have that
\widetilde{Z}_{t}=Y_{s(t)}
is a
3/2
-stable Lévy excursion.
Let
\widetilde{E}_{r}
denote the event that the sum of
v
over all the jumps of
\widetilde{Z}
within some time interval
I
of length at most
R
exceeds
r^{4}(\log\tfrac{1}{r})^{7+11u}
.
For each
q,q^{\prime}\in[0,\infty)
with
0\leq q^{\prime}-q\leq r
, let
A_{[q,q^{\prime}]}
denote the sum of the areas of the components which are cut off by the metric exploration within time interval
[q,q^{\prime}]
and have area at most
cr^{4}(\log r^{-1})^{6+8u}
each.
If such
q,q^{\prime}
satisfy
\sup_{s\in[q,q^{\prime}]}L_{s}\leq r^{2}(\log r^{-1})^{3+4u}
, then
the amount of
\widetilde{Z}_{t}
-time which corresponds to the metric exploration over time interval
[q,q^{\prime}]
is

\int_{q}^{q^{\prime}}Y_{(d(x,y)-s)\vee 0}\,ds\leq(q^{\prime}-q)r^{2}(\log r^{-1})^{3+4u}\leq R,

and therefore

(4.19)

\displaystyle\bigcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}q,q^{\prime}\in[0,\infty)\\
0\leq q^{\prime}-q\leq r\end{subarray}}\biggl\{\sup_{s\in[q,q^{\prime}]}L_{s}\leq r^{2}(\log r^{-1})^{3+4u},\,A_{[q,q^{\prime}]}>r^{4}(\log r^{-1})^{7+11u}\biggr\}\subset\widetilde{E}_{r}.

On the other hand, since the distribution for the time length
\widetilde{\zeta}
of
\widetilde{Z}
is given by a constant times
s^{-5/3}ds
,
we see that
\mu_{\mathrm{BM}}[\widetilde{E}_{r}]
decays to
0
as
r\to 0
faster than any polynomial of
r
, by choosing arbitrarily large
p>0
and integrating
our upper bound on
\mu_{\mathrm{BM}}[\widetilde{E}_{r}\mid\widetilde{\zeta}=s]
from Step 2
with respect to
s^{-5/3}\,ds
on
(0,R^{\prime})
,
[R^{\prime},r^{-p})
and
[r^{-p},\infty)
separately.
In particular, the assertion of the lemma in the case of
\mu_{\mathrm{BM}}
follows from this estimate on
\mu_{\mathrm{BM}}[\widetilde{E}_{r}]
and (
4.19
).

Step 4.
The result of Step 3 can be extended to the setting of an instance sampled from
\mu_{\mathrm{BD,W}}^{L=\ell}
(with the same exploration). Indeed,
let
Z^{\prime}_{t}
denote the Lamperti transform of the time-reversal of the boundary length process under
\mu_{\mathrm{BD,W}}^{L=\ell}
, and let
E_{r}^{\prime}
denote the event that the sum of
v
over all the jumps of
Z^{\prime}
within some time interval
I
of length at most
R
exceeds
r^{4}(\log\tfrac{1}{r})^{7+11u}
,
so that (
4.19
) with
E^{\prime}_{r}
in place of
\widetilde{E}_{r}
holds as events under
\mu_{\mathrm{BD,W}}^{L=\ell}
.
If we start with a Brownian map instance and explore the filled metric ball until the first time
\tau
it has boundary length
\ell
,
then by the strong Markov property of the metric exploration the conditional law of the complement
\mathcal{D}_{\tau}
given
\tau<\infty
is
\mu_{\mathrm{BD,W}}^{L=\ell}
,
and
\{\tau<\infty,\,\mathcal{D}_{\tau}\text{ satisfies }E^{\prime}_{r}\}\subset\widetilde{E}_{r}
as events under
\mu_{\mathrm{BM}}
.
Recall also that
\mu_{\mathrm{BD,W}}^{L=\ell}[\nu(\mathcal{D})\geq cr^{4}(\log r^{-1})^{6+8u}]\geq\ell/(3c^{1/2}r^{2}(\log r^{-1})^{3+4u})
by (
3.6
) and
\ell\in(0,r^{2}(\log r^{-1})^{3+4u}]
and that
\mu_{\mathrm{BM}}[\tau<\infty]=\mu_{\mathrm{BM}}[\sup_{s\geq 0}L_{s}\geq\ell]=b_{0}/\ell

for a constant
b_{0}>0
as shown in the paragraph of (
4.13
). Combining these facts, we get the following upper bound independent of
\ell
on the probability of
E^{\prime}_{r}
:

\displaystyle\mu_{\mathrm{BD,W}}^{L=\ell}[E^{\prime}_{r}\mid\nu(\mathcal{D})\geq cr^{4}(\log r^{-1})^{6+8u}]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mu_{\mathrm{BM}}\bigl[{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\tau<\infty\}}\mu_{\mathrm{BM}}[\mathcal{D}_{\tau}\text{ satisfies }E^{\prime}_{r}\mid\tau<\infty]\bigr]}{\mu_{\mathrm{BM}}[\tau<\infty]\mu_{\mathrm{BD,W}}^{L=\ell}[\nu(\mathcal{D})\geq cr^{4}(\log r^{-1})^{6+8u}]}

\displaystyle\leq 3b_{0}^{-1}c^{1/2}r^{2}(\log r^{-1})^{3+4u}\mu_{\mathrm{BM}}[\tau<\infty,\,\mathcal{D}_{\tau}\text{ satisfies }E^{\prime}_{r}]

(4.20)

\displaystyle\leq 3b_{0}^{-1}c^{1/2}r^{2}(\log r^{-1})^{3+4u}\mu_{\mathrm{BM}}[\widetilde{E}_{r}]

for each
\ell\in(0,r^{2}\log(r^{-1})^{3+4u})
.

Step 5.
On the basis of Steps 3 and 4, we can now obtain the result in the case of the center exploration of an instance
(\mathcal{D},d,\nu)
with law
\mu_{\mathrm{BD}}^{L=\ell}
as follows.
Let
\mathcal{D}_{t}
denote the target component of the center exploration at time
t
for each
t\geq 0
and set
\tau=\inf\{t\geq 0:\nu(\mathcal{D}_{t})<r^{4}(\log r^{-1})^{6+8u}\}
,
so that on the event
\{\tau>0\}
we have
\nu(\mathcal{D}_{\tau})\leq r^{4}(\log\tfrac{1}{r})^{6+8u}\leq\nu(\mathcal{D}_{\tau-})
, where
\mathcal{D}_{\tau-}=\bigcap_{t\in[0,\tau)}\mathcal{D}_{t}
.
Pick a random marked point
y\in\mathcal{D}
according to
\nu/\nu(\mathcal{D})
independently of the center exploration. Then on the event
\{\tau>0\}
,
the conditional probability of
\{y\in\mathcal{D}_{\tau-}\}
given a realization of
\mathcal{D}
and the center exploration is at least
\nu(\mathcal{D}_{\tau-})/\nu(\mathcal{D})\geq r^{4}(\log\tfrac{1}{r})^{6+8u}/\nu(\mathcal{D})
,
and on
\{\tau>0,\,y\in\mathcal{D}_{\tau-}\}
the center exploration agrees with the exploration targeted at
y
over the time interval
[0,\tau)
.
Set
V_{r}=\{\nu(\mathcal{D})\geq cr^{4}(\log\tfrac{1}{r})^{6+8u}\}\subset\{\tau>0\}
, and
recall that
d\mu_{\mathrm{BD,W}}^{L=\ell}=\bigl(\int\nu(\mathcal{D})\,d\mu_{\mathrm{BD}}^{L=\ell}\bigr)^{-1}\nu(\mathcal{D})\,d\mu_{\mathrm{BD}}^{L=\ell}
,
that
\int\nu(\mathcal{D})\,d\mu_{\mathrm{BD}}^{L=\ell}=\ell^{2}
by (
3.7
)
and that
\mu_{\mathrm{BD,W}}^{L=\ell}[V_{r}]/\mu_{\mathrm{BD}}^{L=\ell}[V_{r}]\leq 9\ell^{-2}cr^{4}(\log\tfrac{1}{r})^{6+8u}

by (
3.6
), (
3.7
) and
\ell\in(0,r^{2}(\log\tfrac{1}{r})^{3+4u}]
.
Now, keeping writing
E_{r},A_{r}
for the event and the sum of the areas as in the statement for the
metric exploration targeted at
y

and letting
E^{\mathrm{cen}}_{r},A^{\mathrm{cen}}_{r}
denote those as in the statement for the
center exploration
, we see from (
4.19
)
with
E^{\prime}_{r}
in place of
\widetilde{E}_{r}
for instances of
\mu_{\mathrm{BD,W}}^{L=\ell}
and from (
4.20
) that the following holds:

\displaystyle r^{4}(\log\tfrac{1}{r})^{6+8u}\mu_{\mathrm{BD}}^{L=\ell}[V_{r}\cap E^{\mathrm{cen}}_{r}\cap\{A^{\mathrm{cen}}_{r}>r^{4}(\log\tfrac{1}{r})^{7+12u}\}]

\displaystyle\leq\int_{V_{r}\cap E^{\mathrm{cen}}_{r}\cap\{A^{\mathrm{cen}}_{r}>r^{4}(\log r^{-1})^{7+12u},\,y\in\mathcal{D}_{\tau-}\}}\nu(\mathcal{D})\,d\mu_{\mathrm{BD}}^{L=\ell}

\displaystyle=\int\nu(\mathcal{D})\,d\mu_{\mathrm{BD}}^{L=\ell}\cdot\mu_{\mathrm{BD,W}}^{L=\ell}[V_{r}\cap E^{\mathrm{cen}}_{r}\cap\{A^{\mathrm{cen}}_{r}>r^{4}(\log\tfrac{1}{r})^{7+12u},\,y\in\mathcal{D}_{\tau-}\}]

\displaystyle\leq\ell^{2}\mu_{\mathrm{BD,W}}^{L=\ell}[V_{r}\cap E_{r}\cap\{A_{r}>r^{4}(\log\tfrac{1}{r})^{7+12u},\,y\in\mathcal{D}_{\tau-},\,\tau>r\}]

\displaystyle\qquad+\ell^{2}\mu_{\mathrm{BD,W}}^{L=\ell}[V_{r}\cap E^{\mathrm{cen}}_{r}\cap\{A^{\mathrm{cen}}_{r}>r^{4}(\log\tfrac{1}{r})^{7+12u},\,y\in\mathcal{D}_{\tau-},\,\tau\leq r\}]

\displaystyle\leq\ell^{2}\mu_{\mathrm{BD,W}}^{L=\ell}[V_{r}\cap E_{r}\cap\{A_{r}>r^{4}(\log\tfrac{1}{r})^{7+11u}\}]

\displaystyle\qquad+\ell^{2}\mu_{\mathrm{BD,W}}^{L=\ell}\biggl[V_{r}\cap\biggl\{\sup_{s\in[0,\tau]}L_{s}\leq r^{2}(\log\tfrac{1}{r})^{3+4u},\,A_{[0,\tau]}>r^{4}(\log\tfrac{1}{r})^{7+11u},\,\tau\leq r\biggr\}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq 2\ell^{2}\mu_{\mathrm{BD,W}}^{L=\ell}[V_{r}\cap E^{\prime}_{r}]=2\ell^{2}\mu_{\mathrm{BD,W}}^{L=\ell}[V_{r}]\mu_{\mathrm{BD,W}}^{L=\ell}[E^{\prime}_{r}\mid V_{r}]

\displaystyle\leq 18cr^{4}(\log\tfrac{1}{r})^{6+8u}\mu_{\mathrm{BD}}^{L=\ell}[V_{r}]\mu_{\mathrm{BD,W}}^{L=\ell}[E^{\prime}_{r}\mid V_{r}]

\displaystyle\leq 54b_{0}^{-1}c^{3/2}r^{6}(\log\tfrac{1}{r})^{9+12u}\mu_{\mathrm{BD}}^{L=\ell}[V_{r}]\mu_{\mathrm{BM}}[\widetilde{E}_{r}];

here, to bound the second term of the fourth line from above by that of the fifth line we used the following two facts.
First,
\sup_{s\in[0,\tau]}L_{s}=\sup_{s\in[0,\tau)}L_{s}
on the event
\{\tau>0\}
since
L_{s}
has only downward jumps.
Second, on the event
\{A^{\mathrm{cen}}_{r}>r^{4}(\log\tfrac{1}{r})^{7+12u},\,y\in\mathcal{D}_{\tau-},\,0<\tau\leq r\}
,
the components cut off by the center exploration within the time interval
[\tau,r]

contribute to
A^{\mathrm{cen}}_{r}
by at most the sum of
\nu(\mathcal{D}_{\tau})

and the area of the component cut off at time
\tau
, where the latter is counted only if it is at most
cr^{4}(\log\tfrac{1}{r})^{6+8u}
,
and hence by
y\in\mathcal{D}_{\tau-}
and
0<\tau\leq r
,

\displaystyle A_{[0,\tau]}

\displaystyle\geq A^{\mathrm{cen}}_{r}-\nu(\mathcal{D}_{\tau})-cr^{4}(\log\tfrac{1}{r})^{6+8u}

\displaystyle>r^{4}(\log\tfrac{1}{r})^{7+12u}-(c+1)r^{4}(\log\tfrac{1}{r})^{6+8u}\geq r^{4}(\log\tfrac{1}{r})^{7+11u}.

Consequently, we obtain

\displaystyle\mu_{\mathrm{BD}}^{L=\ell}[E^{\mathrm{cen}}_{r}\cap\{A^{\mathrm{cen}}_{r}>r^{4}(\log\tfrac{1}{r})^{7+12u}\}\mid V_{r}]\leq 54b_{0}^{-1}c^{3/2}r^{2}(\log\tfrac{1}{r})^{3+4u}\mu_{\mathrm{BM}}[\widetilde{E}_{r}],

which has been already shown in Step 3 to decay to
0
as
r\to 0
with a decay rate (independent of
\ell\in(0,r^{2}\log(r^{-1})^{3+4u})
and) faster than any polynomial of
r
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
4.1
, upper bound.

Suppose that
(\mathcal{S},d,\nu,x,y)
has the law
\mu_{\mathrm{BM}}
conditioned on having area at least
cr^{4}(\log\frac{1}{r})^{6+8u}
.
As we mentioned earlier, we will prove the upper bound by dominating the amount of area in
B(x,r)
from above using a subcritical Galton-Watson tree.

Fix
c\geq 1
. We will adjust its value later in the proof.
Let
E_{0,r}
denote the event that any component of
\mathcal{S}\setminus\overline{B(x,s)}

has boundary length at most
r^{2}(\log\frac{1}{r})^{3+4u}
for any
s\in(0,r(\log\frac{1}{r})^{1+u}]
,
so that
\mu_{\mathrm{BM}}[(E_{0,r})^{c}]
decays to
0
as
r\to 0
faster than any polynomial of
r
by Lemma
4.7
.
Let
E_{r}
be as in the statement of Lemma
4.9
.
Moreover, arguing as in the paragraph after (
4.13
) implies that

\displaystyle\mu_{\mathrm{BM}}\big[\nu(\mathcal{S})\geq cr^{4}\log(r^{-1})^{6+8u}\big]=c_{0}\int_{t\geq cr^{4}\log(r^{-1})^{6+8u}}t^{-2}dt=c_{0}c^{-1}r^{-4}\log(r^{-1})^{-6-8u}

for some universal constant
c_{0}\in(0,\infty)
. Combining the above, we obtain that
\mu_{\mathrm{BM}}\big[(E_{0,r})^{c}\,|\,\nu(\mathcal{S})\geq cr^{4}\log(r^{-1})^{6+8u}\big]
decays to
0
as
r\to 0
faster than any polynomial of
r
. Furthermore, applying Lemma
4.6
with
c=\log(r^{-1})^{3+4u}
implies that
\mu_{\mathrm{BM}}\big[(E_{r})^{c}\big]\leq m_{0}\exp(-c_{0}\log(r^{-1})^{3+4u})r^{-2}
for all
r\in(0,1)
sufficiently small, for some universal constants
m_{0},c_{0}\in(0,\infty)
. It follows that
\mu_{\mathrm{BM}}\big[(E_{r})^{c}\,|\,\nu(\mathcal{S})\geq cr^{4}\log(r^{-1})^{6+8u}\big]
decays to
0
as
r\to 0
faster than any polynomial of
r
. Next, we consider the exploration described just above Lemma
4.9
and let
\mathbb{P}
be the probability measure given by conditioning
\mu_{\mathrm{BM}}
on the positive probability event
\{\nu(\mathcal{S})\geq cr^{4}\log(r^{-1})^{6+8u}\}
. Lemma
4.9
implies that the number
N
of components cut off by
B(x,r)
with area at least
cr^{4}\log(r^{-1})^{6+8u}
satisfies
\mathbb{P}\big[E_{r}\cap\{N\geq n\}\big]\leq(1-\delta)\widetilde{p}_{c}^{n-1}
for each
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
c\geq c_{0}
, where
c_{0}\geq 1,\delta\in(0,1)
are as in the statement of Lemma
4.9
. We assume that we have chosen
c
sufficiently large so that
\sum_{n\geq 1}n(1-\delta)\widetilde{p}_{c}^{n-1}<1
. Note that each of these
N
holes are conditionally independent given their boundary lengths. If the boundary length of such a hole is
\ell
, then we recall that its law is given by
\mu_{\mathrm{BD}}^{L=\ell}
conditioned on having area at least
cr^{4}\log(r^{-1})^{6+8u}
. We then branch the exploration into each of these holes by performing the center exploration and then proceed as in Lemma
4.9
. Then Lemma
4.9
implies that inside of each hole, the number of additional holes which are cut off and have area at least
cr^{4}\log(r^{-1})^{6+8u}
is stochastically dominated by a random variable with mean strictly smaller than
1
and with an exponential tail. We have thus shown that the number of holes discovered in the entire branching exploration is dominated from above by a Galton-Watson process with offspring distribution given by a distribution with mean strictly smaller than
1
and with an exponential tail. By our choice of
c
, this Galton-Watson process is subcritical, so that the law of the total progeny exhibits exponential tails. Therefore, the probability that the Galton-Watson tree has more than
\log(r^{-1})^{1+u}
nodes decays to
0
as
r\to 0
faster than any power of
r
.

Next, we note that
[
77
, Lemma 4.12]
implies that the metric net
\cup_{r\geq 0}\partial B^{\bullet}(x,r)
has
\nu
-measure zero for
\mu_{\mathrm{BM}}
-a.e. instance of
(\mathcal{S},d,\nu,x,y)
. We claim that the same is true if
(\mathcal{S},d,\nu,x,y)
is sampled from
\mu_{\mathrm{BD,W}}^{L=\ell}
instead, for each
\ell>0
. Indeed, we fix
r>0
and perform the metric exploration from a sample
(\mathcal{S},d,\nu,x,y)
from
\mu_{\mathrm{BM}}
starting from
x
and targeted at
y
. Then we know that the conditional law of
\mathcal{S}\setminus B^{\bullet}(x,r)
given
L_{r}
is equal to
\mu_{\mathrm{BD,W}}^{L=L_{r}}
. Also, the metric net of
\mathcal{S}\setminus B^{\bullet}(x,r)
is contained in the metric net of
(\mathcal{S},d,\nu,x,y)
and so the claim follows for a sample from
\mu_{\mathrm{BD,W}}^{L=L_{r}}
. Combining with the rescaling property of the Brownian map, we obtain that the claim is true for
\mu_{\mathrm{BD,W}}^{L=\ell}
for each
\ell>0
. Furthermore, observing that the probability measures
\mu_{\mathrm{BD,W}}^{L=\ell}
and
\mu_{\mathrm{BD}}^{L=\ell}
are mutually absolutely continuous for each
\ell>0
, and combining with the scaling property of the Brownian disk, we obtain that the metric net of a sample from
\mu_{\mathrm{BD}}^{L=\ell}
has area zero
\mu_{\mathrm{BD}}^{L=\ell}
-a.e. for each
\ell>0
. Therefore, combining everything we obtain that on the event that
E_{0,r}\cap E_{r}
holds and the Galton-Watson tree has at most
\log(r^{-1})^{1+u}
nodes, we see from Lemma
4.9
that the total mount of area in
B(x,r)
is at most
r^{4}\log(r^{-1})^{8+13u}
off an event whose probability decays to zero as
r\to 0
faster than any power of
r
under
\mathbb{P}
. It follows that the probability that
\nu(B(x,r))>cr^{4}\log(r^{-1})^{8+13u}
under
\mathbb{P}
decays to zero as
r\to 0
faster than any power of
r
. Hence, by picking i.i.d. points from
\frac{\nu}{\nu(\mathcal{S})}
and arguing as in the proof of the lower bound of the theorem, we obtain that
\nu(B(z,r))\leq cr^{4}\log(r^{-1})^{8+14u}
for each
z\in\mathcal{S}
, off an event whose probability under
\mathbb{P}
decays to zero as
r\to 0
faster than any power of
r
. Therefore the proof is complete by observing that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}=\frac{\int_{t\geq cr^{4}\log(r^{-1})^{6+8u}}t^{-3/2}\mu_{\mathrm{BM}}^{A=t}dt}{c^{-1}r^{-4}\log(r^{-1})^{6+8u}}

and combining with the scaling property of the Brownian map.
∎

Remark 4.11
.

Alternatively, the upper bound in Theorem
4.1
can be deduced directly from a result in
[
65
]
. In fact, for a “typical point”
x
,
meaning a point
x
chosen uniformly according to the volume measure, it has been shown in

[
65
, Proposition 11]
that the
k
-th moment of
\nu(B(x,r))
is bounded above by
C_{0}^{k}\,k!\,r^{4k}
,
where
C_{0}
is a constant. Hence, for any
\lambda\in(0,C_{0})
the expectation of
\exp(\lambda\nu(B(x,r))/r^{4}))

is finite, so the probability that
\nu(B(x,r))
is at least a constant times
r^{4}\log(r^{-1})

decays to zero faster than any polynomial, and the upper bound in Theorem
4.1

follows by a union bound. However, we decided to include the above proof based on a branching argument since it is different from the proof in
[
65
]
and it does not rely on such precise moment estimates, so may be of independent interest.

5.
Percolation exploration

Later in this work, we are going to use
{\rm SLE}_{6}
chunks in percolation style arguments
as illustrated in Proposition
5.1
below, the proof of which
is the purpose of this long section. As necessary preparations for its statement,
in Subsection
5.1
we introduce a suitable state space
\mathbb{M}^{\mathrm{CPU}}_{2}

for random quantum surfaces and certain
\mathbb{M}^{\mathrm{CPU}}_{2}
-valued random variables defined through
{\rm SLE}_{6}

explorations of quantum disks and wedges considered in Subsection
3.2.4
.
In Subsection
5.2
we state the main result of this
section (Proposition
5.1
), which formulates a percolation
argument in the setting of a quantum disk weighted by its area, and its analog in the
simpler setting of a quantum wedge (Proposition
5.2
).
We first prove the latter in Subsection
5.3
, and
then the former in Subsection
5.4
on the basis of the latter.

5.1.
Preliminaries:
{\rm SLE}_{6}
hulls as random curve-decorated quantum surfaces

Throughout, we consider a radial
{\rm SLE}_{6}
process
\eta^{\prime}
on
\mathbb{D}
targeted at
0
.
Let
t\in[0,\inf(\eta^{\prime})^{-1}(0))
. In the same way as in Subsection
3.2.4

(see also Appendix
A
), we define the
hull

K_{t}
of
\eta^{\prime}([0,t])

as the complement in
\mathbb{D}
of the
0
-containing component of
\mathbb{D}\setminus\eta^{\prime}([0,t])
, and
we divide the boundary
\partial K_{t}
of
K_{t}
into the
top

\partial K_{t}\cap\mathbb{D}
of
K_{t}
and the

bottom

\partial K_{t}\cap\partial\mathbb{D}
of
K_{t}
. On the event
\{\partial K_{t}\cap\partial\mathbb{D}\not=\partial\mathbb{D}\}
,
i.e., that the bottom of
K_{t}
is not the whole of the unit circle
\partial\mathbb{D}
, we can further divide the top (resp. bottom)
into its left and right sides: the
left
(resp.
right
)
side

of the top is the part which is to the left (resp. right) of
\eta^{\prime}(t)
, and the
left
(resp.
right
)
side

of the bottom is the part which is to the left (resp. right) of
\eta^{\prime}(0)
.

Moreover, we also consider below the space
\mathbb{M}^{\mathrm{CPU}}_{2}
of

curve-decorated quantum surfaces with two marked boundary points
, following
[
43
, Subsection 2.2.5]
.
It is defined as the set of equivalence classes modulo conformal maps of quintuples
(D,\mu,\eta,x,y)
of a simply connected domain

D\subsetneq\mathbb{C}
, a Radon measure
\mu
on
D
, a continuous map
\eta:[0,\infty]\to D\cup\widetilde{\partial}D

with
\eta(0)\in\widetilde{\partial}D
, and
x,y\in\widetilde{\partial}D\setminus\{\eta(0)\}
with
x\not=y

and
\eta(0)\in[x,y]_{\widetilde{\partial}D}^{{\bm{\circlearrowleft}}}
, where
\widetilde{\partial}D
denotes the set of prime ends of
D

and
[x,y]_{\widetilde{\partial}D}^{{\bm{\circlearrowleft}}}
the counterclockwise arc of
\widetilde{\partial}D
from
x
to
y
.
Noting that each equivalence class
\mathcal{K}\in\mathbb{M}^{\mathrm{CPU}}_{2}
has a unique representative of the form

(\mathbb{D},\mu_{\mathcal{K}},\eta_{\mathcal{K}},-\sqrt{-1},\sqrt{-1})
with
\eta_{\mathcal{K}}(0)=1
by the Riemann mapping theorem,
we equip
\mathbb{M}^{\mathrm{CPU}}_{2}
with the
conformal Prokhorov-uniform metric

\mymathbb{d}^{\mathrm{CPU}}_{2}
given by

(5.1)

\mymathbb{d}^{\mathrm{CPU}}_{2}(\mathcal{K}_{1},\mathcal{K}_{2}):=\mymathbb{d}^{\mathrm{P}}_{\mathbb{D}}(\mu_{\mathcal{K}_{1}},\mu_{\mathcal{K}_{2}})+\mymathbb{d}^{\mathrm{U}}_{\mathbb{D}}(\eta_{\mathcal{K}_{1}},\eta_{\mathcal{K}_{2}}),\qquad\mathcal{K}_{1},\mathcal{K}_{2}\in\mathbb{M}^{\mathrm{CPU}}_{2},

where
\mymathbb{d}^{\mathrm{P}}_{\mathbb{D}}
is a complete metric
on the space of Radon measures on
\mathbb{D}
compatible with the vague topology and

\mymathbb{d}^{\mathrm{U}}_{\mathbb{D}}(\eta_{1},\eta_{2}):=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}2^{-n}\sup_{t\in[0,n]}|\eta_{1}(t)-\eta_{2}(t)|
,
so that
\mathbb{M}^{\mathrm{CPU}}_{2}
becomes a complete separable metric space.
Note that a quantum surface
(D,h,\eta,x,y)
parameterized by a simply connected domain

D\subsetneq\mathbb{C}
and equipped with a continuous map
\eta:[0,\infty]\to D\cup\widetilde{\partial}D

with
\eta(0)\in\widetilde{\partial}D
and two marked boundary points

x,y\in\widetilde{\partial}D\setminus\{\eta(0)\}
with
x\not=y
and
\eta(0)\in[x,y]_{\widetilde{\partial}D}^{{\bm{\circlearrowleft}}}

can be identified with an almost-surely defined random element
(D,\mu_{h},\eta,x,y)
of
\mathbb{M}^{\mathrm{CPU}}_{2}
,
since
h
is a measurable function of
\mu_{h}
by
[
18
, Theorem 1.1 and Remark 1.2]
.

{\rm SLE}_{6}
explorations of quantum surfaces as introduced in Subsection
3.2.4

naturally define
\mathbb{M}^{\mathrm{CPU}}_{2}
-valued random variables as follows. Let
\sigma
be an

\mathbb{M}^{\mathrm{CPU}}_{2}
-stopping time
, i.e., a function
\sigma:\mathbb{M}^{\mathrm{CPU}}_{2}\to[0,\infty]

which is a stopping time with respect to the filtration generated by the

\mathbb{M}^{\mathrm{CPU}}_{2}
-valued stochastic process
\{\mathcal{K}_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
on
\mathbb{M}^{\mathrm{CPU}}_{2}

given by
\mathcal{K}_{t}(D,\mu,\eta,x,y):=(D,\mu,\eta(\cdot\wedge t),x,y)
.
We define a Borel subset
E_{\sigma}
of
\mathbb{M}^{\mathrm{CPU}}_{2}
, considered as an event for
\omega\in\mathbb{M}^{\mathrm{CPU}}_{2}
, by

(5.2)

E_{\sigma}:=\Biggl\{\omega=(D_{\omega},\mu_{\omega},\eta_{\omega},x_{\omega},y_{\omega})\in\mathbb{M}^{\mathrm{CPU}}_{2}\Biggm|\begin{minipage}[180.0pt]$\sigma(\omega)\in(0,t_{\eta_{\omega}})$,
$\widetilde{\partial}K^{\omega}_{\sigma(\omega)}\cap\widetilde{\partial}D_{\omega}\not=\widetilde{\partial}D_{\omega}$,
$\eta_{\omega}(\sigma(\omega))\in\widetilde{\partial}D_{\omega}$
\end{minipage}\Biggr\},

where
t_{\eta_{\omega}}:=\inf\eta_{\omega}^{-1}(\eta_{\omega}(\infty))
and, for
t\in[0,t_{\eta_{\omega}})
,

K^{\omega}_{t}
denotes the complement in
D_{\omega}
of the component of

D_{\omega}\setminus\eta_{\omega}([0,t])
whose closure in
D_{\omega}\cup\widetilde{\partial}D_{\omega}
contains
\eta_{\omega}(\infty)
and

\widetilde{\partial}K^{\omega}_{t}
denotes its boundary in
D_{\omega}\cup\widetilde{\partial}D_{\omega}
.
Now let
\ell>0
, suppose that
\mathcal{D}=(\mathbb{D},h,0)
has law
\mu_{\mathrm{QD,W}}^{L=\ell}
, let

\eta^{\prime}
be an independent radial
{\rm SLE}_{6}
on
\mathcal{D}
starting from a uniformly random point
on
\partial\mathcal{D}
, targeted at
0
and parameterized by quantum natural time,
let
x_{\mathcal{D},\eta^{\prime}(0)},y_{\mathcal{D},\eta^{\prime}(0)}\in\partial\mathbb{D}\setminus\{\eta^{\prime}(0)\}
be such that
\eta^{\prime}(0)\in[x_{\mathcal{D},\eta^{\prime}(0)},y_{\mathcal{D},\eta^{\prime}(0)}]_{\partial\mathbb{D}}^{{\bm{\circlearrowleft}}}

and
\nu_{h}([x_{\mathcal{D},\eta^{\prime}(0)},\eta^{\prime}(0)]_{\partial\mathbb{D}}^{{\bm{\circlearrowleft}}})=\nu_{h}([\eta^{\prime}(0),y_{\mathcal{D},\eta^{\prime}(0)}]_{\partial\mathbb{D}}^{{\bm{\circlearrowleft}}})=\ell/4
,
and set
\sigma_{\mathcal{D}}:=\sigma(\mathbb{D},\mu_{h},\eta^{\prime},x_{\mathcal{D},\eta^{\prime}(0)},y_{\mathcal{D},\eta^{\prime}(0)})
.
Then we have
\eta^{\prime}([t_{\eta^{\prime}},\infty])=\{0\}
and
t_{\eta^{\prime}}=\inf(\eta^{\prime})^{-1}(0)<\infty
a.s., and
it follows from Proposition
A.1
that, a.s. on the event

(5.3)

\begin{split}E^{\mathcal{D}}_{\sigma}&:=\{(\mathbb{D},\mu_{h},\eta^{\prime},x_{\mathcal{D},\eta^{\prime}(0)},y_{\mathcal{D},\eta^{\prime}(0)})\in E_{\sigma}\}\\
&=\bigl\{\sigma_{\mathcal{D}}\in(0,t_{\eta^{\prime}}),\,\partial K_{\sigma_{\mathcal{D}}}\cap\partial\mathcal{D}\not=\partial\mathcal{D},\,\eta^{\prime}(\sigma_{\mathcal{D}})\in\partial\mathcal{D}\bigr\},\end{split}

K_{\sigma_{\mathcal{D}}}\setminus\partial K_{\sigma_{\mathcal{D}}}
is a Jordan domain in
\mathbb{C}
with boundary
\partial K_{\sigma_{\mathcal{D}}}
,

\eta^{\prime}([0,\sigma_{\mathcal{D}}])\subset K_{\sigma_{\mathcal{D}}}\cup\partial K_{\sigma_{\mathcal{D}}}
, and the bottom

\partial K_{\sigma_{\mathcal{D}}}\cap\partial\mathcal{D}
of
K_{\sigma_{\mathcal{D}}}
is a compact interval in
\partial\mathcal{D}

containing
\eta^{\prime}(0)
in its interior. In particular, letting

x_{\sigma_{\mathcal{D}}}
and
y_{\sigma_{\mathcal{D}}}
denote the endpoints of the left and right sides,
respectively, of the bottom of
K_{\sigma_{\mathcal{D}}}
other than
\eta^{\prime}(0)
, we see that

(5.4)

\mathcal{N}^{\mathcal{D}}_{\sigma}:=\bigl(K_{\sigma_{\mathcal{D}}}\setminus\partial K_{\sigma_{\mathcal{D}}},\mu_{h}|_{K_{\sigma_{\mathcal{D}}}\setminus\partial K_{\sigma_{\mathcal{D}}}},\eta^{\prime}(\cdot\wedge\sigma_{\mathcal{D}}),x_{\sigma_{\mathcal{D}}},y_{\sigma_{\mathcal{D}}}\bigr)

is an
\mathbb{M}^{\mathrm{CPU}}_{2}
-valued random variable defined a.s. on
E^{\mathcal{D}}_{\sigma}
.

We remark that the construction in the last paragraph can be applied also to a weight-
2
quantum wedge

\mathcal{W}=(\mathbb{H},h,0,\infty)
, an independent chordal
{\rm SLE}_{6}

\eta^{\prime}
on
\mathbb{H}
from
0
to
\infty
parameterized
by quantum natural time, and
x_{\mathcal{W}},y_{\mathcal{W}}\in\partial\mathbb{H}
with
x_{\mathcal{W}}<0<y_{\mathcal{W}}
and
\mu_{h}([x_{\mathcal{W}},0])=\mu_{h}([0,y_{\mathcal{W}}])=1
.
In this case we have
\eta^{\prime}(\infty)=\infty
and
t_{\eta^{\prime}}=\inf(\eta^{\prime})^{-1}(\infty)=\infty
a.s. and,
as introduced in Subsection
3.2.4
, the hull
K_{t}
of
\eta^{\prime}([0,t])
,
its top and bottom are defined for any
t\in[0,\infty)
in the same way as above
with the
0
-containing component replaced by the unbounded component.
Also for each
\mathbb{M}^{\mathrm{CPU}}_{2}
-stopping time
\sigma
, thanks to Proposition
A.2

an
\mathbb{M}^{\mathrm{CPU}}_{2}
-valued random variable
\mathcal{N}^{\mathcal{W}}_{\sigma}
is defined a.s. on the event

(5.5)

E^{\mathcal{W}}_{\sigma}:=\{(\mathbb{H},\mu_{h},\eta^{\prime},x_{\mathcal{W}},y_{\mathcal{W}})\in E_{\sigma}\}=\{\sigma_{\mathcal{W}}\in(0,\infty),\,\eta^{\prime}(\sigma_{\mathcal{W}})\in\partial\mathcal{W}\}

by (
5.4
) with
\sigma_{\mathcal{W}}
in place of
\sigma_{\mathcal{D}}
, where
\sigma_{\mathcal{W}}:=\sigma(\mathbb{H},\mu_{h},\eta^{\prime},x_{\mathcal{W}},y_{\mathcal{W}})
.

5.2.
Statement of percolation exploration

To state the main result of this section (Proposition
5.1
below),
for each
\delta\in(0,\infty)
we define an
\mathbb{M}^{\mathrm{CPU}}_{2}
-stopping time
\sigma_{\delta}
by

(5.6)

\sigma_{\delta}(\omega):=\inf\bigl\{t\in[\delta,\infty)\bigm|\text{$t<t_{\eta_{\omega}}$,
$\widetilde{\partial}K^{\omega}_{t}\cap\widetilde{\partial}D_{\omega}\not=\widetilde{\partial}D_{\omega}$,
$\eta_{\omega}(t)\in\widetilde{\partial}D_{\omega}$}\bigr\}

for
\omega=(D_{\omega},\mu_{\omega},\eta_{\omega},x_{\omega},y_{\omega})\in\mathbb{M}^{\mathrm{CPU}}_{2}

(recall that
t_{\eta_{\omega}}:=\inf\eta_{\omega}^{-1}(\eta_{\omega}(\infty))
).

Proposition 5.1
.

There exist
A_{0}\in[2,\infty)
and
c_{0,\max}\in(0,\infty)
such that for any

A\in[A_{0},\infty)
and any
c_{0}\in(0,c_{0,\max}]
the following is true with

\epsilon_{0}:=c_{0}A^{-2/3}
. Let
\delta>0
, set

\sigma:=\sigma_{\delta/A}\wedge\delta
and let
E
be any Borel subset of
\mathbb{M}^{\mathrm{CPU}}_{2}

such that
\mu_{\mathrm{QW}}^{W=2}\bigl[E^{\mathcal{W}}_{\sigma}\cap\{\mathcal{N}^{\mathcal{W}}_{\sigma}\in E\}\bigm|\sigma<\delta\bigr]\geq 1-\epsilon_{0}
.
Suppose that
\mathcal{D}=(\mathbb{D},h,0)
has law
\mu_{\mathrm{QD,W}}^{L=1}
,
and consider the following exploration of
\mathcal{D}
by radial
{\rm SLE}_{6}
curves.
Set
D_{0}:=\mathbb{D}
,
\mathcal{D}_{0}:=\mathcal{D}
, let
\eta^{\prime}_{0}
be a radial
{\rm SLE}_{6}
on
\mathcal{D}_{0}
starting
from a uniformly random point on
\partial\mathcal{D}_{0}
and targeted at
0
, and set
\Xi_{0}:=\emptyset
.
We then inductively define a sequence
\{(\mathcal{D}_{j}=(D_{j},h|_{D_{j}},0),\eta^{\prime}_{j},\Xi_{j})\}_{j\geq 0}

of triples of a quantum disk
\mathcal{D}_{j}
weighted by its area, a radial
{\rm SLE}_{6}

\eta^{\prime}_{j}
on
\mathcal{D}_{j}

starting from a uniformly random point
\eta^{\prime}_{j}(0)
on
\partial\mathcal{D}_{j}
and targeted at
0
,
and a
2^{\mathbb{Z}\cap[0,j)}
-valued random variable
\Xi_{j}
, as follows.

•

Set
\sigma_{j}:=\sigma\bigl(D_{j},\mu_{h|_{D_{j}}},\eta^{\prime}_{j},x_{\mathcal{D}_{j},\eta^{\prime}_{j}(0)},y_{\mathcal{D}_{j},\eta^{\prime}_{j}(0)}\bigr)

and
E_{j}:=E^{\mathcal{D}_{j}}_{\sigma}\cap\{\sigma_{j}<\delta,\,\mathcal{N}^{\mathcal{D}_{j}}_{\sigma}\in E\}
.
If
\sigma_{j}\geq t_{\eta^{\prime}_{j}}
, set
D_{j+1}:=D_{j}
,
\mathcal{D}_{j+1}:=\mathcal{D}_{j}
,

\eta^{\prime}_{j+1}:=\eta^{\prime}_{j}
and
\Xi_{j+1}:=\Xi_{j}
. If
\sigma_{j}<t_{\eta^{\prime}_{j}}
,
let
D_{j+1}
be the
0
-containing component of
D_{j}\setminus\eta^{\prime}_{j}([0,\sigma_{j}])
,
set
K^{j}_{\sigma_{j}}:=D_{j}\setminus D_{j+1}
, and let
\mathcal{N}_{j}
and
\mathcal{D}_{j+1}

be the quantum surfaces parameterized by
K^{j}_{\sigma_{j}}\setminus\partial K^{j}_{\sigma_{j}}

and
D_{j+1}
, respectively. If
E_{j}
occurs, we also say that
E
occurs for
\mathcal{N}_{j}
.

•

If
\sigma_{j}<t_{\eta^{\prime}_{j}}
, we take an independent radial
{\rm SLE}_{6}

\eta^{\prime}_{j+1}

on
\mathcal{D}_{j+1}
targeted at
0
, choosing its initial point
\eta^{\prime}_{j+1}(0)
and
\Xi_{j+1}
according to the following rule:

(a)

If
E_{j}
occurs, let
\eta_{j+1}^{\prime}(0)
be the leftmost point of
\partial(\mathcal{D}_{j}\setminus\mathcal{D}_{j+1})\cap\partial\mathcal{D}_{j}

and set
\Xi_{j+1}:=\{j\}\cup\{i\in\Xi_{j}\mid\partial\mathcal{D}_{j+1}\cap\partial\mathcal{N}_{i}\not=\emptyset\}
.

(b)

If
E_{j}
does not occur,

\widetilde{\partial}K^{j}_{\sigma_{j}}\cap\widetilde{\partial}D_{j}\not=\widetilde{\partial}D_{j}
and

\bigcup_{i\in\Xi_{j}}(\partial\mathcal{D}_{j+1}\cap\partial\mathcal{N}_{i})\not=\emptyset
,
noting that
[a_{j},b_{j}]_{\partial\mathcal{D}_{j+1}}^{{\bm{\circlearrowleft}}}=\bigcup_{i\in\Xi_{j}}(\partial\mathcal{D}_{j+1}\cap\partial\mathcal{N}_{i})

for unique
a_{j},b_{j}\in\partial\mathcal{D}_{j+1}
,
let
\eta^{\prime}_{j+1}(0)
be the first point on

[a_{j},b_{j}]_{\partial\mathcal{D}_{j+1}}^{{\bm{\circlearrowleft}}}
from
a_{j}
that belongs to

(5.7)

\{b_{j}\}\cup\bigcup\nolimits_{\text{$i\in\Xi_{j}$,\,$\partial\mathcal{D}_{j+1}\cap\partial\mathcal{N}_{i}$ has quantum length at least $\epsilon_{0}\delta^{2/3}$}}\partial\mathcal{N}_{i},

and set
\Xi_{j+1}:=\bigl\{i\in\Xi_{j}\bigm|\emptyset\not=\partial\mathcal{D}_{j+1}\cap\partial\mathcal{N}_{i}\subset[\eta^{\prime}_{j+1}(0),b_{j}]_{\partial\mathcal{D}_{j+1}}^{{\bm{\circlearrowleft}}}\bigr\}
,
where
[b_{j},b_{j}]_{\partial\mathcal{D}_{j+1}}^{{\bm{\circlearrowleft}}}:=\{b_{j}\}
.

(c)

If
E_{j}
does not occur,

\widetilde{\partial}K^{j}_{\sigma_{j}}\cap\widetilde{\partial}D_{j}\not=\widetilde{\partial}D_{j}
and

\bigcup_{i\in\Xi_{j}}(\partial\mathcal{D}_{j+1}\cap\partial\mathcal{N}_{i})=\emptyset
,
let
\eta_{j+1}^{\prime}(0)
be the rightmost point of
\partial(\mathcal{D}_{j}\setminus\mathcal{D}_{j+1})\cap\partial\mathcal{D}_{j}

and set
\Xi_{j+1}:=\emptyset
.

(d)

If
E_{j}
does not occur and

\widetilde{\partial}K^{j}_{\sigma_{j}}\cap\widetilde{\partial}D_{j}=\widetilde{\partial}D_{j}
,
let
\eta^{\prime}_{j+1}(0):=\eta^{\prime}_{j}(\sigma_{j})
and set
\Xi_{j+1}:=\emptyset
.

Noting for any
j\geq 0
that
\bigl\{\text{$\sigma_{j}<t_{\eta^{\prime}_{j}}$, $\partial\mathcal{D}_{j+1}\subset\bigcup_{i\in\Xi_{j+1}}\partial\mathcal{N}_{i}$}\bigr\}\subset E_{j}

and that on
E_{j}
the bottom left of
\mathcal{D}_{j}\setminus\mathcal{D}_{j+1}
can be written as

[x_{\sigma_{\mathcal{D}_{j}}},\eta_{j}^{\prime}(0)]_{\partial\mathcal{N}_{j}}^{{\bm{\circlearrowleft}}}
with

x_{\sigma_{\mathcal{D}_{j}}}
as in (
5.4
), set

(5.8)

N_{\delta}:=\min\biggl\{j\geq 0\biggm|\begin{minipage}[240.5pt]$\sigma_{j}<t_{\eta^{\prime}_{j}}$, $\partial\mathcal{D}_{j+1}\subset\bigcup_{i\in\Xi_{j+1}}\partial\mathcal{N}_{i}$,
$[x_{\sigma_{\mathcal{D}_{j}}},\eta_{j}^{\prime}(0)]_{\partial\mathcal{N}_{j}}^{{\bm{\circlearrowleft}}}\cap\bigcup_{i\in\Xi_{j}}(\partial\mathcal{D}_{j}\cap\partial\mathcal{N}_{i})$
has quantum length at least $\epsilon_{0}\delta^{2/3}$
\end{minipage}\biggr\}.

Let
u>0
and define an event
E_{u,\delta}
by

(5.9)

E_{u,\delta}:=\biggl\{\begin{minipage}[306.0pt]for any $j\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[0,\delta^{-2/3-u}]$ and any $t\in[0,\sigma_{j}]$,
the boundary length of the $0$-containing component of $D_{j}\setminus\eta^{\prime}_{j}([0,t])$
is at least $\delta^{2/3-u}$
\end{minipage}\biggr\}.

Then there exist
c_{1},c_{2},a\in(0,\infty)
determined solely by
A,c_{0},u
such that the following hold:

(i)

\mathbb{P}\bigl[E_{u,\delta}\cap\{N_{\delta}\geq\delta^{-2/3-u}\}\bigr]\leq c_{1}\exp(-c_{2}\delta^{-a})
.

(ii)

(5.10)

\mathbb{P}\!\left[E_{u,\delta}\setminus\left\{\begin{minipage}[230.0pt]for each $0\leq i\leq N_{\delta}$ there exist $n\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[0,\delta^{-u}]$ and
$\{i_{j}\}_{j=1}^{n}\subset\{0,\ldots,N_{\delta}\}$ such that $\partial\mathcal{N}_{i_{n}}\cap\partial\mathcal{D}_{0}\not=\emptyset$,
$i_{1}=i$ and $\partial\mathcal{N}_{i_{j}}\cap\partial\mathcal{N}_{i_{j+1}}\not=\emptyset$ for each $1\leq j<n$
\end{minipage}\right\}\right]\leq c_{1}\exp(-c_{2}\delta^{-a}).

Figure 2.

Left:
Illustration of the setup of Proposition
5.1
. The embedding of
\mathcal{D}
into
\mathbb{D}
is taken so that the interior (resp. boundary) marked point is
0
(resp.
-i
). Chunks for which the event
E
occurs (resp. does not occur) are shown in green (resp. red). We will choose the event
E
so that the
\mathcal{N}_{i}
for which it occurs are necessarily homeomorphic to
\mathbb{D}
, which is why the green chunks have this property while the red chunks do not. Shown is the event that the chunks for which
E
occurs disconnects
\partial\mathbb{D}
from
0
.
Right:
Illustration of the setup for Proposition
5.2
, with the same color scheme as on the left.

The first step in the proof of Proposition
5.1
is a related result in the setting of the half-plane. This setting will be slightly easier to prove because the law of a weight-
2
quantum wedge is invariant under the operation of exploring a chordal
{\rm SLE}_{6}
curve for a given amount of quantum natural time.

Proposition 5.2
.

There exist
A_{0}\in[2,\infty)
and
c_{0,\max}\in(0,\infty)
such that for any

A\in[A_{0},\infty)
and any
c_{0}\in(0,c_{0,\max}]
the following is true with
\epsilon_{0}:=c_{0}A^{-2/3}
.
Set
\sigma:=\sigma_{1/A}\wedge 1
and let
E
be any Borel subset of
\mathbb{M}^{\mathrm{CPU}}_{2}
such that

\mu_{\mathrm{QW}}^{W=2}\bigl[E^{\mathcal{W}}_{\sigma}\cap\{\mathcal{N}^{\mathcal{W}}_{\sigma}\in E\}\bigm|\sigma<1\bigr]\geq 1-\epsilon_{0}
.
Suppose that
\mathcal{W}=(\mathbb{H},h,0,\infty)
has law
\mu_{\mathrm{QW}}^{W=2}
, and consider the following
exploration of
\mathcal{W}
by chordal
{\rm SLE}_{6}
curves. Set
H_{0}:=\mathbb{H}
,
\mathcal{W}_{0}:=\mathcal{W}
and
let
\eta^{\prime}_{0}
be a chordal
{\rm SLE}_{6}
on
\mathcal{W}_{0}
from
0
to
\infty
.
We then inductively define a sequence
\{(\mathcal{W}_{j}=(H_{j},h|_{H_{j}},\eta^{\prime}_{j}(0),\infty),\eta^{\prime}_{j})\}_{j\geq 0}

of pairs of a quantum wedge
\mathcal{W}_{j}
and a chordal
{\rm SLE}_{6}

\eta^{\prime}_{j}
on
\mathcal{W}_{j}
as follows.

•

Set
\sigma_{j}:=\sigma(H_{j},\mu_{h|_{H_{j}}},\eta^{\prime}_{j},x_{\mathcal{W}_{j}},y_{\mathcal{W}_{j}})

and
E_{j}:=E^{\mathcal{W}_{j}}_{\sigma}\cap\{\sigma_{j}<1,\,\mathcal{N}^{\mathcal{W}_{j}}_{\sigma}\in E\}
.
Let
H_{j+1}
be the unbounded component of
H_{j}\setminus\eta^{\prime}_{j}([0,\sigma_{j}])
,
set
K^{j}_{\sigma_{j}}:=H_{j}\setminus H_{j+1}
, and let
\mathcal{N}_{j}
and
\mathcal{W}_{j+1}

be the quantum surfaces parameterized by
K^{j}_{\sigma_{j}}\setminus\partial K^{j}_{\sigma_{j}}

and
H_{j+1}
, respectively. If
E_{j}
occurs, we also say that
E
occurs for
\mathcal{N}_{j}
.

•

Let
\eta_{j+1}^{\prime}
be an independent chordal
{\rm SLE}_{6}
on
\mathcal{W}_{j}
to
\infty

whose initial point
\eta^{\prime}_{j+1}(0)
is chosen according to the following rule.
If
E_{j}
occurs, let
\eta_{j+1}^{\prime}(0)
be the leftmost point of

\partial(\mathcal{W}_{j}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{j+1})\cap\partial\mathcal{W}_{j}
. If
E_{j}
does not occur,
let
\eta^{\prime}_{j+1}(0)
be the first point on
\partial\mathcal{W}_{j+1}
that is to the right
of the rightmost point of
\partial(\mathcal{W}_{j}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{j+1})\cap\partial\mathcal{W}_{j}

and belongs to

(5.11)

(\partial\mathcal{W}_{j+1}\cap\partial\mathcal{W}_{0})\cup\bigcup\nolimits_{\text{$0\leq i<j$,\,$\partial\mathcal{W}_{j+1}\cap\partial\mathcal{N}_{i}$ has quantum length at least $\epsilon_{0}$}}\partial\mathcal{N}_{i}.

Let
L_{j}
denote the quantum length of the top left of
\mathcal{W}_{0}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{j}

(i.e., the part of
\partial(\mathcal{W}_{0}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{j})\cap\mathcal{W}_{0}
which is to the left of
\eta^{\prime}_{j}(0)
)
minus the quantum length of the bottom left of
\mathcal{W}_{0}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{j}

(i.e., the part of
\partial(\mathcal{W}_{0}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{j})\cap\partial\mathcal{W}_{0}
which is to the left of
0
).
Equivalently, set
L_{0}:=0
and let
L_{j+1}-L_{j}
be the quantum length of the part of
\partial\mathcal{W}_{j+1}

from
\eta^{\prime}_{j+1}(0)
to the leftmost point of
\partial(\mathcal{W}_{j}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{j+1})\cap\partial\mathcal{W}_{j}

minus the quantum length of the bottom left of
\mathcal{W}_{j}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{j+1}

(i.e., the part of
\partial(\mathcal{W}_{j}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{j+1})\cap\partial\mathcal{W}_{j}
which is to the left of
\eta^{\prime}_{j}(0)
).
Also let
R^{\mathrm{bot}}_{j}
denote the quantum length of the bottom right of
\mathcal{W}_{0}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{j}

(i.e., the part of
\partial(\mathcal{W}_{0}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{j})\cap\partial\mathcal{W}_{0}
which is to the right of
0
).
Then the following hold:

(i)

There exist constants
c_{1},c_{2}\in(0,\infty)
determined solely by
A,c_{0}

such that
\mathbb{P}[L_{N}\geq-c_{1}N]\leq\exp(-c_{2}N)
for any
N\in\mathbb{N}
.
More precisely,
c_{1}
can be chosen as
c_{1}=\frac{1}{16}cA^{-2/3}\log A

with
c\in(0,\infty)
the constant in Proposition
B.3
.

(ii)

For each
u\in(0,\frac{1}{2})
, there exists a constant
c_{3}\in(0,\infty)
determined solely by
u

such that
\mathbb{P}[R^{\mathrm{bot}}_{N}\geq N/10]\leq c_{3}N^{u-1/2}
for any
N\in\mathbb{N}
.

(iii)

There exist constants
c_{4},c_{5},c_{6}\in(0,\infty)

determined solely by
A,c_{0}
such that for any
u\in[2,\infty)
and any
N\in\mathbb{N}
,

(5.12)

\mathbb{P}\!\left[\begin{minipage}[235.0pt]for each $i\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[0,N]$ there exist $n\in\mathbb{N}\cap[1,c_{4}u^{2}]$ and
$\{i_{j}\}_{j=1}^{n}\subset\mathbb{Z}\cap[0,N]$ such that $i_{1}=i$, $\partial\mathcal{N}_{i_{n}}\cap\partial\mathcal{W}_{0}\neq\emptyset$
and $\partial\mathcal{N}_{i_{j}}\cap\partial\mathcal{N}_{i_{j+1}}\neq\emptyset$ for each $1\leq j<n$
\end{minipage}\right]\geq 1-c_{5}N^{2}\exp(-c_{6}u).

(iv)

There exist constants
c_{7},c_{8},c_{9}\in(0,\infty)

determined solely by
A,c_{0}
such that, with
\partial_{\mathrm{L}}\mathcal{W}:=(-\infty,0)\subset\partial\mathcal{W}
,
for any
u\in[2,\infty)
and any
N\in\mathbb{N}\cap[c_{7}u^{3},\infty)
,

(5.13)

\mathbb{P}\biggl[\begin{minipage}[245.0pt]for each $i\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[0,N]$ there exists $j\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[0,N)$
such that $\lvert j-i\rvert\leq c_{7}u^{3}$, ${\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{j}\cap E_{j+1}}=1$
and $\partial\mathcal{N}_{j}\cap\partial_{\mathrm{L}}\mathcal{W}\not=\emptyset$
\end{minipage}\biggr]\geq 1-c_{8}N^{2}\exp(-c_{9}u).

5.3.
Proof of Proposition
5.2

The proof of Proposition
5.2
is long and divided
into several steps. Until the end of Subsection
5.3
,
we fix the situation of the statement of Proposition
5.2
,
with
A\in[2,\infty)
and
c_{0}\in(0,\infty)
arbitrary and the way of choosing

A_{0},c_{0,\max}
specified in the course of the proof, and we also fix the following setting.
Recall that for each
j\geq 0
,
\mathcal{W}_{j}
is a weight-
2
quantum wedge independent of

\{(\mathcal{W}_{i}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{i+1},h|_{\mathcal{W}_{i}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{i+1}},\eta^{\prime}_{i}|_{[0,\sigma_{i}]},\widetilde{L}_{i})\}_{0\leq i<j}

by the properties of quantum wedges described in Subsections
3.2.2
and
3.2.4
,
where
\widetilde{L}_{i}
denotes the quantum length of the part of
\partial\mathcal{W}_{i+1}

from
\eta^{\prime}_{i+1}(0)
to the leftmost point of
\partial(\mathcal{W}_{i}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{i+1})\cap\partial\mathcal{W}_{i}
.
Let
\{L^{j}_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
(resp.
\{R^{j}_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
) be the left
(resp. right) boundary length process associated with
(\mathcal{W}_{j},\eta^{\prime}_{j})

as introduced in Subsection
3.2.4
,
let
T^{j}
denote the quantum length of the top
\partial(\mathcal{W}_{j}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{j+1})\cap\mathcal{W}_{j}

of
\mathcal{W}_{j}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{j+1}
and
B^{j}_{L}
(resp.
B^{j}_{R}
) the quantum
length of the bottom left (resp. bottom right) of
\mathcal{W}_{j}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{j+1}
.
Then
(\{L^{j}_{t}\}_{t\geq 0},\{R^{j}_{t}\}_{t\geq 0})
is a pair of independent

3/2
-stable Lévy processes with only downward jumps and is independent of

\{(\{L^{i}_{t}\}_{t\in[0,\sigma_{i}]},\{R^{i}_{t}\}_{t\in[0,\sigma_{i}]})\}_{0\leq i<j}
for each
j\geq 0
,
the sequence
\{(\{L^{j}_{t}\}_{t\in[0,\sigma_{j}]},\{R^{j}_{t}\}_{t\in[0,\sigma_{j}]})\}_{j=0}^{{}^{\infty}}

is i.i.d., and we have

(5.14)

\displaystyle 0\leq T^{j}

\displaystyle=(L^{j}_{\sigma_{j}}-\inf\nolimits_{0\leq s\leq\sigma_{j}}L^{j}_{s})+(R^{j}_{\sigma_{j}}-\inf\nolimits_{0\leq s\leq\sigma_{j}}R^{j}_{s}),

(5.15)

\displaystyle 0<B^{j}_{L}

\displaystyle=-\inf\nolimits_{0\leq s\leq\sigma_{j}}L^{j}_{s},\qquad 0<B^{j}_{R}=-\inf\nolimits_{0\leq s\leq\sigma_{j}}R^{j}_{s},

(5.16)

\displaystyle\sigma_{j}

\displaystyle=(\inf\{t\in[1/A,\infty)\mid\eta^{\prime}_{j}(t)\in\partial\mathcal{W}_{j}\})\wedge 1=(\tau^{L}_{j}\wedge\tau^{R}_{j})\wedge 1

(for (
5.16
) recall (
5.6
)),
where
\tau^{L}_{j}:=\inf\{t\in[1/A,\infty)\mid L^{j}_{t}=\inf_{0\leq s\leq t}L^{j}_{s}\}

and
\tau^{R}_{j}:=\inf\{t\in[1/A,\infty)\mid R^{j}_{t}=\inf\nolimits_{0\leq s\leq t}R^{j}_{s}\}
.
Also, following Appendix
B
, let
X^{1},X^{2}
be i.i.d.
3/2
-stable Lévy
processes with only downward jumps and starting from
0
, and set
I^{j}_{t}:=\inf_{0\leq s\leq t}X^{j}_{s}
,

\tau^{j}:=\inf\{t\in[1,\infty)\mid X^{j}_{t}=I^{j}_{t}\}
for
j=1,2
and
\tau:=\tau^{1}\wedge\tau^{2}
,
so that (
5.16
) and the scaling property of
X^{1},X^{2}
imply that

\bigl(\{L^{j}_{t/A}\}_{t\geq 0},\{R^{j}_{t/A}\}_{t\geq 0},A(\tau^{L}_{j}\wedge\tau^{R}_{j})\bigr)
and

\bigl(\{A^{-2/3}X^{1}_{t}\}_{t\geq 0},\{A^{-2/3}X^{2}_{t}\}_{t\geq 0},\tau\bigr)

have the same law for any
j\geq 0
.

We first prove Proposition
5.2
-(
ii
),
which is an easy consequence of the properties mentioned in the previous paragraph,
Proposition
B.5
and (
B.10
).

Proof of Proposition
5.2
-(
ii
).

Setting
R^{\mathrm{bot}}_{0}:=0=:R^{\mathrm{top}}_{0}
and letting
R^{\mathrm{top}}_{j}

denote the quantum length of the part of
\partial\mathcal{W}_{j}
from
\eta^{\prime}_{j}(0)
to the rightmost
point of
\partial(\mathcal{W}_{0}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{j})\cap\partial\mathcal{W}_{0}
for each
j\geq 1
,
we easily see from the definition of the exploration that for any
j\geq 1
,

(5.17)

R^{\mathrm{bot}}_{j}=R^{\mathrm{bot}}_{j-1}+(B^{j-1}_{R}-R^{\mathrm{top}}_{j-1})^{+}\leq R^{\mathrm{bot}}_{j-1}+B^{j-1}_{R}\leq\sum_{k=0}^{j-1}B^{k}_{R}.

As noted in the first paragraph of Subsection
5.3
,

\bigl\{\bigl(\{R^{j}_{t/A}\}_{t\in[0,A\sigma_{j}]},A\sigma_{j}\bigr)\bigr\}_{j=0}^{\infty}

is i.i.d. with the same law as
\bigl(\{A^{-2/3}X^{2}_{t}\}_{t\in[0,\tau\wedge A]},\tau\wedge A\bigr)
,
and therefore
\{B^{j}_{R}\}_{j=0}^{{}^{\infty}}
is i.i.d. with the same law as

-A^{-2/3}I^{2}_{\tau\wedge A}
by (
5.15
). Moreover,
letting
p\in(1,\frac{3}{2})
, by the scaling property of
X^{2}
and

[
20
, Chapter VIII, Proposition 4]
we have

(5.18)

\mathbb{E}\bigl[(B^{0}_{R})^{p}\bigr]=\mathbb{E}\bigl[(A^{-2/3}|I^{2}_{\tau\wedge A}|)^{p}\bigr]\leq\mathbb{E}\bigl[(A^{-2/3}|I^{2}_{A}|)^{p}\bigr]=\mathbb{E}\bigl[|I^{2}_{1}|^{p}\bigr]<\infty,

which further implies that for any
s\in(0,\infty)
,

(5.19)

\displaystyle s\mathbb{P}[B^{0}_{R}\geq s]

\displaystyle\leq s^{1-p}\mathbb{E}\bigl[(B^{0}_{R})^{p}\bigr]\leq s^{1-p}\mathbb{E}\bigl[|I^{2}_{1}|^{p}\bigr],

(5.20)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[B^{0}_{R}]-\mathbb{E}\bigl[B^{0}_{R}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{B^{0}_{R}\leq s\}}\bigr]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigl[B^{0}_{R}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{B^{0}_{R}>s\}}\bigr]\leq s^{1-p}\mathbb{E}\bigl[(B^{0}_{R})^{p}\bigr]\leq s^{1-p}\mathbb{E}\bigl[|I^{2}_{1}|^{p}\bigr].

It follows from (
5.20
) with
p=\frac{5}{4}
,
a version
[
90
, Exercise 1.2.11]
of the weak law of large numbers with an explicit
remainder estimate and (
5.19
) that for any
N\in\mathbb{N}

and any
s\in\mathbb{R}
with
s>N^{-1/4}\mathbb{E}\bigl[|I^{2}_{1}|^{5/4}\bigr]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Biggl[\Biggl|\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}B^{j}_{R}-N\mathbb{E}[B^{0}_{R}]\Biggr|\geq Ns\Biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\Biggl[\Biggl|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}B^{j}_{R}-\mathbb{E}\bigl[B^{0}_{R}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{B^{0}_{R}\leq N\}}\bigr]\Biggr|\geq s-N^{-1/4}\mathbb{E}\bigl[|I^{2}_{1}|^{5/4}\bigr]\Biggr]

\displaystyle\leq 2N^{-1}\bigl(s-N^{-1/4}\mathbb{E}\bigl[|I^{2}_{1}|^{5/4}\bigr]\bigr)^{-2}\int_{0}^{N}t^{1-p}\mathbb{E}\bigl[|I^{2}_{1}|^{p}\bigr]\,dt+N^{1-p}\mathbb{E}\bigl[|I^{2}_{1}|^{p}\bigr]

(5.21)

\displaystyle=\Bigl(2(2-p)^{-1}\bigl(s-N^{-1/4}\mathbb{E}\bigl[|I^{2}_{1}|^{5/4}\bigr]\bigr)^{-2}+1\Bigr)\mathbb{E}\bigl[|I^{2}_{1}|^{p}\bigr]N^{1-p}.

Finally, by Proposition
B.5
and
(
B.10
), as long as
A
is large enough,

\mathbb{E}[B^{0}_{R}]=\mathbb{E}[-A^{-2/3}I^{2}_{\tau\wedge A}]\leq 1/20
,
and for any such
A
and any
N\in\mathbb{N}
with
N\geq 40^{4}\mathbb{E}\bigl[|I^{2}_{1}|^{5/4}\bigr]^{4}

we see from (
5.17
),
\mathbb{E}[B^{0}_{R}]\leq 1/20

and (
5.21
) with
s=1/20
that

\mathbb{P}[R^{\mathrm{bot}}_{N}\geq N/10]\leq c_{3}N^{1-p}

with
c_{3}:=(3200(2-p)^{-1}+1)\mathbb{E}\bigl[|I^{2}_{1}|^{p}\bigr]
,
completing the proof.
∎

Figure 3.

Top:
Shown on the left is the exploration in the statement of Proposition
5.2

when the first chunk satisfies the event
E
. The exploration continues starting from the orange dot.
Shown on the top middle is the exploration when the second chunk also satisfies the event
E
and the exploration continues from the orange dot.
On the top right, the second chunk does not satisfy
E
and the exploration starts from the orange dot.

Bottom:
Shown is the exploration in the proof of Proposition
5.2
,
which is more to the right than the exploration described in the statement of Proposition
5.2
.
Whenever a chunk for which
E
occurs is discovered, the exploration continues from the point which is
\epsilon_{0}
units
of boundary length from the leftmost intersection of the chunk with the surface boundary (orange dot, bottom middle).
Whenever a chunk for which
E
does not occur is discovered, the exploration continues from the rightmost intersection
of the chunk with the surface boundary (orange dot, bottom right).

In order to prove Proposition
5.2
-(
i
),
we will consider a slightly modified exploration, illustrated in Figure
3
,
for which the analog of
\{L_{j}\}_{j=0}^{\infty}
, denoted by
\{L^{\prime}_{j}\}_{j=0}^{\infty}
, dominates

\{L_{j}\}_{j=0}^{\infty}
in the sense that
L_{N}\leq L_{N}^{\prime}
for any
N\geq 1
. Moreover,

L^{\prime}_{N}=\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}(L^{\prime}_{j+1}-L^{\prime}_{j})
and the increments
\{L^{\prime}_{j+1}-L^{\prime}_{j}\}_{j=0}^{\infty}

form a sequence of i.i.d. random variables with negative mean and such that
(L^{\prime}_{j+1}-L^{\prime}_{j})^{+}

has a finite exponential moment. Then Proposition
5.2
-(
i
)
can be derived from the following large deviation bound. This rather explicit large deviation estimate is required
in order to prove that the constants
c_{1},c_{2}
in Proposition
5.2
-(
i
)
can be chosen to be dependent only on
A,c_{0}
.

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
\{Y_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
be i.i.d. real random variables, let

\beta,\delta,K,M\in(0,\infty)
,
p\in(1,\infty)
and assume that

\mathbb{E}[e^{\beta Y_{1}^{+}}]\leq K
,
\mathbb{E}[(Y_{1}^{-})^{p}]\leq M
and

\mathbb{E}[Y_{1}]\leq-\delta
. Then there exists
\lambda\in(0,\infty)

which is an explicit function of
\beta,\delta,K,M,p
such that

(5.22)

\mathbb{P}\Biggl[\sum_{j=1}^{n}Y_{j}\geq-\frac{1}{4}\delta n\Biggr]\leq e^{-\delta\lambda n/8}\qquad\text{for any $n\in\mathbb{N}$.}

Proof.

Set
a:=(2M/\delta)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}
and
b:=\mathbb{E}[(Y_{1}+a)^{+}]
, so that

b\leq\mathbb{E}[Y_{1}^{+}]+a\leq\beta^{-1}K+a
. Then by Hölder’s and Markov’s inequalities,

\displaystyle b-a-\mathbb{E}[Y_{1}]=\mathbb{E}[(Y_{1}+a)^{-}]\leq\mathbb{E}\bigl[Y_{1}^{-}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{Y_{1}^{-}\geq a\}}\bigr]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\big[(Y_{1}^{-})^{p}\big]^{1/p}\,\mathbb{P}\big[Y_{1}^{-}\geq a\big]^{1-1/p}

\displaystyle\leq a^{1-p}\,\mathbb{E}\big[(Y_{1}^{-})^{p}\big]\leq a^{1-p}M=\tfrac{1}{2}\delta,

and hence
a-b\geq-\mathbb{E}[Y_{1}]-\frac{1}{2}\delta\geq\frac{1}{2}\delta
.
Therefore, setting
\widetilde{Y}_{j}:=(Y_{j}+a)^{+}-b
for
j\in\mathbb{N}
and noting that

\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{Y}_{1}]=0
, we see that for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
and any
\lambda\in(0,\beta]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Biggl[\sum_{j=1}^{n}Y_{j}\geq-\frac{1}{4}\delta n\Biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\Biggl[\sum_{j=1}^{n}\widetilde{Y}_{j}\geq\Bigl(a-b-\frac{1}{4}\delta\Bigr)n\Biggr]\leq\mathbb{P}\Biggl[\sum_{j=1}^{n}\widetilde{Y}_{j}\geq\frac{1}{4}\delta n\Biggr]

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\delta\lambda n/4}\,\mathbb{E}\Biggl[\exp\biggl(\lambda\sum_{j=1}^{n}\widetilde{Y}_{j}\biggr)\Biggr]=e^{-\delta\lambda n/4}\,\bigl(\mathbb{E}[e^{\lambda\widetilde{Y}_{1}}]\bigr)^{n}

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\delta\lambda n/4}\biggl(1+\sum_{k=2}^{\infty}\frac{(\lambda/\beta)^{k}}{k!}\mathbb{E}\bigl[(\beta|\widetilde{Y}_{1}|)^{k}\bigr]\biggr)^{n}

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\delta\lambda n/4}\Bigl(1+(\lambda/\beta)^{2}\mathbb{E}\bigl[e^{\beta|\widetilde{Y}_{1}|}\bigr]\Bigr)^{n}

(5.23)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\Bigl(-\delta\lambda n/4+n(\lambda/\beta)^{2}\,\mathbb{E}\bigl[e^{\beta|\widetilde{Y}_{1}|}\bigr]\Bigr).

Now since
|\widetilde{Y}_{1}|\leq Y_{1}^{+}+a+b\leq Y_{1}^{+}+2a+\beta^{-1}K
and thus

\mathbb{E}\bigl[e^{\beta|\widetilde{Y}_{1}|}\bigr]\leq Ke^{2\beta a+K}
,
(
5.22
) follows by choosing

\lambda:=\bigl(\frac{1}{8}\delta\beta^{2}K^{-1}e^{-2\beta a-K}\bigr)\wedge\beta

in (
5.23
).
∎

Proof of Proposition
5.2
-(
i
).

We define
\{L^{\prime}_{j}\}_{j=0}^{\infty}
by
L^{\prime}_{0}:=0
and

(5.24)

L^{\prime}_{j+1}-L^{\prime}_{j}:=(\epsilon_{0}-B^{j}_{L}){\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{j}}+(T^{j}-B^{j}_{L}){\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{j}^{c}}.

Then since
\{(\mathcal{W}_{j}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{j+1},h|_{\mathcal{W}_{j}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{j+1}},\eta^{\prime}_{j}|_{[0,\sigma_{j}]})\}_{j=0}^{\infty}

is i.i.d. and
{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{j}},T^{j},B^{j}_{L},B^{j}_{R}
are measurable with respect to

(\mathcal{W}_{j}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{j+1},h|_{\mathcal{W}_{j}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{j+1}},\eta^{\prime}_{j}|_{[0,\sigma_{j}]})

by (
5.5
), (
5.4
), (
5.14
)
and (
5.15
) for any
j\geq 0
,
we have that
\{({\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{j}},T^{j},B^{j}_{L},B^{j}_{R})\}_{j=0}^{\infty}
is i.i.d. and
thus
\{L^{\prime}_{j+1}-L^{\prime}_{j}\}_{j=0}^{\infty}
is also i.i.d.
Note that
\{L^{\prime}_{j}\}_{j=0}^{\infty}
defined by (
5.24
) has the law
of the analog of
\{L_{j}\}_{j=0}^{\infty}
for another exploration in which
\eta^{\prime}_{j+1}(0)

is instead chosen on the event
E_{j}
to be the point on
\partial\mathcal{W}_{j+1}
which is

\epsilon_{0}
boundary length units to the right of the leftmost point of

\partial(\mathcal{W}_{j}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{j+1})\cap\partial\mathcal{W}_{j}
and on the event
E_{j}^{c}

to be the rightmost point of
\partial(\mathcal{W}_{j}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{j+1})\cap\partial\mathcal{W}_{j}
;
see Figure
3
for an illustration.
This is because
\mathcal{W}_{j}
is a weight-
2
quantum wedge independent of

\{(\mathcal{W}_{i}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{i+1},h|_{\mathcal{W}_{i}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{i+1}},\eta^{\prime}_{i}|_{[0,\sigma_{i}]},\widetilde{L}_{i})\}_{0\leq i<j}

for each
j\geq 0
for either of the ways of choosing
\{\eta^{\prime}_{j+1}(0)\}_{j=0}^{\infty}
, by the
properties of quantum wedges described in Subsections
3.2.2
and
3.2.4
.

We claim that
L_{N}\leq L^{\prime}_{N}
for any
N\geq 1
. Indeed, let
j\geq 0
, set

(5.25)

I_{j}:=\Biggl\{i\Biggm|\begin{minipage}[295.0pt]$0\leq i<j$, $\partial\mathcal{W}_{j+1}\cap\partial\mathcal{N}_{i}$ has at least two
elements and is included in the part of $\partial\mathcal{W}_{j+1}$ from the rightmost point of
$\partial(\mathcal{W}_{j}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{j+1})\cap\partial\mathcal{W}_{j}$ to the rightmost
point of $\partial(\mathcal{W}_{0}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{j+1})\cap\partial\mathcal{W}_{0}$
\end{minipage}\Biggr\},

let
T^{i,j}
denote the quantum length of
\partial\mathcal{W}_{j+1}\cap\partial\mathcal{N}_{i}

for
i\in I_{j}
, set
I^{0}_{j}:=\emptyset
on
E_{j}
and

(5.26)

I^{0}_{j}:=\biggl\{i\in I_{j}\biggm|\begin{minipage}[245.0pt]$\partial\mathcal{W}_{j+1}\cap\partial\mathcal{N}_{i}$ is included in the part of
$\partial\mathcal{W}_{j+1}$ from the rightmost point of
$\partial(\mathcal{W}_{j}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{j+1})\cap\partial\mathcal{W}_{j}$ to $\eta^{\prime}_{j+1}(0)$
\end{minipage}\biggr\}

on
E_{j}^{c}
.
Then we easily see from the definition of
\{(\mathcal{W}_{i},\eta^{\prime}_{i})\}_{i=0}^{\infty}

that
{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{i}}=1
for any
i\in I_{j}
,
that
I^{0}_{j}\subset\{i\in I_{j}\mid T^{i,j}<\epsilon_{0}\}
,
that
I^{0}_{j}\cap I^{0}_{k}=\emptyset
for any
k>j
,
that the part of
\partial\mathcal{W}_{j+1}
from the rightmost point of

\partial(\mathcal{W}_{j}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{j+1})\cap\partial\mathcal{W}_{j}
to the rightmost point of

\partial(\mathcal{W}_{0}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{j+1})\cap\partial\mathcal{W}_{0}

(is a singleton or) consists of closed intervals
\{\partial\mathcal{W}_{j+1}\cap\partial\mathcal{N}_{i}\}_{i\in I_{j}}

with disjoint interiors in
\partial\mathcal{W}_{j+1}
, and thus that

(5.27)

\begin{split}L_{j+1}-L_{j}&=-B^{j}_{L}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{j}}+\Bigl(T^{j}-B^{j}_{L}+\sum\nolimits_{i\in I^{0}_{j}}T^{i,j}\Bigr){\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{j}^{c}}\\
&\leq-B^{j}_{L}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{j}}+(T^{j}-B^{j}_{L}+\epsilon_{0}\#I^{0}_{j}){\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{j}^{c}};\end{split}

here
\#A
denotes the number of elements of a set
A
. It follows from (
5.24
),
(
5.27
), the disjointness of
\{I^{0}_{j}\}_{j=0}^{\infty}
and

I^{0}_{j}\subset\{i\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[0,j)\mid{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{i}}=1\}
that for any
N\geq 1
,

(5.28)

\displaystyle L_{N}

\displaystyle-L^{\prime}_{N}=\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\bigl((L_{j+1}-L_{j})-(L^{\prime}_{j+1}-L^{\prime}_{j})\bigr)\leq\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}(-\epsilon_{0}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{j}}+\epsilon_{0}\#I^{0}_{j})

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon_{0}\bigl(-\#\{j\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[0,N)\mid{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{j}}=1\}+\#\{j\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[0,N-1)\mid{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{j}}=1\}\bigr)\leq 0,

proving that
L_{N}\leq L^{\prime}_{N}
. It thus suffices to prove (
i
)
for
\{L^{\prime}_{j}\}_{j=0}^{\infty}
instead of
\{L_{j}\}_{j=0}^{\infty}
.

Recall that the sequence
\bigl\{\bigl(\{L^{j}_{t/A}\}_{t\in[0,A\sigma_{j}]},\{R^{j}_{t/A}\}_{t\in[0,A\sigma_{j}]},A\sigma_{j}\bigr)\bigr\}_{j=0}^{\infty}

is i.i.d. with the same law as
\bigl(\{A^{-2/3}X^{1}_{t}\}_{t\in[0,\tau\wedge A]},\{A^{-2/3}X^{2}_{t}\}_{t\in[0,\tau\wedge A]},\tau\wedge A\bigr)
.
It thus follows from (
5.24
), (
5.14
),
(
5.15
), Propositions
B.3
and
B.4
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[L^{\prime}_{1}-L^{\prime}_{0}]

\displaystyle=\epsilon_{0}\mathbb{P}[E_{0}]+\mathbb{E}[T^{0}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{0}^{c}}]-\mathbb{E}[B^{0}_{L}]

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon_{0}+\mathbb{E}\bigl[T^{0}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\sigma_{0}<1\}\setminus E_{0}}\bigr]+\mathbb{E}\bigl[T^{0}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\sigma_{0}=1\}}-B^{0}_{L}\bigr]

\displaystyle=\epsilon_{0}+\mathbb{E}\bigl[T^{0}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\sigma_{0}<1\}\setminus E_{0}}\bigr]+A^{-2/3}\mathbb{E}\bigl[(X^{1}_{A}-I^{1}_{A}+X^{2}_{A}-I^{2}_{A}){\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\tau\geq A\}}+I^{1}_{\tau\wedge A}\bigr]

(5.29)

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon_{0}+\mathbb{E}\bigl[T^{0}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\sigma_{0}<1\}\setminus E_{0}}\bigr]+A^{-2/3}(2c^{\prime}-c\log A),

where
c^{\prime}:=\sup_{A^{\prime}\in[1,\infty)}\mathbb{E}[(X^{1}_{A^{\prime}}-I^{1}_{A^{\prime}}){\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\tau\geq A^{\prime}\}}]<\infty

and
c
is the constant in Proposition
B.3
.
Moreover, setting
Z^{j}:=\sup_{0\leq t\leq 1}(X^{j}_{t}-I^{j}_{t})
for
j=1,2
, we have

\mathbb{E}\bigl[e^{\alpha T^{0}}\bigr]\leq\mathbb{E}\bigl[e^{\alpha(Z^{1}+Z^{2})}\bigr]=\mathbb{E}\bigl[e^{\alpha Z^{1}}\bigr]^{2}<\infty

for some
\alpha\in(0,\infty)
by (
5.14
) and Proposition
B.7
,
and therefore by Jensen’s inequality and
\mathbb{P}[E_{0}^{c}\mid\sigma_{0}<1]\leq\epsilon_{0}
we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[T^{0}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\sigma_{0}<1\}\setminus E_{0}}\bigr]

\displaystyle=\alpha^{-1}\mathbb{P}\bigl[\{\sigma_{0}<1\}\setminus E_{0}\bigr]\mathbb{E}\bigl[\alpha T^{0}\bigm|\{\sigma_{0}<1\}\setminus E_{0}\bigr]

\displaystyle\leq\alpha^{-1}\mathbb{P}\bigl[\{\sigma_{0}<1\}\setminus E_{0}\bigr]\log\mathbb{E}\bigl[e^{\alpha T^{0}}\bigm|\{\sigma_{0}<1\}\setminus E_{0}\bigr]

\displaystyle\leq\alpha^{-1}\mathbb{P}\bigl[\{\sigma_{0}<1\}\setminus E_{0}\bigr]\log\frac{\alpha^{\prime}}{\mathbb{P}\bigl[\{\sigma_{0}<1\}\setminus E_{0}\bigr]}

(5.30)

\displaystyle\leq\alpha^{-1}\epsilon_{0}\log(\alpha^{\prime}/\epsilon_{0})

provided
\epsilon_{0}\leq\alpha^{\prime}/e
, where
\alpha^{\prime}:=\mathbb{E}\bigl[e^{\alpha Z^{1}}\bigr]^{2}
.
Combining (
5.29
), (
5.30
) and

\epsilon_{0}=c_{0}A^{-2/3}
, we conclude that

(5.31)

\mathbb{E}[L^{\prime}_{1}-L^{\prime}_{0}]\leq A^{-2/3}\biggl(c_{0}+\alpha^{-1}c_{0}\log\frac{\alpha^{\prime}}{c_{0}A^{-2/3}}+2c^{\prime}-c\log A\biggr)

provided
c_{0}A^{-2/3}\leq\alpha^{\prime}/e
, and hence we obtain
\mathbb{E}[L^{\prime}_{1}-L^{\prime}_{0}]\leq-\frac{1}{4}cA^{-2/3}\log A<0

from (
5.31
) by choosing
c_{0}
arbitrarily from
(0,c\alpha]

and taking
A
large enough so that
c\alpha A^{-2/3}\leq\alpha^{\prime}/e
and

\frac{1}{12}\log A\geq\alpha+2c^{\prime}/c+\log(\alpha^{\prime}/(c\alpha))
. Finally, by
(
5.24
) and
[
20
, Chapter VIII, Proposition 4]
we also have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[e^{\alpha(L^{\prime}_{1}-L^{\prime}_{0})^{+}}\bigr]\leq\mathbb{E}\bigl[e^{\alpha(\epsilon_{0}+T^{0})}\bigr]\leq e^{\alpha c_{0}}\,\mathbb{E}\bigl[e^{\alpha Z^{1}}\bigr]^{2}=e^{\alpha c_{0}}\alpha^{\prime}<\infty,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[((L^{\prime}_{1}-L^{\prime}_{0})^{-})^{p}\bigr]\leq\mathbb{E}\bigl[(B^{0}_{L})^{p}\bigr]\leq\mathbb{E}\bigl[|I^{1}_{1}|^{p}\bigr]<\infty,

where
p\in(1,\frac{3}{2})
is arbitrary.
Altogether, we thus have shown that the sequence
\{L^{\prime}_{j+1}-L^{\prime}_{j}\}_{j=0}^{\infty}
of real random variables
is i.i.d. and satisfies the assumptions of Lemma
5.3

with
\beta:=\alpha
,
\delta:=\frac{1}{4}cA^{-2/3}\log A
,
K:=e^{\alpha c_{0}}\alpha^{\prime}
,

M:=\mathbb{E}\bigl[|I^{1}_{1}|^{5/4}\bigr]
and
p:=\frac{5}{4}
,
which are all determined solely by (the law of
(X^{1},X^{2})
and)
A,c_{0}
.
Therefore by Lemma
5.3
there exists
\lambda\in(0,\infty)

which is an explicit function of
\beta,\delta,K,M,p
and hence determined solely by
A,c_{0}

such that
L^{\prime}_{N}=\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}(L^{\prime}_{j+1}-L^{\prime}_{j})
satisfies

(5.32)

\mathbb{P}\biggl[L^{\prime}_{N}\geq-\frac{1}{4}\delta N\biggr]\leq\exp\Bigl(-\frac{1}{8}\delta\lambda N\Bigr)\qquad\text{for any $N\in\mathbb{N}$,}

which together with
L_{N}\leq L^{\prime}_{N}
proves Proposition
5.2
-(
i
).
∎

We now turn to the proof of Proposition
5.2
-(
iii
).
We briefly describe its main steps before proceeding to the actual proof.
First, in Lemma
5.4

we show that the quantum length of the top right of
\mathcal{W}_{0}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{j}
minus
the quantum length of the bottom right of
\mathcal{W}_{0}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{j}
, with
the influences of the slides of
\{\eta^{\prime}_{i}(0)\}_{0\leq i<j}
to the right caused by
(
5.11
) neglected, grows linearly in
j

with high probability, on the basis of some moment analysis similar to the proof of
Proposition
5.2
-(
i
) above.
Next, in Lemma
5.5
we prove by using
Lemma
5.4
and a comparison argument
similar to (
5.28
) that for any
j\geq 0
,
with some probability uniformly positive in
j
the event
E_{j}
occurs,

\partial\mathcal{N}_{j}\cap\partial\mathcal{W}_{j+1}
has quantum length greater than
3\epsilon_{0}

and the chunks
\{\mathcal{N}_{k}\}_{k\geq j+1}
never enter the rightmost interval of
quantum length
\epsilon_{0}
in
\partial\mathcal{N}_{j}\cap\partial\mathcal{W}_{j+1}

or the part of
\partial\mathcal{W}_{j+1}
to the right of this interval.
Then in Lemma
5.6
we deduce from
Lemma
5.5
that,
with probability exponentially high in
u
, at most
u
of the subintervals

\{\partial\mathcal{N}_{j}\cap\partial\mathcal{W}_{n+1}\mid\text{$0\leq j\leq n$, $E_{j}$ occurs}\}

of
\partial\mathcal{W}_{n+1}
with quantum length in
(0,\epsilon_{0})
can consecutively align
for any
0\leq n\leq N
, so that the slides of
\{\eta^{\prime}_{i}(0)\}_{0\leq j\leq N}

to the right caused by (
5.11
) have quantum lengths
at most
\epsilon_{0}u
. Finally, we combine
Lemmas
5.5
and
5.6

to show that, with probability exponentially high in
u
, for any
0\leq j\leq N
with

\partial\mathcal{N}_{j}\cap(-\infty,0)\not=\emptyset
we have

\bigl(\bigcup_{0<k\leq u^{2}}\partial\mathcal{N}_{j+k}\bigr)\cap(-\infty,0)\not=\emptyset
,
which is easily seen to imply the property stated in
Proposition
5.2
-(
iii
).

Lemma 5.4
.

Let
j\geq 0
and set
S_{j,j}:=0
and

(5.33)

S_{j,k}:=\sum_{i=j+1}^{k}\bigl((T^{i}-B^{i}_{R}){\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{i}}-B^{i}_{R}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{i}^{c}}\bigr)=\sum_{i=j+1}^{k}(T^{i}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{i}}-B^{i}_{R})\quad\text{for $k>j$.}

Let
c^{\prime},c,\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\in(0,\infty)
be the constants as in the proof of
Proposition
5.2
-(
i
) above,
fix an arbitrary
A\in[2,\infty)
satisfying
c\alpha A^{-2/3}\leq\alpha^{\prime}/e
and

\frac{1}{12}\log A\geq 2c^{\prime}/c+\log(\alpha^{\prime}/(c\alpha))
, and for each
n_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
set

(5.34)

q_{n_{0}}:=\mathbb{P}\biggl[\inf_{k\geq n_{0}}\frac{S_{j,j+k}}{k}\geq\frac{1}{12}cA^{-2/3}\log A\biggr],

which is independent of
j\geq 0
. Then, as long as
c_{0}\in(0,c\alpha]
,

(5.35)

\lim_{n_{0}\to\infty}q_{n_{0}}=1\qquad\begin{minipage}[200.0pt]with a rate of convergence determined solely by $A$
(and thus independent of $c_{0}$ and $E$).
\end{minipage}

Proof.

\{({\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{k}},T^{k},B^{k}_{R})\}_{k=0}^{\infty}
is i.i.d. since

\{(\mathcal{W}_{k}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{k+1},h|_{\mathcal{W}_{k}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{k+1}},\eta^{\prime}_{k}|_{[0,\sigma_{k}]})\}_{k=0}^{\infty}

is i.i.d. and
{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{k}},T^{k},B^{k}_{R}
are measurable with respect to

(\mathcal{W}_{k}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{k+1},h|_{\mathcal{W}_{k}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{k+1}},\eta^{\prime}_{k}|_{[0,\sigma_{k}]})

by (
5.5
), (
5.4
), (
5.14
)
and (
5.15
) for any
k\geq 0
, and therefore

q_{n_{0}}
is independent of
j
.

We first show that, as long as
c_{0}\in(0,c\alpha]
,

(5.36)

\mathbb{E}\bigl[T^{0}-B^{0}_{R}\bigr]\in[cA^{-2/3}\log A,\infty)\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbb{E}\bigl[T^{0}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{0}^{c}}\bigr]\leq\frac{3}{4}cA^{-2/3}\log A.

Indeed, noting that
\epsilon_{0}=c_{0}A^{-2/3}\leq c\alpha A^{-2/3}\leq\alpha^{\prime}/e
and that

\frac{1}{12}\log A\geq 2c^{\prime}/c+\log(\alpha^{\prime}/(c\alpha))
, we see from (
5.30
), which requires
\epsilon_{0}\leq\alpha^{\prime}/e
,
and the calculations made in (
5.29
) for
\mathbb{E}\bigl[T^{0}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{0}^{c}}\bigr]
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[T^{0}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{0}^{c}}\bigr]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigl[T^{0}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\sigma_{0}<1\}\setminus E_{0}}\bigr]+\mathbb{E}\bigl[T^{0}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\sigma_{0}=1\}}\bigr]\leq\alpha^{-1}\epsilon_{0}\log(\alpha^{\prime}/\epsilon_{0})+2c^{\prime}A^{-2/3}

\displaystyle\leq A^{-2/3}\bigl(\tfrac{2}{3}c\log A+c\log(\alpha^{\prime}/(c\alpha))+2c^{\prime}\bigr)\leq\tfrac{3}{4}cA^{-2/3}\log A.

Next, for
\mathbb{E}\bigl[T^{0}-B^{0}_{R}\bigr]
, note that

T^{0}-B^{0}_{R}=L^{0}_{\sigma_{0}}+B^{0}_{L}+R^{0}_{\sigma_{0}}

by (
5.14
) and (
5.15
) and that,
since
\bigl(\{L^{0}_{t/A}\}_{t\geq 0},\{R^{0}_{t/A}\}_{t\geq 0},A\sigma_{0}\bigr)
and

\bigl(\{A^{-2/3}X^{1}_{t}\}_{t\geq 0},\{A^{-2/3}X^{2}_{t}\}_{t\geq 0},\tau\wedge A\bigr)

have the same law, so do
(L^{0}_{\sigma_{0}},B^{0}_{L},R^{0}_{\sigma_{0}},B^{0}_{R})
and

A^{-2/3}(X^{1}_{\tau\wedge A},-I^{1}_{\tau\wedge A},X^{2}_{\tau\wedge A},-I^{2}_{\tau\wedge A})
.
Further, for
j=1,2
, for any
t\in[0,\infty)
we have
\mathbb{E}[|X^{j}_{t}|]<\infty

by
[
20
, Chapter VII, Corollary 2-(i) and Chapter VIII, Proposition 4]

and
\mathbb{E}[X^{j}_{t}]=0
by the stationarity and the scaling property of
X^{j}
, and
hence the Markov property of
X^{j}
(see, e.g.,
[
20
, Chapter I, Proposition 6]
)
implies that
X^{j}
is a martingale with respect to the completed filtration generated by
X^{j}
.
It thus follows from the optional sampling theorem and the independence of
\{X^{j},\tau^{3-j}\}

that
\mathbb{E}\bigl[|X^{j}_{\tau\wedge A}|\bigr]<\infty
and
\mathbb{E}\bigl[X^{j}_{\tau\wedge A}\bigr]=0
.
Also, recalling that
c
is the constant from Proposition
B.3
,
by
[
20
, Chapter VIII, Proposition 4]
, the scaling property of
X^{1}

and Proposition
B.3
we have

\infty>A^{2/3}\mathbb{E}\bigl[|I^{1}_{1}|\bigr]=\mathbb{E}\bigl[|I^{1}_{A}|\bigr]\geq\mathbb{E}\bigl[-I^{1}_{\tau\wedge A}\bigr]\geq\mathbb{E}\bigl[-I^{1}_{\tau}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\tau<A\}}\bigr]\geq c\log A.

Combining the facts mentioned above in this paragraph, we see that

\mathbb{E}\bigl[|T^{0}-B^{0}_{R}|\bigr]<\infty
and that

\mathbb{E}\bigl[T^{0}-B^{0}_{R}\bigr]=A^{-2/3}\mathbb{E}\bigl[-I^{1}_{\tau\wedge A}\bigr]\geq cA^{-2/3}\log A
,
proving (
5.36
).

Turning to the proof of (
5.35
),
we define partial sums
\{S^{1}_{k}\}_{k\geq 1},\{S^{2}_{k}\}_{k\geq 1}
of i.i.d. real
random variables by
S^{1}_{k}:=\sum_{i=1}^{k}(T^{i}-B^{i}_{R})
and

S^{2}_{k}:=\sum_{i=1}^{k}T^{i}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{i}^{c}}
for
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
so that
S_{0,k}=S^{1}_{k}-S^{2}_{k}
by (
5.33
). Then
since the law of
T^{0}-B^{0}_{R}
is determined solely by
A
, the strong law of large
numbers together with the first half of (
5.36
) yields

(5.37)

\lim_{n_{0}\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\biggl[\inf_{k\geq n_{0}}\frac{S^{1}_{k}}{k}\geq\frac{11}{12}cA^{-2/3}\log A\biggr]=1\qquad\begin{minipage}[125.0pt]with a rate of convergence determined solely by $A$.
\end{minipage}

On the other hand,

\mathbb{E}\bigl[(T^{0})^{4}\bigr]\leq 24\alpha^{-4}\mathbb{E}\bigl[e^{\alpha T^{0}}\bigr]\leq 24\alpha^{-4}\alpha^{\prime}<\infty

by the choice of
\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}
specified just before and after (
5.30
),
and hence for any
a\in(0,\infty)
and any
k\in\mathbb{N}
, by Markov’s and Hölder’s inequalities we have

\mathbb{P}\biggl[\biggl|\frac{S^{2}_{k}}{k}-\mathbb{E}\bigl[T^{0}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{0}^{c}}\bigr]\biggr|\geq a\biggr]\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}\bigl[|S^{2}_{k}-k\mathbb{E}[T^{0}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{0}^{c}}]|^{4}\bigr]}{(ka)^{4}}\leq\frac{48}{k^{2}a^{4}}\mathbb{E}\bigl[(T^{0})^{4}\bigr]\leq\frac{1152\alpha^{\prime}}{k^{2}a^{4}\alpha^{4}}.

From this inequality with
a=\frac{1}{12}cA^{-2/3}\log A
and the second half of
(
5.36
) we get

(5.38)

\mathbb{P}\Biggl[\bigcup_{k=n_{0}}^{\infty}\biggl\{\frac{S^{2}_{k}}{k}\geq\frac{5}{6}cA^{-2/3}\log A\biggr\}\Biggr]\leq\frac{12^{7}\alpha^{\prime}}{c^{4}\alpha^{4}}\frac{A^{8/3}}{(\log A)^{4}}\sum_{k=n_{0}}^{\infty}\frac{1}{k^{2}}.

Now (
5.35
) follows by

S_{0,k}=S^{1}_{k}-S^{2}_{k}
, (
5.37
)
and (
5.38
).
∎

Lemma 5.5
.

Let
j\geq 0
,
l>j
, let
\{S_{j,k}\}_{k=j}^{\infty}
be as in
Lemma
5.4
,
and define events
\widetilde{E}_{j,l},\widetilde{E}_{j}
by

(5.39)

\displaystyle\widetilde{E}_{j,l}

\displaystyle:=E_{j}\cap\{T^{j}>3\epsilon_{0}\}\cap\bigcap\nolimits_{k=j+1}^{l}\{S_{j,k-1}-B^{k}_{R}>(2(k-j)-3)\epsilon_{0}\},

(5.40)

\displaystyle\widetilde{E}_{j}

\displaystyle:=E_{j}\cap\{T^{j}>3\epsilon_{0}\}\cap\bigcap\nolimits_{k=j+1}^{\infty}\{S_{j,k-1}-B^{k}_{R}>(2(k-j)-3)\epsilon_{0}\}.

Also let
z_{j}\in\partial(\mathcal{W}_{j}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{j+1})\cap\partial\mathcal{W}_{j+1}
be such that the
quantum length of the part of
\partial(\mathcal{W}_{j}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{j+1})\cap\partial\mathcal{W}_{j+1}

from its leftmost point to
z_{j}
is
(2\epsilon_{0})\wedge T^{j}
. Then

(5.41)

\displaystyle\widetilde{E}_{j,l}

\displaystyle\subset\biggl\{\begin{minipage}[228.0pt]$\partial(\mathcal{W}_{k}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{k+1})$ does not intersect the part of
$\partial\mathcal{W}_{j+1}$ which is to the right of $z_{j}$ for any $k\in\mathbb{N}\cap(j,l]$
\end{minipage}\biggr\},

(5.42)

\displaystyle\widetilde{E}_{j}

\displaystyle\subset\biggl\{\begin{minipage}[215.2pt]$\partial(\mathcal{W}_{k}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{k+1})$ does not intersect the part of
$\partial\mathcal{W}_{j+1}$ which is to the right of $z_{j}$ for any $k>j$
\end{minipage}\biggr\}

and, provided
A^{-1}\vee c_{0}
is small enough, there exists
\tilde{q}\in(0,1)
determined solely by
A,c_{0}
such that

(5.43)

\mathbb{P}[\widetilde{E}_{j}]=\mathbb{P}[\widetilde{E}_{0}]\geq 1-\tilde{q}.

Proof.

It is clear that (
5.42
) follows from (
5.41
)
by taking the intersection over
l\in\mathbb{Z}\cap(j,\infty)
. We show
(
5.41
) by a comparison argument similar to
(
5.28
). Let
y_{k}
denote the rightmost point of
