\partial(\mathcal{W}_{k}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{k+1})\cap\partial\mathcal{W}_{k}
for each
k\geq 0
,
and fix any instance of the exploration for which
\widetilde{E}_{j,l}
holds, so that the
quantum length of the part of
\partial(\mathcal{W}_{j}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{j+1})\cap\partial\mathcal{W}_{j+1}

from
z_{j}
to
y_{j}
is
T^{j}-2\epsilon_{0}\in(\epsilon_{0},\infty)
.
Set
k_{0}:=j
,
V_{0}:=0
and
k_{n}:=\inf\{k>k_{n-1}\mid{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{k}}=0\}

for
n\geq 1
. Also for each
n\geq 1
, set

(5.44)

J_{n}:=\biggl\{k\biggm|\begin{minipage}[303.0pt]$k\in\mathbb{Z}\cap(j,k_{n})\setminus\{k_{i}\mid i\geq 1\}$,
$\partial\mathcal{W}_{k_{n}+1}\cap\partial\mathcal{N}_{k}$ has at least two elements and is
included in the part of $\partial\mathcal{W}_{k_{n}+1}$ from $y_{k_{n}}$ to $\eta^{\prime}_{k_{n}+1}(0)$
\end{minipage}\biggr\}

if
k_{n}<\infty
and
J_{n}:=\emptyset
if
k_{n}=\infty
,
let
T^{k,k_{n}}
denote the quantum length of
\partial\mathcal{W}_{k_{n}+1}\cap\partial\mathcal{N}_{k}

for
k\in J_{n}
, set
U_{n}:=\sum_{k\in J_{n}}T^{k,k_{n}}
and
V_{n}:=\sum_{i=1}^{n}U_{i}
,
so that the definition of the exploration easily implies that

T^{k,k_{n}}<\epsilon_{0}
for any
k\in J_{n}
by
{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{k_{n}}}=0

(recall (
5.11
)) and
that
J_{n}\cap J_{i}=\emptyset
for any
i>n
. In particular,

V_{n}\leq\epsilon_{0}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\#J_{i}\leq(k_{n}-j-n)\epsilon_{0}

for any
n\geq 0
.

To show (
5.41
) by induction, let
n\geq 0
satisfy
k_{n}<l

and suppose that
\partial(\mathcal{W}_{k}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{k+1})
does not intersect the part of

\partial\mathcal{W}_{j+1}
which is to the right of
z_{j}
for any
k\in\mathbb{Z}\cap(j,k_{n}]
,
that
\eta^{\prime}_{k_{n}+1}(0)
is located to the left of
z_{j}
in
\partial\mathcal{W}_{k_{n}+1}

and that the quantum length of the part of
\partial\mathcal{W}_{k_{n}+1}
from
\eta^{\prime}_{k_{n}+1}(0)
to
z_{j}

is given by
2\epsilon_{0}+S_{j,k_{n}}-V_{n}
; note that this supposition holds
for
n=0
since
\eta^{\prime}_{j+1}(0)
is the leftmost point of

\partial(\mathcal{W}_{j}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{j+1})\cap\partial\mathcal{W}_{j}
by
{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{j}}=1
.
Then an inductive argument on
k
based on the definition of the exploration and the third part of
(
5.39
) easily shows for any
k\in\mathbb{Z}\cap(k_{n},k_{n+1}\wedge l]
that

\partial(\mathcal{W}_{k}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{k+1})
does not intersect the part of

\partial\mathcal{W}_{j+1}
which is to the right of
z_{j}
and that the quantum length
of the part of
\partial\mathcal{W}_{k+1}
from
y_{k}
to
z_{j}
is given by

2\epsilon_{0}+S_{j,k-1}-B^{k}_{R}-V_{n}
and hence greater than

(2k-k_{n}-j+n-1)\epsilon_{0}\geq(n+1)\epsilon_{0}
by (
5.39
)
and
V_{n}\leq(k_{n}-j-n)\epsilon_{0}
. Moreover,
suppose further that
k_{n+1}<l
. Then
since the definition of the exploration implies also that
the part of
\partial\mathcal{W}_{k_{n+1}+1}
from
y_{k_{n+1}}
to
z_{j}

involves only
\partial\mathcal{W}_{k_{n+1}+1}\cap\partial\mathcal{N}_{k}
for

k\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[j,k_{n+1})\setminus\{k_{i}\mid i\geq 1\}
and has quantum
length greater than
(2k_{n+1}-k_{n}-j+n-1)\epsilon_{0}\geq(k_{n+1}-j)\epsilon_{0}
,
there exists
j_{n+1}\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[j,k_{n+1})\setminus\{k_{i}\mid i\geq 1\}

such that
\partial\mathcal{W}_{k_{n+1}+1}\cap\partial\mathcal{N}_{j_{n+1}}
has quantum length greater than

\epsilon_{0}
. It thus follows from
{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{k_{n+1}}}=0
and the way of
choosing
\eta^{\prime}_{k_{n+1}+1}(0)
(recall (
5.11
))
that the quantum length of the part of
\partial\mathcal{W}_{k_{n+1}+1}
from
y_{k_{n+1}}
to

\eta^{\prime}_{k_{n+1}+1}(0)
is given by
U_{n+1}
, thereby that
\eta^{\prime}_{k_{n+1}+1}(0)

is located to the left of
z_{j}
in
\partial\mathcal{W}_{k_{n+1}+1}
and that
the quantum length of the part of
\partial\mathcal{W}_{k_{n+1}+1}
from
\eta^{\prime}_{k_{n+1}+1}(0)
to
z_{j}
is given by

2\epsilon_{0}+S_{j,k_{n+1}-1}-B^{k_{n+1}}_{R}-V_{n}-U_{n+1}=2\epsilon_{0}+S_{j,k_{n+1}}-V_{n+1}
.
This completes the induction and proves (
5.41
) and thereby (
5.42
).

Next, for (
5.43
) we begin by noting that,
since
\mathcal{W}_{j},\mathcal{W}_{0},\mathcal{W}_{1}
are weight-
2
quantum wedges,
\mathcal{W}_{1}
is independent of

(\mathcal{W}_{0}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{1},h|_{\mathcal{W}_{0}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{1}},\eta^{\prime}_{0}|_{[0,\sigma_{0}]})

and
{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{0}},T^{0}
are measurable with respect to

(\mathcal{W}_{0}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{1},h|_{\mathcal{W}_{0}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{1}},\eta^{\prime}_{0}|_{[0,\sigma_{0}]})

in view of (
5.5
), (
5.4
) and
(
5.14
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}[\widetilde{E}_{j}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}[\widetilde{E}_{0}]

(5.45)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigl[E_{0}\cap\{T^{0}>3\epsilon_{0}\}\bigr]\cdot\mathbb{P}\bigl[\text{$S_{0,k-1}-B^{k}_{R}>(2k-3)\epsilon_{0}$ for any $k\geq 1$}\bigr].

Therefore it suffices to prove that the last two probabilities in (
5.45
)
are bounded from below by positive constants determined solely by
A,c_{0}

as long as
A^{-1}\vee c_{0}
is small enough. To this end,
recall that
X^{1},X^{2}
are i.i.d.
3/2
-stable Lévy processes with only downward jumps
and starting from
0
, and that we have set
I^{j}_{t}:=\inf_{0\leq s\leq t}X^{j}_{s}
,

\tau^{j}:=\inf\{t\in[1,\infty)\mid X^{j}_{t}=I^{j}_{t}\}
for
j=1,2
and
\tau:=\tau^{1}\wedge\tau^{2}
.
By
E_{0}\subset\{\sigma_{0}<1\}
and (
5.14
),

(5.46)

E_{0}\cap\{T^{0}>3\epsilon_{0}\}=E_{0}\cap\bigl\{L^{0}_{\sigma_{1/A,0}}-\inf\nolimits_{0\leq s\leq\sigma_{1/A,0}}L^{0}_{s}+R^{0}_{\sigma_{1/A,0}}-\inf\nolimits_{0\leq s\leq\sigma_{1/A,0}}R^{0}_{s}>3\epsilon_{0}\bigr\},

where
\sigma_{1/A,0}:=\sigma_{1/A}(\mathbb{H},\mu_{h},\eta^{\prime}_{0},0,\infty)
. Since

\bigl(\{L^{0}_{t/A}\}_{t\geq 0},\{R^{0}_{t/A}\}_{t\geq 0},A\sigma_{1/A,0}\bigr)
has the
same law as
\bigl(\{A^{-2/3}X^{1}_{t}\}_{t\geq 0},\{A^{-2/3}X^{2}_{t}\}_{t\geq 0},\tau\bigr)
,
we see in view of
\epsilon_{0}=c_{0}A^{-2/3}
that

(5.47)

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\bigl[L^{0}_{\sigma_{1/A,0}}-\inf\nolimits_{0\leq s\leq\sigma_{1/A,0}}L^{0}_{s}+R^{0}_{\sigma_{1/A,0}}-\inf\nolimits_{0\leq s\leq\sigma_{1/A,0}}R^{0}_{s}>3\epsilon_{0}\bigr]\\
&=\mathbb{P}\bigl[X^{1}_{\tau}-I^{1}_{\tau}+X^{2}_{\tau}-I^{2}_{\tau}>3c_{0}\bigr]\xrightarrow{c_{0}\downarrow 0}\mathbb{P}\bigl[X^{1}_{\tau}-I^{1}_{\tau}+X^{2}_{\tau}-I^{2}_{\tau}>0\bigr]=1,\end{split}

where the last equality follows by the independence of
X^{1},X^{2}
, (
B.1
) and (
B.3
).
Moreover, the assumption on the Borel subset
E
of
\mathbb{M}^{\mathrm{CPU}}_{2}
and (
B.3
) together imply that
there exists
c^{\prime\prime}\in(0,\infty)
determined solely by the law of
(X^{1},X^{2})
such that

(5.48)

\mathbb{P}[E_{0}]\geq(1-\epsilon_{0})\mathbb{P}[\sigma_{0}<1]=(1-\epsilon_{0})\mathbb{P}[\tau<A]\geq(1-c_{0}A^{-2/3})(1-c^{\prime\prime}A^{-2/3}).

It thus follows that
\mathbb{P}\bigl[E_{0}\cap\{T^{0}>3\epsilon_{0}\}\bigr]\geq 1/2>0
,
by choosing first
c_{0}\in(0,1]
small enough on the basis of (
5.47
)
so that
\mathbb{P}\bigl[X^{1}_{\tau}-I^{1}_{\tau}+X^{2}_{\tau}-I^{2}_{\tau}>3c_{0}\bigr]\geq 3/4
,
then taking
A
large enough on the basis of (
5.48
) so that

\mathbb{P}[E_{0}]\geq 3/4
, and combining these with (
5.46
)
and (
5.47
).

To see that the last probability in (
5.45
) is bounded from below by a positive constant determined solely by
A,c_{0}
,
assume that
c_{0}\in(0,c\alpha]
and that
A
is large enough so that

c\alpha A^{-2/3}\leq\alpha^{\prime}/e
and
\frac{1}{12}\log A\geq\bigl(2c^{\prime}/c+\log(\alpha^{\prime}/(c\alpha))\bigr)\vee(4\alpha)
.
Then
\frac{1}{12}cA^{-2/3}\log A\geq 4c\alpha A^{-2/3}\geq 4c_{0}A^{-2/3}=4\epsilon_{0}

and hence
q_{n_{0}}
as in (
5.34
) satisfies

\mathbb{P}\bigl[\inf_{k\geq n_{0}}k^{-1}S_{0,k}>3\epsilon_{0}\bigr]\geq q_{n_{0}}

for any
n_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
. Also, since

\mathbb{P}[B^{k+1}_{R}\geq k^{9/10}]\leq k^{-9/8}\mathbb{E}\bigl[(B^{k+1}_{R})^{5/4}\bigr]\leq k^{-9/8}\mathbb{E}\bigl[|I^{1}_{1}|^{5/4}\bigr]<\infty

for any
k\in\mathbb{N}
by (
5.15
) and
[
20
, Chapter VIII, Proposition 4]
,
for any
n_{0}\in\mathbb{N}\cap(\epsilon_{0}^{-10},\infty)
we have

(5.49)

\mathbb{P}\biggl[\sup_{k\geq n_{0}}\frac{B^{k+1}_{R}}{k}<\epsilon_{0}\biggr]\geq\mathbb{P}\Biggl[\bigcap_{k\geq n_{0}}\{B^{k+1}_{R}<k^{9/10}\}\Biggr]\geq 1-\mathbb{E}\bigl[|I^{1}_{1}|^{5/4}\bigr]\sum_{k=n_{0}}^{\infty}k^{-9/8}.

Thus by (
5.35
) and
(
5.49
) there exists
n_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
determined solely by
A
such that

\mathbb{P}\bigl[\inf_{k\geq n_{0}}k^{-1}S_{0,k}>3\epsilon_{0}\bigr]\geq q_{n_{0}}\geq\frac{7}{8}

and
\mathbb{P}\bigl[\sup_{k\geq n_{0}}k^{-1}B^{k+1}_{R}<\epsilon_{0}\bigr]\geq\frac{7}{8}
,
and then since
\{B^{k}_{R}\}_{k=0}^{\infty}
is i.i.d. with the law determined solely by
A
,
we can further take
M\in(0,\infty)
depending only on
A
so that

\mathbb{P}\bigl[\max_{1\leq k\leq n_{0}}k^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{k}B^{i}_{R}\leq M\bigr]\geq\frac{3}{4}
.
It follows from these inequalities and
S_{0,k-1}-B^{k}_{R}\geq-\sum_{i=1}^{k}B^{i}_{R}

for
k\in\mathbb{N}
that

(5.50)

\mathbb{P}\biggl[\inf_{k\geq n_{0}}\frac{S_{0,k}}{k}>3\epsilon_{0},\,\sup_{k\geq n_{0}}\frac{B^{k+1}_{R}}{k}<\epsilon_{0},\,\min_{1\leq k\leq n_{0}}\frac{S_{0,k-1}-B^{k}_{R}}{k}\geq-M\biggr]\geq\frac{1}{2}.

On the other hand, let
c_{1}\in(0,\infty)
be the constant from
Proposition
B.6
and assume that

A\geq(2/c_{1})^{3/2}
and
c_{0}\leq 1
.
Then by
\epsilon_{0}=c_{0}A^{-2/3}\leq\frac{1}{2}c_{1}c_{0}
and (
B.16
) we have

(5.51)

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\bigl[T^{0}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{0}}>4\epsilon_{0},\,B^{0}_{R}<\epsilon_{0}\bigr]\\
&=\mathbb{P}\bigl[E_{0}\cap\{T^{0}>4\epsilon_{0},\,B^{0}_{R}<\epsilon_{0}\}\bigr]\\
&=\mathbb{P}\bigl[\sigma_{0}<1,\,T^{0}>4\epsilon_{0},\,B^{0}_{R}<\epsilon_{0}\bigr]-\mathbb{P}\bigl[\{\sigma_{0}<1,\,T^{0}>4\epsilon_{0},\,B^{0}_{R}<\epsilon_{0}\}\setminus E_{0}\bigr]\\
&\geq\mathbb{P}\bigl[\tau<A,\,X^{1}_{\tau}-I^{1}_{\tau}>4c_{0},\,I^{2}_{\tau}>-c_{0}\bigr]-\mathbb{P}[E_{0}^{c}\mid\sigma_{0}<1]\\
&\geq c_{1}c_{0}-\epsilon_{0}\geq\tfrac{1}{2}c_{1}c_{0}.\end{split}

Setting
n_{1}:=\min\bigl(\mathbb{N}\cap[(2\epsilon_{0}^{-1}M+4)n_{0},\infty)\bigr)
,
which is determined solely by
A,c_{0}
as a function of
n_{0},M,\epsilon_{0}=c_{0}A^{-2/3}
,
and recalling (
5.33
) and that
\{({\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{k}},T^{k},B^{k}_{R})\}_{k=0}^{\infty}
is i.i.d.,
from (
5.50
) and (
5.51
) now we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl[\text{$S_{0,k-1}-B^{k}_{R}>(2k-3)\epsilon_{0}$ for any $k\geq 1$}\bigr]

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\!\!\left[\begin{minipage}[362.0pt]$\inf\limits_{k\geq n_{0}}\dfrac{S_{n_{1},n_{1}+k}}{k}>3\epsilon_{0}$,
$\sup\limits_{k\geq n_{0}}\dfrac{B^{n_{1}+k+1}_{R}}{k}<\epsilon_{0}$,
$\min\limits_{1\leq k\leq n_{0}}\dfrac{S_{n_{1},n_{1}+k-1}-B^{n_{1}+k}_{R}}{k}\geq-M$,
$T^{k}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{k}}>4\epsilon_{0}$ and
$B^{k}_{R}<\epsilon_{0}$ for any $k\in\{1,\ldots,n_{1}\}$
\end{minipage}\right]

(5.52)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{(c_{1}c_{0}/2)^{n_{1}}}{2}>0

provided
A^{-1}\vee c_{0}
is small enough. Here we have the first inequality in
(
5.52
) since the event in the
first line of (
5.52
) is seen to
hold on the event in the second line as follows: for any
k\in\{1,\ldots,n_{1}\}
we have

S_{0,k-1}-B^{k}_{R}>3\epsilon_{0}(k-1)-\epsilon_{0}\geq(2k-3)\epsilon_{0}
,
for any
k\in\mathbb{N}\cap(n_{1},n_{1}+n_{0}]
we see from
n_{1}\geq(2\epsilon_{0}^{-1}M+4)n_{0}
that

\displaystyle S_{0,k-1}-B^{k}_{R}

\displaystyle=S_{0,n_{1}}+S_{n_{1},k-1}-B^{k}_{R}>3\epsilon_{0}n_{1}-M(k-n_{1})

\displaystyle\geq 2\epsilon_{0}n_{1}+\epsilon_{0}(2\epsilon_{0}^{-1}M+4)n_{0}-Mn_{0}>2(n_{1}+n_{0})\epsilon_{0}>(2k-3)\epsilon_{0},

and for any
k\in\mathbb{N}\cap(n_{1}+n_{0},\infty)
we obtain

\displaystyle S_{0,k-1}-B^{k}_{R}

\displaystyle=S_{0,n_{1}}+S_{n_{1},k-1}-B^{k}_{R}>3\epsilon_{0}n_{1}+(3\epsilon_{0}-\epsilon_{0})(k-n_{1}-1)>(2k-3)\epsilon_{0}.

Thus by (
5.45
), (
5.52
)
and the discussion following (
5.48
) we conclude that

\mathbb{P}[\widetilde{E}_{j}]=\mathbb{P}[\widetilde{E}_{0}]\geq\frac{1}{4}(c_{1}c_{0}/2)^{n_{1}}

provided
A^{-1}\vee c_{0}
is small enough, proving (
5.43
).
∎

Lemma 5.6
.

Let
u\in[2,\infty)
. For each
N\in\mathbb{N}
, let
\widehat{E}_{N}=\widehat{E}_{u,N}
be the event that there exists

0\leq n\leq N
so that for some
m\in\mathbb{N}\cap(u,\infty)

and
0\leq j_{1}<\cdots<j_{m}\leq n
the following hold:

(i)

E_{j_{i}}
occurs for any
1\leq i\leq m
.

(ii)

The boundary length of
\mathcal{I}_{j_{i},n}:=\partial\mathcal{N}_{j_{i}}\cap\partial\mathcal{W}_{n+1}
is in
(0,\epsilon_{0})
for any
1\leq i\leq m
.

(iii)

\mathcal{I}_{j_{i},n}\cap\mathcal{I}_{j_{i+1},n}\not=\emptyset

and
\mathcal{I}_{j_{i+1},n}
is located to the left of
\mathcal{I}_{j_{i},n}
in
\partial\mathcal{W}_{n+1}

for any
1\leq i\leq m-1
.

(Note that
\{\mathcal{I}_{j_{i},n}\}_{i=1}^{m}
are closed subintervals of
\partial\mathcal{W}_{n+1}

with disjoint interiors under (
i
) and (
ii
) by Proposition
A.2
.)
Assume further that
A^{-1}\vee c_{0}
is small enough so that
(
5.43
) holds, let

\tilde{q}
be as in (
5.43
) and
set
\tilde{c}:=\frac{1}{2}\log(\tilde{q}^{-1})
. Then for any
N\in\mathbb{N}
,

(5.53)

\mathbb{P}[\widehat{E}_{N}]\leq\tilde{q}^{-1}N\exp(-\tilde{c}u).

Proof.

Let
j\geq 0
. On the event
E_{j}
, we consider a random tree rooted at
j
with the
set of vertices
\mathcal{T}_{j}\subset\mathbb{Z}\cap[j,\infty)
, defined as follows.
Given
k\in\mathcal{T}_{j}
, we say that
l\in\mathbb{N}\cap(k,\infty)
is a child
of
k
in the tree if the following three conditions are satisfied:

(I)

E_{l}
occurs.

(II)

The boundary length of
\mathcal{I}_{k,l}:=\partial\mathcal{N}_{k}\cap\partial\mathcal{W}_{l+1}
is in
(0,\epsilon_{0})
.

(III)

\mathcal{I}_{k,l}\cap\partial\mathcal{N}_{l}\not=\emptyset

and
\partial\mathcal{N}_{l}
is located to the left of
\mathcal{I}_{k,l}
in
\partial\mathcal{W}_{l+1}
.

Recall that
T^{j}
denotes the quantum length of the top
\partial\mathcal{N}_{j}\cap\mathcal{W}_{j}

of
\mathcal{W}_{j}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{j+1}
. We claim that, provided
A^{-1}\vee c_{0}
is small enough
so that (
5.43
) holds, the conditional law
of the cardinality
\#\mathcal{T}_{j}
of
\mathcal{T}_{j}
given
E_{j}
and
T^{j}
is
stochastically dominated by a geometric distribution whose success probability is explicit
in
\tilde{q}
, with
\tilde{q}
as in (
5.43
).
Indeed, for each
k\geq 0
let
\mathscr{G}_{k}
denote the
\sigma
-algebra generated by

\{(\mathcal{W}_{i}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{i+1},h|_{\mathcal{W}_{i}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{i+1}},\eta^{\prime}_{i}|_{[0,\sigma_{i}]},\widetilde{L}_{i})\}_{0\leq i\leq k}
,
and for
i\geq 0
let
m_{i}
denote the
(i+1)
-th smallest element of

\mathcal{T}_{j}\subset\mathbb{Z}\cap[j,\infty)
on the event
\{\#\mathcal{T}_{j}>i\}

and set
m_{i}:=\infty
on
\{\#\mathcal{T}_{j}\leq i\}\cup E_{j}^{c}
, so that
m_{i}

is a
\{\mathscr{G}_{k}\}
-stopping time. Let
i\geq 0
. Then setting

\widetilde{E}_{m_{i}+1}:=\bigcup_{k\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}}\bigl(\widetilde{E}_{k+1}\cap\{m_{i}=k\}\bigr)
,
on
\widetilde{E}_{m_{i}+1}
we have
T^{m_{i}+1}>3\epsilon_{0}
by (
5.40
),
hence see from (
5.42
) and the definition of the exploration
that (
II
) with
k=m_{i}+1
and (
III
) with any

k\in\{m_{0},\ldots,m_{i}\}
fail to hold for any
l\in\mathbb{N}\cap[m_{i}+2,\infty)
,
and thus obtain
\mathcal{T}_{j}\subset\{m_{0},\ldots,m_{i},m_{i}+1\}
and in particular

\#\mathcal{T}_{j}\leq i+2
. Therefore

(5.54)

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}[m_{i+2}<\infty\mid\mathscr{G}_{m_{i}}]&\leq{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{m_{i}<\infty\}}-\mathbb{P}\bigl[\widetilde{E}_{m_{i}+1}\bigm|\mathscr{G}_{m_{i}}\bigr]\\
&=\bigl(1-\mathbb{P}[\widetilde{E}_{0}]\bigr){\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{m_{i}<\infty\}}\leq\tilde{q}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{m_{i}<\infty\}},\end{split}

where the equality holds by the facts that

\widetilde{E}_{m_{i}+1}\cap\{m_{i}=k\}=\widetilde{E}_{k+1}\cap\{m_{i}=k\}
,

\widetilde{E}_{k+1}
is independent of
\mathscr{G}_{k}
and

\mathbb{P}[\widetilde{E}_{k+1}]=\mathbb{P}[\widetilde{E}_{0}]
for any
k\geq 0
. It thus follows
by an induction on
i
based on (
5.54
) that for any
i\geq 0
,

(5.55)

\mathbb{P}[\#\mathcal{T}_{j}>2i\mid\mathscr{G}_{j}]{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{j}}=\mathbb{P}[m_{2i}<\infty\mid\mathscr{G}_{j}]{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{j}}\leq\tilde{q}^{i}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{j}},

which implies the desired claim.

Now we can conclude the assertion as follows. Let
N\in\mathbb{N}
, and for each
j\geq 0

let
\widehat{E}_{N,j}
be the event that (
i
), (
ii
)
and (
iii
) as in the statement hold for some
m\in\mathbb{N}\cap(u,\infty)

and
j=j_{1}<\cdots<j_{m}\leq n
, so that
\widehat{E}_{N}\subset\bigcup_{j=0}^{N-1}\widehat{E}_{N,j}
.
Then since
\{j_{1},\ldots,j_{m}\}\subset\mathcal{T}_{j}
for such
m
and
j_{1},\ldots,j_{m},n

by the definition of the tree
\mathcal{T}_{j}
given at the beginning of the previous paragraph,
we have
\widehat{E}_{N,j}\subset E_{j}\cap\{\#\mathcal{T}_{j}>u\}
, hence

\mathbb{P}[\widehat{E}_{N,j}]\leq\mathbb{P}\bigl[E_{j}\cap\{\#\mathcal{T}_{j}>u\}\bigr]\leq\tilde{q}^{-1}\exp(-\tilde{c}u)

by (
5.55
), where
\tilde{c}:=\frac{1}{2}\log(\tilde{q}^{-1})
, and thus

\mathbb{P}[\widehat{E}_{N}]\leq\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\mathbb{P}[\widehat{E}_{N,j}]\leq\tilde{q}^{-1}N\exp(-\tilde{c}u)
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
5.2
-(
iii
).

Assume that
A^{-1}\vee c_{0}
is small enough so that Proposition
5.2
-(
i
)
and (
5.43
) hold with
c_{1},c_{2},\tilde{q}
as stated in these places.
Recalling that
\mathcal{W}=(\mathbb{H},h,0,\infty)
is the original quantum wedge on which the exploration is defined,
set
\partial_{\mathrm{L}}\mathcal{W}:=(-\infty,0)\subset\partial\mathcal{W}
.
Let
\mathscr{G}_{k}
denote the
\sigma
-algebra generated by

\{(\mathcal{W}_{i}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{i+1},h|_{\mathcal{W}_{i}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{i+1}},\eta^{\prime}_{i}|_{[0,\sigma_{i}]},\widetilde{L}_{i})\}_{0\leq i\leq k}

for each
k\geq 0
, set
m_{0}:=0
and inductively

m_{j}:=\inf\{i>m_{j-1}\mid\partial\mathcal{N}_{i}\cap\partial_{\mathrm{L}}\mathcal{W}\not=\emptyset\}

for each
j\geq 1
, so that for any
j\geq 0
,
m_{j}
is a
\{\mathscr{G}_{k}\}
-stopping time and
Proposition
5.2
-(
i
) easily implies that
m_{j}<\infty
a.s.
Also let
N\in\mathbb{N}
and set
J_{N}:=\min\{j\geq 0\mid m_{j}\geq N\}
,
so that
J_{N}\leq N
. Then we easily see from the definition of the exploration that
for any
j\geq 0
and any
i\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[m_{j},m_{j+1}]

there exist
i_{1},\ldots,i_{n}\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[m_{j},m_{j+1}]

with
n\leq m_{j+1}-m_{j}
and
i_{1}=i
such that

\partial\mathcal{N}_{i_{n}}\cap\partial_{\mathrm{L}}\mathcal{W}\neq\emptyset
and

\partial\mathcal{N}_{i_{k}}\cap\partial\mathcal{N}_{i_{k+1}}\neq\emptyset
for each
1\leq k\leq n-1
,
i.e., the number of adjacent chunks necessary to get from
\mathcal{N}_{i}
to
\partial_{\mathrm{L}}\mathcal{W}

is at most
m_{j+1}-m_{j}
. It therefore suffices to bound

\max_{0\leq j<J_{N}}(m_{j+1}-m_{j})
.

Let
\widetilde{E}_{j}
be as in (
5.40
) for
j\geq 0
, let
u\in[2,\infty)

and let
\widehat{E}_{N}=\widehat{E}_{u,N}
be as in Lemma
5.6
.
Let
j\geq 0
, define
\mathscr{G}_{m_{j}}
-measurable random variables
R_{j},t_{j}

by
R_{j}:=0
on
\{m_{j}=\infty\}
,

(5.56)

R_{j}:=1+\max\biggl\{m\geq 1\biggm|\begin{minipage}[240.5pt]\eqref{it:chunks_good}, \eqref{it:tops_exposed} and~\eqref{it:adjacent}
in the statement of Lemma~\ref{lem:run_good_chunks_decay_fast} hold with
$n=m_{j}$ for some $0\leq j_{1}<\cdots<j_{m}\leq m_{j}$
\end{minipage}\biggr\}

on
\{m_{j}<\infty\}
(
\max\emptyset:=0
) and

t_{j}:=T^{m_{j}}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{m_{j}}^{c}}:=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}T^{k}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{k}^{c}\cap\{m_{j}=k\}}
,
and set
n_{u}:=\max\bigl(\mathbb{N}\cap[1,u]\bigr)
and

\widetilde{n}_{u}:=(2\epsilon_{0}/c_{1})n_{u}
.

We claim that

(5.57)

\mathbb{P}\bigl[m_{j+1}-m_{j}>\widetilde{n}_{u}R_{j}\bigm|\mathscr{G}_{m_{j}}\bigr]{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{m_{j}<N,\,t_{j}\leq\epsilon_{0}n_{u}\}}\leq(\tilde{q}^{n_{u}-1}+e^{-c_{2}\widetilde{n}_{u}}){\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{m_{j}<N,\,t_{j}\leq\epsilon_{0}n_{u}\}}.

To see this, set
\tau_{j,0}:=m_{j}-1
and inductively

\tau_{j,n}:=\inf\bigl\{k>\tau_{j,n-1}\bigm|\eta^{\prime}_{k+1}(0)\not\in\bigcup_{i=m_{j}}^{k}\partial\mathcal{N}_{i}\bigr\}

for each
n\geq 1
, so that
\tau_{j,n}
is a
\{\mathscr{G}_{k}\}
-stopping time for any
n\geq 1
;
note that
\tau_{j,n}
is the
n
-th smallest
k\geq m_{j}
such that

E_{k}
does not occur and the process of deciding the location of
\eta^{\prime}_{k+1}(0)

according to (
5.11
) involves skipping
some of the intervals
\{\partial\mathcal{W}_{k+1}\cap\partial\mathcal{N}_{i}\}_{0\leq i<m_{j}}

in
\partial\mathcal{W}_{k+1}
whose quantum lengths are in
(0,\epsilon_{0})
.
Then for any
n\geq 1
we have

\{\tau_{j,n+1}<\infty\}\subset\bigcup_{k=0}^{\infty}(\{\tau_{j,n}=k\}\setminus\widetilde{E}_{k+1})

by (
5.40
) and (
5.42
), hence

\mathbb{P}[\tau_{j,n+1}<\infty\mid\mathscr{G}_{\tau_{j,n}}]\leq\tilde{q}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\tau_{j,n}<\infty\}}

by the independence of
\widetilde{E}_{k+1}
and
\mathscr{G}_{k}
and
(
5.43
) with
j=k+1
for
k\geq 0
, thus

\mathbb{P}[\tau_{j,n}<\infty\mid\mathscr{G}_{m_{j}}]\leq\tilde{q}^{n-1}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{m_{j}<\infty\}}

and in particular

\mathbb{P}[\tau_{j,n_{u}}<\infty\mid\mathscr{G}_{m_{j}}]\leq\tilde{q}^{n_{u}-1}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{m_{j}<\infty\}}
.
Therefore, to show (
5.57
) it suffices to prove instead that

(5.58)

\mathbb{P}\bigl[m_{j+1}-m_{j}>\widetilde{n}_{u}R_{j},\,\tau_{j,n_{u}}=\infty\bigm|\mathscr{G}_{m_{j}}\bigr]{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{m_{j}<N,\,t_{j}\leq\epsilon_{0}n_{u}\}}\leq e^{-c_{2}\widetilde{n}_{u}}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{m_{j}<N,\,t_{j}\leq\epsilon_{0}n_{u}\}}.

For this purpose, for each
n\geq 1
let
s_{j,n}
denote the quantum length of the part of

\partial\mathcal{W}_{\tau_{j,n}+1}
between
\eta^{\prime}_{\tau_{j,n}+1}(0)
and the rightmost point of

\bigcup_{i=m_{j}}^{\tau_{j,n}}\partial\mathcal{N}_{i}\cap\partial\mathcal{W}_{\tau_{j,n}+1}
,
so that
s_{j,n}\leq R_{j}\epsilon_{0}
by (
5.11
) and
(
5.56
). Then since, conditionally on
\{m_{j}<\infty\}
,

\bigl\{\bigl(\mathcal{W}_{i+1+m_{j}}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{i+2+m_{j}},h|_{\mathcal{W}_{i+1+m_{j}}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{i+2+m_{j}}},\eta^{\prime}_{i+1+m_{j}}|_{[0,\sigma_{i+1+m_{j}}]},\widetilde{L}_{m_{j},i+1+m_{j}}\bigr)\bigr\}_{i=0}^{\infty},

where
\widetilde{L}_{m_{j},i+1+m_{j}}:=\widetilde{L}_{i+1+m_{j}}-\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}s_{j,n}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\tau_{j,n}=i+1+m_{j}\}}
,
is independent of
\mathscr{G}_{m_{j}}
and has the same law as

\{(\mathcal{W}_{i}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{i+1},h|_{\mathcal{W}_{i}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{i+1}},\eta^{\prime}_{i}|_{[0,\sigma_{i}]},\widetilde{L}_{i})\}_{i=0}^{\infty}
,
it follows that, conditionally on
\{m_{j}<\infty\}
,

\bigl\{L_{k+1+m_{j}}-L_{1+m_{j}}-\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}s_{j,n}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{m_{j}<\tau_{j,n}\leq k+m_{j}\}}\bigr\}_{k=0}^{\infty}

is independent of
\mathscr{G}_{m_{j}}
and has the same law as
\{L_{k}\}_{k=0}^{\infty}
.
On the other hand, noting that
t_{j}+s_{j,1}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\tau_{j,1}=m_{j}\}}
is the quantum length

\widetilde{L}_{m_{j}}
of the part of
\partial\mathcal{W}_{1+m_{j}}
between
\eta_{1+m_{j}}^{\prime}(0)
and
the leftmost point of
\partial(\mathcal{W}_{m_{j}}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{1+m_{j})}\cap\partial\mathcal{W}_{m_{j}}
,
we see from the definition of
m_{j},m_{j+1}
that the quantum length of

\partial(\mathcal{W}_{0}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{1+m_{j}})\cap\partial_{\mathrm{L}}\mathcal{W}

is given by
-(L_{1+m_{j}}-t_{j}-s_{j,1}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\tau_{j,1}=m_{j}\}})
and that
on the event
\{m_{j+1}-m_{j}>\widetilde{n}_{u}R_{j}\}
we have

\partial(\mathcal{W}_{0}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{\widetilde{n}_{u}R_{j}+1+m_{j}})\cap\partial_{\mathrm{L}}\mathcal{W}=\partial(\mathcal{W}_{0}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{1+m_{j}})\cap\partial_{\mathrm{L}}\mathcal{W}

and hence
L_{\widetilde{n}_{u}R_{j}+1+m_{j}}\geq L_{1+m_{j}}-t_{j}-s_{j,1}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\tau_{j,1}=m_{j}\}}
.
Recalling the
\mathscr{G}_{m_{j}}
-measurability of
R_{j}
and
t_{j}=T^{m_{j}}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{m_{j}}^{c}}
,
from the facts in the last two sentences,
s_{j,n}\leq R_{j}\epsilon_{0}
and
Proposition
5.2
-(
i
) we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl[m_{j+1}-m_{j}>\widetilde{n}_{u}R_{j},\,\tau_{j,n_{u}}=\infty\bigm|\mathscr{G}_{m_{j}}\bigr]{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{m_{j}<N,\,t_{j}\leq\epsilon_{0}n_{u}\}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\bigl[L_{\widetilde{n}_{u}R_{j}+1+m_{j}}\geq L_{1+m_{j}}-t_{j}-s_{j,1}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\tau_{j,1}=m_{j}\}},\,\tau_{j,n_{u}}=\infty\bigm|\mathscr{G}_{m_{j}}\bigr]{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{m_{j}<N,\,t_{j}\leq\epsilon_{0}n_{u}\}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\biggl[\begin{minipage}[250.0pt]$L_{\widetilde{n}_{u}R_{j}+1+m_{j}}-L_{1+m_{j}}-\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}s_{j,n}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{m_{j}<\tau_{j,n}\leq\widetilde{n}_{u}R_{j}+m_{j}\}}$ \\
$\geq-R_{j}\epsilon_{0}n_{u}-t_{j}$ \end{minipage}\biggm|\mathscr{G}_{m_{j}}\biggr]{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{m_{j}<N,\,t_{j}\leq\epsilon_{0}n_{u}\}}

\displaystyle=\bigl(\mathbb{P}[L_{k}\geq-(\epsilon_{0}n_{u}/\widetilde{n}_{u})k-t]|_{(k,t)=(\widetilde{n}_{u}R_{j},t_{j})}\bigr){\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{m_{j}<N,\,t_{j}\leq\epsilon_{0}n_{u}\}}

\displaystyle\leq\bigl(\mathbb{P}[L_{k}\geq-(2\epsilon_{0}n_{u}/\widetilde{n}_{u})k]|_{k=\widetilde{n}_{u}R_{j}}\bigr){\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{m_{j}<N,\,t_{j}\leq\epsilon_{0}n_{u}\}}

\displaystyle=\bigl(\mathbb{P}[L_{k}\geq-c_{1}k]|_{k=\widetilde{n}_{u}R_{j}}\bigr){\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{m_{j}<N,\,t_{j}\leq\epsilon_{0}n_{u}\}}\leq e^{-c_{2}\widetilde{n}_{u}}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{m_{j}<N,\,t_{j}\leq\epsilon_{0}n_{u}\}},

proving (
5.58
) and thereby (
5.57
).

Finally, we give similar upper bounds on
\mathbb{P}\bigl[m_{j}<N,\,R_{j}>u+1\bigr]

and
\mathbb{P}\bigl[m_{j}<N,\,t_{j}>\epsilon_{0}n_{u}\bigr]
to conclude the proof.
Indeed,
\{m_{j}<N,\,R_{j}>u+1\}\subset\widehat{E}_{N}

by (
5.56
) and hence

(5.59)

\mathbb{P}\bigl[m_{j}<N,\,R_{j}>u+1\bigr]\leq\mathbb{P}[\widehat{E}_{N}]\leq\tilde{q}^{-1}N\exp(-\tilde{c}u)

by Lemma
5.6
, where
\tilde{c}:=\frac{1}{2}\log(\tilde{q}^{-1})
.
For the latter, noting that
t_{j}=T^{m_{j}}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{m_{j}}^{c}}\leq\max_{0\leq k<N}T^{k}

on the event
\{m_{j}<N\}
, that
\{T^{k}\}_{0\leq k<N}
is i.i.d. and that
T^{0}
is
stochastically dominated by
Z^{1}+Z^{2}
with
Z^{i}:=\sup_{0\leq t\leq 1}(X^{i}_{t}-I^{i}_{t})

for
i=1,2
by (
5.14
) and
\sigma_{0}\leq 1
,
we see from Proposition
B.7
that

(5.60)

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\bigl[m_{j}<N,\,t_{j}>\epsilon_{0}n_{u}\bigr]\leq\mathbb{P}\bigl[\max\nolimits_{0\leq k<N}T^{k}>\epsilon_{0}n_{u}\bigr]\leq N\,\mathbb{P}\bigl[T^{0}>\epsilon_{0}n_{u}\bigr]\\
&\leq N\,\mathbb{P}\bigl[Z^{1}+Z^{2}>\epsilon_{0}n_{u}\bigr]\leq 2N\,\mathbb{P}\bigl[Z^{1}>\tfrac{1}{2}\epsilon_{0}n_{u}\bigr]\leq 2c_{3}N\exp(-\tfrac{1}{2}c_{4}\epsilon_{0}n_{u})\end{split}

for some
c_{3},c_{4}>0
determined solely by the law of
X^{1}
.
Combining (
5.59
) and (
5.60
)
with (
5.57
), we now obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl[\max\nolimits_{0\leq j<J_{N}}(m_{j+1}-m_{j})>(3\epsilon_{0}/c_{1})u^{2}\bigr]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\bigl[\max\nolimits_{0\leq j<J_{N}}(m_{j+1}-m_{j})>\widetilde{n}_{u}(u+1)\bigr]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\mathbb{P}\bigl[m_{j}<N,\,m_{j+1}-m_{j}>\widetilde{n}_{u}(u+1)\bigr]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\bigl(\mathbb{P}\bigl[m_{j}<N,\,m_{j+1}-m_{j}>\widetilde{n}_{u}R_{j}\bigr]+\mathbb{P}\bigl[m_{j}<N,\,R_{j}>u+1\bigr]\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\bigl(\mathbb{E}\bigl[{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{m_{j}<N,\,t_{j}\leq\epsilon_{0}n_{u},\,m_{j+1}-m_{j}>\widetilde{n}_{u}R_{j}\}}\bigr]+\mathbb{P}\bigl[m_{j}<N,\,t_{j}>\epsilon_{0}n_{u}\bigr]+\mathbb{P}[\widehat{E}_{N}]\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq N\bigl(\tilde{q}^{n_{u}-1}+e^{-c_{2}\widetilde{n}_{u}}+2c_{3}N\exp(-\tfrac{1}{2}c_{4}\epsilon_{0}n_{u})+\tilde{q}^{-1}N\exp(-\tilde{c}u)\bigr)

(5.61)

\displaystyle\leq c_{5}N^{2}\exp(-c_{6}u),

where
c_{5}:=\tilde{q}^{-2}+e^{2\epsilon_{0}c_{2}/c_{1}}+2c_{3}e^{c_{4}\epsilon_{0}/2}+\tilde{q}^{-1}

and
c_{6}:=\min\bigl\{2\epsilon_{0}c_{2}/c_{1},\frac{1}{2}c_{4}\epsilon_{0},\tilde{c}\bigr\}
.
Then Proposition
5.2
-(
iii
)
follows from the first paragraph of this proof and (
5.61
).
∎

Finally, in the rest of this subsection we prove
Proposition
5.2
-(
iv
),
on the basis of the following strategy. We first prove in Lemma
5.7

that for each
j\geq 0
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
, with probability tending to
1
as
n\to\infty
,
the amount of quantum length occupied by

\{\partial\mathcal{N}_{i}\mid\text{$j\leq i<j+n$, ${\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{i}\cap E_{i+1}}=0$}\}

in the part of
\partial\mathcal{W}_{j}
to the left of
\eta^{\prime}_{j}(0)
is at most a constant multiple of
A^{-2/3}n
.
This is expected to be much smaller than the quantum length of the part of

\partial(\mathcal{W}_{j}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{j+n})\cap\partial\mathcal{W}_{j}
to the left of
\eta^{\prime}_{j}(0)

provided
A
is large enough, since Proposition
5.2
-(
i
)
indicates that the latter length should typically be at least
(\frac{1}{16}cA^{-2/3}\log A)n
.
While this expectation is not true with arbitrarily high probability because of the possible
slides of the exploration to the right caused by
\{\mathcal{N}_{i}\mid 0\leq i<j\}

(recall (
5.11
)),
we show in Lemma
5.8
that it is true with
some

probability, thanks to the fact that with some probability
\{\mathcal{N}_{i}\mid 0\leq i<j\}

causes no slide to the right by Lemma
5.5
.
As a consequence, with some probability the part of

\partial(\mathcal{W}_{j}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{j+n})\cap\partial\mathcal{W}_{j}
to the left of
\eta^{\prime}_{j}(0)

cannot be covered by
\{\partial\mathcal{N}_{i}\mid\text{$j\leq i<j+n$, ${\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{i}\cap E_{i+1}}=0$}\}

and hence intersects
\partial\mathcal{N}_{i}
for some
i\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[j,j+n)
with

{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{i}\cap E_{i+1}}=1
. By taking
n
large enough, we can further assume
that this
\partial\mathcal{N}_{i}
is sufficiently far away to the left from
\eta^{\prime}_{j}(0)
,
and then
\partial\mathcal{N}_{i}
is going to intersect
\partial_{\mathrm{L}}\mathcal{W}=(-\infty,0)

provided the quantum length of the part of
\partial(\mathcal{W}_{0}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{j})\cap\partial\mathcal{W}_{j}

to the left of
\eta^{\prime}_{j}(0)
is reasonably bounded. Now since, with very high probability,
this boundedness holds for any
j\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[0,N]
with
\partial\mathcal{N}_{j}\cap\partial_{\mathrm{L}}\mathcal{W}\not=\emptyset

by (
5.59
) and (
5.60
) and
such
j
appears sufficiently often by (
5.61
),
we can make trials for sufficiently many
j\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[0,N]
,
each with some probability of success, to find
i\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[j,j+n)
with

{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{i}\cap E_{i+1}}=1
and
\partial\mathcal{N}_{i}\cap\partial_{\mathrm{L}}\mathcal{W}\not=\emptyset

for fixed
n
large enough, which turns out to yield (
5.13
).

Lemma 5.7
.

Let
j\geq 0
and set

(5.62)

S^{\prime}_{j,k}:=\sum_{i=j}^{k-1}B^{i}_{L}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{(E_{i}\cap E_{i+1})^{c}}\qquad\text{for $k>j$.}

Then for any
p\in(1,\frac{3}{2})
, there exist
c_{p,1},c_{p,2}\in(0,\infty)

determined solely by
p
(
c_{p,1}
can be chosen so as to be independent of
p
)
such that, as long as
c_{0}\in(0,1]
, for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

(5.63)

\mathbb{P}\bigl[S^{\prime}_{j,j+n}\geq c_{p,1}A^{-2/3}n\bigr]=\mathbb{P}\bigl[S^{\prime}_{0,n}\geq c_{p,1}A^{-2/3}n\bigr]\leq c_{p,2}A^{2/3}n^{1-p}.

Proof.

Since
\{({\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{i}},B^{i}_{L})\}_{i=0}^{\infty}
is i.i.d. as noted after (
5.24
),
the equality in (
5.63
) holds, and we may therefore assume that
j=0
.
We set
S^{3}_{n}:=\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}B^{i}_{L}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{i}^{c}}
and

S^{4}_{n}:=\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}B^{i}_{L}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{i}\cap E_{i+1}^{c}}
for
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
so that
S^{\prime}_{0,n}=S^{3}_{n}+S^{4}_{n}
, and we will apply to
\{B^{i}_{L}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{i}^{c}}\}_{i=0}^{n-1}

and
\{B^{i}_{L}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{i}\cap E_{i+1}^{c}}\}_{i=0}^{n-1}
separately a version

[
90
, Exercise 1.2.11]
of the weak law of large numbers with an explicit remainder estimate.

Let
p\in[1,\frac{3}{2})
. We first prove that, as long as
c_{0}\in(0,1]
,

(5.64)

\mathbb{E}[(B^{0}_{L})^{p}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{0}^{c}}]\leq c_{p,3}A^{-2/3}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathbb{E}[(B^{0}_{L})^{p}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{0}\cap E_{1}^{c}}]\leq c_{p,3}A^{-2/3}

for some
c_{p,3}\in(0,\infty)
determined solely by
p
. Recall that

\mathbb{E}[(B^{0}_{L})^{p}]\leq\mathbb{E}[|I^{1}_{1}|^{p}]<\infty

by (
5.15
), (
5.16
)
and
[
20
, Chapter VIII, Proposition 4]
, that

\mathbb{P}[E_{0}^{c}\mid\sigma_{0}<1]=1-\mathbb{P}[E_{0}\mid\sigma_{0}<1]\leq\epsilon_{0}=c_{0}A^{-2/3}

by the assumption on the Borel subset
E
of
\mathbb{M}^{\mathrm{CPU}}_{2}
and hence that

\mathbb{P}[E_{1}^{c}]=\mathbb{P}[E_{0}^{c}]=\mathbb{P}[\sigma_{0}=1]+\mathbb{P}[\{\sigma_{0}<1\}\setminus E_{0}]\leq\mathbb{P}[\tau\geq A]+c_{0}A^{-2/3}\leq(c^{\prime\prime}+c_{0})A^{-2/3}

for some
c^{\prime\prime}\in(0,\infty)
determined solely by the law of
(X^{1},X^{2})
by (
B.3
).
By the independence of
(B^{0}_{L})^{p}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{0}},{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{1}^{c}}
we have

(5.65)

\mathbb{E}[(B^{0}_{L})^{p}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{0}\cap E_{1}^{c}}]=\mathbb{E}[(B^{0}_{L})^{p}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{0}}]\mathbb{P}[E_{1}^{c}]\leq\mathbb{E}[|I^{1}_{1}|^{p}](c^{\prime\prime}+c_{0})A^{-2/3}.

For
\mathbb{E}[(B^{0}_{L})^{p}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{0}^{c}}]
, we decompose it as

(5.66)

\mathbb{E}[(B^{0}_{L})^{p}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{0}^{c}}]=\mathbb{E}[(B^{0}_{L})^{p}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\sigma_{0}=1\}}]+\mathbb{E}[(B^{0}_{L})^{p}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\sigma_{0}<1\}\setminus E_{0}}],

and then since
I^{1}_{A}=I^{1}_{1}
a.s. on
\{\tau\geq A\}
, for the first term we have

(5.67)

\mathbb{E}[(B^{0}_{L})^{p}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\sigma_{0}=1\}}]=\mathbb{E}[|A^{-2/3}I^{1}_{A}|^{p}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\tau\geq A\}}]\leq A^{-2p/3}\mathbb{E}[|I^{1}_{1}|^{p}]\leq A^{-2/3}\mathbb{E}[|I^{1}_{1}|^{p}].

For the last term in (
5.66
),
setting
q:=\frac{1}{2}+\frac{3}{4}p^{-1}\in(1,\frac{3}{2}p^{-1})
,
by Hölder’s inequality,
\mathbb{P}[E_{0}^{c}\mid\sigma_{0}<1]\leq c_{0}A^{-2/3}

and Proposition
B.5
we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(B^{0}_{L})^{p}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\sigma_{0}<1\}\setminus E_{0}}]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}[\{\sigma_{0}<1\}\setminus E_{0}]^{1-1/q}\mathbb{E}[(B^{0}_{L})^{pq}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\sigma_{0}<1\}}]^{1/q}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}[\{\sigma_{0}<1\}\setminus E_{0}]^{1-1/q}\mathbb{E}[|A^{-2/3}I^{1}_{\tau}|^{pq}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\tau<A\}}]^{1/q}

\displaystyle\leq(c_{0}A^{-2/3})^{1-1/q}(A^{-2pq/3}c_{pq}A^{2(pq-1)/3})^{1/q}

(5.68)

\displaystyle=c_{0}^{1-1/q}c_{pq}^{1/q}A^{-2/3},

where
c_{pq}\in(0,\infty)
is as in Proposition
B.5

with
pq
in place of
p
. Thus (
5.64
) follows by combining
(
5.65
), (
5.66
),
(
5.67
) and (
5.68
).

Now setting
E^{3}_{i}:=E_{i}^{c}
and
E^{4}_{i}:=E_{i}\cap E_{i+1}^{c}
for
i\geq 0

and letting
p\in(1,\frac{3}{2})
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
l\in\{3,4\}
and
s\in(0,\infty)
, we see from

[
90
, Exercise 1.2.11]
applied to
\{B^{i}_{L}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E^{l}_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{n-1}

and (
5.64
) that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigl[\bigl|S^{l}_{n}-n\mathbb{E}[B^{0}_{L}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E^{l}_{0}\cap\{B^{0}_{L}\leq n\}}]\bigr|\geq ns\Bigr]\leq\frac{2}{ns^{2}}\int_{0}^{n}

\displaystyle t\mathbb{P}[B^{0}_{L}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E^{l}_{0}}\geq t]\,dt+n\mathbb{P}[B^{0}_{L}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E^{l}_{0}}\geq n]

(5.69)

\displaystyle\leq\biggl(\frac{2}{ns^{2}}\int_{0}^{n}t^{1-p}\,dt+n^{1-p}\biggr)\mathbb{E}[(B^{0}_{L})^{p}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E^{l}_{0}}]

\displaystyle\leq n^{1-p}(4s^{-2}+1)c_{p,3}A^{-2/3};

to be precise,
\{B^{i}_{L}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E^{4}_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{n-1}
is not independent
as assumed in
[
90
, Exercise 1.2.11]
, but the inequality in the first line of
(
5.69
) still holds since the covariance of

B^{i}_{L}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E^{4}_{i}\cap\{B^{i}_{L}\leq n\}},B^{k}_{L}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E^{4}_{k}\cap\{B^{k}_{L}\leq n\}}

is
0
for
i,k\geq 0
with
|i-k|\geq 2
by the independence and
at most
0
for
i,k\geq 0
with
|i-k|=1
by

{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E^{4}_{i}\cap\{B^{i}_{L}\leq n\}}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E^{4}_{k}\cap\{B^{k}_{L}\leq n\}}=0

and
B^{i}_{L}\wedge B^{k}_{L}\geq 0
. Finally, noting that
S^{\prime}_{0,n}=S^{3}_{n}+S^{4}_{n}
and that

\mathbb{E}[B^{0}_{L}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E^{l}_{0}\cap\{B^{0}_{L}\leq n\}}]\leq\mathbb{E}[B^{0}_{L}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E^{l}_{0}}]\leq c_{1,3}A^{-2/3}

by (
5.64
), we conclude from (
5.69
) with
s=A^{-2/3}
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}[S^{\prime}_{0,n}\geq 2(c_{1,3}+1)A^{-2/3}n]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}[S^{3}_{n}\geq(c_{1,3}+1)A^{-2/3}n]+\mathbb{P}[S^{4}_{n}\geq(c_{1,3}+1)A^{-2/3}n]

\displaystyle\leq 2n^{1-p}(4A^{4/3}+1)c_{p,3}A^{-2/3}\leq 9c_{p,3}A^{2/3}n^{1-p},

proving (
5.63
).
∎

The essence of Proposition
5.2
-(
iv
)
consists in the following lemma.

Lemma 5.8
.

Let
c\in(0,\infty)
be the constant from Proposition
B.3
,
let
c_{5/4,1}\in(0,\infty)
be as in Lemma
5.7
with
p=\frac{5}{4}
,
define
\{L^{\prime}_{j}\}_{j=0}^{\infty}
by
L^{\prime}_{0}:=0
and (
5.24
),
and let
\{S^{\prime}_{j,k}\}_{j\geq 0,\,k>j}
be as in (
5.62
).
Let
j\geq 0
, and for each
n\geq 2
, let
y_{j,n}\in\partial\mathcal{W}_{j}
be the point to
the left of
\eta^{\prime}_{j}(0)
in
\partial\mathcal{W}_{j}
such that the quantum length of
the part of
\partial\mathcal{W}_{j}
from
\eta^{\prime}_{j}(0)
to
y_{j,n}
is
(\frac{1}{64}cA^{-2/3}\log A)n
,
and define an event
\widetilde{E}^{\prime}_{j,n}
by

(5.70)

\widetilde{E}^{\prime}_{j,n}:=\widetilde{E}_{j,j+n-1}\cap\biggl\{\frac{L^{\prime}_{j}-L^{\prime}_{j+n}}{n}>\frac{1}{16}cA^{-2/3}\log A,\,\frac{S^{\prime}_{j,j+n}}{n}<c_{5/4,1}A^{-2/3}\biggr\},

where
\widetilde{E}_{j,j+n-1}
is as defined in (
5.39
) with
l=j+n-1
.
Then, provided
A^{-1}\vee c_{0}
is small enough, there exist
n_{1}\geq 2
and

\tilde{q}^{\prime}\in(0,1)
determined solely by
A,c_{0}
such that for any
n\geq n_{1}
,

(5.71)

\displaystyle\widetilde{E}^{\prime}_{j,n}\subset\biggl\{\begin{minipage}[260.0pt]there exists $l\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[j,j+n)$ such that ${\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{l}\cap E_{l+1}}=1$ and
$\partial\mathcal{N}_{l}$ intersects the part of $\partial\mathcal{W}_{j}$ which is to the left of $y_{j,n}$
\end{minipage}\biggr\},

(5.72)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}[\widetilde{E}^{\prime}_{j,n}]=\mathbb{P}[\widetilde{E}^{\prime}_{0,n}]\geq 1-\tilde{q}^{\prime}.

Proof.

Assume that
A\geq\exp(64c_{5/4,1}/c)
, that
c_{0}\in(0,1]
and that
A^{-1}\vee c_{0}

is small enough so that (
5.32
) and (
5.43
)
hold with
\delta=\frac{1}{4}cA^{-2/3}\log A,\lambda,\tilde{q}
as stated in these places.
Let
c_{5/4,2}\in(0,\infty)
be as in Lemma
5.7
with
p=\frac{5}{4}
, and set

(5.73)

n_{1}:=\min\biggl(\mathbb{N}\cap\biggl[\max\biggl\{2,\frac{8}{\delta\lambda}\log\frac{4}{1-\tilde{q}},\Bigl(c_{5/4,2}A^{2/3}\frac{4}{1-\tilde{q}}\Bigr)^{4}\biggr\},\infty\biggr)\biggr),

so that
n_{1}
is determined solely by

and satisfies

\exp(-\frac{1}{8}\delta\lambda n_{1})\leq\frac{1}{4}(1-\tilde{q})
and

. Now let

.
Then since
\{({\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{k}},T^{k},B^{k}_{L},B^{k}_{R})\}_{k=0}^{\infty}
is i.i.d. as noted just after
(
5.24
), the equality in (
5.72
) holds,
and we easily see from (
5.43
), (
5.32
)
and (
5.63
) with

that

\mathbb{P}[\widetilde{E}^{\prime}_{0,n}]\geq 1-\tilde{q}^{\prime}
with
\tilde{q}^{\prime}:=\frac{1}{2}(1+\tilde{q})
.

To show (
5.71
), fix any instance of the
exploration for which
\widetilde{E}^{\prime}_{j,n}
holds, and for each
k\geq 0
,
as in the proof of Proposition
5.2
-(

)
define

by

on

and
by (

) with

in place of

on

(recall (

) for

), so that

and

I^{0}_{k}\cap I^{0}_{l}=\emptyset
for any

. Then since

\bigcup_{k=j}^{j+n-1}I^{0}_{k}\subset\mathbb{Z}\cap(j,j+n-1)

by (

), it follows from (

)
in the same way as (

) that

\displaystyle(L_{j+n}-L_{j})-(L^{\prime}_{j+n}-L^{\prime}_{j})

(5.74)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=j}^{j+n-1}\bigl((L_{k+1}-L_{k})-(L^{\prime}_{k+1}-L^{\prime}_{k})\bigr)\leq\sum_{k=j}^{j+n-1}(-\epsilon_{0}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{k}}+\epsilon_{0}\#I^{0}_{k})

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon_{0}\bigl(-\#\{k\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[j,j+n)\mid{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{k}}=1\}+\#\{k\in\mathbb{Z}\cap(j,j+n-1)\mid{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{k}}=1\}\bigr)\leq 0.

Recalling that
\frac{1}{64}cA^{-2/3}\log A\geq c_{5/4,1}A^{-2/3}
by

,
from (

) and the latter part of

\displaystyle L_{j}-L_{j+n}-\sum_{k=j}^{j+n-1}B^{k}_{L}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{(E_{k}\cap E_{k+1})^{c}}-\Bigl(\frac{1}{64}cA^{-2/3}\log A\Bigr)n

(5.75)

\displaystyle\geq L^{\prime}_{j}-L^{\prime}_{j+n}-S^{\prime}_{j,j+n}-\Bigl(\frac{1}{64}cA^{-2/3}\log A\Bigr)n>\Bigl(\frac{1}{32}cA^{-2/3}\log A\Bigr)n>0.

Note that the part of

which is to the left of
\eta^{\prime}_{j}(0)
has quantum length at least
L_{j}-L_{j+n}
and equals

\bigcup_{k=j}^{j+n-1}(\text{the bottom left of $\mathcal{W}_{k}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{k+1}$})\cap\partial\mathcal{W}_{j}
.
Now these facts and (
5.75
)
together imply that

belongs to the interior of this part of

\partial(\mathcal{W}_{j}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{j+n})\cap\partial\mathcal{W}_{j}
and that the part of

\partial(\mathcal{W}_{j}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{j+n})\cap\partial\mathcal{W}_{j}
which is to the left of

has quantum length greater than
\sum_{k=j}^{j+n-1}B^{k}_{L}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{(E_{k}\cap E_{k+1})^{c}}

and hence intersects

.
This last property means that the event in the right-hand side of

5.71
) holds, proving

).
∎

Proof of Proposition

).

5.2
-(

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\bigl[\max\nolimits_{0\leq j<J_{N}}R_{j}>n_{u}+1\bigr]+\mathbb{P}\bigl[\max\nolimits_{0\leq j<J_{N}}t_{j}>\epsilon_{0}n_{u}\bigr]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\bigl(\mathbb{P}\bigl[m_{j}<N,\,R_{j}>n_{u}+1\bigr]+\mathbb{P}\bigl[m_{j}<N,\,t_{j}>\epsilon_{0}n_{u}\bigr]\bigr)

(5.77)

\displaystyle\leq N^{2}\bigl(\tilde{q}^{-1}\exp(-\tilde{c}u)+2c_{3}\exp(-\tfrac{1}{2}c_{4}\epsilon_{0}n_{u})\bigr),

\displaystyle\geq 1-c_{5}N^{2}\exp(-c_{6}u)-Ne^{-\tilde{c}^{\prime}n_{u}}-N^{2}\bigl(\tilde{q}^{-1}\exp(-\tilde{c}u)+2c_{3}\exp(-\tfrac{1}{2}c_{4}\epsilon_{0}n_{u})\bigr)

\displaystyle\geq 1-c_{8}N^{2}\exp(-c_{9}u),

.

)

,

, so that

,

for some

.

),
we have thus proved the following:

for any

,

,
and there exists

such that

{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{l}\cap E_{l+1}}=1
and

.

Suppose for the moment that
\widetilde{J}^{\prime}_{N}\geq 2
, or equivalently,
\widetilde{J}_{N}\geq 2n_{u}
.
Then the left and right ends in
\mathbb{Z}
of the sequence

) are given by

\widetilde{m}_{0}=0
and

, respectively, and we see from

, (
5.79
)
and

that

(5.82)

\begin{split}N-\widetilde{m}_{\widetilde{J}^{\prime}_{N}n_{u}}&\leq N-\widetilde{m}_{\widetilde{J}_{N}-n_{u}+1}\leq N-\widetilde{m}_{\widetilde{J}_{N}}+(n_{u}-1)\bigl((3\epsilon_{0}/c_{1})u^{2}+\widehat{n}_{u}\bigr)\\
&\leq\widehat{n}_{u}+(n_{u}-1)\bigl((3\epsilon_{0}/c_{1})u^{2}+\widehat{n}_{u}\bigr)<n_{u}\bigl((3\epsilon_{0}/c_{1})u^{2}+\widehat{n}_{u}\bigr).\end{split}

Moreover, for any

we also have

(5.83)

\widetilde{m}_{(k+1)n_{u}}\leq\widetilde{m}_{(\widetilde{J}^{\prime}_{N}-1)n_{u}}<\widetilde{m}_{\widetilde{J}^{\prime}_{N}n_{u}-1}\leq\widetilde{m}_{\widetilde{J}_{N}-1}<N-\widehat{n}_{u}<N.

Since

with

,
it follows from (
5.81
),
(
5.82
) and

) that with this choice of

the event in (
5.13
) occurs for the present
instance of the exploration. Finally, we conclude this proof by deducing our supposition

\widetilde{J}_{N}\geq 2n_{u}
from (
5.79
) and
the requirement

; indeed, an induction on

based on

N\geq c_{7}u^{3}\geq 2n_{u}\bigl((3\epsilon_{0}/c_{1})u^{2}+\widehat{n}_{u}\bigr)

and (
5.79
) easily shows that

for any

, whence

\widetilde{m}_{2n_{u}-1}+\widehat{n}_{u}<N
, namely
\widetilde{J}_{N}\geq 2n_{u}
.
∎

5.4.
Proof of Proposition
5.1

The proof of Proposition
5.1
requires
Lemma
5.9
below
in addition to Proposition
5.2
. For each
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we set

D_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}:=\{\omega\mid\text{$\omega=(\omega_{1},\ldots,\omega_{n})\colon[0,\infty)\to\mathbb{R}^{n}$, $\omega$ is cadlag}\}
,
let
\{\mathscr{F}^{n}_{t}\}_{t\in[0,\infty)}
denote the filtration in

D_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}
generated by the coordinate process, and set

\mathscr{F}^{n}_{t+}:=\bigcap_{s\in(t,\infty)}\mathscr{F}^{n}_{s}
for
t\in[0,\infty)
.

Lemma 5.9
.

Let
a\in(0,\infty)
, and
let
Z
be a
3/2
-stable Lévy excursion with only upward jumps. Set
\widetilde{Z}_{t}:=Z_{(\zeta-t)^{-}}
for each
0\leq t\leq\zeta
, i.e., the cadlag modification of the time-reversal of
Z
, where
\zeta
denotes the lifetime of
Z
. We also set
T:=\inf\{t\geq 0:\widetilde{Z}_{t}\geq a\}
and
Y_{t}=\widetilde{Z}_{t+T}
, where we set
Y_{t}:=0
for
t\in[\zeta_{Y},\infty)
with
\zeta_{Y}
denoting the lifetime of
Y
. Then, conditional on the event
\{T<\infty\}
, we have that
\zeta_{Y}<\infty
a.s.,
Y_{t}>0
for any
t\in[0,\zeta_{Y})
a.s. and
\lim_{t\uparrow\zeta_{Y}}Y_{t}=0
a.s.
Moreover, let
X
be a
3/2
-stable Lévy process with only downward jumps with
X_{0}=a
,
and set
\tau_{0}:=\inf\{t\in[0,\infty)\mid X_{t}\leq 0\}
.
Then for any
\{\mathscr{F}^{1}_{t+}\}_{t\in[0,\infty)}
-stopping time
\tau
,

(5.84)

\mathbb{P}[Y\in d\omega,\ \tau(Y)<\zeta_{Y}]|_{\mathscr{F}^{1}_{\tau+}}=\Bigl(\frac{\omega(\tau)}{a}\Bigr)^{-1/2}\mathbb{P}[X\in d\omega,\ \tau(X)<\tau_{0}]|_{\mathscr{F}^{1}_{\tau+}}.

Proof.

Let
Z,\widetilde{Z}
be as in the statement of the lemma and let
n
denote the law of
Z
. Let also
\widetilde{n}
denote the law of
\widetilde{Z}
under
n
. Then
[
23
, Theorem 4, part 2]
implies that
\widetilde{n}[\widetilde{Z}_{t}\in A,t<\zeta\,|\,\mathcal{F}_{s^{+}}^{1}]=\int_{A}p_{t-s}(\widetilde{Z}_{s},x)dx

\widetilde{n}
-a.e. on
\mathcal{F}_{s^{+}}^{1}
, for each
0\leq s\leq t,A\in\mathcal{F}_{t^{+}}^{1}
, where
p_{t}(x,y)=(\frac{x}{y})^{1/2}q_{t}(x,y)
for each
t,x,y>0
, and
q_{t}
denotes the semigroup of
X
killed at the first time that it exits
(0,\infty)
, where
X
is as in the statement of the lemma. Let
\mu
(resp.
\nu
) denote the law of
Y
(resp.
X
). It follows that

\displaystyle\mu\big[Y\in A,t<\zeta_{Y}\big]=\int_{A}p_{t}(a,x)dx=\int_{A}\Bigl(\frac{\omega(t)}{a}\Bigr)^{-1/2}p_{t}(a,x)dx

for each
A\in\mathcal{F}_{t^{+}}^{1}
. This proves (
5.84
) for deterministic times. To prove the result for general stopping times, we first note that it is easy to see that
M_{t}:=\bigl(\frac{\omega(t)}{a}\bigr)^{-1/2}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{t<\tau_{0}\}}
is a non-negative supermartingale under
\nu
. Fix an
(\mathcal{F}_{t^{+}}^{1})
-stopping time
\tau
as in the statement of the lemma, and set
\tau_{n}:=\inf((2^{-n}\mathbb{Z})\cap(\tau,\infty))
for each
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Note that
\tau_{n}
decreases to
\tau
as
n\to\infty

\nu
-a.e. Moreover, we have that
\mu\big[Y\in A,\tau_{n}(Y)<\zeta_{Y}\big]=\int_{A}M_{\tau_{n}}d\nu
for each
A\in\mathcal{F}_{\tau_{n}^{+}}^{1},n\in\mathbb{N}
. Note also that the optional stopping theorem implies that
\mathbb{E}\big[M_{\tau_{n}}\,|\,\mathcal{F}_{\tau_{n}^{+}}^{1}\big]\leq M_{\tau_{m}}

\nu
-a.e., for each
1\leq m\leq n
, and so
(-M_{\tau_{n}})
is a backwards submartingale under
(\mathcal{F}_{\tau_{n}^{+}}^{1})_{n\geq 1}
. Also,
\mathbb{E}\big[-M_{\tau_{n}}\big]\geq-M_{0}=-a
, and so
[
56
, Problem 3.11]
implies that
M_{\tau_{n}}
is a uniformly integrable martingale under
\nu
. It follows that for each
A\in\mathcal{F}_{\tau^{+}}^{1}
, we have that

\displaystyle\mu\big[Y\in A,\tau(Y)<\zeta_{Y}\big]=\lim_{n\to\infty}\mu\big[Y\in A,\tau_{n}(Y)<\zeta_{Y}\big]=\int_{A}M_{\tau}d\nu

and this completes the proof.
∎

We need the following lemma to prove Lemma
5.11
below.

Lemma 5.10

(
[
45
, Theorem 1.2]
)
.

Let
\ell\in(0,\infty)
,
a\in(0,1)
, suppose that
\mathcal{D}=(\mathbb{D},h)
has law
\mu_{\mathrm{QD}}^{L=\ell}
,
let
x\in\partial\mathcal{D}
be chosen uniformly from the boundary measure
\nu_{h}
,
and let
y
be the point of
\partial\mathcal{D}
so that
\nu_{h}([x,y]_{\partial\mathcal{D}}^{{\bm{\circlearrowleft}}})=(1-a)\ell
.
Let
\eta^{\prime}
be an independent chordal
{\rm SLE}_{6}
on
\mathcal{D}
from
x
to
y
parameterized according to quantum
natural time, set
t_{\eta^{\prime}}:=\inf(\eta^{\prime})^{-1}(y)
,
L_{t}:=0=:R_{t}
for
t\in[t_{\eta^{\prime}},\infty)
,
and for each
t\in[0,t_{\eta^{\prime}})
let
L_{t}
(resp.
R_{t}
) denote the boundary length of the clockwise (resp. counterclockwise)
arc of
\widetilde{\partial}\mathbb{D}_{y,t}
from
\eta^{\prime}(t)
to
y
, where
\mathbb{D}_{y,t}
denotes the
component of
\mathbb{D}\setminus\eta^{\prime}([0,t])
with
y\in\partial\mathbb{D}_{y,t}
.
Then
t_{\eta^{\prime}}<\infty
a.s. and
\lim_{t\uparrow t_{\eta^{\prime}}}(L_{t},R_{t})=(0,0)
a.s.
Moreover, let
X^{1},X^{2}
be independent
3/2
-stable Lévy processes with only
downward jumps and with
X^{1}_{0}=a\ell=\ell-X^{2}_{0}
, and set

\tau_{0}:=\inf\{t\in[0,\infty)\mid X^{1}_{t}\wedge X^{2}_{t}\leq 0\}
.
Then for any
\{\mathscr{F}^{2}_{t+}\}_{t\in[0,\infty)}
-stopping time
\tau
,

(5.85)

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\bigl[(L_{\cdot},R_{\cdot})\in d(\omega_{1},\omega_{2}),\ \tau(L_{\cdot},R_{\cdot})<t_{\eta^{\prime}}\bigr]\big|_{\mathscr{F}^{2}_{\tau+}}\\
&=\Bigl(\frac{\omega_{1}(\tau)+\omega_{2}(\tau)}{\ell}\Bigr)^{-5/2}\mathbb{P}\bigl[(X^{1},X^{2})\in d(\omega_{1},\omega_{2}),\ \tau(X^{1},X^{2})<\tau_{0}\bigr]\big|_{\mathscr{F}^{2}_{\tau+}}.\end{split}

Proof.

The first assertion and (
5.85
) for constant
\tau
follow from

[
45
, Theorem 1.2]
combined with the resampling property of the quantum disk
[
35
, Proposition A.8]
. Then (
5.85
) for general
\tau
can be
verified in exactly the same way as in the proof of Lemma
5.9
above.
∎

Combining Lemma
5.10
with Proposition
B.7
,
we obtain the following lemma.

Lemma 5.11
.

Let
\ell\in(0,\infty)
,
a\in(0,1)
, suppose that
\mathcal{D}=(\mathbb{D},h,0)
has law
\mu_{\mathrm{QD,W}}^{L=\ell}
,
let
x\in\partial\mathcal{D}
be chosen uniformly from the boundary measure
\nu_{h}
,
and let
y
be the point of
\partial\mathcal{D}
so that
\nu_{h}([x,y]_{\partial\mathcal{D}}^{{\bm{\circlearrowleft}}})=(1-a)\ell
.
Let
\eta^{\prime}
be an independent radial
{\rm SLE}_{6}
on
\mathcal{D}
from
x
targeted at
0

parameterized according to quantum natural time, set
t_{\eta^{\prime}}:=\inf(\eta^{\prime})^{-1}(0)
,
let
T_{t}
denote the quantum length of
\partial K_{t}\cap\mathbb{D}

for
t\in[0,t_{\eta^{\prime}})
, set
T_{t}:=0
for
t\in[t_{\eta^{\prime}},\infty)
, and set

\tau:=\inf\bigl\{t\in[0,t_{\eta^{\prime}})\bigm|y\in\overline{K_{t}}\bigr\}
,
where
K_{t}
denotes the complement in
\mathbb{D}
of the
0
-containing component of
\mathbb{D}\setminus\eta^{\prime}([0,t])
.
Then
\tau<t_{\eta^{\prime}}
a.s., and there exist constants
c_{1},c_{2},c_{3}\in(0,\infty)

independent of
\ell,a
such that for any
\delta,u\in(0,\infty)
,
as long as
\ell\leq\delta^{2/3}\exp(c_{3}\delta^{-u})
or
a\in[1/10,9/10]
,

(5.86)

\mathbb{P}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,\tau\wedge\delta]}T_{t}\geq\delta^{2/3-u}\biggr]\leq c_{1}\exp(-c_{2}\delta^{-u}).

Proof.

Note that by the locality of
{\rm SLE}_{6}
(see, e.g.,
[
86
, Theorem 3]
),

\eta^{\prime}|_{[0,\tau]}
has the same law as
\widetilde{\eta}^{\prime}|_{[0,\widetilde{\tau}]}

for an independent chordal
{\rm SLE}_{6}

\widetilde{\eta}^{\prime}
on
\mathcal{D}
from
x
to
y

parameterized according to quantum natural time and

\widetilde{\tau}:=\inf\bigl\{t\in[0,t_{\widetilde{\eta}^{\prime}})\bigm|0\in\widetilde{K}_{t}\bigr\}
,
where
t_{\widetilde{\eta}^{\prime}}:=\inf(\widetilde{\eta}^{\prime})^{-1}(y)
and

\widetilde{K}_{t}
denotes the complement in
\mathbb{D}
of the component of

\mathbb{D}\setminus\widetilde{\eta}^{\prime}([0,t])
whose boundary contains
y
.
Then since
\widetilde{\tau}<t_{\widetilde{\eta}^{\prime}}
a.s. by
[
16
, Proposition 11.7]

and
0\not\in\widetilde{\eta}^{\prime}([0,\widetilde{\tau}])
a.s. by
[
16
, Theorem 11.2-(b)]
,
we have
0\not\in\eta^{\prime}([0,\tau])
a.s. and thus
\tau<t_{\eta^{\prime}}
a.s.

To prove (
5.86
), let
\widetilde{T}_{t}
denote the quantum
length of
\partial\widetilde{K}_{t}\cap\mathbb{D}
for
t\in[0,t_{\widetilde{\eta}^{\prime}})
,
set
\widetilde{T}_{t}:=0
for
t\in[t_{\widetilde{\eta}^{\prime}},\infty)
,
and let
L_{t},R_{t},X^{1}_{t},X^{2}_{t},\tau_{0}
be as in Lemma
5.10

with
\widetilde{\eta}^{\prime}
in place of
\eta^{\prime}
. Let
\delta,u\in(0,\infty)
.
Then by
T_{\tau\wedge\delta}\leq\lim_{t\uparrow\tau\wedge\delta}T_{t}
(since both of
L
and
R
have downward jumps),
the locality of
{\rm SLE}_{6}
mentioned above, and (
3.7
),

(5.87)

\displaystyle\mu_{\mathrm{QD,W}}^{L=\ell}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,\tau\wedge\delta]}T_{t}>\delta^{2/3-u}\biggr]

\displaystyle=\mu_{\mathrm{QD,W}}^{L=\ell}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,\tau\wedge\delta)}T_{t}>\delta^{2/3-u}\biggr]=\mu_{\mathrm{QD,W}}^{L=\ell}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,\widetilde{\tau}\wedge\delta)}\widetilde{T}_{t}>\delta^{2/3-u}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\mu_{\mathrm{QD,W}}^{L=\ell}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,\delta]}\widetilde{T}_{t}>\delta^{2/3-u}\biggr]=\int_{\{\sup_{t\in[0,\delta]}\widetilde{T}_{t}>\delta^{2/3-u}\}}\frac{\mu_{h}(\mathcal{D})}{\int\mu_{h}(\mathcal{D})\,d\mu_{\mathrm{QD}}^{L=\ell}}\,d\mu_{\mathrm{QD}}^{L=\ell}

\displaystyle\leq\mu_{\mathrm{QD}}^{L=\ell}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,\delta]}\widetilde{T}_{t}>\delta^{2/3-u}\biggr]^{\frac{1}{5}}\frac{\bigl(\int\mu_{h}(\mathcal{D})^{5/4}\,d\mu_{\mathrm{QD}}^{L=\ell}\bigr)^{4/5}}{\int\mu_{h}(\mathcal{D})\,d\mu_{\mathrm{QD}}^{L=\ell}}=c_{4}\mu_{\mathrm{QD}}^{L=\ell}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,\delta]}\widetilde{T}_{t}>\delta^{2/3-u}\biggr]^{\frac{1}{5}},

where
c_{4}:=\bigl(\int_{0}^{\infty}(2\pi)^{-1/2}s^{-5/4}e^{-1/(2s)}\,ds\bigr)^{4/5}
.

Since (
5.86
) is obvious for
\delta\in[1,\infty)
, we may assume that
\delta\in(0,1)
.
Noting that
\widetilde{T}_{t}=L_{t}-\inf_{s\in[0,t]}L_{s}+R_{t}-\inf_{s\in[0,t]}R_{s}
for any
t\in[0,\infty)
,
define an
\{\mathscr{F}^{2}_{t+}\}_{t\in[0,\infty)}
-stopping time
\tau_{\delta}
by

\tau_{\delta}(\omega_{1},\omega_{2}):=\inf\biggl\{t\in[0,\infty)\biggm|\omega_{1}(t)-\inf_{s\in[0,t]}\omega_{1}(s)+\omega_{2}(t)-\inf_{s\in[0,t]}\omega_{2}(s)>\delta^{2/3-u}\biggr\}.

Then by Lemma
5.10
with
\tau=\tau_{\delta}
, the scaling
property of
X^{1},X^{2}
and Proposition
B.7
, with the constants

c_{1},c_{2}\in(0,\infty)
as in Proposition
B.7
we have

\displaystyle\mu_{\mathrm{QD}}^{L=\ell}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,\delta]}\widetilde{T}_{t}>\delta^{2/3-u}\biggr]=\mu_{\mathrm{QD}}^{L=\ell}[\tau_{\delta}(L_{\cdot},R_{\cdot})<\delta,\,\tau_{\delta}(L_{\cdot},R_{\cdot})<t_{\widetilde{\eta}^{\prime}}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigl[\ell^{5/2}\bigl(X^{1}_{\tau_{\delta}(X^{1},X^{2})}+X^{2}_{\tau_{\delta}(X^{1},X^{2})}\bigr)^{-5/2}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\tau_{\delta}(X^{1},X^{2})<\delta,\,\tau_{\delta}(X^{1},X^{2})<\tau_{0}\}}\bigr]

\displaystyle\leq\ell^{5/2}(\delta^{2/3-u})^{-5/2}\mathbb{P}[\tau_{\delta}(X^{1},X^{2})<\delta]

\displaystyle\leq\ell^{5/2}\delta^{-5/3}\mathbb{P}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\biggl(X^{1}_{t}-\inf_{s\in[0,t]}X^{1}_{s}+X^{2}_{t}-\inf_{s\in[0,t]}X^{2}_{s}\biggr)>\delta^{-u}\biggr]

(5.88)

\displaystyle\leq 2c_{1}\ell^{5/2}\delta^{-5/3}\exp(-c_{2}\delta^{-u}/2).

It thus follows from (
5.87
) and (
5.88
)
that (
5.86
) with
(2c_{1},c_{2}/3)
in place of
(c_{1},c_{2})

holds as long as
\ell\leq\delta^{2/3}\exp(c_{2}\delta^{-u}/15)
.

Next, assume that
\ell\geq\delta^{2/3}\exp(c_{2}\delta^{-u}/15)
and that

a\in[1/10,9/10]
, and define an
\{\mathscr{F}^{2}_{t+}\}_{t\in[0,\infty)}
-stopping time
\tau^{\prime}
by

(5.89)

\tau^{\prime}(\omega_{1},\omega_{2}):=\inf\{t\in[0,\infty)\mid\omega_{1}(t)\wedge\omega_{2}(t)<\ell/20\}.

Then
\tau^{\prime}(L_{\cdot},R_{\cdot})<t_{\widetilde{\eta}^{\prime}}
a.s. since

\lim_{t\uparrow t_{\widetilde{\eta}^{\prime}}}(L_{t},R_{t})=(0,0)
a.s. by Lemma
5.10
, and we also have

X^{1}_{\tau^{\prime}(X^{1},X^{2})}+X^{2}_{\tau^{\prime}(X^{1},X^{2})}\geq X^{1}_{\tau^{\prime}(X^{1},X^{2})}\vee X^{2}_{\tau^{\prime}(X^{1},X^{2})}\geq\ell/20

a.s. on
\{\tau^{\prime}(X^{1},X^{2})<\tau_{0}\}
by noting that

(X^{1}_{t}-\lim_{s\uparrow t}X^{1}_{s})(X^{2}_{t}-\lim_{s\uparrow t}X^{2}_{s})=0

for any
t\in(0,\infty)
a.s. by the independence of
X^{1},X^{2}
. Therefore by
Lemma
5.10
with
\tau=\tau^{\prime},\tau_{\delta}
,
the last inequality in (
5.88
),
X^{1}_{0}\wedge X^{2}_{0}\geq\ell/10
,
the scaling property of
X^{1},X^{2}
,
[
20
, Chapter VIII, Proposition 4]

and
\delta^{-2/3}\ell\geq\exp(c_{2}\delta^{-u}/15)
we obtain

\displaystyle\mu_{\mathrm{QD}}^{L=\ell}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,\delta]}\widetilde{T}_{t}>\delta^{2/3-u}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\mu_{\mathrm{QD}}^{L=\ell}[\tau^{\prime}(L_{\cdot},R_{\cdot})\leq\delta]+\mu_{\mathrm{QD}}^{L=\ell}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,\delta]}\widetilde{T}_{t}>\delta^{2/3-u},\,\tau^{\prime}(L_{\cdot},R_{\cdot})>\delta\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\mu_{\mathrm{QD}}^{L=\ell}[\tau^{\prime}(L_{\cdot},R_{\cdot})\leq\delta]+\mu_{\mathrm{QD}}^{L=\ell}\bigl[\tau_{\delta}(L_{\cdot},R_{\cdot})<\delta\wedge t_{\widetilde{\eta}^{\prime}},\,L_{\tau_{\delta}(L_{\cdot},R_{\cdot})}+R_{\tau_{\delta}(L_{\cdot},R_{\cdot})}\geq\ell/20\bigr]

\displaystyle\leq 20^{5/2}\bigl(\mathbb{P}[\tau^{\prime}(X^{1},X^{2})\leq\delta]+\mathbb{P}[\tau_{\delta}(X^{1},X^{2})<\delta]\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq 20^{5/2}\biggl(\mathbb{P}\biggl[\min_{k\in\{1,2\}}\inf_{t\in[0,1]}(X^{k}_{t}-X^{k}_{0})\leq-\delta^{-2/3}\frac{\ell}{20}\biggr]+2c_{1}\exp(-c_{2}\delta^{-u}/2)\biggr)

(5.90)

\displaystyle\leq 20^{5/2}\bigl(c_{5}(\delta^{-2/3}\ell/20)^{-3/2}+2c_{1}\exp(-c_{2}\delta^{-u}/2)\bigr)\leq c_{6}\exp(-c_{2}\delta^{-u}/10)

for some constants
c_{5},c_{6}\in(0,\infty)
determined solely by the law of
X^{1}-X^{1}_{0}
.
Thus (
5.87
) and (
5.90
)
yield (
5.86
) when
\ell\geq\delta^{2/3}\exp(c_{2}\delta^{-u}/15)

and
a\in[1/10,9/10]
, completing the proof.
∎

Suppose now that we have the setup of Proposition
5.2
. We scale time by
\delta\in(0,1)
and lengths by
\delta^{2/3}
so that each chunk
\mathcal{N}_{j}
is drawn by a chordal
{\rm SLE}_{6}
curve stopped at a stopping time which is at most
\delta
and note that the setup is scale invariant. For every
j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, we let
(L^{j},R^{j})
be the pair of
3/2
-stable Lévy processes describing the boundary length evolution of the quantum surface parameterized by
\mathcal{N}_{j}
. We also define a process
X=(X_{t})_{t\geq 0}
as follows. First, we set
X_{t}=L_{t}^{0}+R_{t}^{0}
for each
t\in[0,\sigma_{0}]
. Fix
j\in\mathbb{N}
and suppose that we have defined
X_{t}
for each
t\in[0,\sigma_{j-1}]
. Then we set
X_{t}=X_{\sigma_{j-1}}+L_{t}^{j}+R_{t}^{j}
for each
t\in[\sigma_{j-1},\sigma_{j}]
and we proceed inductively. Since both of
L^{j}
and
L^{j}
are
3/2
-stable Lévy processes, the sequences
(L^{j})_{j\geq 0},(R^{j})_{j\geq 0}
are i.i.d. and the exploration is constructed in a Markovian way, we obtain that
X
has the law of a
3/2
-stable Lévy process. We note that
X
has only downward jumps and a jump occurs whenever a curve
\eta_{j}^{\prime}
either finishes tracing an
{\rm SLE}_{6}
bubble whose boundary is bounded away from
\partial\mathbb{H}
or it disconnects points of
\partial\mathbb{H}
from
\infty
. In either case, we have that the size of the jump of
X
is equal to the quantum boundary length of the quantum surface parameterized by the region that
\eta_{j}^{\prime}
disconnects from
\infty
. Moreover, we define inductively a collection of quantum surfaces
\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}=(\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
as follows. First, for
t\in[0,\sigma_{0}]
, we let
\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{t}
be the quantum surface parameterized by the unbounded connected component of
\mathbb{H}\setminus\eta_{0}^{\prime}([0,t])
. Fix
j\in\mathbb{N}
and suppose that we have defined
\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{t}
for
t\in[0,\sigma_{j-1}]
. Then for
t\in[\sigma_{j-1},\sigma_{j}]
, we let
\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{t}
be the quantum surface parameterized by the unbounded connected component of
\mathbb{H}\setminus\left(\eta_{j}^{\prime}([0,t])\cup(\cup_{i=0}^{j-1}\mathcal{N}_{i})\right)
. Then for each
t\geq 0
, we have that
X_{t}
is equal to the boundary length of
\partial\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{t}\setminus\partial\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{0}
minus the boundary length of
\partial\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{0}\setminus\partial\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{t}
.

Let us now briefly describe the main strategy for proving Proposition
5.1
. First, we will show in Lemma
5.12
that with positive probability (uniform in
\ell>0,\delta\in(0,1)
), we have that the chordal exploration in Proposition
5.2
disconnects from
\infty
at least
\ell/2
units of quantum boundary length in
(-\infty,0)
starting from
0
during the first
\lfloor\ell\delta^{-2/3-u/3}\rfloor+1
number of steps and using only good chunks, without disconnecting from
\infty
too many units of quantum area. Next, in Lemma
5.13
, we show that the same is true if we consider the chordal
{\rm SLE}_{6}
exploration on top of a quantum disk instead. For this, we are going to use Lemma
5.10
in order to compare the laws of the two explorations on top of the quantum wedge and the quantum disk respectively. Moreover, using Lemma
5.9
, we show in Lemma
5.14
that the statement of Lemma
5.13
still holds if we consider the radial
{\rm SLE}_{6}
exploration instead. Finally, we complete the proof of Proposition
5.1
by applying Lemma
5.14
iteratively.

Lemma 5.12
.

Suppose that we have the setup of Proposition
5.2
and the setup described in the previous paragraphs. Fix
0<u_{1}<u<1/3
,
\ell\in[\delta^{2/3-u},\delta^{-u_{1}}]
and set
N=\lfloor\ell\delta^{-2/3-u/3}\rfloor+1
. Let
b>0
(resp.
\widetilde{b}>0
) be such that
\nu_{h}([-b,0])=\ell/2
(resp.
\nu_{h}([-\widetilde{b},0])=3\ell/4)
. We let
E
be the event that the following hold.

(i)

There exists
1\leq j\leq N/2
such that the first
j
number of chunks in the exploration disconnect
[-b,0]
but not
[-\widetilde{b},0]
from
\infty
, and
\partial\mathcal{W}_{j}
intersects the boundaries of only good chunks.

(ii)

We have that
\sup_{0\leq t\leq 2\ell\delta^{1/3-u/3}}|X_{t}|<\ell\delta^{u/200}
and
\inf_{0\leq t\leq\delta}L_{t}^{0}>-\ell\delta^{u/200}
. Moreover, the total amount of quantum area disconnected from
\infty
by the first
N
number of chunks is at most a constant times
\delta^{u/18}
where the implicit constant depends only on
c_{0},A,u
and
u_{1}
.

(iii)

The chunk
\mathcal{N}_{0}
is good and the top of
\partial\mathcal{N}_{0}
is not disconnected from
\infty
by the first
N
number of chunks of the exploration. Moreover, the boundary length of the part of
\partial\mathcal{N}_{0}
contained in the boundary of the unbounded connected component of
\mathbb{H}\setminus\cup_{i=0}^{N}\mathcal{N}_{i}
is at least
\epsilon_{0}\delta^{2/3}
.

(iv)

For each
i\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[0,N]
, there exists
n\in[1,\log(\delta^{-1})^{3}]\cap\mathbb{N}
and
\{i_{j}\}_{j=1}^{n}\subseteq\mathbb{Z}\cap[0,N]
such that
i_{1}=i>i_{2}>\cdots>i_{n},\partial\mathcal{N}_{i_{n}}\cap\partial\mathcal{W}_{0}\neq\emptyset
and
\partial\mathcal{N}_{i_{j}}\cap\partial\mathcal{N}_{i_{j+1}}\neq\emptyset
, for each
1\leq j<n
.

Then there exist
\widetilde{q}
,
\delta_{0}\in(0,1)
depending only on
A,c_{0},u
and
u_{1}
such that for each
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
, we have that
\mathbb{P}[E]\geq 1-\widetilde{q}
.

Proof.

Step 1. (i) holds with high probability
. First we note that part (
i
) of Proposition
5.2
implies that there exist constants
c_{1},c_{2}>0
depending only on
A,c_{0}
such that
\mathbb{P}\big[L_{\lfloor N/2\rfloor}\geq-c_{1}N\delta^{2/3}\big]\leq e^{-c_{2}N}
, where
L_{j}
is defined as in the statement of Proposition
5.2
. Note that if
L_{\lfloor N/2\rfloor}<-c_{1}N\delta^{2/3}
, then we have that the first
\lfloor N/2\rfloor
number of chunks of the exploration have disconnected from
\infty
at least
c_{1}N\delta^{2/3}
units of quantum boundary length in
(-\infty,0)
starting from
0
. Note also that
c_{1}N\delta^{2/3}\geq c_{1}\ell\delta^{-u/3}>\ell/2
for each
\delta\in(0,1)
sufficiently small. In particular, the first
\lfloor N/2\rfloor
chunks of the exploration disconnect
[-b,0]
from
\infty
off an event with probability at most
e^{-c_{2}N}
. Moreover,
[
20
, Chapter VIII, Proposition 4]
implies that
\mathbb{P}\big[\inf_{0\leq t\leq\delta}L_{t}^{j}<-\ell\delta^{u/100}/16\big]\lesssim\ell^{-3/2}\delta^{1-3u/200}
for each
j
, with the implicit constant being universal. It follows by taking a union bound over all
0\leq j\leq N
and since
\ell\geq\delta^{2/3-u}
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big[\inf_{0\leq t\leq\delta}L_{t}^{j}<-\ell\delta^{u/100}/16\,\,\text{for some}\,\,0\leq j\leq N\big]\lesssim\delta^{a(u)}

where
a(u)>0
depends only on
u
and the implicit constant is universal. Also, part (
iv
) of Proposition
5.2
implies that there exist constants
c_{3},c_{4}>0
depending only on
A,c_{0}
such that off an event with probability at most
c_{3}N^{2}\exp(-c_{4}\delta^{-u/200})
the following holds. For each
i\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[0,N]
there exists
j\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[0,N)
such that
|j-i|\leq\delta^{-u/200},{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{j}\cap E_{j+1}}=1
and
\partial\mathcal{N}_{j}\cap\partial\mathcal{W}_{\text{L}}\neq\emptyset
.

Suppose that all of the events described in the previous paragraph hold and let
i
be the smallest
i\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[0,N/2]
such that the first
i
number of chunks of the exploration disconnect
[-b,0]
from
\infty
. Then there exists
j>i
such that
|j-i|\leq 2\delta^{-u/200},{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{E_{j}\cap E_{j+1}}=1
and
\partial\mathcal{N}_{j}\cap\partial\mathcal{W}_{\text{L}}\neq\emptyset
. Since
\inf_{0\leq t\leq\delta}L_{t}^{i}>-\ell\delta^{u/100}/16>-\ell/16
, we obtain that
\partial\mathcal{N}_{i}\cap\partial\mathcal{W}_{\text{L}}\subseteq[-b_{1},0]
, where
b_{1}>0
is such that
\nu_{h}([-b_{1},0])=\ell/2+\ell/16
. Furthermore, the boundary length of
\partial(\mathcal{W}_{i}\setminus\mathcal{W}_{j})\cap\partial_{\text{L}}\mathcal{W}
is at most
-\sum_{m=i+1}^{j}\inf_{0\leq t\leq\delta}L_{t}^{m}\leq\ell\delta^{u/200}/8<\ell/8
, and so we obtain that
\partial\mathcal{N}_{j}\cap\partial_{\text{L}}\mathcal{W}\subseteq[-b_{2},0]
where
b_{2}>0
is such that
\nu_{h}([-b_{2},0])=11\ell/16<3\ell/4
. It follows that there exist
a>0,\delta_{0}\in(0,1)
depending only on
A,c_{0}
and
u
such that for each
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
, we have off an event with probability at most
\delta^{a}
that there exists
1\leq j\leq N/2
such that
E_{j}
occurs and the chunks discovered during the first
j
steps of the exploration disconnect
[-b,0]
but not
[-\widetilde{b},0]
from
\infty
. Moreover, it follows from the way that we have defined the exploration that
\mathcal{N}_{m}
is good for each
0\leq m\leq j
such that
\partial\mathcal{N}_{m}\cap\partial\mathcal{W}_{j}\neq\emptyset
. Therefore (i) holds with high probability.

Step 2. (ii) holds with high probability
. Fix
p\in(1,3/2)
. Then
[
20
, Chapter VII,Corollary 2]
combined with
[
20
, Chapter VIII, Proposition 4]
imply that
\mathbb{E}\big[\sup_{0\leq t\leq 1}|X_{t}|^{p}\big]<\infty
. Thus, the maximal inequality for martingales combined with scaling imply that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{0\leq t\leq 2\ell\delta^{1/3-u/3}}|X_{t}|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{0\leq t\leq 2\ell\delta^{1/3-u/3}}|X_{t}|^{p}\right]^{1/p}

\displaystyle=(2\ell\delta^{1/3-u/3})^{2/3}\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{0\leq t\leq 1}|X_{t}|^{p}\right]^{1/p}\lesssim\ell^{2/3}\delta^{2/9-2u/9}.

Hence, Markov’s inequality implies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\sup_{0\leq t\leq 2\ell^{1/3-u/3}}|X_{t}|\geq\ell\delta^{u/200}\right]\lesssim\ell^{-1}\delta^{-u/200}\ell^{2/3}\delta^{2/9-2u/9}\lesssim\delta^{u/9-u/200}

since we also have that
\ell\geq\delta^{2/3-u}
. We note that during the first
N
steps of the exploration, we have that at most
2\ell\delta^{1/3-u/3}
units of time elapsed for
X
. Also, the expectation of the sums of squares of the jumps of
X
of size at most
\ell
made in the time interval
[0,2\ell\delta^{1/3-u/3}]
, is given by
c2\ell\delta^{1/3-u/3}\int_{x=0}^{\ell}x^{2}x^{-5/2}dx=4c\ell^{3/2}\delta^{1/3-u/3}
, where
c>0
is a universal constant. Furthermore, the number of jumps of size larger than
\ell
made by
X
in
[0,2\ell\delta^{1/3-u/3}]
has the law of a Poisson random variable with mean
2c\ell\delta^{1/3-u/3}\int_{x=\ell}^{\infty}x^{-5/2}dx=4c\ell^{-1/2}\delta^{1/3-u/3}/3
. It follows that the probability of having a jump of size at least
\ell
in
[0,2\ell\delta^{1/3-u/3}]
is given by
1-\exp(-4c\ell^{-1/2}\delta^{1/3-u/3}/3)
which is at most
4c\delta^{u/6}/3
since recall that
\ell\geq\delta^{2/3-u}
. Suppose that we are working on the event that we don’t have a jump of size at least
\ell
in
[0,2\ell\delta^{1/3-u/3}]
. Then
[
35
, Theorem 1.16]
implies that the conditional expectation of the total quantum area disconnected from
\infty
by the first
N
number of chunks given the sizes of the jumps of
X
in
[0,2\ell\delta^{1/3-u/3}]
is at most
4c\ell^{3/2}\delta^{1/3-u/3}
. It follows from the Markov property that the conditional probability that more than
\ell^{3/2}\delta^{1/3-u/2+u/9}
units of quantum area is disconnected from
\infty
by the first
N
number of chunks of the exploration is at most
4c\ell^{-3/2}\delta^{-1/3+u/2-u/9}\ell^{3/2}\delta^{1/3-u/3}/3=4c\delta^{u/18}/3
. Note that
\ell^{3/2}\delta^{1/3-u/2+u/9}\leq\delta^{-3u_{1}/2+1/3-7u/8}
since
\ell\leq\delta^{-u_{1}}
. Therefore, possibly by taking the constant
a
in Step 1 to be smaller, we can assume that (ii) holds off an event with probability at most
\delta^{a}
for all
\delta\in(0,1)
sufficiently small (depending only on
A,c_{0},u_{1}
and
u
).

Step 3. Conclusion of the proof
. We note that Lemma
5.5
implies that provided
A^{-1}\vee c_{0}
is small enough, we have that there exists
\widetilde{q}\in(0,1)
depending only on
A,c_{0}
such that with probability at least
1-\widetilde{q}
, the following holds. The chunk
\mathcal{N}_{0}
is good and the top of
\partial\mathcal{N}_{0}
is never disconnected from
\infty
by the exploration. Note also that if the latter occurs, we have that the boundary length of the part of
\partial\mathcal{N}_{0}
contained in the boundary of the unbounded connected component of
\mathbb{H}\setminus\cup_{i=0}^{N}\mathcal{N}_{i}
is at least
\epsilon_{0}\delta^{2/3}
. This proves (iii). Finally, for (iv), we note that part (
iii
) of Proposition
5.2
combined with the fact that
\ell\leq\delta^{-u_{1}}
imply that there exist constants
c_{5},c_{6}>0
depending only on
A,c_{0},u
and
u_{1}
such that for all
\delta\in(0,1)
sufficiently small (depending only on
A,c_{0},u
and
u_{1}
), we have that off an event with probability at most
c_{5}\exp(-c_{6}\log(\delta^{-1})^{3/2})
the claim in part (iv) of the statement of the lemma holds. Therefore, possibly by taking
\widetilde{q}\in(0,1)
to be smaller and for all
\delta\in(0,1)
sufficiently small (depending only on
A,c_{0},u
and
u_{1}
), we can assume that parts (i)-(iv) hold simultaneously with probability at least
\widetilde{q}
. This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Next, we will prove that the statement of Lemma
5.12
still holds if we perform the analogous exploration using chordal
{\rm SLE}_{6}
chunks on top of a quantum disk instead. This is the content of the following lemma.

Lemma 5.13
.

Fix
0<u_{1}<u<1/3
and
\delta^{2/3-u}\leq\ell\leq\delta^{-u_{1}}
for
\delta\in(0,1)
. Suppose that
\mathcal{D}=(\mathbb{D},h)
is a sample from
\mu_{\mathrm{QD}}^{L=\ell}
and let
x\in\partial\mathbb{D}
be chosen uniformly according to
\nu_{h}
. Let also
y
be the point which is antipodal to
x
(with respect to quantum boundary length),
z
be the point on the clockwise arc of
\partial\mathbb{D}
from
x
to
y
such that the boundary length of the clockwise arc of
\partial\mathbb{D}
from
x
to
z
is equal to
\ell/4
, and let
w
(resp.
\widetilde{z}
) be the point on the clockwise (resp. counterclockwise) arc of
\partial\mathbb{D}
from
x
to
y
such that the boundary length of the clockwise (resp. counterclockwise) arc of
\partial\mathbb{D}
from
x
to
w
(resp. from
x
to
\widetilde{z}
) is equal to
3\ell/8
(resp.
\ell/4
). Suppose that we perform the exploration on top of
\mathcal{D}
in the same way that we did on top of the quantum wedge except that each chunk is formed by a chordal
{\rm SLE}_{6}
starting from the marked point of the chunk and targeted at
y
