. We stop the exploration at the first time that we discover a chunk which contains
y
, so that the corresponding chordal
{\rm SLE}_{6}
curve hits
y
before the chunk is formed. Let
E_{1}
be the event that the following hold.

(i)

There exists
1\leq j\leq N/2
such that the first
j
number of chunks of the exploration disconnect from booth
y
and
0
the clockwise arc of
\partial\mathbb{D}
from
x
to
z
without disconnecting from
y
either the clockwise arc of
\partial\mathbb{D}
from
x
to
w
or the counterclockwise arc of
\partial\mathbb{D}
from
x
to
\widetilde{z}
. Also, if
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{0},\ldots,\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}
are the corresponding chunks of the exploration, we have that the boundary of the connected component of
\mathbb{D}\setminus\cup_{i=0}^{j}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}
containing
0
intersects the boundaries of only good chunks.

(ii)

\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{0}
is good and the top of
\partial\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{0}
is not disconnected either from
0
or
y
and the boundary length of the part of
\partial\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{0}
contained in the boundary of the connected component of
\mathbb{D}\setminus\cup_{i=0}^{j}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}
containing
0
is in
[\epsilon_{0}\delta^{2/3},\ell\delta^{u/200}]
.

(iii)

For each
0\leq i\leq N/2
, we let
D_{i}
be the connected component of
\mathbb{D}\setminus\cup_{m=0}^{i}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{m}
whose boundary contains
y
. Then, we have that the boundary length of
\mathbb{D}\cap\partial D_{i}
is at most
\ell/100
plus the boundary length of
\partial\mathbb{D}\setminus\partial D_{i}
.

(iv)

For each
0\leq i\leq j
, there exist
\{i_{j}\}_{j=1}^{n}\subseteq\mathbb{Z}\cap[0,j]
and
1\leq n\leq\log(\delta^{-1})^{3}
, such that
i=i_{1}>i_{2}>\cdots>i_{n}
,
\partial\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i_{n}}\cap\partial\mathbb{D}
, and
\partial\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i_{m+1}}\cap\partial\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i_{m}}\neq\emptyset
for each
0\leq m\leq n-1
.

Then, there exist
p_{1},\delta_{0}\in(0,1)
depending only on
A,c_{0},u_{1}
and
u
, such that for each
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
, we have that
\mathbb{P}[E_{1}]\geq p_{1}
.

Proof.

Suppose that we have the setup of Lemma
5.12
. Let
\widetilde{E}
be the event defined in the same way as
E
but with
\ell/2
in place of
\ell
and for the exploration with respect to the quantum disk
\mathcal{D}
instead and the marked points
0
and
\infty
replaced by
x
and
y
respectively. Fix
j\in\mathbb{N}
. Then Lemma
5.10
implies that conditional on the event that the exploration in
\mathcal{D}
has not ended during the first
j-1
steps and on the boundary length
\ell_{j-1}^{\text{L}}
(resp.
\ell_{j-1}^{\text{R}}
) of the clockwise (resp. counterclockwise) arc of
\partial D_{j-1}
from
\eta_{j}^{\prime}(0)
to
y
, we have that the Radon-Nikodym derivative of the law of
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}
with respect to the law of
\mathcal{N}_{j}
(when both viewed as quantum surfaces) is given by
\Bigl(\frac{L_{\sigma_{j}}^{j}+R_{\sigma_{j}}^{j}}{\ell_{j-1}^{\text{L}}+\ell_{j-1}^{\text{R}}}+1\Bigr)^{-5/2}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\sigma_{j}<S^{j}\}}
, where
S^{j}
is the first time
t
such that either
L_{t}^{j}\leq-\ell_{j-1}^{\text{L}}
or
R_{t}^{j}\leq-\ell_{j-1}^{\text{R}}
. Let
j_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
be the smallest integer for which condition (i) of Lemma
5.12
is satisfied. Therefore, the Radon-Nikodym derivative of the law of the quantum surfaces
(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{0},\ldots,\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j_{0}})
with respect to the law of the quantum surfaces
(\mathcal{N}_{0},\ldots,\mathcal{N}_{j_{0}})
is given by
\Bigl(\frac{\ell_{j_{0}}^{\text{L}}+\ell_{j_{0}}^{\text{R}}}{\ell}\Bigr)^{-5/2}
. Note that
\ell_{j_{0}}^{\text{L}}+\ell_{j_{0}}^{\text{R}}<2\ell
and
\ell_{j_{0}}^{\text{L}}+\ell_{j_{0}}^{\text{R}}>\ell/2
if
E
occurs which implies that
\Bigl(\frac{\ell_{j_{0}}^{\text{L}}+\ell_{j_{0}}^{\text{R}}}{\ell}\Bigr)^{-5/2}\asymp 1
with the implicit constants being universal. Thus, combining with Lemma
5.12
, there exist
\delta_{0},\widetilde{p}\in(0,1)
depending only on
A,c_{0},u
and
u_{1}
such that
\mathbb{P}\big[\widetilde{E}\big]\geq\widetilde{p}
for each
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
.

Next, conditionally on
h
, we sample
w\in\mathbb{D}
independently according to the probability measure
\frac{\mu_{h}}{\mu_{h}(\mathbb{D})}
on
\mathbb{D}
, and set
\widetilde{h}:=h\circ\phi^{-1}+Q\log|(\phi^{-1})^{\prime}|
where
\phi:\mathbb{D}\to\mathbb{D}
is the conformal transformation such that
\phi(w)=0
and
\phi^{\prime}(w)>0
. Then the marginal law of
(\mathbb{D},\widetilde{h})
is given by
\mu_{\mathrm{QD,W}}^{L=\ell}
. Suppose that
\widetilde{E}
occurs. Then we have that the quantum area of
\cup_{i=0}^{j_{0}}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}
with respect to
h
is at most
\ell^{3/2}\delta^{1/3-u/2+u/9}
. Also, by scaling, we have that the probability that
\mu_{h}(\mathbb{D})
is at least
\ell^{2}\delta^{u/18}
tends to
1
as
\delta\to 0
, at a rate which is uniform in
\delta
. Hence, by possibly taking
\widetilde{p},\delta_{0}\in(0,1)
to be smaller, we can assume that the probability of
\widetilde{E}\cap\{\mu_{h}(\mathbb{D})\geq\ell^{2}\delta^{u/18}\}
under
\mu_{\mathrm{QD}}^{L=\ell}
is at least
\widetilde{p}
. Note that if
\widetilde{E}\cap\{\mu_{h}(\mathbb{D})\geq\ell^{2}\delta^{u/18}\}
occurs, then we have that conditional on
h
, the probability that
w
lies in
\cup_{i=0}^{j_{0}}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}
is at most
\frac{\ell^{3/2}\delta^{1/3-u/2+u/9}}{\ell^{2}\delta^{u/18}}\leq\delta^{u/18}
. Therefore, by possibly taking
\widetilde{p},\delta_{0}\in(0,1)
to be smaller, we can assume that
\mu_{\mathrm{QD,W}}^{L=\ell}\big[\widetilde{E}_{1}\big]\geq\widetilde{p}
, where
\widetilde{E}_{1}
is the event defined in the same way as the event
E_{1}
of Lemma
5.12
except that we consider the exploration with respect to
\widetilde{h}
instead of
h
. It follows that

\displaystyle\widetilde{p}\leq\mu_{\mathrm{QD,W}}^{L=\ell}\big[\widetilde{E}_{1}\big]\leq\mu_{\mathrm{QD}}^{L=\ell}\big[E_{1}\big]^{1/5}\frac{(\int\mu_{h}(\mathbb{D})^{5/4}d\mu_{\mathrm{QD}}^{L=\ell})^{4/5}}{\int\mu_{h}(\mathbb{D})d\mu_{\mathrm{QD}}^{L=\ell}}\lesssim\mu_{\mathrm{QD}}^{L=\ell}\big[E_{1}\big]^{1/5},

where the implicit constant is universal. This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Now that we have stated and proved Lemma
5.13
, we can state and prove the analogous version for the exploration using radial
{\rm SLE}_{6}
chunks instead.

Lemma 5.14
.

Fix
0<u_{1}<u<1/3
and
\delta^{2/3-u}\leq\ell\leq\delta^{-u_{1}}
for
\delta\in(0,1)
. Suppose that
\mathcal{D}=(\mathbb{D},h,0)
has law given by
\mu_{\mathrm{QD,W}}^{L=\ell}
. Suppose also that we perform the exploration using radial
{\rm SLE}_{6}
chunks as in the statement of Proposition
5.1
. Let
\widetilde{E}_{1}
be the event defined in the same way as the event
E_{1}
of Lemma
5.13
except that we consider the radial
{\rm SLE}_{6}
exploration. Then, there exist
p_{0},\delta_{0}\in(0,1)
depending only on
A,c_{0},u_{1}
and
u
such that
\mathbb{P}\big[\widetilde{E}_{1}\big]\geq p_{0}
for each
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
.

Proof.

First, we note that the radial
{\rm SLE}_{6}
curves used to construct the chunks
(\widehat{\mathcal{N}}_{j})
for the radial exploration can be coupled with chordal
{\rm SLE}_{6}
curves starting from the same point so that they agree up until the first time that they disconnect
y
from
0
. Moreover, let
X
be the overall boundary length process for the radial exploration. Then, by
[
75
, Theorem 7.3]
, the exploration is determined by
X
, the conditionally independent family of quantum disks which are cut out, and the orientations of their boundaries. We note that the boundaries are oriented i.i.d. with equal probability
\frac{1}{2}
given
X
. We can use these orientations in order to determine the boundary length process
L
(resp.
R
) from the tip of the exploration clockwise (resp. counterclockwise) to
y
, up until the first time that the radial exploration disconnects
y
from
0
. Indeed, this is because
L
(resp.
R
) is equal to the process which is formed by applying the deterministic function which recovers a
3/2
-stable Lévy process from its jumps to the downward jumps of
X
which correspond to quantum disks whose boundaries have a counterclockwise (resp. clockwise) direction. This implies that the following is true. Fix
j\in\mathbb{N}
and suppose that we are working on the event that the first
j-1
chunks of the exploration do not disconnect
y
from
0
. Let also
\ell_{j-1}
be the boundary length of the component containing
0
after
j-1
steps of radial exploration. Then, it follows by combining Lemmas
5.9
and
5.10
that the Radon-Nikodym derivative of the law of
\widehat{\mathcal{N}}_{j}
on the event that
\widehat{\mathcal{N}}_{j}
does not disconnect
y
from
0
with respect to the law of
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}
where
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}
is as in Lemma
5.13
, is given by
\left(\frac{\ell_{j}}{\ell_{j-1}}\right)^{2}
, where
\ell_{j}
is the boundary length of the connected component of
\mathbb{D}\setminus\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j}
containing
0
. It follows that on the events
\widetilde{E}_{1},E_{1}
, if
j_{0}
is as in Lemma
5.13
, then the Radon-Nikodym derivative of the law of
(\widehat{\mathcal{N}}_{0},\ldots,\widehat{\mathcal{N}}_{j_{0}})
with respect to the law of
(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{0},\ldots,\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{j_{0}})
is given by
\left(\frac{\widetilde{\ell}}{\ell}\right)^{2}
, where
\widetilde{\ell}
is the boundary length of the component of
\mathbb{D}\setminus\cup_{i=0}^{j_{0}}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{i}
containing
0
. Since
\frac{\widetilde{\ell}}{\ell}\asymp 1
on
E_{1}
with the implicit constants being universal, the proof of the lemma is complete.
∎

Now we are ready to prove Proposition
5.1
. The main idea of the proof is to apply Lemma
5.14
iteratively up until we find the desired chain of good chunks as in the statement of Proposition
5.1
. The conditions in the definition of the event
\widetilde{E}_{1}
in Lemma
5.14
will guarantee that it is possible to construct the desired chain with high probability since the probability of
\widetilde{E}_{1}
is bounded from below by a constant which is uniform in
\delta
.

Now, we proceed to the details of the proof. Suppose that we have the setup of the statement of Proposition
5.1
. Fix
u_{1}\in(0,u/3)
and let
\widetilde{E}_{u_{1},\delta}
be the event that for each
j\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[0,\delta^{-2/3-u}]
and each
t\in[0,\sigma_{j}]
, the boundary length of the
0
-containing connected component of
D_{j}\setminus\eta_{j}^{\prime}([0,t])
is at most
\delta^{-u_{1}}
. We define sequences of marked points
\{\widetilde{x}_{j}\}_{j\geq 0},\{\widetilde{y}_{j}\}_{j\geq 0},\{\widetilde{z}_{j}\}_{j\geq 0}
and domains
\{\widetilde{D}_{j}\}_{j\geq 0}
as follows. First, we pick
x\in\partial\mathbb{D}
uniformly according to the boundary length measure and let
y
be the point on
\partial\mathbb{D}
which is antipodal to
x
with respect to the boundary length measure.. Let also
z
be the point on the clockwise arc of
\partial\mathbb{D}
from
x
to
y
such that the clockwise arc of
\partial\mathbb{D}
from
x
to
z
has boundary length equal to
1/4
. Then we set
\widetilde{x}_{0}=x,\widetilde{y}_{0}=y
and
\widetilde{z}_{0}=z
. We also set
\widetilde{D}_{0}=\mathbb{D}=D_{0}
. Fix
j\in\mathbb{N}
and suppose that we have defined marked points
\{(\widetilde{x}_{i},\widetilde{y}_{i},\widetilde{z}_{i})\}_{i=0}^{j}
and domains
\{\widetilde{D}_{i}\}_{i=0}^{j}
. Let
\ell_{j}
be the boundary length of
\partial\widetilde{D}_{j}
and set
N_{j}=\lfloor\ell_{j}\delta^{-2/3-u/3}\rfloor+1
. Let also
F_{j}
be the event that the event
\widetilde{E}_{1}
defined in Lemma
5.14
occurs for the quantum surface parameterized by
\widetilde{D}_{j}
. We also let
\widetilde{w}_{j}
be the point on the clockwise arc of
\partial\widetilde{D}_{j}
from
\widetilde{x}_{j}
to
\widetilde{y}_{j}
with boundary length distance on
\partial\widetilde{D}_{j}
from
\widetilde{x}_{j}
equal to
3\ell_{j}/8
. Suppose that
F_{j}
occurs. Then, we let
j_{0}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
be the first
0\leq m\leq N_{j}
for which we can find the desired sequence of good chunks in the definition of
F_{j}
during the first
m
steps of the exploration and starting after the last chunk discovered during the formation of
\partial\widetilde{D}_{j}
. Then, we let
\widetilde{x}_{j+1}
be the marked point of the exploration in
\widetilde{D}_{j}
after we have performed it for
j_{0}
times. Suppose that
F_{j}
does not occur. Then, at least one of the following has to occur for the exploration in
\widetilde{D}_{j}
before
\widetilde{w}_{j}
is disconnected from
0
for the first time.

(i)

There is a chunk which cannot be connected to
\partial\widetilde{D}_{j}
by at most
\log(\delta^{-1})^{3}
number of chunks discovered before that chunk.

(ii)

\widetilde{z}_{j}
is not disconnected from
0
by good chunks.

(iii)

The first chunk is not good.

(iv)

The first chunk is good but it is disconnected from
0
before
\widetilde{z}_{j}
is disconnected from
0
.

(v)

N_{j}
number of chunks have been explored.

(vi)

\widetilde{z}_{j}
and
\widetilde{w}_{j}
are disconnected from
0
simultaneously.

If (
i
) occurs, we let
\widetilde{x}_{j+1}
be the marked point of the exploration after we discover the first chunk which cannot be connected to
\partial\mathbb{D}
by at most
\log(\delta^{-1})^{3}
number of chunks discovered before that chunk. If either (
ii
) or (
vi
) occurs, we let
\widetilde{x}_{j+1}
be the marked point of the exploration after we discover the first chunk
\mathcal{N}
which disconnects
\widetilde{z}_{j}
from
0
. If (
iii
) occurs, we let
\widetilde{x}_{j+1}
be the marked point of the exploration after we discover the first chunk of the exploration in
\widetilde{D}_{j}
. If (
iv
) occurs, we let
\widetilde{x}_{j+1}
be the marked point of the exploration after we discover the first chunk which disconnects from
0
the first chunk of the exploration in
\widetilde{D}_{j}
. Finally, if (
v
) occurs, we let
\widetilde{x}_{j+1}
be the marked point of the exploration in
\widetilde{D}_{j}
after
N_{j}
number of steps. In any case, we let
\widetilde{D}_{j+1}
be the connected component containing
0
in the exploration in
\widetilde{D}_{j}
whose boundary contains
\widetilde{x}_{j+1}
. Also, we let
\widetilde{y}_{j+1}
be the point on
\partial\widetilde{D}_{j+1}
with boundary length distance from
\widetilde{x}_{j+1}
in
\partial\widetilde{D}_{j+1}
equal to
\ell_{j+1}/2
, where
\ell_{j+1}
is the boundary length of
\partial\widetilde{D}_{j+1}
. Moreover, we let
\widetilde{z}_{j+1}
(resp.
\widetilde{w}_{j+1}
) be the point on the clockwise arc of
\partial\widetilde{D}_{j+1}
from
\widetilde{x}_{j+1}
to
\widetilde{y}_{j+1}
with boundary length distance from
\widetilde{x}_{j+1}
equal to
\ell_{j+1}/4
(resp.
3\ell_{j+1}/8
), and set
N_{j+1}:=\lfloor\ell_{j+1}\delta^{-2/3-u/3}\rfloor+1
. We then let
F_{j+1}
be the event that the event
\widetilde{E}_{1}
defined in Lemma
5.14
occurs for the quantum surface parameterized by
\widetilde{D}_{j+1}
.

Proof of Proposition
5.1
.

Suppose that we have the setup described in the above paragraphs. For each
j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, we let
G_{j}^{1}
be the event that the boundary length of
\widetilde{D}_{j}
is at least
\delta^{-2/3-u}
and
G_{j}^{2}
the event that the boundary length of
\partial\widetilde{D}_{j}
is at most
\delta^{-u_{1}}
. Let also
\mathcal{F}_{j}
be the
\sigma
-algebra generated by the chunks discovered up until
\partial\widetilde{D}_{j}
is formed. Lemma
5.14
implies that there exists
p_{0}\in(0,1)
depending only on
A,c_{0},u
and
u_{1}
such that
\mathbb{P}\big[F_{j}\,|\,\mathcal{F}_{j}\big]{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{G_{j}}\geq p_{0}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{G_{j}}
for each
j\in\mathbb{N}
a.s., where
G_{j}=G_{j}^{1}\cap G_{j}^{2}
. Thus, by iterating and possibly taking
p_{0}\in(0,1)
to be smaller (depending only on
A,c_{0},u
and
u_{1}
), we can assume that
\mathbb{P}\big[\widetilde{F}_{j}\,|\,\mathcal{F}_{j}\big]{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{G_{j}}\geq p_{0}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{G_{j}}
for each
j\in\mathbb{N}
a.s., where
\widetilde{F}_{j}=\cup_{i=j}^{j+4}F_{i}
. Suppose that
\widetilde{F}_{j}\cap E_{u,\delta}\cap\widetilde{E}_{u_{1},\delta}
occurs for some
0\leq j\leq\delta^{-u/3}
. Then, we will show that the required events in the statement of Proposition
5.1
occur as well. Indeed, first we note that it is easy to see that the exploration disconnects
\widetilde{x}_{j}
from
0
using only good chunks. Also, since
F_{j}
occurs, if
\mathcal{N}
is the first chunk discovered in the exploration in
\widetilde{D}_{j}
, then the boundary length of the part of
\mathcal{N}
contained in
\widetilde{D}_{j}
is in
[\epsilon_{0}\delta^{2/3},\ell_{j}\delta^{u/200}]
. Thus, possibly by taking
p_{0}\in(0,1)
to be smaller (depending only on
A,c_{0},u
and
u_{0}
), we can assume that the aforementioned part of
\partial\mathcal{N}
is disconnected from
0
by the exploration in
\widetilde{D}_{j}
. It follows that both of the conditions in the definition of
N_{\delta}
hold and that
N_{\delta}\leq 5(1+\delta^{-2/3-u_{0}-u/3})\leq\delta^{-2/3-u}
for all
\delta\in(0,1)
sufficiently small (depending only on
A,c_{0},u
and
u_{1}
). Furthermore, every chunk discovered in the exploration in
\widetilde{D}_{j}
up until
\partial\widetilde{D}_{j+1}
is formed can be connected to
\partial\widetilde{D}_{j}
using at most
\log(\delta^{-1})^{3}
number of chunks. Since the definition of the
\widetilde{D}_{i}
’s implies that for each
i
, we have that every chunk discovered in the exploration in
\widetilde{D}_{i}
up until
\partial\widetilde{D}_{i+1}
is formed can be connected to
\partial\widetilde{D}_{i}
using at most
2\log(\delta^{-1})^{3}
number of chunks, we obtain that every chunk discovered in up until
\partial\widetilde{D}_{i+1}
is formed can be connected to
\partial\mathbb{D}
using at most
10\delta^{-u/3}\log(\delta^{-1})^{3}
number of chunks and the latter is at most
\delta^{-u}
for
\delta\in(0,1)
sufficiently small (depending only on
A,c_{0},u_{1}
and
u
). Therefore, the events in (
i
) and (
ii
) occur.

Finally, to complete the proof, we
\widetilde{F}
be the event that
F_{5j}
occurs for some
0\leq j\leq\delta^{-u/3}/5-1
. It follows from the previous paragraph that there exist constants
c_{1},c_{2}>0
depending only on
p_{0}
such that
\mathbb{P}\big[\widetilde{F}^{c}\cap E_{u,\delta}\cap\widetilde{E}_{u_{1},\delta}\big]\leq c_{1}\exp(-c_{2}\delta^{-u/3})
. Therefore, it suffices to give appropriate upper bounds for
\mathbb{P}\big[\widetilde{E}_{u_{1},\delta}^{c}\big]
. But the latter follows by combining Lemma
5.9
with Proposition
B.7
. In particular, we obtain that there exist universal constants
c_{3},c_{4}>0
such that
\mathbb{P}\big[\widetilde{E}_{u_{1},\delta}^{c}\big]\leq c_{3}\exp(-c_{4}\delta^{-u_{1}})
. This completes the proof.
∎

6.
Quenched bounds for the expected exit time from a metric ball

In this section we will prove one main ingredient which is used to prove the upper bound in the heat kernel estimate, namely quenched upper and lower bounds for the exit time of a Liouville Brownian motion from a ball. For a set
A\subseteq\mathcal{S}
, we let
\tau_{A}
be the exit time of the Liouville Brownian motion from
A
. Let
\mu_{\mathrm{QSPH}}
denote the law of the infinite quantum sphere.

Theorem 6.1
.

There exists a deterministic constant
\kappa>0
so that the following is true. For
\mu_{\mathrm{QSPH}}
-a.e. instance
(\mathcal{S},h,x,y)
there exists
r_{0}>0
random such that for every
z\in\mathcal{S}
and
r\in(0,r_{0})
we have that

(6.1)

r^{4}(\log r^{-1})^{-\kappa}\leq E_{z}[\tau_{B_{h}(z,r)}]\leq r^{4}(\log r^{-1})^{\kappa},

where the expectation is over just the Brownian motion and the Brownian map instance is fixed.

We note that (
6.1
) is equivalent to proving that

(6.2)

r^{4}(\log r^{-1})^{-\kappa}\leq\int_{\mathcal{S}}G_{B_{h}(z,r)}(z,y)\,d\mu_{h}(y)\leq r^{4}(\log r^{-1})^{\kappa}

where
G_{B_{h}(z,r)}
is the Green’s function on the ball
B_{h}(z,r)
. To establish (
6.1
) we will in fact establish (
6.2
).
Also, we note that
[
73
, Theorem 1.2]
implies that there exists a deterministic constant
\alpha\in(0,1)
such that
\mu_{\mathrm{QSPH}}
-a.e. there exists a random constant
C\geq 1
such that for all
u,v\in\mathcal{S}
we have that

(6.3)

C^{-1}d(u,v)^{1/\alpha}\leq d_{h}(u,v)\leq Cd(u,v)^{\alpha},

where we recall that
d
denotes the Euclidean metric on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
and we assume that
(\mathcal{S},x,y)
is parameterized by
\mathbb{S}^{2}
.

The proof of the upper bound in Theorem
6.1
is straightforward and short and given in Subsection
6.1
. The proof of the lower bound is much more involved and given in the remainder of this section; see Figure
4
for an illustration of the proof.

6.1.
Proof of the exit time upper bound

The upper bound in Theorem
6.1
follows from (
6.3
) and the upper volume growth estimate in Theorem
4.1
by the following argument.

Proof of Theorem
6.1
, upper bound.

Let
B=B_{h}(z,r)
be fixed. By (
6.3
) we have
B\subset B\big(z,c_{1}r^{\alpha}\big)
for some finite random constant
c_{1}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle E_{z}\big[\tau_{B}\big]\;=\;\int_{B}G_{B}(z,u)\,d\mu_{h}(u)\;\leq\;\int_{B}G_{B(z,2c_{1}r^{\alpha})}(z,u)\,d\mu_{h}(u).

On the other hand, recall that by (
3.16
)

\displaystyle G_{B(0,1/2)}(u,v)\;=\;\frac{1}{\pi}\log\frac{1}{d(u,v)}+F(u,v)

for some continuous function
F:B(0,1/2)\times B(0,1/2)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
. Hence, for all
u\in B
,

\displaystyle G_{B(z,2c_{1}r^{\alpha})}(z,u)

\displaystyle\;=\;G_{B(0,2c_{1}r^{\alpha})}(0,u-z)\;=\;G_{B(0,1/2)}\big(0,\tfrac{u-z}{4c_{1}r^{\alpha}}\big)

\displaystyle\;=\;\frac{1}{\pi}\log\frac{4c_{1}r^{\alpha}}{d(z,u)}+F\big(0,\tfrac{u-z}{4c_{1}r^{\alpha}}\big)

\displaystyle\;\leq\;\frac{1}{\pi}\log\frac{cr^{\alpha}}{d_{h}(z,u)^{1/\alpha}}+\sup_{B(0,1/4)}F(0,\cdot)\;=\;\frac{1}{\pi}\log\!\big(r^{\alpha}d_{h}(z,u)^{-1/\alpha}\big)+\widetilde{c},

for some random constants
c,\widetilde{c}
, where we used the second inequality in (
6.3
) in the fourth step. For abbreviation we introduce the sets
A_{n}:=B_{h}(z,2^{-n}r)\setminus B_{h}(z,2^{-n-1}r)
,
n\geq 0
. Then the upper estimate on the volume growth in Theorem
4.1
gives that there exists a deterministic constant
\kappa>0
such that
\mu_{\mathrm{QSPH}}
-a.e. there exists random
r_{0}>0
such that for each
z\in\mathcal{S},r\in(0,r_{0})
, we have that

\displaystyle\mu_{h}(A_{n})\leq\big(2^{-n}r\big)^{4}\big(\log\big(2^{n}/r\big)\big)^{\kappa}\leq 2^{-4n}r^{4}\big(n\log 2+\log(r^{-1})\big)^{\kappa}.

By combining the above estimates we obtain that

\displaystyle E_{z}\big[\tau_{B}\big]

\displaystyle\;\leq\;\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{B}\log\!\big(r^{\alpha}d_{h}(z,u)^{-1/\alpha}\big)\,d\mu_{h}(u)+\widetilde{c}\,\mu_{h}(B)

\displaystyle\;\leq\;\frac{1}{\pi}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\int_{A_{n}\cap B}\log\!\big(r^{\alpha}d_{h}(z,u)^{-1/\alpha}\big)\,d\mu_{h}(u)+\widetilde{c}\,\mu_{h}(B)

\displaystyle\;\leq\;\frac{1}{\pi}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\log\!\big(r^{\alpha}(2^{-n-1}r)^{-1/\alpha}\big)\,\mu_{h}(A_{n}\cap B)+\widetilde{c}\,\mu_{h}(B)

\displaystyle\;\leq\;\frac{1-\alpha^{2}}{\pi\alpha}\log(r^{-1})\,\mu_{h}(B)+\widetilde{c}\,\mu_{h}(B)+\frac{\log 2}{\alpha\pi}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(n+1)\,\mu_{h}(A_{n})

\displaystyle\;\leq\;\widehat{c}\,\log(r^{-1})\,\mu_{h}(B)+\widehat{c}\,r^{4}\,\log(r^{-1})^{\kappa},

for some random constant
\widehat{c}
and the claim follows from the upper estimate in Theorem
4.1
.
∎

6.2.
Definition of the good event

Figure 4.
Illustration of the main step in the proof of the exit time lower bound from Theorem
6.1
. We will construct annuli consisting of “good”
{\rm SLE}_{6}
chunks (green) so that whenever a Liouville Brownian motion passes through such an annulus it is likely to take at least a certain amount of time to do so. The definition of the good chunk (Subsection
6.2
) includes a lower bound on the amount of quantum mass that it has disconnected away from its boundary. We will control the size of the good annuli in Subsection
6.3
. The purpose of Subsection
6.4
is to show that whenever a Liouville Brownian motion passes through such an annulus, it is very likely to enter the interior of such a good chunk and hence take a certain amount of time to pass through. The proof is completed in Subsection
6.5
, where we show that these good annuli are likely to occur all over our quantum sphere.

Throughout this section, we will make use of the notation introduced in Subsections
5.1
and
5.2
some of which we now recall. Suppose that we have a quantum surface
\mathcal{D}
which is homeomorphic to
\mathbb{D}
.
For
x,y\in\partial\mathcal{D}
, we let
[x,y]_{\partial\mathcal{D}}^{\bm{\circlearrowright}}
(resp.
[x,y]_{\partial\mathcal{D}}^{\bm{\circlearrowleft}}
) denote the clockwise (resp. counterclockwise) arc of
\partial\mathcal{D}
from
x
to
y
.

We are now going to make a particular choice of the event
E
in the context of Proposition
5.1
, where
E
is considered as a Borel subset of
\mathbb{M}^{\mathrm{CPU}}_{2}
. Fix
\delta\in(0,1)
and
A\geq A_{0}
with
A_{0}\in[2,\infty)
as in Proposition
5.1
.
Suppose that we consider one of the following two quantum surfaces. Either we let
\mathcal{D}=(\mathbb{D},h,0)
have law
\mu_{\mathrm{QD,W}}^{L=\ell}
and
\eta^{\prime}
be an independent radial
{\rm SLE}_{6}
starting from a uniformly random point on the boundary and targeted at
0
, or we let

\mathcal{W}=(\mathbb{H},h,0,\infty)
have law
\mu_{\mathrm{QW}}^{W=2}
and
\eta^{\prime}
be an independent chordal
{\rm SLE}_{6}
on
\mathbb{H}
from
0
to
\infty
. Recall the definition of
\sigma_{\delta/A}
in (
5.6
),
and set
\sigma=\delta\wedge\sigma_{\delta/A}
. Let
\mathcal{N}=\mathcal{N}_{\sigma}^{\mathcal{D}}
or
\mathcal{N}=\mathcal{N}_{\sigma}^{\mathcal{W}}
, respectively, be the quantum surface disconnected from
0
by
\eta^{\prime}([0,\sigma])
, namely that parameterized by the interior of the hull
K_{\sigma}
of
\eta^{\prime}([0,\sigma])
.
Recall that the top (resp. bottom) of
\partial\mathcal{N}
is given by
\partial\mathcal{N}\cap\mathcal{D}
(resp.
\partial\mathcal{N}\cap\partial\mathcal{D}
).
The left (resp. right) side of the top is the part of the top which is to the left (resp. right) of
\eta^{\prime}(\sigma)
. Similarly, the left (resp. right) side of the bottom is the part of the bottom which is to the left (resp. right) of
\eta^{\prime}(0)
.
Fix
M\geq 1
and
u,p>0
. Conditioned on
\sigma=\sigma_{\delta/A}
(which implies that
\mathcal{N}
is simply connected and either the top left or the top right of
\mathcal{N}
has zero length), let
E
be the event that the following additional properties hold.

(I)

The length of the top, bottom left, and bottom right of
\mathcal{N}
are all at least
\delta^{2/3}/M
.

(II)

The
d_{h|_{\mathcal{N}}}
-diameter of
\mathcal{N}
is at most
M\delta^{1/3}
.

(III)

Let
\varphi\colon\mathcal{N}\to\mathbb{D}
be the unique conformal transformation which takes the bottom left point to
-1
, the bottom middle point
\eta^{\prime}(0)
to
-i
, and the bottom right point to
1
and consider the embedding of
\mathcal{N}
into
\mathbb{D}
induced by
\varphi
. Then the quantum mass assigned to
B(0,1/2)
is at least
\delta^{4/3}/M
. Also, for each
r\in(0,M^{-1})
, every point with quantum metric distance at least
\delta^{1/3}r
from
\partial\mathbb{D}
(with respect to the field
h|_{\mathcal{N}}\circ\varphi^{-1}+Q\log|(\varphi^{-1})^{\prime}|
) has Euclidean distance at least
r^{M}
from
\partial\mathbb{D}
.

(IV)

For every
\epsilon\in(0,\delta^{2/3}/M)
and points
x,y\in\partial\mathcal{N}
such that both
[x,y]_{\partial\mathcal{N}}^{\bm{\circlearrowright}}
and
[x,y]_{\partial\mathcal{N}}^{\bm{\circlearrowleft}}
have boundary length at least
\epsilon
we have that their
d_{h}
-distance in
\mathcal{N}
is at least
\epsilon^{M}
. If either
[x,y]_{\partial\mathcal{N}}^{\bm{\circlearrowright}}
or
[x,y]_{\partial\mathcal{N}}^{\bm{\circlearrowleft}}
has boundary length at most
\epsilon
then their
d_{h}
-distance in
\mathcal{N}
is at most
\epsilon^{1/M}
.

(V)

For every
\epsilon\in(0,\delta^{1/3}/M)
and
x,y\in\partial\mathcal{N}
, the
\epsilon
-neighborhood (with respect to
d_{h}
in
\mathcal{N}
) of
[x,y]_{\partial\mathcal{N}}^{\bm{\circlearrowleft}}
has quantum mass at least
\epsilon^{2+u}/M
times the length of
[x,y]_{\partial\mathcal{N}}^{\bm{\circlearrowleft}}
. The same is also true with
[x,y]_{\partial\mathcal{N}}^{\bm{\circlearrowright}}
in place of
[x,y]_{\partial\mathcal{N}}^{\bm{\circlearrowleft}}
.

(VI)

For every
\epsilon\in(0,\delta^{1/3}/M)
, the quantum area of the
\epsilon
-neighborhood of
\partial\mathcal{N}
is at most
\epsilon^{p}\delta^{p}
.

Proposition 6.2
.

Let
A_{0}\in[2,\infty)
and
c_{0,\mathrm{max}}\in(0,\infty)
be as in Proposition
5.1
. For each
A\geq A_{0}
and
c_{0}\in(0,c_{0,\mathrm{max}}]
there exist
M_{0}\in[1,\infty)
and
p_{0}>0
, depending only
A
,
c_{0}
and
u
, such that for all
M\geq M_{0}
and
p\in(0,p_{0})
,
\mu_{\mathrm{QW}}^{W=2}\bigl[E^{\mathcal{W}}_{\sigma}\cap\{\mathcal{N}\in E\}\bigm|\sigma<\delta\bigr]\geq 1-c_{0}A^{-2/3}
.

In order to start to prove Proposition
6.2
, we first need to recall the following lower bound regarding the amount of mass near the boundary for a quantum disk
[
46
, Lemma 3.4]
.

Lemma 6.3
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{D}=(\mathbb{D},h,0)
has law
\mu_{\mathrm{QD,W}}^{L=\ell}
. For each
u>0
there a.s. exists
c>0
such that for each
\epsilon\in(0,1)
and
x,y\in\partial\mathbb{D}
the LQG area of the
\epsilon
-neighborhood of
[x,y]_{\partial\mathcal{D}}^{\bm{\circlearrowleft}}
is at least
c\epsilon^{2+u}
times the length of
[x,y]_{\partial\mathcal{D}}^{\bm{\circlearrowleft}}
. The same holds with
[x,y]_{\partial\mathcal{D}}^{\bm{\circlearrowright}}
in place of
[x,y]_{\partial\mathcal{D}}^{\bm{\circlearrowleft}}
.

We also need the following upper bound for distances between points on the boundary of a quantum disk established in
[
46
, Lemma 3.2]
.

Lemma 6.4
.

Fix
\ell>0
and let
\mathcal{D}=(\mathbb{D},h)
have law
\mu_{\mathrm{QD}}^{L=\ell}
or
\mu_{\mathrm{QD,W}}^{L=\ell}
. For each
\zeta>0
there a.s. exists
C>0
so that for all
x,y\in\partial\mathbb{D}
we have that

d_{h}(x,y)\leq C\nu_{h}([x,y]_{\partial\mathcal{D}}^{\bm{\circlearrowleft}})^{1/2}\left(|\log\nu_{h}([x,y]_{\partial\mathcal{D}}^{\bm{\circlearrowleft}})|+1\right)^{7/4+\zeta}

and the same is true with
[x,y]_{\partial\mathcal{D}}^{\bm{\circlearrowright}}
in place of
[x,y]_{\partial\mathcal{D}}^{\bm{\circlearrowleft}}
.
If we let
C
be the smallest constant for which this is satisfied, then for
A>1
we have that
\mathbb{P}[C>A]
decays to
0
as
A\to\infty
faster than any negative power of
A
.

Lemma 6.5
.

There exists a deterministic constant
\widetilde{\beta}>0
such that the following is true. Suppose that
\mathcal{D}=(\mathcal{D},h,0)
has law
\mu_{\mathrm{QD,W}}^{L=1}
. Then, a.s. under
\mu_{\mathrm{QD,W}}^{L=1}
, the quantum boundary length measure
\nu_{h}
is
\widetilde{\beta}
-Hölder continuous with respect to the Euclidean metric.

Proof.

First, we note that if
\widetilde{h}
has the law of a free boundary GFF on
\mathbb{D}
with some fixed normalization, then
[
84
, Proposition 3.7]
implies that for every
p\in(1,p_{*})
, there exists
C_{p}<\infty
such that
\mathbb{E}\big[\nu_{\widetilde{h}}(A)^{p}\big]\leq C_{p}\mathop{\text{diam}}(A)^{p}
for each
A\subseteq\partial\mathcal{D}
Borel, where
\zeta_{p}=(2+\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4})p-\frac{\gamma^{2}p^{2}}{4}
,
p_{*}
is the unique
p_{*}>1
such that
\zeta_{p_{*}}=2
, and
\mathop{\text{diam}}
denotes Euclidean diameter. Hence, by choosing
p\in(1,p_{*})
such that
\zeta_{p}>1
and combining with Kolmogorov’s criterion, we obtain that there exists
\widetilde{\beta}>0
deterministic such that
\nu_{\widetilde{h}}
is a.s.
\widetilde{\beta}
-Hölder continuous with respect to the Euclidean metric.

Now, we recall some results from
[
5
]
. Suppose that
f
is sampled from the group
\text{conf}(\mathbb{H})
of conformal automorphisms of
\mathbb{H}
when the latter is endowed with the Haar measure, and let
h
be sampled from the infinite measure of a weight-
2
quantum disk with
\gamma=\sqrt{8/3}
(see
[
35
, Section 4.5]
) weighted by
\nu_{h}(\partial\mathbb{H})^{-2}
. Then,
[
5
, Theorem 1.2]
implies that there exists a constant
C
such that the law of
h\circ f^{-1}+Q\log|(f^{-1})^{\prime}|
is given by
C
times the law of
\widehat{h}:=\widetilde{h}-2Q\log|\cdot|_{+}+c
, where
\widetilde{h}
is a free boundary GFF on
\mathbb{H}
and
c
is sampled from the infinite measure on
\mathbb{R}
given by
\exp(-Qc)dc
. Consider the conformal transformation
\psi:\mathbb{H}\to\mathbb{D}
given by
\psi(z)=\frac{z-i}{z+i}
. Note that
\widetilde{h}\circ\psi^{-1}
is a free boundary GFF on
\mathbb{D}
with some fixed normalization and
-2Q\log\max\{|\psi^{-1}(\cdot)|,1\}+Q\log|(\psi^{-1})^{\prime}(\cdot)|+c=O(1)
uniformly in
\partial\mathbb{D}
. Hence, combining with Kahane’s convexity inequality, we obtain that the quantum boundary length of
\widehat{h}\circ\psi^{-1}+Q\log|(\psi^{-1})^{\prime}|
is a.e.
\widetilde{\beta}
-Hölder continuous on
\partial\mathbb{D}
with respect to the Euclidean metric. Therefore, the same is a.e. true for
h\circ f^{-1}+Q\log|(f^{-1})^{\prime}|
and since the event that the quantum boundary length is
\widetilde{\beta}
-Hölder continuous with respect to the Euclidean metric is invariant under the coordinate change formula for quantum surfaces, we obtain that a sample from the infinite measure of a weigh-
2
quantum disk satisfies the above property a.e. Thus, combining with disintegration with respect to the total boundary length (see
[
35
, Section 4.5]
), we obtain that if
(\mathbb{D},h)
is sampled from
\mu_{\mathrm{QD}}^{L=1}
, then its boundary length is a.s.
\widetilde{\beta}
-Hölder continuous with respect to the Euclidean metric. Therefore, the same is true for a sample
(\mathbb{D},h,0)
from
\mu_{\mathrm{QD,W}}^{L=1}
by absolute continuity.
∎

Lemma 6.6
.

There exists a constant
\beta>0
so that the following is true. Suppose that
\ell>0
and
\mathcal{D}=(\mathbb{D},h,0)
has law
\mu_{\mathrm{QD,W}}^{L=\ell}
. There is a.s.
\epsilon_{0}>0
so that for all
\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{0})
and
a,b\in\partial\mathcal{D}
with both
\nu_{h}([a,b]_{\partial\mathcal{D}}^{\bm{\circlearrowright}})\geq\epsilon
and
\nu_{h}([a,b]_{\partial\mathcal{D}}^{\bm{\circlearrowleft}})\geq\epsilon
we have that
d_{h}(a,b)\geq\epsilon^{\beta}
.

Proof.

First, we note that a sample from
\mu_{\mathrm{QD,W}}^{L=\ell}
can be obtained by starting with a sample from
\mu_{\mathrm{QD,W}}^{L=1}
and then multiplying lengths with
\ell
, distances with
\ell^{1/2}
and areas by
\ell^{2}
. Thus, it suffices to prove the claim in the case that
\ell=1
. Suppose that
(\mathcal{S},x,y)
has distribution
\mu_{\mathrm{QSPH}}
which we can assume that it is parameterized by the Euclidean sphere
\mathbb{S}^{2}
. Recall that the metric
d_{h}
on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
is Hölder continuous with respect to the Euclidean metric
d
on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
, i.e., there exists
\alpha\in(0,1)
deterministic and
C\geq 1
random such that (
6.3
) holds for each
u,v\in\mathbb{S}^{2}
. Let
\tau
be the smallest
r>0
such that the boundary length of
B_{h,y}^{\bullet}(x,r)
is equal to
1
. Conditional on
\tau<\infty
, the quantum surface
\mathcal{D}
parameterized by
\mathcal{S}\setminus B_{h,y}^{\bullet}(x,r)
and marked by
y
has law
\mu_{\mathrm{QD,W}}^{L=1}
. Now, suppose that we conformally map
\mathcal{D}
to
\mathbb{D}
with
y
sent to
0
, and consider the surface parameterized by
\mathbb{D}
. In particular, we consider the conformal transformation
\phi
mapping
\mathcal{D}
onto
\mathbb{D}
such that
\phi(y)=0
and
\phi^{\prime}(y)>0
, and set
\widetilde{h}:=h\circ\phi^{-1}+Q\log|(\phi^{-1})^{\prime}|
. Note that there a.s. exists
A>0
such that
\text{dist}_{d}(y,\partial\mathcal{D})\geq A
. Fix
p>1
sufficiently large and deterministic (to be chosen) and let
a,b\in\partial\mathbb{D}
such that both
\nu_{\widetilde{h}}([a,b]_{\partial\mathbb{D}}^{\bm{\circlearrowright}})\geq\epsilon
and
\nu_{\widetilde{h}}([a,b]_{\partial\mathbb{D}}^{\bm{\circlearrowleft}})\geq\epsilon
. Then, we have that both
\nu_{h}([\widetilde{a},\widetilde{b}]_{\partial\mathcal{D}}^{\bm{\circlearrowright}})\geq\epsilon
and
\nu_{h}([\widetilde{a},\widetilde{}b]_{\partial\mathcal{D}}^{\bm{\circlearrowleft}})\geq\epsilon
with
\widetilde{a}=\phi^{-1}(a)
and
\widetilde{b}=\phi^{-1}(b)
. Suppose that
d_{h}(\widetilde{a},\widetilde{b})\leq d_{\widetilde{h}}(a,b)\leq\epsilon^{p}
. Then, we have that
d(\widetilde{a},\widetilde{b})\leq C^{\alpha}\epsilon^{\alpha p}<A
for
\epsilon>0
sufficiently small. Moreover, since
d_{\widetilde{h}}
is a.s. equivalent to the Euclidean metric on
\mathbb{D}
, we obtain that it is a.s. the case that
B_{d_{\widetilde{h}}}(z,\epsilon)\subseteq\overline{\mathbb{D}}\cap B_{d}(z,1/2)
for all
z\in\partial\mathbb{D}
and all
\epsilon>0
sufficiently small. In particular, for
\epsilon>0
sufficiently small, we have that the geodesic
\gamma
with respect to
d_{\widetilde{h}}
from
a
to
b
disconnects from
0
either
[a,b]_{\partial\mathbb{D}}^{\bm{\circlearrowright}}
or
[a,b]_{\partial\mathbb{D}}^{\bm{\circlearrowleft}}
, and so
\phi^{-1}(\gamma)
disconnects from
y
either
[\widetilde{a},\widetilde{b}]_{\partial\mathcal{D}}^{\bm{\circlearrowright}}
or
[\widetilde{a},\widetilde{b}]_{\partial\mathcal{D}}^{\bm{\circlearrowleft}}
. Note that
d_{h}(\widetilde{a},z)\leq\epsilon^{p}
for all
z\in\phi^{-1}(\gamma)
, since
\phi^{-1}(\gamma)
is the geodesic in
\mathcal{D}
connecting
\widetilde{a}
to
\widetilde{b}
with respect to the interior-internal metric
d_{h}|_{\mathcal{D}}
. It follows that
B_{d}(\widetilde{a},C^{\alpha}\epsilon^{\alpha p})
disconnects from
y
either
[\widetilde{a},\widetilde{b}]_{\partial\mathcal{D}}^{\bm{\circlearrowright}}
or
[\widetilde{a},\widetilde{b}]_{\partial\mathcal{D}}^{\bm{\circlearrowleft}}
for all
\epsilon>0
sufficiently small. We can assume that the latter holds. Then, the Beurling estimate implies that for all
\epsilon>0
sufficiently small, the probability that a Brownian motion starting from
y
exits
\mathcal{D}
in
[\widetilde{a},\widetilde{b}]_{\partial\mathcal{D}}^{\bm{\circlearrowleft}}
is
\lesssim\epsilon^{\alpha p/2}
, where the implicit constant depends only on
C^{\alpha}
and
A
. This implies that
[\widetilde{a},\widetilde{b}]_{\partial\mathcal{D}}^{\bm{\circlearrowleft}}
gets mapped to an arc in
\partial\mathbb{D}
with Euclidean length at most
O(\epsilon^{\alpha p/2})
. Finally, to complete the proof, we let
\widetilde{\beta}
be the constant of Lemma
6.5
and choose
p>1
such that
\alpha p\widetilde{\beta}/2>1
. Then, the
\mu_{\mathrm{QD,W}}^{L=1}
-a.e.
\widetilde{\beta}
-Hölder continuity of
\nu_{\widetilde{h}}
with respect to
d
shown in Lemma
6.5
implies that the quantum length of
[a,b]_{\partial\mathbb{D}}^{\bm{\circlearrowleft}}
is at most
O(\epsilon^{\alpha p\widetilde{\beta}/2})
for all
\epsilon>0
sufficiently small. But that is a contradiction since
\nu_{\widetilde{h}}([a,b]_{\partial\mathbb{D}}^{\bm{\circlearrowleft}})\geq\epsilon
. Hence,
d_{\widetilde{h}}(a,b)\geq\epsilon^{p}
and this completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 6.7
.

Fix
\ell>0,u\in(0,2)
and let
\mathcal{D}=(\mathcal{D},h,0)
be a sample from
\mu_{\mathrm{QD,W}}^{L=\ell}
. Then,
\mu_{\mathrm{QD,W}}^{L=\ell}
-a.e., there exist random constants
C>0,\delta_{0}\in(0,1)
such that the quantum area of the
\delta
-neighborhood of
\partial\mathbb{D}
with respect to
d_{h}
is at most
C\delta^{2-u}
for all
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
.

Proof.

We assume that we have the setup of the proof of Lemma
6.4
given in
[
46
]
. In particular, we let
p:[0,1]\to\mathcal{D}
be the quotient map introduced in
[
46
, Section 3.1]
. Then, we know from
[
46
, Section 3.1]
that
\partial\mathcal{D}=p(\{T_{r}:r\in[0,\ell]\})
, where
T_{r}=\inf\{t\geq 0:B_{t}=-r\}
and
B
is a standard Brownian motion coupled with
\mathcal{D}
. Fix
\widetilde{u}\in(0,1/2)
sufficiently small (to be chosen). Then, Lemma
6.4
implies that there a.s. exists a constant
C>0
such that
d_{h}(x,y)\leq C\nu_{h}([x,y]_{\partial\mathcal{D}}^{\bm{\circlearrowright}})^{\frac{1}{2}-\widetilde{u}}
for all
x,y\in\partial\mathcal{D}
. Moreover, by possibly taking
C
to be larger, we can assume using
[
46
, Lemma 3.3]
that
\mu_{h}(B_{d_{h}}(z,\delta))\leq C\delta^{4-\widetilde{u}}
for all
z\in\partial\mathcal{D}
,
\delta\in(0,1)
. Let
\delta_{0}\in(0,1/2)
be sufficiently small such that
\widetilde{\delta}\in(0,1/2)
where
\widetilde{\delta}>0
is such that
C\widetilde{\delta}^{1/2-\widetilde{u}}=\delta
. Then, we have that
p(\{T_{r}:r\in[(k-1)\widetilde{\delta},k\widetilde{\delta}]\})\subseteq B_{d_{h}}(T((k-1)\widetilde{\delta}),\delta)
for all
1\leq k\leq\widetilde{\delta}^{-1}

. It follows that the
\delta
-neighborhood of
\partial\mathcal{D}
with respect to
d_{h}
is contained in
\cup_{k=1}^{\widetilde{\delta}^{-1}}B_{d_{h}}(p(T((k-1)\widetilde{\delta})),2\delta)
. Therefore, a union bound implies that the quantum area of the
\delta
-neighborhood of
\partial\mathcal{D}
with respect to
d_{h}
is at most
\lesssim\delta^{4-\widetilde{u}-\frac{2}{1-2\widetilde{u}}}
, and so the proof of the lemma is complete by choosing
\widetilde{u}
such that
4-\widetilde{u}-\frac{2}{1-2\widetilde{u}}>2-u
.
∎

Proposition 6.8
.

Fix
\epsilon\in(0,1)
and let
\mathcal{D}=(\mathbb{D},h)
be sampled from
\mu_{\mathrm{QD,W}}^{L=1}
. Define the event
E(h)
that conditions (
II
)-(
VI
) hold for the quantum surface
\mathcal{D}
instead of
\mathcal{N}
and with
\delta=1
. Then, we have that
\mu_{\mathrm{QD,W}}^{L=1}\big[E(h)\big]\geq 1-\epsilon
provided
M
is large enough and
p
is small enough.

Proof.

Recall that a sample from
\mu_{\mathrm{QD,W}}^{L=1}
can be produced as follows. Let
(\mathcal{S},h,x,y)
be a sample from
\mu_{\mathrm{QSPH}}
and let
\eta^{\prime}
be an independent whole-plane
{\rm SLE}_{6}
in
\mathcal{S}
from
x
to
y
parameterized by quantum natural time. Let also
L
be the process describing the boundary length evolution of the connected component
U_{t}
of
\mathcal{S}\setminus\eta^{\prime}([0,t])
containing
y
. We let
\tau
be the first time
t
that
L_{t}=1
. Then, conditional on
\tau<\infty
, we let
\mathcal{D}
be the surface obtained by conformally mapping
U_{\tau}
onto
\mathcal{D}
using the conformal map
\psi:U_{\tau}\to\mathcal{D}
such that
\psi(y)=0
and
\psi^{\prime}(y)>0
and then applying the coordinate change formula for quantum surfaces. Since
\mathcal{D}
is a metric space of finite diameter a.s., we can arrange so that part (
II
) holds with probability as close to
1
as we want by taking
M
sufficiently large. Moreover, since
\mu_{h}(B(0,1/2))>0

\mu_{\mathrm{QD,W}}^{L=1}
-a.s. and there exists a deterministic constant
\alpha\in(0,1)
such that the metric in
\mathcal{S}
is
\alpha
-Hölder continuous with respect to the Euclidean metric and
\psi
is Hölder continuous with some fixed and deterministic exponent (see
[
85
, Theorem 5.2]
), we obtain that part (
III
) holds with probability as close to
1
as we want by taking
M
sufficiently large. Also, it follows by combining Lemmas
6.4
and
6.6
that part (
IV
) holds with arbitrarily high probability provided we choose
M
sufficiently large. Furthermore, by arguing in the same way but using Lemma
6.3
instead, we obtain that part (
V
) holds with arbitrarily high probability if we choose
M
sufficiently large. Finally, Lemma
6.7
implies that part (
VI
) holds with arbitrarily high probability as well if we choose
M
large enough and
p
small enough. This completes the proof.
∎

Next, we focus on proving Proposition
6.2
. First, we will prove that condition (
IV
) holds with high probability provided
M
is sufficiently large. We begin by proving that the lower bound on quantum distances in condition (
IV
) holds with high probability. Since the proof of the lower bound will be technical, we will give its proof in the next two lemmas. First, we will deal with the case that both of the boundary points
x
and
y
lie on
\mathbb{H}\cap\partial\mathcal{N}
. This is the content of the following lemma.

Figure 5.
Illustration of the proof of Lemma
6.9
.
Top left
: The chunk
\mathcal{N}
.
Top right:
An
{\rm SLE}_{6}
process
\widetilde{\eta}^{\prime}
in
\mathbb{H}
from
0
to
x
coupled to agree with
\eta^{\prime}
up until they first separate
x
from
\infty
, shown on the event that the hull that it separates from
\infty
parameterizes
\mathcal{N}
.
Bottom:
The right (standing from
x
looking towards
0
) boundary of
\widetilde{\eta}^{\prime}
is
\widetilde{\eta}
, which we view as a curve from
x
to
0
.

Lemma 6.9
.

Let
\mathcal{W}=(\mathbb{H},h,0,\infty)
have law
\mu_{\mathrm{QW}}^{W=2}
and let
\eta^{\prime}
be a chordal
{\rm SLE}_{6}
in
\mathbb{H}
from
0
to
\infty
which is independent of
\mathcal{W}
. Fix
\ell>0
and let
x>0
be such that
\nu_{h}([0,x])=\ell
. Let also
\tau
be the first time that
\eta^{\prime}
disconnects
x
from
\infty
. Moreover, let
(K_{t})
denote the hulls of
\eta^{\prime}
and let
\mathcal{N}
be the quantum surface parameterized by the interior of
K_{\tau}
. Then, there a.s. exist
\epsilon_{0}\in(0,1)
and
M\in(1,\infty)
such that for all
\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{0})
the following holds. Let
a,b\in\mathbb{H}\cap\partial\mathcal{N}
be such that
\nu_{h}([a,b]_{\partial\mathcal{N}}^{\bm{\circlearrowright}})\geq\epsilon
and
\nu_{h}([a,b]_{\partial\mathcal{N}}^{\bm{\circlearrowleft}})\geq\epsilon
. Then, we have that
d_{h|_{\mathcal{N}}}(a,b)\geq\epsilon^{M}
, where
d_{h|_{\mathcal{N}}}
denotes the interior-internal metric on
\mathcal{N}
with respect to
h
.

Proof.

Step 1. Overview and setup.
First we note that the locality property of
{\rm SLE}_{6}
implies that we can couple
\eta^{\prime}
with an
{\rm SLE}_{6}

\widetilde{\eta}^{\prime}
in
\mathbb{H}
from
0
to
x
such that
\eta^{\prime}
and
\widetilde{\eta}^{\prime}
agree up until the first time that they disconnect
x
from
\infty
. From now on, we assume that we are working with this coupling. Let
\widetilde{\eta}
be the left outer boundary of
\widetilde{\eta}^{\prime}
and for
\rho\geq 0
, we let
\widetilde{\tau}_{\rho}
be the first time
t
that
\text{dist}(\widetilde{\eta}(t),\mathbb{R}_{-})\leq\rho
. Note that
\widetilde{\tau}_{\rho}<\infty
a.s. for all
\rho\geq 0
. Let also
\widetilde{D}_{\rho}
be the unbounded connected component of
\mathbb{H}\setminus\widetilde{\eta}([0,\widetilde{\tau}_{\rho}])
. Moreover, we fix
0<\rho_{2}<\rho_{1}<x
and let
\widetilde{I}
be the counterclockwise segment traced by
\widetilde{\eta}
between times
\widetilde{\sigma}
and
\widetilde{\tau}_{\rho_{1}}
, where
\widetilde{\sigma}
is the last time before time
\widetilde{\tau}_{\rho_{1}}
that
\widetilde{\eta}
intersects
[x,\infty)
(when viewed as a set of prime ends on the left side of
\widetilde{\eta}
). We also set
\widetilde{J}=\widetilde{\eta}([\widetilde{\sigma},\widetilde{\tau}_{\rho_{2}}])
. The proof of the lemma consists of three steps. In Step 2, we will prove that the lower bound on quantum distances in condition (
IV
) holds for a quantum wedge
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}
parameterized by
\mathbb{H}
of weight
\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2}=\frac{4}{3}
for points on compact intervals on
\mathbb{R}
which are bounded away from
0
. Since the law of the field
h
restricted to a small neighborhood of
\widetilde{I}
in
\mathcal{N}
is absolutely continuous with a quantum wedge of weight
\frac{4}{3}
, we will deduce in Step 3 the claim of the lemma for
a,b\in\widetilde{I}\cap\partial\mathcal{N}
and the field
h|_{\mathbb{H}\setminus\widetilde{J}}
in place of
h|_{\mathcal{N}}
. Finally, we will complete the proof in Step 4 using the time-reversal invariance of the law of
\widetilde{\eta}
.

Step 2. Proof of the claim for a quantum wedge of weight
\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2}=\frac{4}{3}
.
Let
\widetilde{h}
be a free boundary GFF on
\mathbb{H}
with the additive constant taken so that its average on
\mathbb{H}\cap\partial\mathbb{D}
is equal to
0
. Then Proposition
6.8
combined with the argument in Lemma
6.5
imply that for every fixed
-\infty<a<b<\infty
, there a.s. exist
\epsilon_{0}\in(0,1),M\in(1,\infty)
such that the following is true. For all
\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{0})
and all
z,w\in[a,b]
such that
\nu_{\widetilde{h}}([z,w])\geq\epsilon
, we have that
d_{\widetilde{h}}(z,w)\geq\epsilon^{M}
, where
d_{\widetilde{h}}
denotes the interior-internal metric on
\mathbb{H}
with respect to
\widetilde{h}
.

Fix
0<a<b
. Then the above implies that there a.s. exists
\epsilon_{0}\in(0,1)
such that for all
\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{0})
and all
a\leq z<w\leq b
such that
\nu_{\widetilde{h}}([z,w])\geq\epsilon
, we have that
d_{\widetilde{h}|_{A_{r,R}}}(z,w)\geq\epsilon^{M}
, for all
0<r<a<b<R
, where
A_{r,R}:=B(0,R)\setminus\overline{B(0,r)}
. Note that if we fix such
r,R
, the above event is determined by
\widetilde{h}|_{A_{r,R}}
. Suppose that
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}=(\mathbb{H},\widehat{h},0,\infty)
has the law of a weight
\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2}=\frac{4}{3}
quantum wedge with the circle average embedding. Then, for fixed
0<r<a<b<R
, the laws of
\widehat{h}|_{A_{r,R}}
and
\widetilde{h}|_{A_{r,R}}
are mutually absolutely continuous and so it is a.s. the case that there exist
\epsilon_{0}\in(0,1),M\in(1,\infty)
such that for all
\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{0})
, if
z,w\in[a,b]
are such that
\nu_{\widehat{h}}([z,w])\geq\epsilon
, we have that
d_{\widehat{h}|_{A_{r,R}}}(z,w)\geq\epsilon^{M}
. Note that
d_{\widehat{h}}
a.s. induces the Euclidean topology (see
[
54
, Theorem 1.3]
). Moreover, the function
t\to\nu_{\widehat{h}}([0,t])
is a homeomorphism on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
with respect to the Euclidean topology (and hence with respect to the topology induced by
d_{\widehat{h}}
on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
), which implies that it is a.s. the case that there exists
\delta_{0}\in(0,1)
such that
d_{\widehat{h}}(z,w)\geq\delta_{0}
for all
z,w\in[a,b]
such that
\nu_{\widehat{h}}([z,w])\geq\epsilon_{0}
. Also, since
\text{dist}_{d_{\widehat{h}}}([a,b],\partial A_{r,R})>0
a.s., possibly by taking
\epsilon_{0}
to be smaller, we can assume that
d_{\widehat{h}}(z,w)=d_{\widehat{h}|_{A_{r,R}}}(z,w)
for all
z,w\in[a,b]
such that
\nu_{\widehat{h}}([z,w])\leq\epsilon_{0}
. Combining, we obtain that possibly by taking
M
to larger, we have that
d_{\widehat{h}}(z,w)\geq\epsilon^{M}
for all
a\leq z<w\leq b
such that
\nu_{\widehat{h}}([z,w])\geq\epsilon
, and all
\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{0})
. The same holds for any
-\infty<a<b<0
by symmetry.

Step 3. Proof of the claim for the field
h|_{\mathbb{H}\setminus\widetilde{J}}
.
Next, we note that It follows from
[
79
, Section 2]
that
\widetilde{\eta}
has the law of an
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}(\frac{\kappa}{2}-2;\kappa-4)
process in
\mathbb{H}
from
x
to
0
, where
\kappa=\frac{8}{3}
and the force points are located at
x^{-}
and
x^{+}
respectively. Let also
\eta
be an
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}(\frac{\kappa}{2}-2;\kappa-4)
process in
\mathbb{H}
from
x
to
\infty
with the force points located at
x^{-}
and
x^{+}
respectively, which is independent of
\mathcal{W}
. Then, combining
[
88
, Proposition 1.7]
with
[
35
, Theorem 1.2]
, we obtain the following description of the quantum surface parameterized by the region to the left of
\eta
. Let
\phi
be the conformal transformation mapping the connected component of
\mathbb{H}\setminus\eta
lying to the left of
\eta
onto
\mathbb{H}
such that
\phi(x)=0,\phi(\infty)=\infty
and
\phi(0)=-1
. Then, if we parameterize the field
\check{h}:=h\circ\phi^{-1}+Q\log|(\phi^{-1})^{\prime}|
by the circle average embedding, it has the same law with the field obtained when we parameterize
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}
by the circle average embedding. For
\rho\geq 0
, we let
\tau_{\rho}
be the first time
t
that
\text{dist}(\eta(t),\mathbb{R}_{-})\leq\rho
. Suppose that we are working on the event that
\tau_{\rho_{2}}<\infty
, where
0<\rho_{2}<\rho_{1}<x
are small but fixed. Let
I
be the counterclockwise segment traced by
\eta
between the last time before
\tau_{\rho_{1}}
that it intersects
[x,\infty)
and time
\tau_{\rho_{1}}
(seen as set of prime ends on the left side of
\eta
). Then, if we apply the results of Step 2 for
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}
on the time-interval
\phi(I)
, we obtain that there a.s. exist
\epsilon_{0}\in(0,1),M\in(1,\infty)
such that for all
\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{0})
, the following is true. Let
a,b\in I
be such that
\eta
hits
a
before it hits
b
and
\nu_{h}([a,b])\geq\epsilon
. Then, it holds that
d_{\check{h}}(\phi(a),\phi(b))\geq\epsilon^{M}
. Suppose that
\epsilon_{0}\in(0,1)
is chosen such that
\epsilon_{0}<\text{dist}_{d_{\check{h}}}(\phi(I),\phi(\eta([\tau_{\rho_{2}},\infty))))
. We claim that if we further assume that
\nu_{h}([a,b])\leq\epsilon_{0}
, we have that
d_{h|_{D_{\rho_{2}}}}(a,b)=d_{h|_{\phi^{-1}(\mathbb{H})}}(a,b)
, where
D_{\rho}
denotes the unbounded connected component of
\mathbb{H}\setminus\eta([0,\tau_{\rho}])
. Indeed, clearly we have that
d_{h|_{D_{\rho_{2}}}}(a,b)\leq d_{h|_{\phi^{-1}(\mathbb{H})}}(a,b)
. Let
\gamma
be a
d_{h|_{D_{\rho_{2}}}}
-geodesic path in
D_{\rho_{2}}
from
a
to
b
. Suppose that
\gamma
intersects
\eta((\tau_{\rho_{2}},\infty))
, and let
t
be the last time that
\gamma
hits
\eta((\tau_{\rho_{2}},\infty))
, when
\gamma
is parameterized by
[0,1]
and
\gamma(0)=a,\gamma(1)=b
. Then, we have that the
d_{h}
-length of
\gamma|_{[t,1]}
is at least
\text{dist}_{d_{\check{h}}}(\phi(I),\phi(\eta([\tau_{\rho_{2}},\infty))))\geq\epsilon_{0}
and so
d_{h|_{D_{\rho_{2}}}}(a,b)\geq\text{dist}_{d_{\check{h}}}(\phi(I),\phi(\eta([\tau_{\rho_{2}},\infty))))\geq\epsilon_{0}
, but that is a contradiction. Thus, it follows that
d_{h|_{D_{\rho_{2}}}}(a,b)=d_{h|_{\phi^{-1}(\mathbb{H})}}(a,b)
. Combining everything, we obtain that the following is true a.s. on the event that
\tau_{\rho_{2}}<\infty
. There exists
\epsilon_{0}\in(0,1),M\in(1,\infty)
such that for all
\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{0})
, the following holds. Let
a,b\in I
be such that
\eta
hits
a
before
b
and
\epsilon\leq\nu_{h}([a,b]_{\partial\mathcal{N}}^{\bm{\circlearrowleft}})\leq\epsilon_{0}
. Then
d_{h|_{D_{\rho_{2}}}}(a,b)\geq\epsilon^{M}
. Also, it follows from
[
86
, Theorem 6]
that the law of
\widetilde{\eta}|_{[0,\widetilde{\tau}_{\rho_{2}}]}
is absolutely continuous with respect to the law of
\eta|_{[0,\tau_{\rho_{2}}]}
when the latter is restricted to the event that
\tau_{\rho_{2}}<\infty
. It follows that there a.s. exist
\epsilon_{0}\in(0,1),M\in(1,\infty)
such that for all
\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{0})
, the following is true. Let
a,b\in\widetilde{I}
be such that
a
is hit before
b
by
\widetilde{\eta}
and
\epsilon\leq\nu_{h}([a,b])\leq\epsilon_{0}
. Then, it holds that
d_{h|_{\widetilde{D}_{\rho_{2}}}}(a,b)\geq\epsilon^{M}
. We claim that
d_{h|_{\mathbb{H}\setminus\widetilde{J}}}(a,b)\geq\epsilon^{M}
for such points as well, possibly by taking
\epsilon_{0}
to be smaller. Indeed, let
\gamma
be a path in
\mathbb{H}\setminus\widetilde{J}
connecting
a
to
b
with
\gamma:(0,1)\to\mathbb{H}\setminus\widetilde{J}
and
\gamma(0)=a,\gamma(1)=b
. If
\gamma
is contained in
\widetilde{D}_{\rho_{2}}
, then clearly the
d_{h}
-length of
\gamma
is at least
\epsilon^{M}
since
d_{h|_{\widetilde{D}_{\rho_{2}}}}(a,b)\geq\epsilon^{M}
. Suppose that
\gamma
intersects
\partial\widetilde{D}_{\rho_{2}}\setminus\partial(\mathbb{H}\setminus\widetilde{J})
. Then we have that
\gamma
intersects
\widetilde{\eta}([0,\widetilde{\sigma}])
and let
t
be the last time that this occurs. Then we have that
\gamma|_{(t,1)}
is a path in
\widetilde{D}_{\rho_{2}}
connecting
\gamma(t)
to
b
and the boundary length of the counterclockwise arc of
\partial\widetilde{D}_{\rho_{2}}
from
\gamma(t)
to
b
is at least
\epsilon
. Hence, the
d_{h}
-length of
\gamma|_{(t,1)}
is at least
\epsilon^{M}
and so the
d_{h}
-length of
\gamma
is at least
\epsilon^{M}
in any case. It follows that
d_{h|_{\mathbb{H}\setminus\widetilde{J}}}(a,b)\geq\epsilon^{M}
.

Step 4. Conclusion of the proof.
Now, let
\widehat{\eta}
be the time-reversal of
\widetilde{\eta}
and note that
\widehat{\eta}
has the law of an
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}(\kappa-4;\frac{\kappa}{2}-2)
process in
\mathbb{H}
from
0
to
x
, where the force points are located at
0^{-}
and
0^{+}
respectively
[
71
]
. Similarly, for
\rho\in[0,1)
, we set
\widehat{\tau}_{\rho}=\inf\{t\geq 0:\text{dist}(\widehat{\eta}(t),[x,\infty))<\rho\}
and let
\widehat{\sigma}
be the last time before time
\widehat{\tau}_{\rho_{1}}
that
\widehat{\eta}
hits
\mathbb{R}_{-}
. Set also
\widehat{J}=\widehat{\eta}([\widehat{\sigma},\widehat{\tau}_{\rho_{2}}])
. Similarly with Step 3, possibly by taking
\epsilon_{0}\in(0,1)
to be smaller and
M\in(1,\infty)
to be larger, we have that the following holds for all
\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{0})
. Let
a,b
be points on
\widehat{J}
viewed as prime ends on the right side of
\widehat{\eta}
such that
\widehat{\eta}
hits
a
before it hits
b
and
\epsilon\leq\nu_{h}([a,b])\leq\epsilon_{0}
. Then we have that
d_{h|_{\mathbb{H}\setminus\widehat{J}}}(a,b)\geq\epsilon^{M}
. Suppose that these two events hold. Note that we can choose
0<\rho_{2}<\rho_{1}<x
sufficiently small such that
\widetilde{\eta}([\widetilde{\tau}_{\rho_{1}},\infty))\cap\widehat{\eta}([\widehat{\tau}_{\rho_{1}},\infty))=\emptyset
with as high probability as we want. Suppose that this occurs as well. Note also that in that case, we have that
\widetilde{J}\cup\widehat{J}=\mathbb{H}\cap\partial\mathcal{N}
. Moreover, possibly by taking
\epsilon_{0}\in(0,1)
to be smaller, we can assume that the boundary length distance in
\partial\mathcal{N}
between
\widetilde{\eta}([\widetilde{\tau}_{\rho_{1}},\infty))\cap\partial\mathcal{N}
and
\widehat{\eta}([\widehat{\tau}_{\rho_{1}},\infty))\cap\partial\mathcal{N}
is at least
\epsilon_{0}
. Fix
a,b\in\mathbb{H}\cap\partial\mathcal{N}
such that
\epsilon\leq\min\{\nu_{h}([a,b]_{\partial\mathcal{N}}^{\bm{\circlearrowright}}),\nu_{h}([a,b]_{\partial\mathcal{N}}^{\bm{\circlearrowleft}})\}\leq\epsilon_{0}
. Without loss of generality, we can assume that
[a,b]_{\partial\mathcal{N}}^{\bm{\circlearrowleft}}\subseteq\mathbb{H}
. Suppose that
a\in\widetilde{\eta}([0,\widetilde{\tau}_{\rho_{1}}])
. If
b\in\widetilde{\eta}([0,\widetilde{\tau}_{\rho_{1}}])
, then we have that
d_{h|_{\mathcal{N}}}(a,b)\geq d_{h|_{\mathbb{H}\setminus\widetilde{J}}}(a,b)\geq\epsilon^{M}
. If
b\notin\widetilde{\eta}([0,\widetilde{\tau}_{\rho_{1}}])
, then we must have that
b\in\widehat{\eta}([0,\widehat{\tau}_{\rho_{1}}])
. Suppose that
a\notin\widehat{\eta}([0,\widehat{\tau}_{\rho_{1}}])
. If
b\in\widehat{\eta}([0,\widehat{\tau}_{\rho_{1}}])
, then clearly we have that
d_{h|_{\mathcal{N}}}(a,b)\geq d_{h|_{\mathbb{H}\setminus\widehat{J}}}(a,b)\geq\epsilon^{M}
. If
b\notin\widehat{\eta}([0,\widehat{\tau}_{\rho_{1}}])
, then we must have that the boundary length distance in
\partial\mathcal{N}
between
a
and
b
is at least
\epsilon_{0}
, and that is a contradiction. Therefore, in any case, we have that
d_{h|_{\mathcal{N}}}(a,b)\geq\epsilon^{M}
. Moreover, following similar arguments as the ones given in the previous paragraphs, we obtain that there a.s. exists
\delta_{0}>0
such that
d_{h|_{\mathcal{N}}}(a,b)\geq\delta_{0}
for all
a,b\in\partial\mathcal{N}
such that
[a,b]_{\partial\mathcal{N}}^{\bm{\circlearrowleft}}\subseteq\partial\mathcal{N}
and
\nu_{h}([a,b]_{\partial\mathcal{N}}^{\bm{\circlearrowleft}})\geq\epsilon_{0}
. Combining everything, we obtain that there a.s. exist
\epsilon_{0}\in(0,1),M\in(1,\infty)
such that for all
\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{0})
, the following holds. Let
a,b\in\mathbb{H}\cap\partial\mathcal{N}
be such that
\nu_{h}([a,b]_{\partial\mathcal{N}}^{\bm{\circlearrowright}})\geq\epsilon
and
\nu_{h}([a,b]_{\partial\mathcal{N}}^{\bm{\circlearrowleft}})\geq\epsilon
. Then we have that
d_{h|_{\mathcal{N}}}(a,b)\geq\epsilon^{M}
. This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Now we are ready to prove that condition (
IV
) holds with high probability in the context of Proposition
6.2
.

Lemma 6.10
.

Suppose that we have the setup of Lemma
6.9
. Then there a.s. exist
\epsilon_{0}\in(0,1)
and
M\in(1,\infty)
such that for all
\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{0})
the following hold. Let
a,b\in\partial\mathcal{N}
be such that
\nu_{h}([a,b]_{\partial\mathcal{N}}^{\bm{\circlearrowright}})\geq\epsilon
and
\nu_{h}([a,b]_{\partial\mathcal{N}}^{\bm{\circlearrowleft}})\geq\epsilon
. Then, we have that
d_{h|_{\mathcal{N}}}(a,b)\geq\epsilon^{M}
. Also, we have that if
z,w\in\partial\mathcal{N}
are such that either
\nu_{h}([z,w]_{\partial\mathcal{N}}^{\bm{\circlearrowright}})\leq\epsilon
or
\nu_{h}([z,w]_{\partial\mathcal{N}}^{\bm{\circlearrowleft}})\leq\epsilon
, then
d_{h|_{\mathcal{N}}}(z,w)\leq\epsilon^{1/M}
.

Proof.

We will only prove the first claim of the lemma (lower bound) since the second claim (upper bound) follows from similar arguments. Lemma
6.9
implies that it suffices to prove the claim in the case that either both
a
and
b
lie on
\mathbb{R}\cap\partial\mathcal{N}
or one of the points lies on
\mathbb{R}\cap\partial\mathcal{N}
and the other one lies on
\mathbb{H}\cap\partial\mathcal{N}
. We will first prove the claim in the case that
\{a,b\}\subseteq\mathbb{R}\cap\partial\mathcal{N}
. Recall the decomposition
\mu_{\mathrm{QD,W}}^{L=1}[\,\cdot\,]=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mu_{\mathrm{QD,W}}^{A=\alpha,L=1}[\,\cdot\,]\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi\alpha^{3}}}e^{-1/(2\alpha)}d\alpha
. Also, Proposition
6.8
implies that the following is true for
\mu_{\mathrm{QD,W}}^{L=1}
-a.e. instance
(\mathbb{H},\check{h})
. There exist
\epsilon_{0}\in(0,1),M\in(1,\infty)
such that the following holds for all
\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{0})
. Let
a<b\in\mathbb{R}
be such that
\nu_{\check{h}}([a,b])\geq\epsilon
and
\nu_{\check{h}}(\mathbb{R}\setminus[a,b])\geq\epsilon
. Then we have that
d_{\check{h}}(a,b)\geq\epsilon^{M}
. Thus, combining with the above disintegration, we obtain that the same is a.e. true if we replace
\mu_{\mathrm{QD,W}}^{L=1}
by
\mu_{\mathrm{QD,W}}^{A=\alpha,L=1}
for all
\alpha>0
. In particular, it holds when
\alpha=1
. Let
(\mathbb{H},\check{h})
be a sample from
\mu_{\mathrm{QD,W}}^{A=1,L=1}
and let
y\in\partial\mathbb{H}
be sampled uniformly according to
\nu_{\check{h}}
. Fix
p\in(0,1)
. Then Lemma
4.2
implies that there exists
\widetilde{a}>0
depending only on
p
and a coupling between
(\mathbb{H},\check{h})
and
(\mathbb{H},h)
such that with probability at least
1-\frac{p}{2}
, we have that the metric spaces
B_{\check{h}}(0,\widetilde{a})
and
B_{h}(0,\widetilde{a})
agree in the sense of Lemma
4.2
. Thus, combining with the scale invariance of the law of
\mathcal{W}
(see
[
35
, Proposition 4.7]
), we obtain that for
R>0
fixed, there exist
\epsilon_{0}\in(0,1),M\in(1,\infty)
depending only on
p
and
R
, such that the following holds with probability at least
1-\frac{p}{2}
. For all
\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{0})
, we have that if
z<w\in\mathbb{R}
are such that
[z,w]\subseteq B_{h}(0,R)
and
\nu_{h}([z,w])\geq\epsilon
, we have that
d_{h}(z,w)\geq\epsilon^{M}
. Also, we can choose
R>0
sufficiently large such that
K_{\tau}\subseteq B_{h}(0,R)
with probability at least
1-\frac{p}{2}
. Combining, we obtain that there exist
\epsilon_{0}\in(0,1),M\in(1,\infty)
depending only on
p
, such that the following holds with probability at least
1-p
. For all
\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{0})
and all
z<w\in\mathbb{R}\cap K_{\tau}
such that
\nu_{h}([z,w])\geq\epsilon
, we have that
d_{h|_{\mathcal{N}}}(z,w)\geq d_{h}(z,w)\geq\epsilon^{M}
. This completes the proof of the lower bound in the case that both points lie on
\mathbb{R}\cap\partial\mathcal{N}
since
p\in(0,1)
was arbitrary.

Now, we prove the lower bound in the case that one of the points lies on
\mathbb{R}\cap\partial\mathcal{N}
and the other one lies on
\mathbb{H}\cap\partial\mathcal{N}
. Let
U
be the connected component of
\mathbb{H}\setminus\eta^{\prime}([0,\tau])
whose boundary contains
x
. Then combining
[
35
, Theorem 1.18]
with
[
4
, Corollary 4.3]
, we obtain that the quantum surface
(U,h|_{U})
can be sampled as follows. Let
\mathcal{D}_{1},\mathcal{D}
be two independent samples from the infinite measures on quantum disks of weights
W=\gamma^{2}-2=\frac{2}{3}
and
\gamma^{2}-W=2
respectively, conditioned on the event that
\ell_{1}<\ell<\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}
, where
\ell_{1},\ell_{2}
are the right boundary lengths of
\mathcal{D}_{1}
and
\mathcal{D}
respectively. Note that both of
\mathcal{D}_{1}
and
\mathcal{D}
are equipped with two marked points. Then the quantum surface
(U,h|_{U})
equipped with the first and last boundary point hit by
\eta
has the same law as the marginal of
\mathcal{D}
under the above conditional law. It follows by combining with Proposition
6.8
that it is a.s. the case that possibly by taking
\epsilon_{0}\in(0,1)
to be smaller and
M\in(1,\infty)
to be larger, we have that for all
\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{0})
, if
z,w\in\partial U
are such that
\min\{\nu_{h}([z,w]_{\partial U}^{\bm{\circlearrowleft}}),\nu_{h}([z,w]_{\partial U}^{\bm{\circlearrowright}})\}\geq\epsilon
, then
d_{h|_{U}}(z,w)\geq\epsilon^{M}
. Moreover, Lemma
6.4
combined with absolute continuity imply that there a.s. exists
C<\infty
such that
d_{h|_{U}}(z,w)\leq C(\min\{\nu_{h}([z,w]_{\partial U}^{\bm{\circlearrowleft}}),\nu_{h}([z,w]_{\partial U}^{\bm{\circlearrowright}})\})^{1//3}
for all
z,w\in\partial U
. Furthermore, we can assume that
\text{dist}_{d_{h|_{\mathcal{N}}}}([0,c],\mathbb{H}\cap\partial\mathcal{N})\geq\epsilon_{0}
, where
c=\frac{a+b}{2}
and
a
(resp.
b
) is the first (resp. last) point of
\partial U
visited by
\eta^{\prime}
, since
\text{dist}_{d_{h|_{\mathcal{N}}}}([0,c],\mathbb{H}\cap\partial\mathcal{N})>0
a.s. Now, fix
\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{0})
and let
z\in[c,b],w\in\mathbb{H}\cap\partial\mathcal{N}
be such that
\nu_{h}([z,w]_{\partial\mathcal{N}}^{\bm{\circlearrowleft}})\geq\epsilon
and
\nu_{h}([z,w]_{\partial\mathcal{N}}^{\bm{\circlearrowright}})\geq\epsilon
. Suppose that
\nu_{h}([z,b])\leq\epsilon^{M^{2}}
. Then, we have that
d_{h|_{\mathcal{N}}}(z,b)\leq d_{h|_{U}}(z,b)\leq C\epsilon^{M^{2}/3}
and so combining with triangle inequality and Lemma
6.9
, we obtain that
d_{h|_{\mathcal{N}}}(z,w)\geq\epsilon^{M}/2
possibly by taking
\epsilon_{0}>0
to be smaller. Suppose that
\nu_{h}([z,b])>\epsilon^{M^{2}}
. Then, we have that
\text{dist}_{d_{h|_{U}}}(z,\mathbb{H}\cap\partial U)\geq\epsilon^{M^{3}}
and note that any path
P
in
\mathcal{N}
connecting
w
to
z
has to intersect
\partial U
. Suppose that
P
is parameterized by
[0,1]
and
P(0)=w,P(1)=z
. Let
t
be the last time that
P
hits
\mathbb{H}\cap\partial U
. Then
P|_{(t,1)}
is a path in
U
connecting some point on
\mathbb{H}\cap\partial U
to
z
and so the
d_{h}
-length of
P|_{(t,1)}
is at least
\epsilon^{M^{3}}
. It follows that
d_{h|_{\mathcal{N}}}(z,w)\geq\epsilon^{M^{3}}
since
P
was arbitrary. Combining, we obtain that there a.s. exist
\epsilon_{0}\in(0,1),M\in(1,\infty)
such that the following holds for all
\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{0})
. Let
z\in[0,b],w\in\mathbb{H}\cap\partial\mathcal{N}
be such that
\min\{\nu_{h}([z,w]_{\partial\mathcal{N}}^{\bm{\circlearrowleft}}),\nu_{h}([z,w]_{\partial\mathcal{N}}^{\bm{\circlearrowright}})\}\geq\epsilon
. Then we have that
d_{h|_{\mathcal{N}}}(z,w)\geq\epsilon^{M}
.

Note that since the law of the outer boundary of
\eta^{\prime}
when targeted at
x
has time-reversal symmetry and the law of
\mathcal{W}
is invariant under translating horizontally by a fixed number of quantum length units (see
[
88
, Proposition 1.7]
), we obtain that the law of the quantum surface parameterized by
\mathcal{N}
is the same with the law of the quantum surface parameterized by
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}
, where
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}
is defined as follows. We let
y\in\mathbb{R}_{-}
be such that
\nu_{h}([y,0])=\ell
and draw
\eta^{\prime}
up until the first time that it disconnects
y
from
\infty
. Then
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}
is the quantum surface induced by the restriction of
h
to the hull of the above curve stopped at the above time. Then, arguing as in the previous paragraph, we obtain that there a.s. exist
\epsilon_{0}\in(0,1),M\in(1,\infty)
such that the following is true for all
\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{0})
. Let
z,w\in\partial\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}
be such that
z\in\mathbb{H}\cap\partial\widetilde{\mathcal{N}},w\in\mathbb{R}_{-}\cap\partial\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}
and

\min\{\nu_{h}([z,w]_{\partial\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}}^{\bm{\circlearrowleft}}),\nu_{h}([z,w]_{\partial\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}}^{\bm{\circlearrowright}})\}\geq\epsilon
. Then it holds that
d_{h|_{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}}}(z,w)\geq\epsilon^{M}
. Therefore, combining with the result of the previous paragraph, we complete the proof of the lower bound in the lemma statement in the case that one of the points lies on
\mathbb{H}\cap\partial\mathcal{N}
and the other on
\mathbb{R}\cap\partial\mathcal{N}
. This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Next, we state that conditions (
V
) and (
VI
) hold with high probability in the context of Proposition
6.2
provided
M
is large enough and
p
is small enough. We will not give a detailed proof since it follows from a combination of Proposition
6.8
with the arguments presented in the proofs of Lemmas
6.9
and
6.10
.

Lemma 6.11
.

Fix
u>0
and let
p>0
be a constant (corresponding to condition (
VI
)) which is small enough as specified in Proposition
6.8
. Suppose that we have the setup of Lemmas
6.9
and
6.10
. Then there a.s. exist
\epsilon_{0}\in(0,1)
and
M\in(1,\infty)
such that for all
\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{0})
the following hold. For all
x,y\in\partial\mathcal{N}
, the
\epsilon
-neighborhood of
[x,y]_{\partial\mathcal{N}}^{\bm{\circlearrowleft}}
with respect to
d_{h|_{\mathcal{N}}}
has quantum area at least
\frac{\epsilon^{2+u}}{M}
. The same is also true with
[x,y]_{\partial\mathcal{N}}^{\bm{\circlearrowright}}
in place of
[x,y]_{\partial\mathcal{N}}^{\bm{\circlearrowleft}}
. Moreover, the quantum area of the
\epsilon
-neighborhood of
\partial\mathcal{N}
with respect to
d_{h|_{\mathcal{N}}}
is at most
\epsilon^{p}
.

Proof.

The two claims of the lemma essentially follow from the same arguments presented in Lemmas
6.9
and
6.10
combined with Proposition
6.8
.
∎

Now we prove that conditions (
II
) and (
III
) hold with high probability if we choose
M
sufficiently large. We first prove in the following lemma that condition (
II
) holds with high probability provided we choose
M
sufficiently large.

Lemma 6.12
.

Suppose that we have the setup of Lemmas
6.9
-
6.11
. Then we have that the
d_{h|_{\mathcal{N}}}
-diameter of
\mathcal{N}
is finite a.s.

Proof.

Suppose that we have the setup of the proof of Lemma
6.9
. Fix
p_{1}\in(0,1)
and let
\rho_{1}>0
be chosen such that with probability at least
1-\frac{p_{1}}{2}
, we have that
\widehat{\eta}([0,\widehat{\tau}_{\rho_{1}}])\cap\partial U\neq\emptyset
, where both
\widehat{\eta},\widehat{\tau}_{\rho}
are defined in Step 4 in the proof of Lemma
6.9
and
U
is the connected component of
\mathbb{H}\setminus\eta^{\prime}([0,\tau])
whose boundary contains
x
. Let also
\check{h}
be the random field introduced in Step 3 in the proof of Lemma
6.9
. Since the
d_{\check{h}}
-diameter of any compact set
K\subseteq\overline{\mathbb{H}}
is finite a.s. (see
[
54
, Theorem 1.3]
), arguing as in Step 3 in the proof of Lemma
6.9
gives that the
d_{h|_{\mathbb{H}\setminus\widetilde{\eta}([\widetilde{\sigma},\widetilde{\tau}_{\rho_{1}}])}}
-diameter of
\mathcal{N}
is finite a.s., where
\widetilde{\eta},\widetilde{\tau}_{\rho}
and
\widetilde{J}
are defined in Step 1 in the proof of Lemma
6.9
. Also, since the quantum surface parameterized by
U
has the law of a quantum disk conditioned on a positive probability event (see the proof of Lemma
6.10
), we obtain that the
d_{h|_{U}}
-diameter of
U
is finite a.s. Hence, there exists
M>1
sufficiently large such that with probability at least
1-\frac{p_{1}}{2}
, both of the
d_{h|_{\mathbb{H}\setminus\widehat{\eta}([\widehat{\sigma},\widehat{\tau}_{\rho_{1}}])}}
-diameter of
\mathcal{N}
and the
d_{h|_{U}}
-diameter of
U
are at most
M
. Suppose that we are working on the event that the above holds and that
\widehat{\eta}([0,\widehat{\tau}_{\rho_{1}}])\cap\partial U\neq\emptyset
. Similarly, we can assume in addition that the
d_{h|_{\mathbb{H}\setminus\widetilde{\eta}([\widetilde{\sigma},\widetilde{\tau}_{\rho_{1}}])}}
-diameter of
\mathcal{N}
is at most
M
and that
\widetilde{\eta}([\widetilde{\tau}_{\rho_{1}},\infty))\cap\widehat{\eta}([\widehat{\tau}_{\rho_{1}},\infty))=\emptyset
. Let
t
be the first time that
\widehat{\eta}
intersects
\partial U
. Fix
z\in\partial\mathcal{N}\cap\widehat{\eta}([0,t))
and let
P:(0,1)\to\mathbb{H}\setminus\widehat{\eta}([\widehat{\sigma},\widehat{\tau}_{\rho_{1}}])
be a path such that
P(0)=0,P(1)=z
and such that the
d_{h}
-length of
P
is at most
M
. If
P
doesn’t intersect
\overline{U}
, then it stays in
\mathcal{N}
and so we have that
d_{h|_{\mathcal{N}}}(0,z)\leq M
. Suppose that
P\cap\overline{U}\neq\emptyset
, and let
s_{1}
be the last time that
P
intersects
\partial U
. Then we have that
P|_{(s_{1},1)}
is a path in
\mathcal{N}
from
P(s_{1})
to
z
with
d_{h}
-length at most
M
. Let also
s_{2}
be the first time that
P
intersects
\partial U
and let
Q
be a path in
U
from
P(s_{2})
to
P(s_{1})
with
d_{h}
-length at most
M
. Then the concatenation
\widetilde{P}
of
P|_{(0,s_{2})},Q
and
P|_{(s_{1},1)}
is a path in
\mathcal{N}
from
0
to
z
with
d_{h}
-length at most
3M
. Hence
d_{h|_{\mathcal{N}}}(0,z)\leq 3M
. Next, we fix
z\in\partial\mathcal{N}\cap\widehat{\eta}((t,\infty))
and note that
z\in\partial\mathcal{N}\cap\widetilde{\eta}([0,\widetilde{\tau}_{\rho_{1}}])
. Let
A
be a path in
\mathbb{H}\setminus\widehat{\eta}([\widehat{\sigma},\widehat{\tau}_{\rho_{1}}])
from
0
to
z
with
d_{h}
-length at most
M
and let
s_{1}
be the first time that
A
intersects
\partial U
(it has to intersect
\partial U
in order to eventually hit
z
). Let also
\widetilde{A}
be a path in
\mathbb{H}\setminus\widetilde{\eta}([\widetilde{\sigma},\widetilde{\tau}_{\rho_{1}}])
from
A(s_{1})
to
z
with
d_{h}
-length at most
M
and let
s_{2}
be the last time that
\widetilde{A}
intersects
\partial U
. Then the path
\widetilde{A}|_{(s_{2},1)}
has to stay in
\mathcal{N}
and has
d_{h}
-length at most
M
. Let also
B
be a path in
U
from
A(s_{1})
to
\widetilde{A}(s_{2})
with
d_{h}
-length at most
M
, and let
\widehat{A}
be the concatenation of
A|_{(0,s_{1})},B
, and
\widetilde{A}|_{(s_{2},1)}
. Then
\widehat{A}
is a path in
\mathcal{N}
from
0
to
z
with
d_{h}
-length at most
3M
. It follows that
d_{h|_{\mathcal{N}}}(0,z)\leq 3M
for all
z\in\widehat{\eta}([\widehat{\sigma},t)\cup(t,\infty))\cap\partial\mathcal{N}
and the same bound holds for
z=\widehat{\eta}(t)
by the continuity of the quantum metric with respect to the Euclidean metric. Therefore, we obtain that the
d_{h|_{\mathcal{N}}}
-diameter of
\mathcal{N}
is at most
6M
with probability at least
1-p_{1}
. The proof is then complete since
p_{1}\in(0,1)
was arbitrary.
∎

The final step before proving Proposition
6.2
is to prove that condition (
III
) holds with high probability if
M
is large enough. This is the content of the following lemma.

Lemma 6.13
.

Suppose that we have the setup of Lemmas
6.9
-
6.12
and let
\varphi\colon\mathcal{N}\to\mathbb{D}
be the conformal transformation as in condition (
III
). Then there a.s. exists
M>1
such that for all
r\in(0,M^{-1})
the following hold. The quantum area assigned to
B(0,1/2)
with respect to the field
h|_{\mathcal{N}}\circ\varphi^{-1}+Q\log|(\varphi^{-1})^{\prime}|
is at least
M^{-1}
. Moreover, every point with quantum distance at least
r
from
\partial\mathbb{D}
(with respect to the field
h|_{\mathcal{N}}\circ\varphi^{-1}+Q\log|(\varphi^{-1})^{\prime}|
) has Euclidean distance at least
r^{M}
from
\partial\mathbb{D}
.

We will describe the setup of the proof of Lemma
6.13
before proceeding with its proof. Note that it is a.s. the case that on the event that
\sigma=\sigma_{\delta/A}
, we have that
\sigma=\tau_{x}
for some
x\in\mathbb{Q}\setminus\{0\}
, where
\tau_{x}
denotes the first time that
\eta^{\prime}
disconnects
x
from
\infty
. Hence it suffices to prove the claim of the lemma in the case that
\sigma=\tau_{x}
for some
x\in\mathbb{Q}\setminus\{0\}
fixed. Without loss of generality we can assume that
x>0
.

Let
(K_{t})
denote the family of hulls of
\eta^{\prime}
. Note that the locality property of
{\rm SLE}_{6}
implies that
\eta^{\prime}
can be coupled with a chordal
{\rm SLE}_{6}

\widetilde{\eta}^{\prime}
in
\mathbb{H}
from
0
to
x
stopped at the first time
\widetilde{\tau}_{x}
that
\widetilde{\eta}^{\prime}
disconnects
x
from
\infty
such that
\eta^{\prime}|_{[0,\tau_{x}]}=\widetilde{\eta}^{\prime}|_{[0,\widetilde{\tau}_{x}]}
. Let also
\widetilde{\eta}
denote the left outer boundary of
\widetilde{\eta}^{\prime}
and note that
[
70
, Theorem 1.4]
implies that
\widetilde{\eta}
has the law of an
{\rm SLE}_{\frac{8}{3}}(\frac{8}{3}-2;\frac{8}{3}-4)
process in
\mathbb{H}
from
x
to
0
with the force points located at
x^{-}
and
x^{+}
respectively. Let
U
be the connected component whose boundary contains
0
of the complement in
\mathbb{H}
of the curve
\widetilde{\eta}
stopped at the first time that it disconnects
0
from
\infty
. Similarly we let
V
be the connected component whose boundary contains
x
of the complement in
\mathbb{H}
of the time-reversal of
\widetilde{\eta}
stopped at the first time that it disconnects
x
from
\infty
. Moreover we let
G
be the connected component of
\mathbb{H}\setminus\widetilde{\eta}
lying to the left of
\widetilde{\eta}
. Let
f:U\to\mathbb{D},g:V\to\mathbb{D}
and
\psi:G\to\mathbb{D}
be conformal transformations defined in some arbitrary but fixed way.

We note that all of the maps
f,g
and
\psi
are a.s. well-defined due to Proposition
A.1
and the time-reversal symmetry of
\widetilde{\eta}
(see
[
71
, Theorem 1.1]
). Recall that
[
85
, Theorem 5.2]
implies that there exists deterministic constant
\alpha\in(0,1)
such that all of the maps
f^{-1},g^{-1}
and
\psi^{-1}
are
\alpha
-Hölder continuous a.s.

Proof of Lemma
6.13
.

Step 1. Outline.

Suppose that we have the setup described in the paragraphs just after the statement of Lemma
6.13
.
The first claim of the lemma (lower bound on the quantum mass assigned to
B(0,1/2)
) follows since the quantum area measure assigns positive mass to every open set a.s. Hence, we will focus on proving the second claim of the lemma. In Step 2, we will prove that it is a.s. the case that there exist
r_{0}\in(0,1),M>1
such that for all
r\in(0,r_{0})
and all
z\in\mathcal{N}
such that
\text{dist}_{d_{h|_{\mathcal{N}}}}(z,\partial\mathcal{N})\geq r
, we have that

\displaystyle B(z,r^{M})\subseteq B_{h|_{\mathcal{N}}}(z,r)\subseteq\mathcal{N}.

Then in Step 3, we will show that possibly by taking
M
to be larger and
r_{0}
to be smaller, we have that there a.s. exists (random)
c_{0}>0
such that if
z,r
are as above, we have that the probability that a complex Brownian motion starting from
z
intersects
\psi^{-1}(B(0,1/2))
before exiting
\mathcal{N}
for the first time is at least
c_{0}r^{M}
, and then conclude the proof of the lemma.

Step 2.
B(z,r^{M})\subseteq B_{h|_{\mathcal{N}}}(z,r)
.

First, we note that
[
54
, Proposition 1.8]
implies that there exists a deterministic constant
\widetilde{\beta}\in(0,1)
such that
d_{\widetilde{h}}|_{K}
is a.s.
\widetilde{\beta}
-Hölder continuous with respect to the Euclidean metric for all
K\subseteq\overline{\mathbb{H}}
compact, where
\widetilde{h}
is a free boundary GFF on
\mathbb{H}
with the additive constant taken so that the average of
\widetilde{h}
on
\mathbb{H}\cap\partial\mathbb{D}
is equal to zero. Then, arguing as in Step 2 in the proof of Lemma
6.9
, we obtain that for all
0<r<R
fixed,
d_{h}|_{A_{r,R}}
is a.s.
\widetilde{\beta}
-Hölder continuous with respect to the Euclidean metric, where
A_{r,R}=\mathbb{H}\cap(B(0,R)\setminus\overline{B(0,r)})
. Moreover, combining with the invariance of the law of
\mathcal{W}
when translating horizontally by fixed number of units of boundary length (see
[
88
, Proposition 1.7]
