), we obtain that
d_{h}|_{\mathbb{H}\cap B(0,R)}
is a.s.
\widetilde{\beta}
-Hölder continuous with respect to the Euclidean metric for all
R>0
. Let
R>0
be such that
\mathcal{N}\subseteq\mathbb{H}\cap B(0,R)
. Note that if
z\in\mathcal{N}
is such that
\text{dist}_{d_{h|_{\mathcal{N}}}}(z,\partial\mathcal{N})>r
, we have that
B_{h|_{\mathcal{N}}}(z,r)=B_{h}(z,r)
. Therefore, combining with the Hölder continuity of
h|_{\mathbb{H}\cap B(0,R)}
with respect to the Euclidean metric, we obtain that it is a.s. the case that there exists
M>1
large and
r_{0}>0
small such that for all
z\in\mathcal{N}
and all
r\in(0,r_{0})
such that
\text{dist}_{d_{h|_{\mathcal{N}}}}(z,\partial\mathcal{N})>r
, we have that
B(z,r^{M})\subseteq B_{h|_{\mathcal{N}}}(z,r)
. In particular, the Euclidean distance of
z
from
\partial\mathcal{N}
is at least
r^{M}
.

Step 3. Conclusion of the proof.

Let
r_{0}\in(0,1),M>1
be as in Step 2 and fix
r\in(0,r_{0}),z\in\mathcal{N}
such that
\text{dist}_{d_{h|_{\mathcal{N}}}}(z,\partial\mathcal{N})>r
. Then Step 2 implies that

\displaystyle B(z,r^{M})\subseteq B_{h|_{\mathcal{N}}}(z,r)\subseteq\mathcal{N}.

We will show that possibly by taking
M
to be larger, we have that the probability that a complex Brownian motion starting from
z
intersects
\psi^{-1}(B(0,1/2))
before exiting
\mathcal{N}
for the first time is at least
r^{M}
.

Let
\widetilde{I}
be the arc traced by
\widetilde{\eta}
up until the last time that it intersects
\mathbb{R}_{+}
and let
\widetilde{J}
be the arc traced by the time-reversal of
\widetilde{\eta}
up until the last time that it intersects
\mathbb{R}_{-}
. Then we have the following cases.

Case 1.
B_{h|_{\mathcal{N}}}(z,r)\cap(\widetilde{I}\cup\widetilde{J})=\emptyset
.

Then we have that
B(z,r^{M})\subseteq G
. Note that there exists
C>1
such that

\displaystyle|\psi^{-1}(x)-\psi^{-1}(y)|\leq C|x-y|^{\alpha}\quad\text{for all}\quad x,y\in\mathbb{D}.

In particular we have that
\text{dist}(\psi(z),\partial\mathbb{D})\gtrsim r^{M/\alpha}
and so the probability that a complex Brownian motion starting from
\psi(z)
intersects
B(0,1/2)
before exiting
\mathbb{D}
for the first time is
\gtrsim r^{M/\alpha}
. By conformal invariance we obtain that the probability that a complex Brownian motion starting from
z
intersects
\psi^{-1}(B(0,1/2))
before exiting
\mathbb{D}
for the first time is
\gtrsim r^{M/\alpha}
.

Case 2.
B_{h|_{\mathcal{N}}}(z,r)\cap\widetilde{I}\neq\emptyset
.

Possibly by taking
r_{0}\in(0,1)
to be smaller, we can assume that
B_{h|_{\mathcal{N}}}(z,r)\cap\widetilde{J}=\emptyset
. Hence we have that
B_{h|_{\mathcal{N}}}(z,r)=B_{h|_{V}}(z,r)
and so
B(z,r^{M})\subseteq V
. As in Case 1, we let
C>1
be such that

\displaystyle|g^{-1}(x)-g^{-1}(y)|\leq C|x-y|^{\alpha}\quad\text{for all}\quad x,y\in\mathbb{D},

and then we have that

\displaystyle B(g(z),C^{-1/\alpha}r^{M/\alpha})\subseteq g(B(z,r^{M}))\subseteq\mathbb{D}.

Therefore arguing as in Case 1, we have that the probability that a complex Brownian motion starting from
z
intersects
\psi^{-1}(B(0,1/2))
before exiting
\mathcal{N}
for the first time is
\gtrsim r^{M/\alpha}
.

Case 3.
B_{h|_{\mathcal{N}}}(z,r)\cap\widetilde{J}\neq\emptyset
.

Arguing as in Cases 1 and 2, we obtain that the probability that a complex Brownian motion starting from
z
intersects
\psi^{-1}(B(0,1/2))
before exiting
\mathcal{N}
for the first time is
\gtrsim r^{M/\alpha}
.

Combining Cases 1,2 and 3, we obtain that there a.s. exists
c_{0}>0
such that the probability that a complex Brownian motion starting from
z
intersects
\psi^{-1}(B(0,1/2))
before exiting
\mathcal{N}
for the first time is at least
c_{0}r^{M/\alpha}
. It follows that the probability that a complex Brownian motion starting from
\varphi(z)
intersects
\varphi(\psi^{-1}(B(0,1/2)))
before exiting
\mathbb{D}
for the first time is at least
c_{0}r^{M/\alpha}
. Set
d:=\text{dist}(\varphi(\psi^{-1}(B(0,1/2))),\partial\mathbb{D})>0
and suppose that
\text{dist}(\varphi(z),\partial\mathbb{D})<r^{3M/\alpha}
. Then the Beurling estimate implies that the probability that a complex Brownian motion starting from
\varphi(z)
intersects
\varphi(\psi^{-1}(B(0,1/2)))
before exiting
\mathbb{D}
for the first time is at most a universal constant times
(r^{3M/\alpha}/d)^{1/2}
. In particular we have that
r^{M/\alpha}\lesssim r^{3M/(2\alpha)}
and so we obtain a contradiction possibly by taking
r_{0}\in(0,1)
to be smaller. It follows that

\displaystyle\text{dist}(\varphi(z),\partial\mathbb{D})\gtrsim r^{3M/\alpha}

and so this completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Proof of Proposition
6.2
.

First we note that when the quantum natural time is scaled by
\delta
, we have that the quantum boundary length, the quantum metric distance and the quantum area are scaled by
\delta^{2/3},\delta^{1/3}
and
\delta^{4/3}
respectively. Hence, combining with the scale invariance of the law of
\mathcal{W}
(
[
35
, Proposition 4.7]
), we obtain that it suffices to prove the claim of the proposition when
\delta=1
. Hence, from now on, we assume that
\delta=1
. Fix
p_{1}\in(0,1)
. For all
k\in\mathbb{N}
and
N\in\mathbb{N}
, we let
x_{k,N}
be the point on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
such that
\nu_{h}([0,x_{k,N}])=\frac{k}{N}
. For all
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{-}
and
N\in\mathbb{N}
we also let
x_{k,N}
be the point on
\mathbb{R}_{-}
such that
\nu_{h}([x_{k,N},0])=\frac{-k}{N}
. For
x\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}
, we let
\eta_{x}^{\prime}
be a chordal
{\rm SLE}_{6}
in
\mathbb{H}
from
0
to
x
which is independent of
\mathcal{W}
. Then by
[
87
, Section 4.2]
, we obtain that there exists a coupling of
(\mathcal{W},\eta^{\prime},(\eta_{x}^{\prime})_{x\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}})
such that for all
x\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}
, the curves
\eta^{\prime},\eta_{x}^{\prime}
agree up until the first time that they disconnect
x
from
\infty
. Also, we can choose
M_{1},N\in\mathbb{N}
large enough such that with probability at least
1-\frac{p_{1}}{2}
, we have that
\mathcal{N}=\mathcal{N}_{x_{k,N}}
for some
k\in[-NM_{1},NM_{1}]\cap\mathbb{Z}
, where
\mathcal{N}_{x_{k,N}}
denotes the quantum surface parameterized by the hull of
\eta_{x_{k,N}}^{\prime}
stopped at the first time that it disconnects
x_{k,N}
from
\infty
. Let also
E_{x_{k,N}}
be the event defined in the same way as
E
but with
\mathcal{N}_{x_{k,N}}
in place of
\mathcal{N}
. Note that the quantum boundary lengths of the top, bottom left, and bottom right of
\mathcal{N}_{x_{k,N}}
are all positive a.s. Therefore, combining with Lemmas
6.9
-
6.13
, we obtain that we can choose
M\in(1,\infty)
large enough and
p>0
small enough such that conditions (
I
)-(
VI
) all hold with probability at least
1-\frac{p_{1}}{4NM_{1}}
. Thus, taking a union bound over all
k\in[-NM_{1},NM_{1}]\cap\mathbb{Z}
gives that
E
holds with probability at least
1-p_{1}
for the above choice of
M,p
. This completes the proof of the proposition.
∎

6.3.
Size bounds for the disconnecting good annulus

Fix
\delta>0
and suppose that we perform the exploration as in Proposition
5.1
until the first time that
0
is disconnected from
\partial\mathcal{D}
by chunks for which
E
occurs. Let
\mathcal{A}
be quantum surface parameterized by the cluster of chunks
\mathcal{N}
for which
E
occurs with the property that there exist chunks
\mathcal{N}_{i_{1}},\ldots,\mathcal{N}_{i_{n}}
which are discovered by the exploration for which
E
occurs for all of them with
\mathcal{N}_{i_{1}}=\mathcal{N}
,
\mathcal{N}_{i_{n}}
on the boundary of the connected component which contains
0
, and with
\partial\mathcal{N}_{i_{j}}\cap\partial\mathcal{N}_{i_{j+1}}\neq\emptyset
for each
1\leq j\leq n-1
. We define the inner boundary of
\mathcal{A}
to be the boundary of the connected component of
\mathbb{C}\setminus\mathcal{A}
which contains
0
. We define the outer boundary of
\mathcal{A}
to be the boundary of the unbounded connected component of
\mathbb{C}\setminus\mathcal{A}
.

Proposition 6.14
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{D}=(\mathbb{D},h,0)
has law
\mu_{\mathrm{QD,W}}^{L=\ell}
.
Fix
\delta\in(0,1)
. Suppose that we perform the exploration as in Proposition
5.1
until the first time that
0
is disconnected from
\partial\mathcal{D}
by chunks for which
E
occurs and let
\mathcal{A}
be as above. Fix
\widetilde{u}\in(0,4)
,
s_{0}>0
and suppose that we are working on the event that
\mu_{h}(B_{h}(z,s))<s^{4-\widetilde{u}}
for all
s\in(0,s_{0}),z\in\mathcal{D}
, and
\mu_{h}(B_{h}(z,s))>s^{4+\widetilde{u}}
for all
s\in(0,s_{0}),z\in\mathcal{D}
such that
\text{dist}_{d_{h}}(z,\partial\mathcal{D})>s
. Then, there exist a constant
p_{1}>0
depending only on
u,\widetilde{u}
and
M
, and a constant
\delta_{0}\in(0,1)
depending only on
u,\widetilde{u},M
and
s_{0}
such that the distance (with respect to the interior-internal metric on
\mathcal{A}
) between the inner and outer boundary of
\mathcal{A}
is at least
\delta^{p_{1}}
. Moreover, there exists a constant
p_{0}>0
depending only on
u
such that the following holds. There exist constants
c_{1},c_{2},\alpha>0
depending only on
\ell,u
such that for all
\delta\in(0,1)
sufficiently small (depending only on
\ell,u
), on the event
E_{u,\delta}
defined in the statement of Proposition
5.1
, off an event with probability at most
c_{1}\exp(-c_{2}\delta^{-\alpha})
, we have that
\mathcal{A}
is contained in the
\delta^{p_{0}}
-neighborhood of
\partial\mathcal{D}
with respect to
d_{h}
.

We will begin by establishing the lower bound in Proposition
6.14
, the main input of which is the next lemma. Suppose that we have two
{\rm SLE}_{6}
chunks
\mathcal{N}_{1}
,
\mathcal{N}_{2}
from the exploration. We say that
\mathcal{N}_{1}
comes before
\mathcal{N}_{2}
, or
\mathcal{N}_{2}
comes after
\mathcal{N}_{1}
, if
\mathcal{N}_{1}
is discovered by the exploration before
\mathcal{N}_{2}
. We say that
\mathcal{N}_{1}
is adjacent to
\mathcal{N}_{2}
if the following is true. First,
E
occurs for both
\mathcal{N}_{1}
and
\mathcal{N}_{2}
. Second,
\mathcal{N}_{2}
is the first chunk discovered after
\mathcal{N}_{1}
for which
E
occurs whose boundary has non-empty intersection with
\partial\mathcal{N}_{1}
. Then by the way that the exploration is defined, we know that an interval on the right bottom of
\mathcal{N}_{2}
(whose left endpoint is the initial point of the
{\rm SLE}_{6}
in
\mathcal{N}_{2}
) is contained in the top of
\mathcal{N}_{1}
if both of
\partial\mathcal{N}_{1}
and
\partial\mathcal{N}_{2}
intersect the inner boundary of
\mathcal{A}
.

Lemma 6.15
.

Suppose that we have the setup described in Proposition
5.1
. Fix
\widetilde{u}\in(0,4)
and
s_{0}>0
and suppose that we are working on the event that
\mu_{h}(B_{h}(z,s))<s^{4-\widetilde{u}}
for all
s\in(0,s_{0}),z\in\mathcal{D}
, and
\mu_{h}(B_{h}(z,s))>s^{4+\widetilde{u}}
for all
s\in(0,s_{0}),z\in\mathcal{D}
such that
\text{dist}_{d_{h}}(z,\partial\mathcal{D})>s
. Then, there exist a constant
q>0
depending only on
u
and
\widetilde{u}
and a constant
\delta_{0}\in(0,1)
depending only on
u,\widetilde{u},M
, and
s_{0}
such that the following holds for all
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
. Suppose that
\mathcal{N}_{1},\mathcal{N}_{2}
are two radial
{\rm SLE}_{6}
chunks which are adjacent to each other with
\mathcal{N}_{2}
coming after
\mathcal{N}_{1}
. Moreover, we assume that both of
\partial\mathcal{N}_{1}
and
\partial\mathcal{N}_{2}
intersect the inner boundary of
\mathcal{A}
. Then, we have that the diameter of
\partial\mathcal{N}_{1}\cap\partial\mathcal{N}_{2}
with respect to
d_{h}
is at least
\delta^{q}
.

Proof.

First, we note that since both of
\partial\mathcal{N}_{1}
and
\partial\mathcal{N}_{2}
intersect the inner boundary of
\mathcal{A}
, we have that
I:=\partial\mathcal{N}_{1}\cap\partial\mathcal{N}_{2}
is an interval contained in in the right bottom of
\mathcal{N}_{2}
and at the top of
\mathcal{N}_{1}
, and such that its left endpoint is the initial point of the radial
{\rm SLE}_{6}
in
\mathcal{N}_{2}
. In particular, we have that
I
is the bottom right of
\mathcal{N}_{2}
. Hence, it follows from part (
I
) that the quantum boundary length of
I
is at least
\delta^{2/3}/M
. Note that if
\delta_{0}\in(0,1)
is such that
\delta_{0}<M^{-3/2}
, we have that
\delta<\delta^{1/3}/M
, and so part (
V
) in the definition of
E
implies that the amount of volume in
\mathcal{N}_{1}
which has distance in
\mathcal{N}_{1}
at most
\delta
to
I
, is at least
\delta^{2+u}
times the length of
I
, i.e., at least
\delta^{8/3+u}/M
. Let
q=\frac{8/3+3u}{4-\widetilde{u}}>0
. We pick
\delta_{0}
sufficiently small so that we also have
\delta_{0}^{q}<s_{0}
. Then, we have that the amount of area in any ball in
\mathcal{D}
with respect to
d_{h}
of radius
\delta^{q}
is at most
\delta^{8/3+3u}
, for all
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
. Therefore, if we further choose
\delta_{0}\in(0,1)
such that
\delta_{0}<M^{-1/(2u)}
, then we have that
I
cannot be contained in such a ball, which implies that the
d_{h}
-diameter of
I
is at least
\delta^{q}
.
∎

Figure 6.
Illustration of the setup of the proof of the lower bound of Proposition
6.14
.

Proof of Proposition
6.14
, lower bound.

Suppose that
\delta_{0}
and
q
are as in Lemma
6.15
. Let
\mathcal{N}_{1},\mathcal{N}_{2}
be two adjacent radial
{\rm SLE}_{6}
chunks with
\mathcal{N}_{2}
coming after
\mathcal{N}_{1}
and such that both of
\partial\mathcal{N}_{1}
and
\partial\mathcal{N}_{2}
intersect the inner boundary of
\mathcal{A}
. We will show that the diameter of the interval
I:=\partial\mathcal{N}_{1}\cap\partial\mathcal{N}_{2}
with respect to the interior-internal metric of
\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{1}\cup\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{2}
is at least
3\delta^{qM}
by possibly taking
q
to be larger (depending only on
u,\widetilde{u}
and
M
) and
\delta_{0}\in(0,1)
to be smaller (depending only on
u,\widetilde{u},M
and
s_{0}
). Indeed, let
a
and
b
be the two endpoints of
I
with
a
lying on the inner boundary of
\mathcal{A}
. Let also
\gamma
be the geodesic in
\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{1}\cup\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{2}
from
a
to
b
with respect to the interior-internal metric. Suppose that the length of
\gamma
(with respect to the interior-internal metric) is at most
\delta^{qM}
. We can assume that
q>2/3
and by possibly taking
\delta_{0}
to be smaller (depending only on
u,\widetilde{u},M
and
s_{0}
), we can assume that
\delta^{q}<\delta^{2/3}/M
. Then, either
\gamma
intersects every subinterval of
I
with boundary length at most
\delta^{q}
or it does not. Suppose that the former holds. Fix
z\in I
and let
x,y\in I
be such that
z\in[x,y]_{\partial\mathcal{N}_{1}}^{\bm{\circlearrowleft}}
and
\nu_{h}([x,y]_{\partial\mathcal{N}_{1}}^{\bm{\circlearrowleft}})<\delta^{q}
. Fix also
w\in\gamma\cap[x,y]_{\partial\mathcal{N}_{1}}^{\bm{\circlearrowleft}}
. By condition (
IV
) of
E
we obtain that the distance between
z
and
w
with respect to the interior-internal metric in
\mathcal{N}_{1}
is at most
\delta^{q/M}
and so the same holds for the distance between
z
and
w
with respect to the interior-internal metric in
\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{1}\cup\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{2}
. Hence, the distance between
a
and
z
with respect to the interior-internal metric in
\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{1}\cup\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{2}
is at most
3\delta^{qM}+\delta^{q/M}
by the triangle inequality. Since
z
was arbitrary, it follows that the diameter of
I
with respect to
d_{h}
is at most
6\delta^{qM}+2\delta^{q/M}
. But this is a contradiction due to Lemma
6.15
by possibly taking
q>0
to be larger (depending only on
u,\widetilde{u}
and
M
) and
\delta_{0}\in(0,1)
to be smaller (depending only on
u,\widetilde{u},M
and
s_{0}
). Thus, there exists a subinterval
J
of
I
with boundary length at least
\delta^{q}
and such that
\gamma\cap J=\emptyset
. This implies that there exists a segment of
\gamma
which connects two points
a^{\prime},b^{\prime}\in I
with boundary length distance at least
\delta^{q}
and this segment stays in either
\mathcal{N}_{1}
or
\mathcal{N}_{2}
. Then, condition (
IV
) of
E
implies that the length of the aforementioned segment with respect to the interior-internal metric in
\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{1}\cup\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{2}
is at least
\delta^{qM}
. Therefore, combining everything and possibly taking
q
to be larger (depending only on
u,\widetilde{u}
and
M
), we have that the distance between
a
and
b
with respect to the interior-internal metric in
\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{1}\cup\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{2}
is at least
3\delta^{qM}
.

See Figure
6
for an illustration of the notation for what follows. Now, let
\mathcal{N}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{N}_{n}
be the chunks intersecting the inner boundary of
\mathcal{A}
such that for all
1\leq i\leq n-1
, we have that
\mathcal{N}_{i}
is adjacent to
\mathcal{N}_{i+1}
and comes before
\mathcal{N}_{i+1}
, and set
I_{i}:=\partial\mathcal{N}_{i}\cap\partial\mathcal{N}_{i+1}
. Set also
\mathcal{N}_{0}=\mathcal{N}_{n}
and
I_{0}=\partial\mathcal{N}_{n}\cap\partial\mathcal{N}_{1}
. Let
w
be a point lying in the inner boundary of
\mathcal{A}
. Then, there exists
1\leq i\leq n
such that
w\in\partial\mathcal{N}_{i}\setminus(I_{i-1}\cup I_{i})
. Let
v\in\partial\mathcal{N}_{i-1}\cup\partial\mathcal{N}_{i}\setminus I_{i-1}
be such that
v
does not lie in the inner boundary of
\mathcal{A}
. Let also
a_{i-1},b_{i-1}
be the two endpoints of
I_{i-1}
such that
a_{i-1}
lies in the inner boundary of
\mathcal{A}
. Suppose that the boundary length distance of
w
from
a_{i-1}
is at most
\delta^{qM^{2}}
. Then, condition (
IV
) of
E
implies that the distance between
a_{i-1}
and
b_{i-1}
with respect to the interior-internal metric in
\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{i-1}\cup\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{i}
is at most
\delta^{qM}
. If the boundary length distance of
v
from
b_{i-1}
is at most
\delta^{qM^{2}}
, then combining with the triangle inequality, we obtain that the distance between
w
and
v
with respect to the interior-internal metric in
\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{i-1}\cup\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{i}
is at least
\delta^{qM}
. Suppose that the boundary length distance of
w
from
a_{i-1}
is at least
\delta^{qM^{2}}
. Then, condition (
IV
) of
E
implies that the distance between
I_{i-1}
and
w
with respect to the interior-internal metric in
\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{1}\cup\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{2}
is at least
\delta^{qM^{3}}
. If
v\in\partial\mathcal{N}_{i-1}
, then the distance between
w
and
v
with respect to the metric in
\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{1}\cup\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{2}
is at least
\delta^{qM^{3}}
. Suppose that
v\in\partial\mathcal{N}_{i}
. Note that the boundary lengths of
I_{i-1}
and
I_{i}
are both at least
\delta^{2/3}/M
and so condition (
IV
) implies that the distance between the component of
\partial\mathcal{N}_{i}\setminus(I_{i-1}\cup I_{i})
intersecting the inner boundary of
\mathcal{A}
and the component of
\partial\mathcal{N}_{i}\setminus(I_{i-1}\cup I_{i})
not intersecting the inner boundary of
\mathcal{A}
is at least
\delta^{qM}
with respect to the metric in
\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{i}
. Moreover, the way that the exploration is defined implies that the boundary length of the counterclockwsie arc of
\partial\mathcal{N}_{i}
from
a_{i-1}
to
a_{i}
is at least
\epsilon_{0}\delta^{2/3}
and so combining with (
IV
), we obtain that the distance between
a_{i-1}
and
I_{i}
with respect to the interior-internal metric in
\mathcal{N}_{i}
is at least
\epsilon_{0}^{M}\delta^{2M/3}
. In particular, the triangle inequality implies that the distance between
w
and
I_{i}
with respect to the metric in
\mathcal{N}_{i}
is at least
\epsilon_{0}^{M}\delta^{2M/3}/2
. It follows that the distance between
w
and
v
in
\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{i-1}\cup\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{i}
is at least
\delta^{qM^{3}}
. Note that if
w
has boundary length distance at least
\delta^{qM}
from both of
I_{i-1}
and
I_{i}
, then (
IV
) implies that the
d_{h}
-distance in
\mathcal{N}_{i}
between
w
and the part of
\partial\mathcal{N}_{i}
not contained in the inner boundary of
\mathcal{A}
is at least
\delta^{qM^{3}}
. Combining everything, we obtain that the following is true. If
w
lies in the part of
\partial\mathcal{N}_{i}
contained in the inner boundary of
\mathcal{A}
with boundary length distance from
I_{i}
at least
\delta^{qM}
, and
v
lies in the part of
\partial(\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{i-1}\cup\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{i}\cup\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{i+1})
not contained in the inner boundary of
\mathcal{A}
, then the distance between
w
and
v
with respect to the metric in
\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{i-1}\cup\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{i}\cup\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{i+1}
is at least
\delta^{qM^{3}}
. A similar argument shows that if
w
and
v
are as above but
w
has boundary length distance from
I_{i-1}
at least
\delta^{qM}
instead, then the distance between
w
and
v
in
\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{i-1}\cup\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{i}\cup\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{i+1}
is at least
\delta^{qM^{3}}
. It follows that if
w
lies in the part of
\partial\mathcal{N}_{i}
contained in the inner boundary of
\mathcal{A}
and
v
lies in the part of
\partial(\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{i-1}\cup\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{i}\cup\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{i+1})
not contained in the inner boundary of
\mathcal{A}
, then we have that the distance in
\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{i-1}\cup\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{i}\cup\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{i+1}
between
w
and
v
is at least
\delta^{qM^{3}}
.

To finish the proof, we let
\partial^{\text{in}}\mathcal{A}
(resp.
\partial^{\text{out}}\mathcal{A}
) be the inner (resp. outer) boundary of
\mathcal{A}
. Fix
w\in\partial^{\text{in}}\mathcal{A},v\in\partial^{\text{out}}\mathcal{A}
and let
\gamma
be a geodesic in
\mathcal{A}
from
w
to
v
with respect to the interior-internal metric in
\mathcal{A}
. Then, there exists
1\leq i\leq n
such that
w\in\partial\mathcal{N}_{i}
, and let
\widetilde{v}
be the last point of
\partial(\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{i-1}\cup\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{i}\cup\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{i+1})\setminus\partial^{\text{in}}\mathcal{A}
hit by
\gamma
. Then, the results of the previous paragraph imply that the
d_{h}
-length of the part of
\gamma
from the last time that it hits
\widetilde{v}
to the first time that it hits
w
is at least
\delta^{qM^{3}}
and so the distance between
w
and
v
with respect to the interior-internal metric in
\mathcal{A}
is at least
\delta^{qM^{3}}
. This completes the proof of the lower bound.
∎

We now work towards proving the upper bound in Proposition
6.14
. First, we state the following version of Lemma
6.4
.

Lemma 6.16
.

Fix
\ell>0,u\in(0,1/2)
and let
\mathcal{D}=(\mathbb{D},h,0)
be a sample from
\mu_{\mathrm{QD,W}}^{L=\ell}
. Then, there a.s. exists
C>0
such that for all
x,y\in\partial\mathbb{D}
we have that
d_{h}(x,y)\leq C\nu_{h}([x,y]_{\partial\mathcal{D}}^{\bm{\circlearrowleft}})^{1/2-u}

and the same is true with
[x,y]_{\partial\mathcal{D}}^{\bm{\circlearrowright}}
in place of
[x,y]_{\partial\mathcal{D}}^{\bm{\circlearrowleft}}
. Moreover, there exist constants
c_{1},c_{2}>0
depending only on
\ell
and a constant
\alpha>0
depending only on
u
such that if
C>0
is the smallest constant for which the above bound holds, then we have that
\mathbb{P}\big[C>A\big]\leq c_{1}\exp(-c_{2}A^{\alpha})
for all
A>1
.

Proof.

It follows from an argument which is similar to the one given in the proof of Lemma
6.4
.
∎

Next, we state and prove the following lemma which gives an upper bound with high probability on the diameter of the outer boundary of a radial
{\rm SLE}_{6}
curve drawn for at most
\delta
units of quantum natural time on top of a weighted quantum disk.

Lemma 6.17
.

Fix
u\in(0,1/3)
. Then, there exists
\widetilde{u}\in(0,u)
such that the following holds. Suppose that
\mathcal{D}=(\mathbb{D},h,0)
has law
\mu_{\mathrm{QD,W}}^{L=\ell}
with
\ell\leq\delta^{-\widetilde{u}/2}
. Let
\eta^{\prime}
be a radial
{\rm SLE}_{6}
curve in
\mathbb{D}
, independent of
\mathcal{D}
, starting from a point chosen uniformly at random on
\partial\mathbb{D}
with respect to
\nu_{h}
and targeted at
0
. Fix
\delta\in(0,1)
and suppose that
\sigma
is a stopping time for
\eta^{\prime}
which is a.s. at most
\delta
. Then, there exist constants
c_{1},c_{2},\alpha>0
depending only on
u
such that on the event that the boundary length of the component of
\mathbb{D}\setminus\eta^{\prime}([0,t])
containing
0
is at least
\delta^{2/3-u}
for all
t\in[0,\sigma]
, we have that off an event with probability at most
c_{1}\exp(-c_{2}\delta^{-\alpha})
, the
d_{h}
-diameter of the outer boundary of
\eta^{\prime}([0,\sigma])
is at most
\delta^{1/3-u}
.

Proof.

Fix
\widetilde{u}\in(0,1/3)
small (to be chosen and depending only on
u
). Suppose that
x_{1}\in\partial\mathbb{D}
is chosen uniformly at random from the quantum boundary measure and
y_{1}\in\partial\mathbb{D}
is the unique point on
\partial\mathbb{D}
such that the boundary lengths of
[x_{1},y_{1}]_{\partial\mathbb{D}}^{\bm{\circlearrowright}}
and
[x_{1},y_{1}]_{\partial\mathbb{D}}^{\bm{\circlearrowleft}}
are both equal to
\ell/2
. We let
\sigma_{1}
be the first time that
\eta^{\prime}
disconnects
y_{1}
from
0
. Suppose that we have defined marked points
x_{1},\ldots,x_{j},y_{1},\ldots,y_{j}
and stopping times
\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{j}
. Then, we set
x_{j+1}=\eta^{\prime}(\sigma_{j})
and let
y_{j+1}
be the point on the boundary of the component
D_{j}
of
\mathbb{D}\setminus\eta^{\prime}([0,\sigma_{j}])
containing
0
which is antipodal to
x_{j+1}
with respect to quantum boundary length. Then, we let
\sigma_{j+1}
be the first time that
\eta^{\prime}
disconnects
y_{j+1}
from
0
. We set
N:=\inf\{j\in\mathbb{N}:\sigma\leq\sigma_{j}\}
. Suppose that the boundary length
\ell_{j}
of
\partial D_{j}
is at least
\delta^{2/3-u}
. We note that when scaling the boundary length by
\ell_{j}^{-1}
, we have that the quantum natural time is scaled by
\ell_{j}^{-3/2}
and
\ell_{j}^{3/2}\geq\delta^{1-3u/2}>\delta
. Hence, it follows that there exists a universal constant
p\in(0,1)
such that
\mathbb{P}\big[\sigma_{j+1}-\sigma_{j}>\delta\,|\,\eta^{\prime}|_{[0,\sigma_{j}]}\big]\geq 1-p
a.s. It follows that on the event that the boundary length of
\mathbb{D}\setminus\eta^{\prime}([0,t])
is at least
\delta^{2/3-u}
for all
t\in[0,\sigma]
, we have that
N<\delta^{-\widetilde{u}}
off an event with probability at most
\exp(\log(p)\delta^{-\widetilde{u}})
. From now on, we assume that we are working on the event that
N<\delta^{-\widetilde{u}}
.

Combining Lemma
5.9
with
[
20
, Chapter VII, Corollary 2]
gives that on the event that the boundary length of the
0
-containing connected component of
\mathbb{D}\setminus\eta^{\prime}([0,t])
is at least
\delta^{2/3-u}
for all
t\in[0,\sigma]
, we have that off an event with probability at most
\lesssim\delta^{-1/3+u/2}\exp(-\delta^{-2/3-\widetilde{u}})
(with the implicit constant being universal), the boundary length of the
0
-containing connected component of
\mathbb{D}\setminus\eta^{\prime}([0,t])
is at most
\delta^{-\widetilde{u}}
for all
t\in[0,\sigma]
. Suppose that this event occurs as well. Moreover, Lemma
5.11
implies that there exist universal constants
c_{1},c_{2}>0
such that off an event with probability at most
c_{1}\exp(-c_{2}\delta^{-\widetilde{u}})
, we have that for all
1\leq j\leq N
, the boundary length of the part of
\eta^{\prime}|_{[\sigma_{j-1},\sigma_{j}]}
contained in
D_{j-1}
is at most
\delta^{2/3-\widetilde{u}}
, where
\sigma_{0}=0
. Suppose that this event occurs as well. Furthermore, Lemma
6.16
implies that there exists a constant
\alpha>0
depending only on
\widetilde{u}
such that by possibly taking
c_{1}
to be larger and
c_{2}
to be smaller (depending only on
\widetilde{u}
), we have that for all
j\in\mathbb{N}
, off an event with probability at most
c_{1}\exp(-c_{2}\delta^{-\alpha})
,

\ell_{j}^{-1/2}d_{h}(x,y)\leq\delta^{-\widetilde{u}}(\ell_{j}^{-1}\nu_{h}([x,y]_{\partial D_{j}}^{\bm{\circlearrowleft}}))^{1/2-\widetilde{u}}

for all
x,y\in\partial D_{j}
and the same is true with
[x,y]_{\partial D_{j}}^{\bm{\circlearrowright}}
in place of
[x,y]_{\partial D_{j}}^{\bm{\circlearrowleft}}
. Therefore, combining everything, we obtain that the following is true. There exist constants
c_{3},c_{4},\alpha>0
depending only on
\widetilde{u}
and
u
such that on the event that the boundary length of the
0
-containing connected component of
\mathbb{D}\setminus\eta^{\prime}([0,t])
is at least
\delta^{2/3-u}
for all
t\in[0,\sigma]
, off an event with probability at most
c_{3}\exp(-c_{4}\delta^{-\alpha})
, it holds that
N<\delta^{-\widetilde{u}},\ell_{j}\leq\delta^{-\widetilde{u}}
, the boundary length of the part of
\eta^{\prime}|_{[\sigma_{j},\sigma_{j+1}]}
contained in
D_{j}
is at most
\delta^{2/3-\widetilde{u}}
, and
\ell_{j}^{-1/2}d_{h}(x,y)\leq\delta^{-\widetilde{u}}(\ell_{j}^{-1}\nu_{h}([x,y]_{\partial D_{j}}^{\bm{\circlearrowleft}}))^{1/2-\widetilde{u}}

for all
x,y\in\partial D_{j}
and the same is true with
[x,y]_{\partial D_{j}}^{\bm{\circlearrowright}}
in place of
[x,y]_{\partial D_{j}}^{\bm{\circlearrowleft}}
, for all
1\leq j\leq N
. In particular, we have that the
d_{h}
-diameter of the part of
\eta^{\prime}|_{[\sigma_{j},\sigma_{j+1}]}
contained in
D_{j}
is at most
\delta^{-\widetilde{u}}\ell_{j}^{\widetilde{u}}\delta^{(1/2-\widetilde{u})(2/3-\widetilde{u})}\leq\delta^{-\widetilde{u}-\widetilde{u}^{2}}\delta^{(1/2-\widetilde{u})(2/3-\widetilde{u})}
. It follows by a union bound that the
d_{h}
-diameter of the outer boundary of
\eta^{\prime}([0,\sigma])
is at most
\delta^{-2\widetilde{u}-\widetilde{u}^{2}}\delta^{(1/2-\widetilde{u})(2/3-\widetilde{u})}
. So the proof is complete if we choose
\widetilde{u}>0
sufficiently small (depending only on
u
).
∎

Proof of Proposition
6.14
, upper bound.

Let
\widetilde{u}\in(0,u/3)
be as in Lemma
6.17
, fix
u_{0}\in(0,1)
(to be chosen), and let
\widetilde{E}_{\widetilde{u},\delta}
be the event that for all
j\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[0,\delta^{-2/3-u}],t\in[0,\sigma_{j}]
, the boundary length of the
0
-containing connected component of
D_{j}\setminus\eta_{l}^{\prime}([0,t])
is at most
\delta^{-\widetilde{u}}
. Then, combining Proposition
5.1
with Lemma
6.17
, we obtain that there exist constants
c_{1},c_{2},\alpha>0
depending only on
\ell
and
u
such that for all
\delta\in(0,1)
, the following holds. On the event
E_{u,\delta}\cap\widetilde{E}_{\widetilde{u},\delta}
, off an event with probability at most
c_{1}\exp(-c_{2}\delta^{-\alpha})
, the following conditions hold. The set
\mathcal{A}
consists of at most
\delta^{-2/3-u}
number of chunks and disconnects
0
from
\partial\mathbb{D}
, and for every chunk
\mathcal{N}
in
\mathcal{A}
, there exists
1\leq n\leq\delta^{-u_{0}}
and chunks
\mathcal{N}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{N}_{n}
discovered during the first
\delta^{-2/3-u}
number of steps in the exploration such that
\partial\mathcal{N}_{n}\cap\partial\mathbb{D}\neq\emptyset,\mathcal{N}=\mathcal{N}_{1}
, and
\partial\mathcal{N}_{j}\cap\partial\mathcal{N}_{j+1}\neq\emptyset
, for all
1\leq j\leq n-1
. Moreover, if
\mathcal{N}
is a chunk discovered during the first
\delta^{-2/3-u}
steps of the exploration and
\eta^{\prime}
is the corresponding radial
{\rm SLE}_{6}
curve which makes
\mathcal{N}
, then we have that the
d_{h}
-diameter of the outer boundary of
\eta^{\prime}
in
\mathcal{N}
is at most
\delta^{1/3-u}
. Suppose that the above hold. Fix
z\in\mathcal{A}
and let
\mathcal{N}
be the chunk in
\mathcal{A}
such that
z\in\mathcal{A}
. Let also
\mathcal{N}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{N}_{n}
be the chunks in
\mathcal{A}
such that
\mathcal{N}=\mathcal{N}_{1},\partial\mathcal{N}_{n}\cap\partial\mathbb{D}\neq\emptyset
, and
\partial\mathcal{N}_{j}\cap\partial\mathcal{N}_{j+1}\neq\emptyset
, for all
1\leq j\leq n-1
. Note that condition (
II
) of
E
implies that the
d_{h}
-distance of
z
from
\partial\mathcal{A}
is at most
M\delta^{1/3}
. Since
\partial\mathcal{N}_{j}\cap\partial\mathcal{N}_{j+1}
consists only of points lying in the outer boundary of both
\eta_{j}^{\prime}([0,\sigma_{j}])
and
\eta_{j+1}^{\prime}([0,\sigma_{j+1}])
, and
\partial\mathcal{N}_{n}\cap\partial\mathbb{D}\neq\emptyset
, a union bound gives that the
d_{h}
-distance of
z
from
\partial\mathbb{D}
is at most
M\delta^{1/3}+\delta^{1/3-u-u_{0}}
, where we choose
u_{0}>0
such that
u_{0}<1/3-u
. Furthermore, the proof of Proposition
5.1
implies that by possibly taking
c_{1}
to be larger and
c_{2},\alpha>0
to be smaller (depending only on
\ell,u
), we can assume that
\mathbb{P}\big[\widetilde{E}_{\widetilde{u},\delta}\big]\leq c_{1}\exp(-c_{2}\delta^{-\alpha})
. Combining everything, we complete the proof.
∎

6.4.
{\rm SLE}_{6}
hull cannot be too skinny

The main purpose of this subsection is to prove Lemma
6.18
which roughly states that with very high probability, we have that whenever the whole-plane
{\rm SLE}_{6}
drawn on top of an independent sample from
\mu_{\mathrm{QSPH}}
and parameterized by quantum natural time travels quantum distance at least
\delta^{1/3}\log(\delta^{-1})^{\kappa}
, then at least
\delta
units of quantum natural time have elapsed. The main component of the proof of Lemma
6.18
is Lemma
6.19
which is similar to Lemma
6.17
except that it provides us with a better bound on the diameter of the outer boundary of the radial
{\rm SLE}_{6}
curve of the form
\delta^{1/3}\log(\delta^{-1})^{\kappa}
. We will first prove Lemma
6.18
using Lemma
6.19
and then prove Lemma
6.19
.

Lemma 6.18
.

There exists
\kappa>0
so that the following is true. Suppose that
(\mathcal{S},x,y)
has distribution
\mu_{\mathrm{QSPH}}
and
\eta^{\prime}
is an independent whole-plane
{\rm SLE}_{6}
from
x
to
y
which is parameterized by quantum natural time. Suppose that
\delta>0
. The
\mu_{\mathrm{QSPH}}
measure of the event that there exists
k\in\mathbb{N}
so that the
d_{h}
-diameter of the outer boundary of
\eta^{\prime}([(k-1)\delta,k\delta])
is at least
\delta^{1/3}(\log\delta^{-1})^{\kappa}
decays to
0
as
\delta\to 0
faster than any power of
\delta
. Moreover, the
\mu_{\mathrm{QSPH}}
measure of the event that
\eta^{\prime}
travels
d_{h}
-distance
\delta^{1/3}
without disconnecting at least
\delta^{4/3}(\log\delta^{-1})^{-\kappa}
units of quantum mass from
y
decays to
0
as
\delta\to 0
faster than any power of
\delta
.

Lemma 6.19
.

Fix
u>0
. Then, there exists a constant
\kappa>0
depending only on
u
such that for all
\delta\in(0,1)
,
0<\ell\leq\log(\delta^{-1})^{u}
, the following holds. Let
\mathcal{D}=(\mathbb{D},h,0)
be a sample from
\mu_{\mathrm{QD,W}}^{L=\ell}
and let
\eta^{\prime}
be a radial
{\rm SLE}_{6}
on
\mathbb{D}
, independent of
\mathcal{D}
, starting from a point chosen uniformly at random on
\partial\mathbb{D}
according to
\nu_{h}
and targeted at
0
. Then, off an event with probability decaying to
0
as
\delta\to 0
faster than any power of
\delta
, we have that the
d_{h}
-diameter of the outer boundary of
\eta^{\prime}([0,\delta])
is at most
\delta^{1/3}\log(\delta^{-1})^{\kappa}
.

Proof of Lemma
6.18
.

Step 1. The boundary length process cannot be too large.
Let
\kappa>0
be the constant of Lemma
6.19
with
u=2
. First, we will show that if
(L_{t})
is the boundary length process corresponding to
\eta^{\prime}
, then
\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}L_{t}\leq\log(\delta^{-1})^{2}
off an event whose
\mu_{\mathrm{QSPH}}
measure decays to
0
as
\delta\to 0
faster than any power of
\delta
(where
T
denotes the time duration of the excursion
L
). Indeed, Lemma
5.9
combined with
[
20
, Chapter VII, Corollary 2]
implies that there exist universal constants
c_{1},c_{2}>0
such that conditional on
\tau:=\inf\{t\geq 0:L_{t}=1\}<\infty
, the measure under
\mu_{\mathrm{QSPH}}
of the event that
(L_{t+\tau})
exceeds
\log(\delta^{-1})^{2}
is at most
c_{1}\exp(-c_{2}\log(\delta^{-1})^{2})
. Hence, since
\mu_{\mathrm{QSPH}}\big[\tau<\infty\big]\in(0,\infty)
, we obtain that possibly by taking
c_{1}
to be larger and
c_{2}
to be smaller,
\mu_{\mathrm{QSPH}}\big[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}L_{t}>\log(\delta^{-1})^{2}\big]\leq c_{1}\exp(-c_{2}\log(\delta^{-1})^{2})
for all
\delta\in(0,1)
.

Step 2. Proof of the first claim of the lemma.
We will start by proving the
k=1
case first. For each
t\geq 0
, we let
U_{t}
be the connected component of
\mathcal{S}\setminus\eta^{\prime}([0,t])
containing
y
and set
\widetilde{U}_{t}=\mathcal{S}\setminus\overline{U}_{t}
. Let
\widetilde{\tau}_{\delta}
be the first time
t
that the distance between
x
and
\partial\widetilde{U}_{t}
with respect to the interior-internal metric in
\widetilde{U}_{t}
is at least
\delta
. Note that the interior-internal metric in
\widetilde{U}_{t}
is determined by the restriction to
\widetilde{U}_{t}
of the field
h
generating
(\mathcal{S},x,y)
. It follows from the proof of
[
75
, Theorem 1.2]
that on the event
\{T>t\}
, the path-decorated quantum surface
(\widetilde{U}_{t},h|_{\widetilde{U}_{t}},\eta^{\prime}|_{[0,t]})
is a.s. determined by the ordered sequence of oriented marked components cut out by
\eta^{\prime}|_{[0,t]}
from
y
viewed as quantum surfaces. It follows that
\widetilde{\tau}_{\delta}
is a stopping time with respect to the filtration
(\mathcal{F}_{t})
, where
\mathcal{F}_{t}
is the
\sigma
-algebra generated by the collection of quantum disks that
\eta^{\prime}|_{[0,t]}
has separated from
y
, each marked by the last point on their boundary visited by
\eta^{\prime}
and oriented by the direction in which
\eta^{\prime}
has traced their boundary. Set
\tau_{\delta}=\widetilde{\tau}_{\delta}\wedge\delta
. Then,
[
75
, Proposition 6.4]
implies that conditional on
\mathcal{F}_{\tau_{\delta}}
, the conditional law of the surface parameterized by
U_{\tau_{\delta}}
is that of
\mu_{\mathrm{QD,W}}^{L=L_{\tau_{\delta}}}
. If
\tau_{\delta}=\delta
, this implies that the
d_{h}
-diameter of
\eta^{\prime}([0,\delta])
is at most
\delta^{1/3}
and so this handles the case that
k=1
. If
\tau_{\delta}<\delta
, then we can apply Lemma
6.19
to obtain that conditional on
\tau_{\delta}<\delta
, the
d_{h}
-diameter of the outer boundary of
\eta^{\prime}([\tau_{\delta},\delta])
is at most
\delta^{1/3}\log(\delta^{-1})^{\kappa}
off an event whose
\mu_{\mathrm{QSPH}}
measure tends to
0
as
\delta\to 0
faster than any power of
\delta
. Hence, by possibly taking
\kappa
to be larger, we can assume that the
d_{h}
-diameter of the outer boundary of
\eta^{\prime}([0,\delta])
is at most
\delta^{1/3}\log(\delta^{-1})^{\kappa}
off an event whose
\mu_{\mathrm{QSPH}}
measure tends to
0
as
\delta\to 0
faster than any power of
\delta
. For general
k\in\mathbb{N}
, we note that conditional on
\mathcal{F}_{k\delta}
and on the event that
T\geq k\delta
, the law of the surface parameterized by
U_{k\delta}
is that of
\mu_{\mathrm{QD,W}}^{L=L_{k\delta}}
, and so by applying again Lemma
6.19
, we obtain that the
d_{h}
-diameter of the outer boundary of
\eta^{\prime}([(k-1)\delta,k\delta])
is at most
\delta^{1/3}\log(\delta^{-1})^{\kappa}
off an event whose
\mu_{\mathrm{QSPH}}
measure tends to
0
as
\delta\to 0
faster than any power of
\delta
. Since
\mu_{\mathrm{QSPH}}\big[T\geq k\delta\big]
is at most a constant times
\delta^{-2/3}k^{-2/3}
, (recall the discussion at the end of Subsection
3.2.4
), the first assertion follows by taking a union bound over
k\in\mathbb{N}
.

Step 3. Proof of the second claim of the lemma.
We now turn to the second assertion of the lemma. The time-reversal of
L_{t}
is a
3/2
-stable Lévy excursion with only upward jumps. This implies that for each
k\in\mathbb{N},a>0
, conditional on
\{T\geq k\delta\}
, the number of jumps in
[k\delta/3,(k+1)\delta/3]
with size at least
a
has the Poisson distribution with mean given by a constant times
(\delta/3)\int_{a}^{\infty}s^{-5/2}ds
which, in turn, is equal to a constant times
\delta a^{-3/2}
. By (
4.1
), (
4.2
), the conditional probability given
\{T\geq k\delta\}
that the time-reversal of
L
makes fewer than
(\log\delta^{-1})^{\kappa}
jumps in
[k\delta/3,(k+1)\delta/3]
of size at least
\delta^{2/3}(\log\delta^{-1})^{-\kappa}
decays as
\delta\to 0
faster than any power of
\delta
. By applying a union bound over integer multiples of
\delta/3
, we see that this holds for all such multiples of
\delta/3
simultaneously off an event whose
\mu_{\mathrm{QSPH}}
measure tends to
0
as
\delta\to 0
faster than any power of
\delta
.

We now consider
L_{t}
in the forward time direction again. Then each interval of length
\delta
contains at least one interval (for the time-reversal of
L_{t}
) whose endpoints are an integer multiple of
\delta/3
. Therefore the
\mu_{\mathrm{QSPH}}
measure of the event that there exists an interval of length
\delta
in which
L
makes fewer than
(\log\delta^{-1})^{\kappa}
downward jumps of size at least
\delta^{2/3}(\log\delta^{-1})^{-\kappa}
decays to
0
as
\delta\to 0
faster than any power of
\delta
. Each downward jump of
L_{t}
corresponds to a quantum disk whose boundary length is given by the size of the downward jump. Moreover, these quantum disks are conditionally independent given
L
. The probability that a quantum disk with boundary length at least
\delta^{2/3}(\log\delta^{-1})^{-\kappa}
has area at least
\delta^{4/3}(\log\delta^{-1})^{-2\kappa}
is positive uniformly in
\delta
. Therefore by binomial concentration, the probability that fewer than a fixed fraction of these disks have area at least
\delta^{4/3}(\log\delta^{-1})^{-2\kappa}
decays to
0
as
\delta\to 0
faster than any power of
\delta
. Combining these observations with scaling and the first assertion of the lemma implies the second assertion (up to a redefinition of
\kappa
).
∎

Next, it remains to prove Lemma
6.19
. First, we will state and prove the following lemma which gives an upper bound on the diameter of a radial
{\rm SLE}_{6}
chunk with high probability.

Lemma 6.20
.

Fix
a>0,L_{0}<\infty
. Then, there exists a constant
\kappa>0
depending only on
a,L_{0}
such that for all
\ell\in(0,L_{0})
, the following is true. Let
\mathcal{D}=(\mathbb{D},h)
be a sample from
\mu_{\mathrm{QD,W}}^{L=\ell}
. Then, on the event that the quantum area of
\mathcal{D}
is at most
a
, off an event with probability decaying to
0
as
\delta\to 0
faster than any power of
\delta
, we have that the
d_{h}
-diameter of
\mathcal{D}
is at most
\log(\delta^{-1})^{\kappa}
.

Proof.

Fix
\kappa>0
sufficiently large (to be chosen). Then,
[
77
, Lemma 4.25]
combined with Hölder’s inequality to compare the laws
\mu_{\mathrm{QD,W}}^{L=\ell}
and
\mu_{\mathrm{QD}}^{L=\ell}
imply that there exists a constant
c\in[1,\infty)
depending only on
a,L_{0}
such that if
d^{*}=\sup_{z\in\mathbb{D}}\text{dist}_{d_{h}}(z,\partial\mathbb{D})
, then on the event that
\mu_{h}(\mathbb{D})\leq a
, we have that
d^{*}\leq\log(\delta^{-1})^{\kappa}
off an event with probability at most
c\exp(-c^{-1}\log(\delta^{-1})^{4\kappa/3})
. Fix
u\in(0,1/2)
and consider the field
\widetilde{h}
obtained by scaling lengths by
\ell
, distances by
\ell^{1/2}
and areas by
\ell^{2}
. Then, Lemma
6.16
combined with scaling imply that there exist universal constants
c_{1},c_{2}
and a constant
\beta>0
depending only on
u
such that off an event with probability at most
c_{1}\exp(-c_{2}\log(\delta^{-1})^{\kappa\beta})
, we have that
d_{h}(x,y)\leq\log(\delta^{-1})^{\kappa}\ell^{1+u}\leq L_{0}^{1+u}\log(\delta^{-1})^{\kappa}
for all
x,y\in\partial\mathbb{D}
. Thus, if we choose
\kappa
such that
\kappa>\max\{\beta^{-1},1\}
, then on the event that
\mu_{h}(\mathbb{D})\leq a
, off an event with probability decaying to
0
as
\delta\to 0
faster than any power of
\delta
, we have that
d_{h}(x,y)\leq L_{0}^{1+u}\log(\delta^{-1})^{\kappa}
for all
x,y\in\partial\mathbb{D}
and
\text{dist}_{d_{h}}(z,\partial\mathbb{D})\leq\log(\delta^{-1})^{\kappa}
for all
z\in\mathbb{D}
, which implies that
d_{h}(x,y)\leq 2\log(\delta^{-1})^{\kappa}+L_{0}^{1+u}\log(\delta^{-1})^{\kappa}
for all
z,w\in\mathbb{D}
. This completes the proof.
∎

Next, we mention the following useful result whose proof is essentially the same with the proof of Lemma
6.4
.

Lemma 6.21
.

There exist universal constants
c_{1},c_{2}>0
such that the following is true. Fix
\ell>0,\zeta\in(0,1)
and let
\mathcal{D}=(\mathbb{D},h,0)
be a sample from
\mu_{\mathrm{QD,W}}^{L=\ell}
. Then, for all
k,n\in\mathbb{N}
such that
n\ell^{-1}\geq 1,k\geq e
, off an event with probability at most
c_{1}n^{1/2}\exp(-c_{2}\log(\log(k))\log(k))
, the following is true. There exist points
x_{1},\ldots,x_{N}
on
\partial\mathbb{D}
such that the intervals
[x_{j},x_{j+1}]_{\partial\mathbb{D}}^{\bm{\circlearrowleft}}
form a partition of
\partial\mathbb{D}
and the boundary length of
[x_{j},x_{j+1}]_{\partial\mathbb{D}}^{\bm{\circlearrowleft}}
is given by
\ell n^{-1/2}
and the
d_{h}
-diameter of
[x_{j},x_{j+1}]_{\partial\mathbb{D}}^{\bm{\circlearrowleft}}
is at most
8\ell^{1/2}n^{-1/4}\log(k)^{7/4+\zeta}
for all
1\leq j\leq N-1
.

Proof.

It follows from the argument used to prove Lemma
6.4
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
6.19
.

Fix
u>0
. We define marked points
(x_{j}),(y_{j})
and stopping times
(\sigma_{j})
as in the proof of Lemma
6.17
. We let
N_{\delta}
be the first
j\in\mathbb{N}
such that either
\sigma_{j}-\sigma_{j-1}\geq\delta
or
\ell_{j}<\delta^{2/3}
and
\mu_{h}(\mathbb{D})<M\delta^{4/3}
, where
\ell_{j}
denotes the boundary length of the
0
-containing connected component of
\mathbb{D}\setminus\eta^{\prime}([0,\sigma_{j}])
, and
M>1
is a universal constant (to be chosen). We claim that there exists a universal constant
p\in(0,1)
such that conditional on
\eta^{\prime}|_{[0,\sigma_{j-1}]}
, a.s. we have that either
\sigma_{j}-\sigma_{j-1}\geq\delta
or
\ell_{j}<\delta^{2/3}
and
\mu_{h}(\mathbb{D})<M\delta^{4/3}
, with probability at least
p
. Indeed, suppose that we are working on the event that
\ell_{j}\geq\delta^{2/3}
. Note that by arguing as in the proof of Lemma
6.17
, we obtain that there exists a universal constant
p\in(0,1)
such that
\mathbb{P}\big[\sigma_{j}-\sigma_{j-1}\geq\delta\,|\,\eta^{\prime}|_{[0,\sigma_{j-1}]}\big]\geq p
a.s. on
\{\ell_{j}\geq\delta^{2/3}/2\}
. Suppose that we are working on the event
\ell_{j}<\delta^{2/3}
. By possibly decreasing
p
, taking
M
sufficiently large and applying scaling, we have that
\mathbb{P}\big[\nu_{h}(\partial D_{j})<\ell_{j},\mu_{h}(D_{j})<M\ell_{j}^{2}\,|\,\eta^{\prime}|_{[0,\sigma_{j-1}]}\big]\geq p
. This proves the claim. It follows that
N_{\delta}<\log(\delta^{-1})^{2}
off an event whose probability decays to
0
as
\delta\to 0
faster than any power of
\delta
. The proof of Lemma
6.17
implies that off an event whose probability decays to
0
as
\delta\to 0
faster than any power of
\delta
, we have that the boundary length of the
0
-containing connected component of
\mathbb{D}\setminus\eta^{\prime}([0,t])
is at most
2\log(\delta^{-1})^{2}
for all
t\in[0,\delta]
, and the boundary length of the part of
\eta^{\prime}|_{[\sigma_{j-1},\sigma_{j}]}
contained in
D_{j-1}
is at most
\delta^{2/3}\log(\delta^{-1})^{2}
for all
1\leq j\leq\log(\delta^{-1})^{2}
.

Let
\kappa>0
be the constant of Lemma
6.20
with
L_{0}=1
and
a=M
. Then, Lemma
6.20
combined with scaling imply that a.s. on the event that
\sigma_{N_{\delta}}-\sigma_{N_{\delta}-1}<\delta
, we have off an event whose probability decays to
0
as
\delta\to 0
faster than any power of
\delta
that the
d_{h}
-diameter of
D_{N_{\delta}}
is at most
\delta^{1/3}\log(\delta^{-1})^{\kappa}
. Furthermore, Lemma
6.21
applied with
n=\lfloor\delta^{-4/3}\ell_{j}\rfloor,\zeta=1
and
k=\lfloor\delta^{-1}\rfloor
implies that for
j\in\mathbb{N}
fixed, off an event whose probability tends to
0
as
\delta\to 0
faster than any power of
\delta
, we have that there exists a partition
[x_{i},x_{i+1})_{\partial D_{j}}^{{\bm{\circlearrowleft}}},1\leq i\leq N_{j}-1
of
\partial D_{j}
such that the boundary length of
[x_{i},x_{i+1})_{\partial D_{j}}^{{\bm{\circlearrowleft}}}
is given by
\ell_{j}n^{-1/2}
and its
d_{h}
-diameter is at most
8\ell_{j}^{1/2}n^{-1/4}\log(\delta^{-1})^{9/4}
for all
1\leq i\leq N_{j}-1
. Let
\widetilde{x}_{j}
(resp.
\widetilde{y}_{j}
) be the leftmost (resp. rightmost) point of
\eta_{j}^{\prime}([\sigma_{j-1},\sigma_{j}])\cap\partial D_{j}
and let
1\leq i\leq N_{j}-1
be such that
\widetilde{x}_{j}\in[x_{i},x_{i+1})_{\partial D_{j}}^{{\bm{\circlearrowleft}}}
. Note that by the end of the previous paragraph, we have that the boundary length of the counterclockwise arc of
\partial D_{j}
from
\widetilde{x}_{j}
to
\widetilde{y}_{j}
is at most
\delta^{2/3}\log(\delta^{-1})^{2}
off an event whose probability tends to zero as
\delta\to 0
faster than any power of
\delta
. Then, since
\ell_{j}n^{-1/2}\geq\delta^{2/3}
, it follows that at most
\log(\delta^{-1})^{2}
intervals of the form
[x_{m},x_{m+1})_{\partial\mathbb{D}}^{{\bm{\circlearrowleft}}}
are needed to cover the counterclockwise arc of
\partial D_{j}
from
\widetilde{x}_{j}
to
\widetilde{y}_{j}
, and so the latter has
d_{h}
-diameter at most
\lesssim\delta^{1/3}\log(\delta^{-1})^{17/4}
, where the implicit constant is universal. Combining everything, we obtain that there exists a universal constant
c>0
such that off an event whose probability tends to
0
as
\delta\to 0
faster than any power of
\delta
, the following hold.

(i)

N_{\delta}<\log(\delta^{-1})^{2}
.

(ii)

The
d_{h}
-diameter of
D_{N_{\delta}}
is at most
\delta^{1/3}\log(\delta^{-1})^{\kappa}
.

(iii)

The
d_{h}
-diameter of the part of
\eta^{\prime}|_{[\sigma_{j-1},\sigma_{j}]}
contained in
D_{j}
is at most
c\delta^{1/3}\log(\delta^{-1})^{2}
for all
1\leq j\leq\log(\delta^{-1})^{2}
.

Then, the proof of the lemma is complete by taking a union bound and possibly taking
\kappa
to be larger.
∎

6.5.
Proof of the exit time lower bound

Now we focus on proving the lower bound of Theorem
6.1
. The main ingredient of the proof of the lower bound is Lemma
6.22
which roughly states that if
(\mathcal{S},h,x,y)
is a sample from
\mu_{\mathrm{QSPH}}
and we truncate on an event whose complement has small
\mu_{\mathrm{QSPH}}
measure, then the desired lower bound holds for the metric balls centered at the quantum typical point
x
.

The main idea behind the proof of Lemma
6.22
is that Propositions
5.1
and
6.14
allow us to construct sufficiently many annuli
\mathcal{A}
centered at
x
which have the following property. If we start a complex Brownian motion
B
from
x
which is independent from
(\mathcal{S},h,x,y)
, then
B
has to disconnect from
\infty
a sufficiently large amount of quantum area while making a crossing of such an annulus
\mathcal{A}
between its inner and outer boundaries.

The proof of the lower bound in Theorem
6.1
will be complete by combining with the fact that off an event whose
\mu_{\mathrm{QSPH}}
measure tends to
0
as
r\to 0
faster than any power of
r
, we can cover
\mathcal{S}
by at most
r^{-A}
many metric balls of radius
r
and centered at quantum typical points, for some finite and deterministic constant
A
.

Lemma 6.22
.

There exists a constant
\kappa>0
such that the following is true. Fix
u\in(0,1/3),r_{0}>0
and suppose that we are working on the event that
r^{4}\log(r^{-1})^{-6-u}\leq\mu_{h}(B_{h}(z,r)))\leq r^{4}\log(r^{-1})^{8+u}
,
\frac{\mu_{h}(B_{h}(z,r)))}{\mu_{h}(\mathcal{S})}\geq r^{4+u}
, and
\mathop{\text{diam}}(\mathcal{S})\geq 6r
for all
z\in\mathcal{S}
,
r\in(0,r_{0})
, where
(\mathcal{S},h,x,y)
has law
\mu_{\mathrm{QSPH}}
. Then, we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{x}\big[\tau_{B_{h}(x,r)}\big]\geq r^{4}\log(r^{-1})^{-\kappa}

off an event whose
\mu_{\mathrm{QSPH}}
measure tends to
0
as
r\to 0
faster than any power of
r
, where the expectation is over the Brownian motion with
\mathcal{S}
fixed.

Proof.

Step 1. Setup and overview of the proof strategy.
Suppose that we have the setup of Proposition
5.1
with the above choice of
u
and the event
E
defined in Subsection
6.2
. Proposition
6.2
implies that we can choose
M>1
sufficiently large such that the statement of Proposition
5.1
applies. Fix
r>0
and suppose that the event in the statement of the lemma holds, and set
N_{r}=r^{-4-u}
. Let
(z_{j})
be a sequence of points chosen i.i.d. from
\mu_{h}
. Then, the proof of the lower bound of Theorem
4.1
implies that
\mathcal{S}\subseteq\cup_{j=1}^{N_{r}}B_{h}(z_{j},r)
off an event whose
\mu_{\mathrm{QSPH}}
measure tends to
0
as
r\to 0
faster than any power of
r
. The main idea of the proof of the lemma is roughly the following. First, we will show that off an event whose
\mu_{\mathrm{QSPH}}
measure tends to
0
as
r\to 0
faster than any power of
r
, the following holds. Fix
1\leq j\leq N_{r}
such that
d_{h}(x,z_{j})\geq r
. Then, we can find an annulus
\mathcal{A}_{j}
contained in
B_{h}(x,r)
which consists of radial
{\rm SLE}_{6}
chunks and disconnects
B_{h}(x,r/2)
from
z_{j}
as in the statement of Proposition
6.14
. Moreover, the
d_{h}
-distance with respect to the interior-internal metric in
\mathcal{A}_{j}
between the inner and outer boundaries of
\mathcal{A}_{j}
is at least
r\log(r^{-1})^{-p}
for some constant
p
. We will show this in Step 2. Next, in Step 3, we will show that the following holds with high probability. If we start a Brownian motion from
x
, then it will disconnect at least
r\log(r^{-1})^{-q}
units of quantum area while crossing
\mathcal{A}_{j}
and before exiting
B_{h}(x,r)
for some constant
q>0
and some
1\leq j\leq N_{r}
. Then, we will conclude the proof in Step 4 arguing as in the proof of the upper bound of Theorem
6.1
.

Step 2. Constructing good annuli with high probability.
Let
\widetilde{u}\in(0,1/3)
be the constant in Lemma
6.17
. Fix
a_{1},a_{2}>0
(to be chosen) and for
1\leq j\leq N_{r}
, let
\tau_{1}^{j}
be the first time after
r/2
that
L^{j}
goes above
r^{2}\log(r^{-1})^{-a_{1}}
, where
L^{j}
is the process describing the boundary length evolution of the metric exploration from
x
to
z_{j}
. Given that we have defined
\tau_{1}^{j},\ldots,\tau_{m-1}^{j}
, we let
\tau_{m}^{j}
be the first time after
\tau_{m-1}^{j}+r\log(r^{-1})^{-a_{2}}
that
L^{j}
goes above
r\log(r^{-1})^{-a_{1}}
. Lemma
4.6
implies that
\sup_{0\leq s\leq r}L_{s}^{j}<r^{2}\log(r^{-1})^{2}
for all
1\leq j\leq N_{r}
, off an event whose
\mu_{\mathrm{QSPH}}
measure tends to
0
as
r\to 0
faster than any power of
r
. Thus, from now on, we can assume that we are working on that event. Note that there exists a universal constant
p_{0}\in(0,1)
such that if
Y
is the time-reversal of a
3/2
-stable CSBP excursion starting from
1
, then
Y
hits
0
before time
1
with probability at least
p_{0}
. It follows that the
\mu_{\mathrm{QSPH}}
measure of the event that
\tau_{1}^{j}>\frac{r}{2}+r\log(r^{-1})^{-a_{2}}
is at most
\exp(\log(1-p_{0})\log(r^{-1})^{-a_{2}+a_{1}/2})
. Thus, if we choose
a_{1},a_{2}
such that
a_{2}<a_{1}/2-1
, we have that
\tau_{1}^{j}\leq\frac{r}{2}+r\log(r^{-1})^{-a_{2}}
for all
1\leq j\leq N_{r}
, off an event whose
\mu_{\mathrm{QSPH}}
measure tends to
0
as
r\to 0
faster than any power of
r
. Similarly, conditionally on
\tau_{m-1}^{j}<\infty
, we have that
\tau_{m}^{j}-\tau_{m-1}^{j}\leq 2r\log(r^{-1})^{-a_{2}}
off an event whose
\mu_{\mathrm{QSPH}}
measure tends to
0
as
r\to 0
faster than any power of
r
. Fix
1<a_{3}<a_{2}
. Then, we have that
\tau_{\log(r^{-1})^{a_{3}}}^{j}<\infty
off an event whose
\mu_{\mathrm{QSPH}}
measure tends to
0
as
r\to 0
faster than any power of
r
. Fix also
a_{4}>1
(to be chosen). Let
\mathcal{D}_{1}^{j}
be the surface parameterized by
\mathcal{S}\setminus B_{h,z_{j}}^{\bullet}(x,\tau_{1}^{j})
. Suppose that we perform the exploration in
\mathcal{D}_{1}^{j}
as in Proposition
5.1
up until either the boundary length of the
z_{j}
-containing component does not lie in
[L_{\tau_{1}^{j}}^{j}/2,L_{\tau_{1}^{j}}^{j}\log(r^{-1})^{-a_{4}\widetilde{u}}]
or we have discovered a chunk which cannot be connected to
\partial\mathcal{D}_{1}^{j}
by a sequence of at most
\log(r^{-1})^{a_{4}u}
number of chunks which have already been discovered or we have discovered
\log(r^{-1})^{a_{4}(2/3+u)}
number of chunks. Let
\mathcal{N}_{1}^{1,j},\ldots,\mathcal{N}_{N}^{1,j}
be the chunks that have been completely discovered up until that point. Lemma
6.17
combined with scaling imply that conditionally on
\tau_{1}^{j}<\infty
, the probability that there exists
1\leq i\leq N
such that the
d_{h}
-diameter of the outer boundary of
\mathcal{N}_{i}^{1,j}
is at least
\log(r^{-1})^{-a_{4}(1/3-u)}(L_{\tau_{1}^{j}}^{j})^{1/2}
is at most
c_{1}\log(r^{-1})^{a_{4}(2/3+u)}\exp(-c_{2}\log(r^{-1})^{\alpha a_{4}})
, where
c_{1},c_{2},\alpha>0
depend only on
u
. Thus, if we choose
a_{4}>\alpha^{-1}
, then we have that the above probability tends to
0
as
r\to 0
faster than any power of
r
. Also, by construction, we have that if the above event occurs, then for all
1\leq i\leq N
, every point on the outer boundary of
\mathcal{N}_{i}^{1,j}
has
d_{h}
-distance from
B_{h}(x,\tau_{1}^{j})
at most
r\log(r^{-1})^{a_{4}(2u-1/3)+1}
. In particular, if we choose
a_{4}
so that
1+(2u-1/3)a_{4}<-a_{2}
, we have that the outer boundary of
\mathcal{N}_{i}^{1,j}
is contained in
B_{h}(x,\tau_{1}^{j}+r\log(r^{-1})^{-a_{2}})
for all
1\leq i\leq N
.

Now, we perform the exploration in
\mathcal{D}_{1}^{j}
as in Proposition
5.1
with
\delta=\log(r^{-1})^{-a_{4}}
after we scale lengths by
(L_{\tau_{1}^{j}}^{j})^{-1}
, distances by
(L_{\tau_{1}^{j}}^{j})^{-1/2}
and quantum natural time by
(L_{\tau_{1}^{j}}^{j})^{-3/2}
. Let
A_{1}^{j}
be the event that the elements of
\mathcal{N}_{1}^{1,j},\ldots,\mathcal{N}_{N}^{1,j}
for which the event
E
in Proposition
6.2
occurs disconnect
x
from
z_{j}
. We also let
B_{1}^{j}
be the intersection of
A_{1}^{j}
with the event that the annulus
\mathcal{A}
formed by the
\mathcal{N}_{i}^{1,j}
’s is contained in the
r\log(r^{-1})^{1-p_{0}a_{4}}

d_{h}
-neighborhood of
\partial\mathcal{D}_{1}^{j}
, where
p_{0}
is the constant in Proposition
6.14
. We pick
a_{4}
so that
a_{4}>p_{0}^{-1}
. Combining Propositions
5.1
and
6.14
, we obtain that there exists a constant
q>0
depending only on
u
such that
\mu_{\mathrm{QSPH}}\big[B_{1}^{j}\,|\,\tau_{1}^{j}<\infty\big]\geq q
. Note that the choices of
a_{1},a_{2},a_{4}
imply that
\mathcal{N}_{1}^{1,j},\ldots,\mathcal{N}_{N}^{1,j}
are all contained in
B_{h,z_{j}}^{\bullet}(x,\tau_{1}^{j}+r\log(r^{-1})^{-a_{2}})
, if we further assume that
1-p_{0}a_{4}<-a_{2}
. Moreover, it is easy to see that the conditions in the statement of the lemma imply that the conditions in Proposition
6.14
hold for the field in
\mathcal{D}_{1}^{j}
obtained by rescaling
h
. Hence, it follows from Proposition
6.14
that the distance with respect to
d_{h}
between the inner and outer boundaries of
\mathcal{A}
is at least
r\log(r^{-1})^{-p_{1}a_{4}-a_{2}/2}
, where
p_{1}
is the constant in the statement of Proposition
6.14
. Furthermore, for all
i\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\tau_{i}^{j}<\infty
, we define events
A_{i}^{j},B_{i}^{j}
, and
{\rm SLE}_{6}
chunks
\mathcal{N}_{k}^{i,j}
as above. The same analysis as above implies that given
\tau_{i}^{j}<\infty
, the conditional probability that
B_{i}^{j}
occurs is positive uniformly. Let
I_{j}
be the smallest
i
such that
B_{i}^{j}
occurs. Then, the above analysis implies that the
\mu_{\mathrm{QSPH}}
measure of the event that
I_{j}>\log(r^{-1})^{a_{3}}
for some
1\leq j\leq N_{r}
such that
d_{h}(x,z_{j})\geq r
tends to
0
as
r\to 0
faster than any power of
r
. Note that if
\mathcal{A}_{j}
is the annulus corresponding to
I_{j}
, then the choice of the constants
a_{1},a_{2},a_{3}
and
a_{4}
implies that
\mathcal{A}_{j}\subseteq B_{h,z_{j}}^{\bullet}(x,r)\setminus B_{h,z_{j}}^{\bullet}(x,r)
.

Step 3. A Brownian motion starting from
x
disconnects
r\log(r^{-1})^{-q}
units of quantum area while crossing
\mathcal{A}_{j}
for the first time with high probability
. Suppose that we are working on the event that
I_{j}\leq\log(r^{-1})^{a_{3}}
for all
1\leq j\leq N_{r}
. Let
D_{1},\ldots,D_{m}
denote the connected components of
\mathcal{S}\setminus B_{h}(x,r)
with the property that they contain a point whose
d_{h}
-distance from the boundary of the component is at least
r
. Then
z_{j}\in\cup_{i=1}^{m}D_{i}
for some
1\leq j\leq N_{r}
. Fix
i,j
such that
z_{j}\in D_{i}
. Let
K
be the set of points disconnected from
z_{j}
by a Brownian motion starting from
x
and run until the first time that it exits
B_{h,z_{j}}^{\bullet}(x,r)
. Then,
[
60
, Proposition 6.32]
implies that
K
has the same law as the hull of a whole-plane
{\rm SLE}_{6}
which is independent of
(\mathcal{S},x,y)
, starting from
x
and stopped upon hitting
B_{h,z_{j}}^{\bullet}(x,r)
since we further have that
B_{h,z_{j}}^{\bullet}(x,r)
is a Jordan domain (see
[
77
, Theorem 1.1]
). Then, we have that the
{\rm SLE}_{6}
hull has to pass through the annulus
\mathcal{A}_{j}
and upon doing so, it has to make a crossing in
\mathcal{A}_{j}
from the outer to the inner boundary of
\mathcal{A}_{j}
. In particular, it has to travel
d_{h}
-distance at least
r\log(r^{-1})^{-a_{4}p_{1}-a_{1}/2}
. Then, Lemma
6.18
implies that there exists a constant
q>0
depending only in
a_{1},a_{4}
such that off an event whose
\mu_{\mathrm{QSPH}}
measure decays to
0
as
r\to 0
faster than any power of
r
, we have that the whole-plane
{\rm SLE}_{6}
disconnects from
z_{j}
at least
r^{4}\log(r^{-1})^{-q}
units of quantum area while crossing
\mathcal{A}_{j}
for the the first time.

Step 4. Conclusion of the proof
. Fix
\beta>1
large (to be chosen and depending only on
a_{1},a_{2},a_{3},a_{4}
and
q
) and suppose that
r
is sufficiently small such that
\log(r^{-1})^{-\beta}<\log(r^{-1})^{-a_{4}/3}/M
. Then, condition (
VI
) implies that the
\left(L_{\tau_{I_{j}}^{j}}^{j}\right)^{1/2}\log(r^{-1})^{-\beta}

d_{h}
-neighborhood of the boundary of a good chunk
\mathcal{N}
of
\mathcal{A}_{j}
has area at most
r\log(r^{-1})^{1-p\beta-pa_{4}/3}
, where
p
is the constant in (
VI
). Since
L_{\tau_{I_{j}}^{j}}^{j}\geq r\log(r^{-1})^{-a_{1}}
, possibly by taking
\beta
to be larger, we can assume that the
r\log(r^{-1})^{-\beta}

d_{h}
-neighborhood of the boundary of a good chunk
\mathcal{N}
of
\mathcal{A}_{j}
has area at most
r\log(r^{-1})^{1-p\beta-pa_{4}/3}
. It follows that by taking
\beta
sufficiently large (depending only on
a_{1},a_{2},a_{3},a_{4}
and
q
), we can assume that the union of the
r^{1/2}\log(r^{-1})^{-\beta}

d_{h}
-neighborhoods of the boundaries of the chunks in
\mathcal{A}_{j}
has area at most
r\log(r^{-1})^{-q}
. Therefore, we obtain that the hull of the whole-plane
{\rm SLE}_{6}
must exit the
r^{1/2}\log(r^{-1})^{-\beta}

d_{h}
-neighborhood of the boundaries of the chunks and so the same is true for the Brownian motion. Let
\mathcal{N}
be the first chunk in
\mathcal{A}_{j}
that the Brownian motion hits the part of
\mathcal{N}
which has
d_{h}
-distance at least
r^{1/2}\log(r^{-1})^{-\beta}
from
\partial\mathcal{N}
and let
w
be the first such point it hits. Let
\phi:\mathcal{N}\to\mathbb{D}
be the conformal transformation as in (
III
). Then, condition (
III
) implies that
\phi(w)
has Euclidean distance at least
\log(r^{-1})^{-\widetilde{q}}
from
\partial\mathbb{D}
, where
\widetilde{q}=M(\beta+1-a_{3}/3)
. This implies that the probability that the Brownian motion (conformally mapped) hits
B(0,1/4)
before leaving
\mathbb{D}
is at least a constant times
\log(r^{-1})^{-\widetilde{q}}
. Moreover, it follows from (
III
) that the quantum mass assigned to
B(0,1/2)
with respect to the embedding of
\mathcal{N}
into
\mathbb{D}
induced by
\phi
is at least
\log(r^{-1})^{-4a_{4}/3}/M
. Since
G_{B(0,1/2)}(z,w)\gtrsim 1
for all
z,w\in B(0,1/2)
with the implicit constant being universal, (
3.14
) implies that the total amount of time in
B(0,1/2)
that the LBM starting from
w\in\partial B(0,1/4)
spends up until the first time that it exits
B(0,1/2)
is at least a universal constant times
\log(r^{-1})^{-4a_{3}/3}/M
. Combining everything, we obtain that
\mathbb{E}_{x}\big[\tau_{B_{h}(x,r)}\big]
is at least a universal constant times
\log(r^{-1})^{-\widetilde{q}-4a_{3}/3}/M
. This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Proof of Theorem
6.1
, lower bound.

Suppose that
(\mathcal{S},x,y)
has distribution
\mu_{\mathrm{QSPH}}
. Since the metric in
\mathcal{S}
is bi-Hölder continuous with respect to the Euclidean metric, we know that there exists
\alpha\in(0,1)
deterministic such that
\mu_{\mathrm{QSPH}}
-a.e., there exists
r_{0}\in(0,1)
such that
B_{h}(z,r^{1/\alpha^{2}})\subseteq B(z,r^{1/\alpha})\subseteq B_{h}(z,r)
for all
z\in\mathcal{S},r\in(0,r_{0})
. Fix
r_{0}\in(0,1)
and suppose that we are working on the event that for all
r\in(0,r_{0})
, the above holds and and in addition we have that
\mathop{\text{diam}}(\mathcal{S})\geq 6r,r^{4}\log(r^{-1})^{-6-u}\leq\mu_{h}(B_{h}(z,r))\leq r^{4}\log(r^{-1})^{8+u}
and
\frac{\mu_{h}(B_{h}(z,r))}{\mu_{h}(\mathcal{S})}\geq r^{4+u}
for all
z\in\mathcal{S}
, where
u\in(0,1/3)
is fixed. Note that by Theorem
4.1
and since
\mathcal{S}
is a finite metric space
\mu_{\mathrm{QSPH}}
-a.e., we obtain that
\mu_{\mathrm{QSPH}}
-a.e., there exists
r_{0}\in(0,1)
satisfying these properties.

Let
(z_{j})
be a sequence of points in
\mathcal{S}
chosen i.i.d. from
\mu_{h}
normalized to be a probability measure. With
N_{r}=r^{-u-4/\alpha^{2}}
, we know from the proof of Lemma
6.22
that
\mathcal{S}\subseteq\cup_{j=1}^{N_{r}}B_{h}(z_{j},(r/4)^{1/\alpha^{2}})
off an event whose
\mu_{\mathrm{QSPH}}
measure tends to
0
as
r\to 0
faster than any power of
r
. Then, Lemma
6.22
implies that there exists a universal constant
\kappa>0
such that off an event whose
\mu_{\mathrm{QSPH}}
measure tends to
0
as
r\to 0
faster than any power of
r
, the following holds for all
1\leq j\leq N_{r}
simultaneously. The conditional expectation given
\mathcal{S}
of the amount of time that the LBM starting from
z_{j}
spends in
B_{h}(z_{j},r)\setminus B_{h}(z_{j},r/2)
before leaving
B_{h}(z_{j},r)
is at least
r^{4}\log(r^{-1})^{-\kappa}
. Fix
1\leq j\leq N_{r}
and
z\in B(z_{j},(r/4)^{1/\alpha})\subseteq B_{h}(z_{j},r/2)
. We note that the Radon-Nikodym derivative between harmonic measure on
\partial B(z_{j},(r/4)^{1/\alpha})
as seen from
z
and from
z_{j}
is bounded from above and below by universal constants. By integrating over their first hitting point of
\partial B(z_{j},(r/4)^{1/\alpha})
, we thus have that if
B^{z}
(resp.
B^{z_{j}}
) is an LBM starting from
z
(resp.
z_{j}
) then the expected amount of time that
B^{z}
spends in
B_{h}(z_{j},r)\setminus B_{h}(z_{j},r/2)
before exiting
B_{h}(z_{j},r)
is comparable to the expected amount of time that
B^{z_{j}}
spends in
B_{h}(z_{j},r)\setminus B_{h}(z_{j},r/2)
. Hence, the proof is complete possibly by taking
\kappa
to be larger since
\mathcal{S}\subseteq\cup_{j=1}^{N_{r}}B(z_{j},(r/4)^{1/\alpha})
.
∎

7.
Bounds on the Liouville heat kernel

In this section we prove Theorems
1.1
and
1.2
. We start with Theorem
1.1
, which is now a direct consequence of the results in the previous sections.

Proof of Theorem
1.1
.

First, we will show the desired upper bound for the LBM killed upon exiting bounded domains in
\mathbb{C}
whose closure does not contain
0
. Also, by scaling and disintegration with respect to area, it suffices to prove the desired upper bound when
h
has the law of the unit area LQG sphere. Fix
U\subseteq\mathbb{C}
non-empty and open and such that
0\notin\overline{U}
. Let also
D\subseteq\mathbb{C}
be a Jordan domain such that
\overline{U}\subseteq D
and
0\notin\overline{D}
. Note that
[
9
, Lemma 3.9]
implies that the law of a zero-boundary GFF on
D
is mutually absolutely continuous with respect to the law of the restriction to
D
of a massive GFF on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
with constant positive mass. Hence, combining
[
3
, Theorem 1.2]
with absolute continuity, we obtain that there a.s. exists a random constant
C_{1}<\infty
such that
\widetilde{p}_{t}(x,y)=\widetilde{p}_{t}(y,x)\leq C_{1}t^{-1}\log(t^{-1})
for all
x,y\in D
,
t\in(0,1/2]
, where
\widetilde{p}_{t}(\cdot,\cdot)
denotes the heat kernel of the LBM killed at the first time that it exits
D
, with respect to a zero-boundary GFF
h^{0}
on
D
. Note that the definition of the law of
h
given in
[
35
, Section 4.5]
combined with the proof of
[
70
, Proposition 3.4]
imply that the laws of
h|_{D}
and
h^{0}|_{D}
are mutually absolutely continuous. Hence, it follows that for a.e. instance of
h
, there exists a random constant
C<\infty
such that

p_{t}^{D}(x,y)=p_{t}^{D}(y,x)\leq Ct^{-1}\log(t^{-1})
for all
x,y\in D
,
t\in(0,1/2]
,
where
p_{t}^{D}(\cdot,\cdot)
denotes the Liouville heat kernel for the LBM with respect to
h
when killed at the first time that it exits
D
. Thus, it follows that for all
(x_{0},r)\in D\times(0,2^{-1/4})
with
B_{h}(x_{0},2^{1/4}r)\subseteq D
,
t\in(0,r^{4})\subseteq(0,1/2)
,
A\subseteq B_{h}(x_{0},r)
Borel and
x\in B_{h}(x_{0},r)
, we have that

\displaystyle P_{x}\big[X_{t}\in A,t<\tau_{B_{h}(x_{0},r)}\big]

\displaystyle=\int_{A}p_{t}^{B_{h}(x_{0},r)}(x,y)\,d\mu_{h}(y)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{A}p_{t}^{D}(x,y)\,d\mu_{h}(y)\leq Ct^{-1}\log(e+t^{-1})\mu_{h}(A).

In particular, condition
\mathrm{(DU)}
holds for
h
.

Next, we proceed to prove that condition
\mathrm{(E)}
holds. Indeed, Theorem
6.1
implies that (
2.3
) holds with
\beta=4
and
\kappa_{\mathrm{el}}=\kappa_{\mathrm{eu}}=\kappa
for all
(x,r)\in D\times(0,1)
such that
B_{h}(x,r)\subseteq D
, where
\kappa
is the constant in the statement of Theorem
6.1
. Therefore, by further setting
\alpha_{1}=\alpha_{2}=\beta_{1}=\beta_{2}=4
, Lemma
2.11
combined with Theorem
2.12
and since
\text{dist}_{d_{h}}(\overline{U},\mathbb{S}^{2}\setminus D)>0
, we obtain that the desired upper bound holds for all
(t,x,y)\in(0,1/2]\times\mathbb{S}^{2}\times U
. Now, suppose that conditional on
(\mathbb{S}^{2},h)
, we choose independently a point
z
from
\mu_{h}
and let
\phi:\mathbb{S}^{2}\to\mathbb{S}^{2}
be a conformal map such that
\phi(0)=0
and
\phi(z)=\infty
. Then, it follows from
[
35
, Proposition A.8]
that
\widetilde{h}:=h\circ\phi^{-1}+Q\log|(\phi^{-1})^{\prime}|
has the same law with
h
modulo a scaling factor, where
h
and
\widetilde{h}
are considered to have the embedding introduced in
[
35
, Section 4.5]
. Note that
z\notin\{0,\infty\}
a.s. Hence, combining the fact that the desired upper bound on the heat kernel holds in
U
with
[
13
, Theorem 1.3]
, we obtain that there a.s. exist open neighborhoods
U_{1}
and
U_{2}
of
0
and
\infty
respectively, and random constants
C_{1},C_{2}
such that

(7.1)

p_{t}(u,v)\;\leq\;\frac{C_{1}(\log t^{-1})^{\kappa}}{t}\,\exp\Biggl(-C_{2}\biggl(\frac{d_{h}(u,v)^{4}}{t}\biggr)^{\!\frac{1}{3}}\biggl(\log\biggl(e+\frac{d_{h}(u,v)}{t}\biggr)\biggr)^{\!-\kappa}\Biggr)

for all
t\in(0,1/2],(x,y)\in(\mathbb{S}^{2}\times U_{1})\cup(\mathbb{S}^{2}\times U_{2})
. Finally, we have already shown that (
7.1
) holds with
t\in(0,1/2]
and
x,y
both lying in a bounded domain in
\mathbb{C}
with positive distance from
0
. Combining, we complete the proof.
∎

As the first step in proving the lower bound in Theorem
1.2
we record
an on-diagonal heat kernel lower bound as a further consequence of the results in the previous sections.

Lemma 7.1
.

There exists a deterministic constant
\kappa>0
such that for
\mu_{\mathrm{QSPH}}
-a.e. instance
(\mathcal{S},x,y)
there exists
C\geq 1
such that for all
u\in\mathcal{S}
and
t\in(0,\frac{1}{2}]
,

(7.2)

p_{t}(u,u)\geq\frac{1}{Ct(\log t^{-1})^{\kappa}}.

Proof.

First, we note that assumption (
2.1
) holds for a.e. instance
(\mathcal{S},x,y)
of a sample from the fixed area Brownian map, since (
2.1
) is a.s. true for the restriction of the GFF on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
to every open and bounded subset of
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, and the law of the latter is locally absolutely continuous with respect to the law of the fixed area Brownian map as explained in the proof of Theorem
1.1
. Hence, it follows by scaling that assumption (
2.1
) holds for
\mu_{\mathrm{QSPH}}
-a.e. instance
(\mathcal{S},x,y)
. Moreover, assumptions
\mathrm{(V)}_{\leq}
and
\mathrm{(E)}
have been established for
\mu_{\mathrm{QSPH}}
in Theorems
4.1

and
6.1
, respectively. Therefore, the proof is complete by combining with Theorem
2.1
.
∎

In order to prove the off-diagonal lower bound, we want to construct a chain out of order
(d(u,v)^{4}/t)^{1/3}
sets which connect
u
to
v
, each of diameter of order
(t/d(u,v))^{1/3}
between which the Brownian motion can move with positive probability. The sets that we will use will be given by annuli of
{\rm SLE}_{6}
chunks using a certain good event as in the exit time lower bound. In what follows, we will define the good event in Subsection
7.1
. We will then establish various properties of the corresponding good annuli in Subsection
7.2
. We will complete the proof of Theorem
1.2
in Subsection
7.3
.

7.1.
The good event

We are now going to give the definition of the good event that we will use for the chunks of
{\rm SLE}_{6}
.

Fix
\delta\in(0,1]
and
M>1
, fix a constant
p>0
(corresponding to condition (
VI
) in Subsection
6.2
) which is small enough as specified in Proposition
6.8
, and let
\mathcal{D}=(\mathbb{D},h,0)
be a sample from
\mu_{\mathrm{QD,W}}^{L=\ell}
. Let also
\eta^{\prime}
be a radial
{\rm SLE}_{6}
in
\mathbb{D}
started from the point
x\in\partial\mathbb{D}
which is sampled uniformly from
\nu_{h}
(normalized to be a probability measure) and targeted at
0
, and such that
\eta^{\prime}
is independent of
\mathcal{D}
. Let
y
be the point on
\partial\mathbb{D}
which is antipodal to
x
with respect to
\nu_{h}
and let
\widetilde{\sigma}
be the first time after
\delta/M
that the curve
\eta^{\prime}
is in the boundary and let
\sigma=\widetilde{\sigma}\wedge\delta
. In the case that
\mathcal{W}=(\mathbb{H},h,0,\infty)
is a sample from
\mu_{\mathrm{QW}}^{W=2}
, we let
\eta^{\prime}
be a chordal
{\rm SLE}_{6}
in
\mathbb{H}
from
0
to
\infty
which is independent of
\mathcal{W}
and it is parameterized by quantum natural time with respect to
h
. We define stopping times
\sigma,\widetilde{\sigma}
analogously. In either case, we let
E
be the event that
\sigma=\widetilde{\sigma}
and the following conditions hold for the quantum surface
\mathcal{N}
disconnected by
\eta^{\prime}([0,\sigma])
. (Note that
\sigma=\widetilde{\sigma}
implies that
\mathcal{N}
is homeomorphic to
\mathbb{D}
.)

(I)

For every
r\in(0,\delta^{1/3}M^{-1})
and every
x,y\in\mathcal{N}
with
d_{h}
-distance at least
r
from
\partial\mathcal{N}
there exists
t\in[M^{-M}\delta^{4/3}/(\log\tfrac{1}{r})^{M},M^{M}\delta^{4/3}(\log\tfrac{1}{r})^{M}]
so that

\int_{B_{h}(y,r)}p_{t}^{\mathcal{N}}(x,w)\,d\mu_{h}(w)\geq r^{M},

where
p^{\mathcal{N}}
denotes the Liouville heat kernel on
\mathcal{N}
.

(II)

For every
z\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}
and
r\in(0,\delta^{1/3}/M)
there exists
w\in\mathcal{N}
with
d_{h}(z,w)\leq r
and
B_{h}(w,2r^{M})\subseteq\mathcal{N}
.

(III)

The conditions in the event
E
used for the exit time lower bound, described in Subsection
6.2
, hold with the parameter
M
.

The rest of this subsection is devoted to the proof of the following statement.

Proposition 7.2
.

For every
p_{0}\in(0,1)
there exists
M\geq 1
depending only on
p_{0}
so that
\mu_{\mathrm{QW}}^{W=2}\bigl[E^{\mathcal{W}}_{\sigma}\cap\{E\,\,\text{holds for}\,\,\mathcal{N}\}\bigm|\sigma<\delta\bigr]\geq 1-p_{0}
for all
\delta\in(0,1]
.

First we note that by scaling, it suffices to prove that conditions (
I
)–(
III
) hold with high probability (if
M
is sufficiently large) when
\delta=1
. From now on, we will assume that
\delta=1
. Also the fact that condition (
III
) holds with as high probability as we want (provided we choose
M
sufficiently large) follows from Proposition
6.2
.

Next we focus on proving that condition (
II
) holds with high probability if we choose
M
large enough. The main idea in order to prove the claim is to prove that condition (
II
) holds with high probability when
\mathcal{N}
is the radial
{\rm SLE}_{6}
chunk drawn on top of an independent sample from
\mu_{\mathrm{QD,W}}^{L=1}
. Then the claim will follow by arguing in the same way as in Steps 3 and 4 in the proof of Lemma
6.9
and the second paragraph of the proof of Lemma
6.10
. We believe that this approach will make it easier for the reader to understand the proof of Proposition
7.2
(instead of proving directly the claim for the quantum wedge without comparing the law of the latter with that of a quantum disk), since similar arguments have been presented in Section
6
.

Let us now prove that condition (
II
) holds with high probability when the radial
{\rm SLE}_{6}
chunk
\mathcal{N}
is drawn on top of an independent sample from
\mu_{\mathrm{QD,W}}^{L=1}
. This is the content of the following lemma.

Lemma 7.3
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{D}=(\mathbb{D},h,0)
has law
\mu_{\mathrm{QD,W}}^{L=\ell}
for
\ell>0
fixed. Then, there exists a deterministic constant
M>1
such that
\mu_{\mathrm{QD,W}}^{L=\ell}
-a.s. there exists (random)
r_{0}\in(0,1)
such that for all
z\in\overline{\mathbb{D}},r\in(0,r_{0})
, there exists
w\in\mathbb{D}
with
d_{h}(z,w)\leq r
and
B_{h}(w,2r^{M})\subseteq\mathbb{D}
.

The main ingredient in the proof of Lemma
7.3
is the following lemma which states that the LQG distance with respect to a sample from
\mu_{\mathrm{QD,W}}^{L=\ell}
for
\ell>0
(when parameterized by
\mathbb{D}
) is bi-Hölder continuous with respect to the Euclidean metric (with deterministic exponents) a.s.

Lemma 7.4
.

Suppose that we have the setup of Lemma
7.3
. Then, there exists a deterministic constant
\beta\in(0,1)
such that
\mu_{\mathrm{QD,W}}^{L=\ell}
-a.s. there exists
C>1
such that

\displaystyle C^{-1}|z-w|^{1/\beta}\leq d_{h}(z,w)\leq C|z-w|^{\beta}\quad\text{for all}\quad z,w\in\mathbb{D}.

Proof.

First we will show the claim of the lemma for a free boundary GFF
\widehat{h}
on
\mathbb{D}
normalized so that the value of its harmonic part at
0
is equal to zero and then use the same argument as in the proof of Lemma
6.5
to deduce the claim for the weighted quantum disk.

Step 1. Proof of the claim for a free boundary GFF on
\mathbb{D}
.

We note that
\widehat{h}
can be sampled as follows. Let
\widetilde{h}
be a free boundary GFF on
\mathbb{H}
such that its average on
\mathbb{H}\cap\partial\mathbb{D}
is equal to zero and consider the conformal transformation
F:\mathbb{H}\to\mathbb{D}
such that
F(z)=-\frac{z-i}{z+i}
. Let also
\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}
denote the harmonic part of
\widetilde{h}
. Then we have that
\widehat{h}
can be sampled as
\widehat{h}=\widetilde{h}\circ F^{-1}-\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}(i)
. Moreover
[
54
, Proposition 1.8]
implies that there exists deterministic constant
\beta\in(0,1)
such that the following is true a.s. For every compact set
K\subseteq\overline{\mathbb{H}}
, there exists (random) constant
C>1
such that

\displaystyle C^{-1}|z-w|^{1/\beta}\leq d_{\widetilde{h}}(z,w)\leq C|z-w|^{\beta}\quad\text{for all}\quad z,w\in K.

Thus the same is true with
\widetilde{h}-\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}(i)
in place of
\widetilde{h}
since

\displaystyle d_{\widetilde{h}-\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}(i)}(z,w)=\exp\left(-\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}(i)/\sqrt{6}\right)d_{\widetilde{h}}(z,w)\quad\text{for all}\quad z,w\in\mathbb{H}.

Furthermore it holds that
d_{\widetilde{h}\circ F^{-1}+Q\log|(F^{-1})^{\prime}|}(z,w)=d_{\widetilde{h}-\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}(i)}(F^{-1}(z),F^{-1}(w))
and there exists deterministic constant
M>1
such that
M^{-1}\leq|(F^{-1})^{\prime}(w)|\leq M
for all
w\in\mathbb{D}\setminus B(-1,1/4)
. It follows that there a.s. exists a random constant
C>1
such that

(7.3)

\displaystyle C^{-1}|z-w|^{1/\beta}\leq d_{\widehat{h}}(z,w)\leq C|z-w|^{\beta}\quad\text{for all}\quad z,w\in\overline{\mathbb{D}}\setminus B(-1,1/4).

Note that the random fields
h(z)
and
h(-z)
have the same law and so combining with (
7.3
), we obtain that it is a.s. the case that there exists
C>1
such that

(7.4)

\displaystyle C^{-1}|z-w|^{1/\beta}\leq d_{\widehat{h}}(z,w)\leq C|z-w|^{\beta}\quad\text{for all}\quad z,w\in\mathbb{D}\cap B(-1,1/4).

Fix
z\in\mathbb{D}\setminus\overline{B(-1,1/4)}
,
w\in B(-1,1/4)\cap\mathbb{D}
and let
y
be the point of intersection between
\mathbb{D}\cap\partial B(-1,1/4)
and the segment
[z,w]
. Then combining (
7.3
) and (
7.4
) we obtain that

\displaystyle d_{\widehat{h}}(z,w)\leq d_{\widehat{h}}(z,y)+d_{\widehat{h}}(y,w)\leq 2C|z-w|^{\beta}.

To show the lower bound for
d_{\widehat{h}}(z,w)
, we fix
\epsilon>0
. Since
d_{\widehat{h}}
is a length metric, we obtain that there exists a path
P:[0,1]\to\mathbb{D}
such that
P(0)=w,P(1)=z
and the
d_{\widehat{h}}
-length of
P
is at most
d_{\widehat{h}}(z,w)+\epsilon
. Let
x
be the first point of
\mathbb{D}\cap\partial B(-1,1/4)
that
P
intersects. Then either
|z-x|\geq|z-w|/2
or
|w-x|\geq|z-w|/2
