so that

\eta^{\prime}([T_{b_{n+1}},T_{b}])\cap\overline{K_{T_{b_{n}}}}=\emptyset
.
Then
\{b_{n}\}_{n=0}^{\infty},\{T_{b_{n}}\}_{n=0}^{\infty},\{\eta^{\prime}(T_{b_{n}})\}_{n=0}^{\infty}

are strictly increasing,
\lim_{n\to\infty}b_{n}=b
, and for any
n\geq 0
we have

\min(\partial K_{T_{b_{n}}}\cap\mathbb{R})=a
by
\eta^{\prime}([T_{b_{0}},T_{b_{n}}])\cap(-\infty,0]=\emptyset
,

\overline{\partial^{\mathrm{top}}K_{0,T_{b_{n}}}}\subset\eta^{\prime}([0,T_{b_{n}}])
, and

\overline{\partial^{\mathrm{top}}K_{T_{b_{n}},T_{b_{n+1}}}}\subset\eta^{\prime}([T_{b_{n}},T_{b_{n+1}}])
,
which in turn is included in
\overline{\mathbb{H}}\setminus\overline{K_{T_{b_{n-1}}}}
if
n\geq 1
.
This last property combined with (
A.9
) further implies that

\overline{\partial^{\mathrm{top}}K_{T_{b_{n}},T_{b_{n+1}}}}\cap\overline{\partial^{\mathrm{top}}K_{0,T_{b_{n}}}}

is a singleton
\{z_{n}\}
for any
n\geq 0
and that

z_{n}\in\partial^{\mathrm{top}}K_{T_{b_{n-1}},T_{b_{n}}}
for any
n\geq 1
.
Using these properties together with (
A.9
), we can define
a simple closed curve
\gamma:[-1,\infty]\to\overline{\mathbb{H}}
by

\gamma(\infty):=b
,
\gamma(-t):=(1-t)a+tb
for
t\in[0,1]
,

\gamma|_{[0,1]}
being a homeomorphism to the closed interval in

\overline{\partial^{\mathrm{top}}K_{0,T_{b_{0}}}}
from
a
to
z_{0}
, and

\gamma|_{[n,n+1]}
being a homeomorphism to the closed interval in

\overline{\partial^{\mathrm{top}}K_{T_{b_{n-1}},T_{b_{n}}}}
from
z_{n-1}

to
z_{n}
for each
n\geq 1
; note that
\lim_{s\to\infty}\gamma(s)=b

by
\lim_{n\to\infty}b_{n}=b
, (
A.15
),
the continuity of
\eta^{\prime}
at
T_{b}
and the fact that

\overline{\partial^{\mathrm{top}}K_{T_{b_{n}},T_{b_{n+1}}}}\subset\eta^{\prime}([T_{b_{n}},T_{b_{n+1}}])

for any
n\geq 0
. Then
\gamma((0,\infty))\subset\mathbb{H}\cap\eta^{\prime}([0,T_{b}])
,

\gamma([-1,0])=[a,b]
, and it is elementary to see from the construction of
\gamma

that
\eta^{\prime}([0,T_{b}])
is included in the closure in
\mathbb{C}
of the (bounded)
Jordan domain with boundary
\gamma([-1,\infty])
, which together immediately show

\gamma([-1,\infty])=\partial K_{T_{b}}
and that
b=\eta^{\prime}(t)
satisfies
(
A.9
) with
0
in place of
a
.

Step 8.
It remains to prove that
t=T_{b}
under the setting of Step 6.
For this purpose, noting (
A.15
) and the fact that

\eta^{\prime}((T_{b},s])\cap\mathbb{H}\not\subset K_{T_{b}}
for any
s\in(T_{b},\infty)

as noted just after (
A.3
), suppose that
T_{b}<t
, and
choose

so that
\eta^{\prime}(s)\in\mathbb{H}\setminus K_{T_{b}}
and

|\eta^{\prime}(r)-b|<b
for any

. Set

,
so that by combining (
A.9
) with
0
in place of

from Step 7,
(
A.14
),
\eta^{\prime}(T_{b})=b
from (
A.15
),

\eta^{\prime}([T_{b},s])\cap\{z\in\mathbb{C}\mid\text{Re}(z)\leq 0\}=\emptyset

and
\eta^{\prime}([s,\infty))\subset\overline{\mathbb{H}\setminus K_{s}}
, we get
a<0
,

(A.16)

\partial K_{s}\cap\mathbb{R}=\partial K_{T_{b}}\cap\mathbb{R}=[a,b],

\eta^{\prime}([s,\infty))\cap(a,b)=\emptyset
, and
U\cap K_{s}=U\cap K_{T_{b}}
for some
open neighborhood

of
a
in
\mathbb{C}
. In particular, since
\mathbb{H}\setminus K_{T_{b}}

is a Jordan domain in
\mathbb{C}\cup\{\infty\}
by (
A.9
) with
0

in place of
a
, the point
a\in\partial(\mathbb{H}\setminus K_{s})
corresponds to a unique
element of
\widetilde{\partial}(\mathbb{H}\setminus K_{s})
, hence

by (
A.16
), (
A.11
) and

, and
it thus follows that

(A.17)

(a-\varepsilon,b)\cap\eta^{\prime}([s,\infty))=\emptyset\qquad\text{for some $\varepsilon\in(0,\infty)$.}

Now we use the notation and the results from Step 4. Note that by
(
A.17
) we have

(a-\varepsilon,b)\subset\partial(\mathbb{H}\setminus K_{s,\infty})
and hence that
by Carathéodory’s theorem
[
81
, Theorem 2.6]
the conformal map

\varphi_{s}:\mathbb{H}\setminus K_{s,\infty}\to\mathbb{H}
extends continuously to a bounded

\mathbb{R}
-valued strictly increasing map on
(a-\varepsilon,b)
, which then
satisfies
\varphi_{s}(0)=0
and
(\varphi_{s}^{-1})^{-1}(u)=\{\varphi_{s}(u)\}

for any
u\in(a-\varepsilon,b)
. Set
\varphi_{s}(b-):=\lim_{u\uparrow b}\varphi_{s}(u)
.
Recalling (
A.8
) and noting that

\varphi_{s}(a)\in\eta^{\prime s}_{L}
by (
A.16
) and that

\varphi_{s}(b-)\in\eta^{\prime s}_{R}
by (
A.13
) and (
A.15
), we define simple curves

\gamma_{L},\gamma_{R}:[0,\infty)\to\overline{\mathbb{H}}
starting from
0
and tending to
\infty

by
\gamma_{L}(u):=\varphi_{s}(ua)
and
\gamma_{R}(u):=\varphi_{s}(ub)
for
u\in[0,1)
,

\gamma_{L}|_{[1,\infty)}
being a homeomorphism to the interval in
\eta^{\prime s}_{L}

from
\varphi_{s}(a)
to
\infty
and
\gamma_{R}|_{[1,\infty)}
being a homeomorphism
to the interval in
\eta^{\prime s}_{R}
from
\varphi_{s}(b-)
to
\infty
. Then

(\gamma_{L}((1,\infty))\cup\gamma_{R}((1,\infty)))\cap[\varphi_{s}(a),\varphi_{s}(b-))=\emptyset

and therefore

(A.18)

\bigl(\varphi_{s}^{-1}\circ\gamma_{L}((1,\infty))\cup\varphi_{s}^{-1}\circ\gamma_{R}((1,\infty))\bigr)\cap[a,b)=\emptyset.

Moreover, noting that
\varphi_{s}(\eta^{\prime}([0,s)))
in (
A.8
),
precisely speaking, denotes the image of the lift
\overline{\eta}^{\prime}_{s}
of
\eta^{\prime}|_{[0,s)}
to
\mathbb{H}

under
\varphi_{s}^{-1}
, i.e., the continuous map
\overline{\eta}^{\prime}_{s}:[0,s)\to\overline{\mathbb{H}}

satisfying
\eta^{\prime}|_{[0,s)}=\varphi_{s}^{-1}\circ\overline{\eta}^{\prime}_{s}
, that

\overline{\eta}^{\prime}_{s}(T_{b})=\lim_{\mathbb{Q}\ni u\uparrow b}\varphi_{s}(\eta^{\prime}(T_{u}))=\varphi_{s}(b-)

by (
A.13
) and (
A.15
), and that

\eta^{\prime s}_{L}\cup\eta^{\prime s}_{R}\subset\overline{\eta}^{\prime}_{s}([0,s))
, we have

(A.19)

\varphi_{s}^{-1}\bigl((\eta^{\prime s}_{L}\cup\eta^{\prime s}_{R})\setminus\{\varphi_{s}(b-)\}\bigr)\subset\varphi_{s}^{-1}\bigl(\overline{\eta}^{\prime}_{s}([0,s))\setminus\{\overline{\eta}^{\prime}_{s}(T_{b})\}\bigr)\subset\eta^{\prime}([0,s)\setminus\{T_{b}\}).

Since
b\not\in\eta^{\prime}([0,s)\setminus\{T_{b}\})
by
\eta^{\prime}(T_{b})=b=\eta^{\prime}(t)
,
T_{b}<s<t

and (
A.6
), it follows from (
A.19
),
(
A.18
) and (
A.16
) that

(A.20)

\varphi_{s}^{-1}\circ\gamma_{L}((1,\infty))\cup\varphi_{s}^{-1}\circ\gamma_{R}((1,\infty))\subset\eta^{\prime}([0,s))\setminus[a,b]=\eta^{\prime}([0,s))\cap\mathbb{H}.

On the other hand, (
A.8
) combined with the definition of

\eta^{\prime s}_{L}
and
\eta^{\prime s}_{R}
implies that
\eta^{\prime s}_{L}
(resp.
\eta^{\prime s}_{R}
)
is located to the left (resp. right) of
\eta^{\prime s}_{R}
(resp.
\eta^{\prime s}_{L}
), i.e.,
included in the closure in
\mathbb{C}
of the component of

\mathbb{C}\setminus(\eta^{\prime s}_{R}\cup(-\infty,0])

(resp.
\mathbb{C}\setminus(\eta^{\prime s}_{L}\cup[0,\infty))
not containing
-i
,
and
\overline{\eta}^{\prime}_{s}([0,s))
is located both to the left of
\eta^{\prime s}_{R}

and to the right of
\eta^{\prime s}_{L}
.
Set
\tau_{R}:=\inf\{u\in[1,\infty]\mid\gamma_{R}(u)\in\gamma_{L}([0,\infty])\}

and
\{\tau_{L}\}:=\gamma_{L}^{-1}(\gamma_{R}(\tau_{R}))
, where

\gamma_{R}(\infty):=\infty=:\gamma_{L}(\infty)
, so that
\tau_{R},\tau_{L}\in(1,\infty]
.
Further, recalling (
A.20
), choose a simple curve

\gamma_{s}\subset\varphi_{s}^{-1}\circ\gamma_{L}([1,\tau_{L}])\cup\varphi_{s}^{-1}\circ\gamma_{R}([1,\tau_{R}])(\subset\eta^{\prime}([0,s))\cap\mathbb{H})

from
b
to
a
, which is possible by
[
91
, Theorem II.(5.1)]
, and
let
U_{s}
denote the (bounded) Jordan domain with boundary
\gamma_{s}\cup[a,b]
.
Then we easily see from
\gamma_{s}\subset\eta^{\prime}([0,s))\cap\mathbb{H}
and the above-mentioned
topological configuration of
\overline{\eta}^{\prime}_{s}([0,s))
in relation to
\eta^{\prime s}_{R}

and
\eta^{\prime s}_{L}
that
U_{s}\subset K_{s}
and that
\eta^{\prime}([0,s])\setminus\overline{U_{s}}

is included in the union
C_{s}
of
\{\eta^{\prime}(s)\}=\{\varphi_{s}^{-1}(\infty)\}
and the image by

\varphi_{s}^{-1}
of the part of
\mathbb{C}
located both to the left of

\gamma_{R}([\tau_{R},\infty))
and to the right of
\gamma_{L}([\tau_{L},\infty))
.
Since
C_{s}
is a compact subset of
\mathbb{H}
by (
A.7
) and
(
A.20
), it follows that
V\cap K_{s}=V\cap\overline{U_{s}}\cap\mathbb{H}

for some open neighborhood
V
of
b
in
\mathbb{C}
, which together with the definition
of
U_{s}
shows that
b\in\partial(\mathbb{H}\setminus K_{s})
corresponds to a unique element
of
\widetilde{\partial}(\mathbb{H}\setminus K_{s})
. Thus
b\not\in\eta^{\prime}((s,\infty))

by (
A.16
) and (
A.11
), which contradicts the
definition
b=\eta^{\prime}(t)
of
b
in view of
t\in(s,\infty)
and hence proves that
t=T_{b}
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
A.1
.

We set
\tau:=\inf\{t\in[0,\inf(\eta^{\prime})^{-1}(0))\mid\partial K_{t}\cap\partial\mathbb{D}=\partial\mathbb{D}\}
, so that

\{t\in(0,\inf(\eta^{\prime})^{-1}(0))\mid\text{$\partial K_{t}\cap\partial\mathbb{D}\not=\partial\mathbb{D}$, $\eta^{\prime}(t)\in\partial\mathbb{D}$}\}\subset[0,\tau)
.
Also let
\overline{\eta}^{\prime}
be a chordal
{\rm SLE}_{6}
on
\mathbb{H}
from
0
to
\infty
,
set
\eta^{\prime}_{*}(t):=x\exp(i\overline{\eta}^{\prime}(t))
for
t\in[0,\infty)
,
define
K^{*}_{t}
for
t\in[0,\infty)
to be the complement in
\mathbb{D}
of the

0
-containing component of
\mathbb{D}\setminus\eta^{\prime}_{*}([0,t])
, and set

\tau_{*}:=\inf\{t\in[0,\infty)\mid\partial K^{*}_{t}\cap\partial\mathbb{D}=\partial\mathbb{D}\}
.
Then since
\{\eta^{\prime}\}_{t\in[0,\tau)}
has the same law as a time reparameterization of

\{\eta^{\prime}_{*}\}_{t\in[0,\tau_{*})}
by a version
[
60
, Proposition 6.22]
of
the locality of
{\rm SLE}_{6}
, it suffices to show the assertion for
\{\eta^{\prime}_{*}\}_{t\in[0,\tau_{*})}
.

Fix any instance of
\overline{\eta}^{\prime}
with the property (
A.2
)
for any
t\in(0,\infty)
with
\overline{\eta}^{\prime}(t)\in\mathbb{R}
, which occurs
a.s. by Proposition
A.2
. Let
t\in(0,\infty)

satisfy
\partial K^{*}_{t}\cap\partial\mathbb{D}\not=\partial\mathbb{D}
, which means that
we can take a piecewise linear simple curve
\gamma^{t}:[0,1]\to\overline{\mathbb{D}}\setminus\eta^{\prime}_{*}([0,t])

with
\gamma^{t}(0)=0
,
\gamma^{t}([0,1))\subset\mathbb{D}
and
\gamma^{t}(1)\in\partial\mathbb{D}
.
Then
D_{t}:=\mathbb{D}\setminus\gamma^{t}([0,1))
is a simply connected domain with

0\not\in D_{t}
and hence there exists a unique continuous branch
f_{t}
of

-i\log(\cdot/x)
on
\widetilde{D}_{t}:=D_{t}\cup(\partial\mathbb{D}\setminus\{\gamma^{t}(1)\})

with
f_{t}(x)=0
, so that

x\exp(i\cdot)|_{f_{t}(\widetilde{D}_{t})}:f_{t}(\widetilde{D}_{t})\to\widetilde{D}_{t}

is a homeomorphism with inverse
f_{t}
. Now since
\eta^{\prime}_{*}([0,t])\subset\widetilde{D}_{t}
,

x\exp(if_{t}(\eta^{\prime}_{*}(s)))=\eta^{\prime}_{*}(s)=x\exp(i\overline{\eta}^{\prime}(s))

for any
s\in[0,t]
and
f_{t}(\eta^{\prime}_{*}(0))=f_{t}(x)=0=\overline{\eta}^{\prime}(0)
,
it follows that
f_{t}\circ\eta^{\prime}_{*}|_{[0,t]}=\overline{\eta}^{\prime}|_{[0,t]}
and thus
that
f_{t}(K^{*}_{t})
is the complement in
\mathbb{H}
of the unbounded component of

\mathbb{H}\setminus f_{t}(\eta^{\prime}_{*}([0,t]))=\mathbb{H}\setminus\overline{\eta}^{\prime}([0,t])
.
In particular, if it also holds that
\eta^{\prime}_{*}(t)\in\partial\mathbb{D}
,
then
\overline{\eta}^{\prime}(t)=f_{t}(\eta^{\prime}_{*}(t))\in\mathbb{R}
, hence

f_{t}(K^{*}_{t})
has the property (
A.2
), and
therefore its image
K^{*}_{t}
by the homeomorphism
x\exp(i\cdot)|_{V_{t}}

on a neighborhood
V_{t}
of
f_{t}(\widetilde{D}_{t})
in
\mathbb{C}

has the property (
A.1
), completing the proof.
∎

Appendix B
Some Lévy process estimates

In this appendix, we prove some estimates on the
3/2
-stable Lévy processes with only downward jumps
(Propositions
B.3
,
B.4
,

B.5
,
B.6
and

B.7
below), on which our proof of
Proposition
5.2
heavily relies.

Let
X^{1},X^{2}
be i.i.d.
3/2
-stable Lévy processes with only downward jumps and starting from
0
.
Let
I_{t}^{j}=\inf_{0\leq s\leq t}X_{s}^{j}
and
S_{t}^{j}=\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}X_{s}^{j}
for
j=1,2
, respectively,
be the running infimum and supremum of
X^{j}
. We let
\tau^{j}=\inf\{t\geq 1\mid X_{t}^{j}=I_{t}^{j}\}
for
j=1,2
and
\tau=\tau^{1}\wedge\tau^{2}
.
Note that by
[
20
, Chapter VII, Theorem 1 and Chapter VI, Proposition 3]
we have

(B.1)

\mathbb{P}[\tau^{j}=x]\leq\mathbb{P}[X^{j}_{x}=I^{j}_{x}]=0\qquad\text{for any $x\in[1,\infty)$.}

Lemma B.1
.

There exists a constant
c>0
such that
\mathbb{P}[\tau^{j}\geq x]=cx^{-1/3}(1+o(1))
as
x\to\infty
.

Proof.

Let
x\in(1,\infty)
. Then setting
F(y)=y^{-1/2}\mathbb{P}[I^{2}_{1}>-y]
for
y\in(0,\infty)
,
by the Markov property of
X^{1}
and the scaling property of
X^{2}
we have that

(B.2)

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}[\tau^{1}>x\,|\,I_{1}^{1},X_{1}^{1}]=\mathbb{P}[I^{2}_{x-1}>I_{1}^{1}-X_{1}^{1}\,|\,X_{1}^{1},I_{1}^{1}]=\mathbb{P}[I^{2}_{1}>(x-1)^{-2/3}(I_{1}^{1}-X_{1}^{1})\,|\,X_{1}^{1},I_{1}^{1}]\\
&=(x-1)^{-1/3}(X_{1}^{1}-I_{1}^{1})^{1/2}F\bigl((x-1)^{-2/3}(X_{1}^{1}-I_{1}^{1})\bigr)\\
&\leq(x-1)^{-1/3}(X^{1}_{1}-I^{1}_{1})^{1/2}\Bigl(F\bigl((x-1)^{-2/3}(X_{1}^{1}-I_{1}^{1})\bigr){\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{X^{1}_{1}-I^{1}_{1}\leq(x-1)^{2/3}\}}+1\Bigr)\end{split}

and that
\lim_{y\downarrow 0}F(y)=c
for some
c>0

by
[
20
, Chapter VIII, Proposition 2]
.
Since
X_{1}^{1}-I_{1}^{1}
has a finite mean
by
[
20
, Chapter VI, Proposition 3 and Chapter VII, Corollary 2-(i)]

and hence a finite
1/2
-moment, it follows by an application of the dominated convergence
theorem based on (
B.2
) and
\lim_{y\downarrow 0}F(y)=c
that

\mathbb{P}[\tau^{1}\geq x]=\mathbb{E}\bigl[\mathbb{P}[\tau^{1}>x\,|\,I_{1}^{1},X_{1}^{1}]\bigr]=c\mathbb{E}[(X_{1}^{1}-I_{1}^{1})^{1/2}]x^{-1/3}(1+o(1))
as
x\to\infty
.
∎

As a consequence of Lemma
B.1
, there exists a constant
c>0
such that

(B.3)

\mathbb{P}[\tau\geq x]=\mathbb{P}[\tau^{1}\geq x]\,\mathbb{P}[\tau^{2}\geq x]=cx^{-2/3}(1+o(1))\qquad\text{as $x\to\infty$.}

Lemma B.2
.

Set
\tau^{1}_{0}=\inf\{t\geq 0:X^{1}_{t}<-1\}
. Then there exists a constant

c>0
such that for any
x\in[1,\infty)
,

(B.4)

\mathbb{P}\bigl[\tau^{1}_{0}<1,\,X^{1}_{\tau^{1}_{0}}<-x\bigr]\geq cx^{-3/2}.

Proof.

For
j=1,2
, by
[
90
, Theorem 4.3.7, Corollary 4.2.17 and Theorem 4.2.8]

we can decompose
X^{j}
uniquely as
X^{j}_{t}=X^{j,0}_{t}+X^{j,1}_{t}
, where

X^{j,0},X^{j,1}
are independent Lévy processes with
X^{j,0}
having jumps only
in
[-2,0)
and
X^{j,1}
a compound Poisson process with jumps only in
(-\infty,-2)
.
Then
X^{1,0},X^{1,1},X^{2,0},X^{2,1}
are independent,
X^{1,0},X^{2,0}

have the same law, so do
X^{1,1},X^{2,1}
, and hence the process

X=\{X_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
defined by
X_{t}=X^{1,0}_{t}+X^{2,1}_{t}

has the same law as
X^{1}
. Set
\tau_{0}=\inf\{t\geq 0:X_{t}<-1\}
.

Let
E^{1}
denote the event that
\sup_{0\leq t<1}|X^{1}_{t}|\leq 1
, so that

\mathbb{P}[E^{1}]>0
by
[
20
, Chapter VIII, Proposition 3]
and

X^{1}|_{[0,1)}=X^{1,0}|_{[0,1)}
a.s. on
E^{1}
. Let
x\in[2,\infty)
, and recall that
the numbers
N^{2}_{[-x,-2)},N^{2}_{(-\infty,-x)}
of jumps in
[-x,-2),(-\infty,-x)
,
respectively, made by
X^{2,1}|_{[0,1)}
are independent and have the Poisson
distribution with mean
c(2^{-3/2}-x^{-3/2}),cx^{-3/2}
, respectively,
for some
c>0
independent of
x
. Since

\{\tau_{0}<1,\,X_{\tau_{0}}<-(x-1)\}\supset E^{1}\cap\bigl\{N^{2}_{[-x,-2)}=0<N^{2}_{(-\infty,-x)}\bigr\}

a.s., it follows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl[\tau^{1}_{0}<1,\,X^{1}_{\tau^{1}_{0}}<-(x-1)\bigr]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigl[\tau_{0}<1,\,X_{\tau_{0}}<-(x-1)\bigr]

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\bigl[E^{1}\cap\bigl\{N^{2}_{[-x,-2)}=0<N^{2}_{(-\infty,-x)}\bigr\}\bigr]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}[E^{1}]\cdot\mathbb{P}\bigl[N^{2}_{[-x,-2)}=0\bigr]\cdot\mathbb{P}\bigl[N^{2}_{(-\infty,-x)}\geq 1\bigr]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}[E^{1}]e^{-c(2^{-3/2}-x^{-3/2})}\bigl(1-e^{-cx^{-3/2}}\bigr)\geq c^{\prime}(x-1)^{-3/2}

for some
c^{\prime}>0
independent of
x\in[2,\infty)
, proving (
B.4
).
∎

Proposition B.3
.

There exists a constant
c>0
so that
\mathbb{E}[-I^{1}_{\tau}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\tau<A\}}]\geq c\log A

for any
A\in[2,\infty)
.

Proof.

We first claim that

(B.5)

\mathbb{P}[X_{k}^{1}\geq\alpha k^{2/3}\,|\,\tau^{1}\geq k]\to 0\quad\text{as}\quad\alpha\to\infty\quad\text{uniformly in $k$}.

To see this, fix
\alpha\in[2,\infty)
. For each
k\in\mathbb{N}
, let
\sigma_{k}^{1}=\inf\{t\geq 0:X_{t}^{1}=k^{2/3}\}
.
Then since
X^{1}
has only downward jumps, we have
\{X^{1}_{k}\geq\alpha k^{2/3}\}\subset\{\sigma^{1}_{k}\leq k\}
and hence that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}[X_{k}^{1}\geq\alpha k^{2/3}\,|\,\tau^{1}\geq k]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}[X_{k}^{1}\geq\alpha k^{2/3},\ \sigma_{k}^{1}\leq k\,|\,\tau^{1}\geq k]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}[X_{k}^{1}\geq\alpha k^{2/3}\,|\,\sigma_{k}^{1}\leq k,\tau^{1}\geq k]

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{P}[X_{k}^{1}\geq\alpha k^{2/3},\tau^{1}\geq k\,|\,\sigma_{k}^{1}\leq k,\tau^{1}\geq\sigma_{k}^{1}]}{\mathbb{P}[\tau^{1}\geq k\,|\,\sigma_{k}^{1}\leq k,\tau^{1}\geq\sigma_{k}^{1}]}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathbb{P}\bigl[\sup_{\sigma^{1}_{k}\leq t\leq\sigma^{1}_{k}+k}X^{1}_{t}\geq\alpha k^{2/3}\,|\,\sigma_{k}^{1}\leq k,\tau^{1}\geq\sigma_{k}^{1}\bigr]}{\mathbb{P}[\tau^{1}\geq k\,|\,\sigma_{k}^{1}\leq k,\tau^{1}\geq\sigma_{k}^{1}]}.

Applying the strong Markov property of
X^{1}
at the time
\sigma_{k}^{1}

and the scaling property of
X^{1}
, we easily see
that the denominator is at least
\mathbb{P}[I^{1}_{1}>-1]
,
which is positive by
[
20
, Chapter VIII, Proposition 2]
, and that
the numerator is at most
\mathbb{P}[S^{1}_{1}\geq\alpha-1]
, which tends to
0

as
\alpha\to\infty
, proving (
B.5
).
On the other hand, since
I^{1}_{k}=I^{1}_{1}
on
\{\tau^{1}>k\}
,
from Lemma
B.1
and
[
20
, Chapter VIII, Proposition 4]
we obtain

(B.6)

\mathbb{P}[I_{k}^{1}\leq-\alpha k^{2/3}\,|\,\tau^{1}\geq k]\leq\frac{\mathbb{P}[I^{1}_{1}\leq-\alpha k^{2/3}]}{\mathbb{P}[\tau^{1}\geq k]}\leq c\alpha^{-3/2}k^{-1}k^{1/3}\leq c\alpha^{-3/2}

for any
k\in\mathbb{N}
for some
c>0
.

Further, there exists
p_{0}>0
so that

(B.7)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}[X_{k}^{1}\geq I_{k}^{1}+1\,|\,\tau^{1}\geq k]\geq p_{0}\qquad\text{for all $k\in\mathbb{N}$.}

Indeed, for any
t\in[2,\infty)
and for any
k\in\mathbb{N}
, we have
\mathbb{P}[X_{1}^{1}\geq I_{1}^{1}+t]>0

by
[
20
, Chapter VI, Proposition 3 and Chapter VII, Corollary 2-(i)]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}[X^{1}_{k}\geq I^{1}_{k}+t,\,\tau^{1}\geq k]

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{P}[\inf_{k\leq t\leq k+1}(X^{1}_{t}-X^{1}_{k})>-1,\,X^{1}_{k}\geq I^{1}_{k}+t,\,\tau^{1}\geq k]}{\mathbb{P}[I^{1}_{1}>-1]}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}[I^{1}_{1}>-1]^{-1}\mathbb{P}[X^{1}_{k+1}\geq I^{1}_{k+1}+t-1,\,\tau^{1}\geq k+1],

and hence
\mathbb{P}[X_{k}^{1}\geq I_{k}^{1}+1\,|\,\tau^{1}\geq k]>0
.
Also, noting that
\mathbb{P}[I^{1}_{1}\leq-1]>0
by
[
20
, Chapter VIII, Proposition 4]
, for any
k\in\mathbb{N}
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}[X^{1}_{k}<I^{1}_{k}+1\,|\,\tau^{1}\geq k]

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{P}[\inf_{k\leq t\leq k+1}(X^{1}_{t}-X^{1}_{k})\leq-1,\,X^{1}_{k}<I^{1}_{k}+1\,|\,\tau^{1}\geq k]}{\mathbb{P}[I^{1}_{1}\leq-1]}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathbb{P}[\tau^{1}<k+1\,|\,\tau^{1}\geq k]}{\mathbb{P}[I^{1}_{1}\leq-1]}=\frac{1-\mathbb{P}[\tau^{1}\geq k+1]/\mathbb{P}[\tau^{1}\geq k]}{\mathbb{P}[I^{1}_{1}\leq-1]}\xrightarrow{k\to\infty}0,

where the last limit follows by Lemma
B.1
. The above results together yield (
B.7
).

Now, combining (
B.5
), (
B.6
) and (
B.7
), we can choose
\alpha\in[2,\infty)
sufficiently large so that

(B.8)

\mathbb{P}\bigl[I_{k}^{1}+1\leq X_{k}^{1}\leq\alpha k^{2/3},\,I_{k}^{1}\geq-\alpha k^{2/3}\,|\,\tau^{1}\geq k\bigr]\geq p_{0}/2\qquad\text{for all $k\in\mathbb{N}$.}

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}
, set
\tau^{1}_{k}=\inf\{t\geq 0:X^{1}_{t+k}-X^{1}_{k}<-1\}
and let
E^{1}_{k}

denote the event that
\tau^{1}_{k}<1
and
X^{1}_{\tau^{1}_{k}+k}-X^{1}_{k}<-2\alpha k^{2/3}
.
Then since
\{X^{1}_{t+k}-X^{1}_{k}\}_{t\geq 0}
is independent of
X^{1}|_{[0,k]}

and has the same law as
X^{1}
, we have
\mathbb{P}[E^{1}_{k}]\geq c^{\prime}k^{-1}
for a constant
c^{\prime}>0

independent of
k
by Lemma
B.2
, and it follows that

(B.9)

\mathbb{P}[E_{k}^{1}\,|\,I_{k}^{1}+1\leq X_{k}^{1}\leq\alpha k^{2/3},\,I_{k}^{1}\geq-\alpha k^{2/3},\,\tau^{1}\geq k]=\mathbb{P}[E_{k}^{1}]\geq c^{\prime}k^{-1}.

Further, on the event
E_{k}^{1}\cap\{I_{k}^{1}+1\leq X_{k}^{1}\leq\alpha k^{2/3},\,I_{k}^{1}\geq-\alpha k^{2/3},\,\tau^{1}\geq k,\tau^{2}\geq k+1\}

we have
\tau=\tau^{1}=\tau^{1}_{k}+k\in[k,k+1)
and
I^{1}_{\tau}\leq-\alpha k^{2/3}
.
It therefore follows that there exist constants
c_{1},c_{2}>0
so that for any
A\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle[-I^{1}_{\tau}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\tau<A\}}]

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{k=1}^{A-1}\alpha k^{2/3}\mathbb{P}\bigl[E_{k}^{1}\cap\{I_{k}^{1}+1\leq X_{k}^{1}\leq\alpha k^{2/3},\,I_{k}^{1}\geq-\alpha k^{2/3},\,\tau^{1}\geq k,\,\tau^{2}\geq k+1\}\bigr]

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{k=1}^{A-1}k^{2/3}c_{1}k^{-1}\mathbb{P}[\tau^{1}\geq k]\,\mathbb{P}[\tau^{2}\geq k+1]\quad\!\text{(by~\eqref{eqn:x1alphakbound}, \eqref{eqn:ek1givlbd}, $X^{1},X^{2}$ independent})

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{k=1}^{A-1}c_{2}k^{-1}\quad\text{(by Lemma~\ref{lem:tauj_tail})}

\displaystyle\geq c_{2}\log A.

This proves the result.
∎

Proposition B.4
.

\sup_{A\in[1,\infty)}\mathbb{E}[(X^{1}_{A}-I^{1}_{A}){\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\tau\geq A\}}]<\infty
.

Proof.

Let
A\in[1,\infty)
. Since
I^{1}_{A}=I^{1}_{1}
on the event
\{\tau>A\}
and

\mathbb{E}[|I_{1}^{1}|]<\infty
by
[
20
, Chapter VIII, Proposition 4]
,
setting
c_{1}:=\mathbb{E}[|I^{1}_{1}|]
and using (
B.1
), we have

(B.10)

\mathbb{E}[-I^{1}_{A}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\tau\geq A\}}]=\mathbb{E}[-I^{1}_{1}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\tau>A\}}]\leq\mathbb{E}[|I^{1}_{1}|]=c_{1}<\infty.

Set
\sigma^{1}_{A}=\inf\{t\geq 0:X^{1}_{t}=A^{2/3}\}
.
By the independence of
X^{1},X^{2}
and Lemma
B.1
we have that

(B.11)

\mathbb{E}[X^{1}_{A}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\tau\geq A\}}]=\mathbb{P}[\tau^{2}\geq A]\,\mathbb{E}[X^{1}_{A}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\tau^{1}\geq A\}}]\asymp A^{-1/3}\,\mathbb{E}[X^{1}_{A}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\tau^{1}\geq A\}}].

We moreover have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[X^{1}_{A}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\tau^{1}\geq A\}}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigl[X^{1}_{A}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\tau^{1}\geq A\}}\bigl({\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\sigma^{1}_{A}\geq A\}}+{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\sigma^{1}_{A}<A\}}\bigr)\bigr]

\displaystyle\leq A^{2/3}\mathbb{P}[\tau^{1}\geq A]+\mathbb{E}\bigl[X^{1}_{A}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\sigma^{1}_{A}<A\leq\tau^{1}\}}\bigr]

\displaystyle\lesssim A^{1/3}+\mathbb{E}\bigl[X^{1}_{A}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\sigma^{1}_{A}<A\leq\tau^{1}\}}\bigr]\quad\text{(by Lemma~\ref{lem:tauj_tail})}

\displaystyle\leq A^{1/3}+\mathbb{E}\bigl[\bigl(X^{1}_{A}-X^{1}_{\sigma^{1}_{A}}\bigr){\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\sigma^{1}_{A}<A\leq\tau^{1}\}}\bigr]+A^{2/3}\,\mathbb{P}[\tau^{1}\geq A]

\displaystyle\lesssim A^{1/3}+\mathbb{E}\bigl[\sup\nolimits_{0\leq t\leq A}\bigl(X^{1}_{t+\sigma^{1}_{A}}-X^{1}_{\sigma^{1}_{A}}\bigr){\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\sigma^{1}_{A}<\tau^{1}\}}\bigr]\quad\text{(by Lemma~\ref{lem:tauj_tail})}

\displaystyle=A^{1/3}+\mathbb{E}[S^{1}_{A}]\,\mathbb{P}[\sigma^{1}_{A}<\tau^{1}]\quad\text{(by strong Markov at time $\sigma^{1}_{A}$)}

(B.12)

\displaystyle\lesssim A^{1/3}+A^{2/3}\,\mathbb{P}[\sigma^{1}_{A}<\tau^{1}],

where the strong Markov property of
X^{1}
refers to
[
20
, Chapter I, Proposition 6]

and we used in the last step that
\mathbb{E}[S^{1}_{A}]=A^{2/3}\mathbb{E}[S^{1}_{1}]\asymp A^{2/3}

by the scaling property of
X^{1}
and
[
20
, Chapter VII, Corollary 2-(i)]
.
Further, by considering
\{X^{1}_{t+1}-X^{1}_{1}\}_{t\geq 0}
, which is independent of

X^{1}|_{[0,1]}
and has the same law as
X^{2}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}[\sigma^{1}_{A}<\tau^{1}]\leq\mathbb{P}[\sigma^{1}_{A}\leq 1]+\mathbb{P}\bigl[\text{$X^{1}_{1}<A^{2/3}$, $X^{2}$ hits $A^{2/3}-X^{1}_{1}$ before hitting $(-\infty,I^{1}_{1}-X^{1}_{1}]$}\bigr].

Then
\mathbb{P}[\sigma^{1}_{A}\leq 1]\leq\mathbb{P}[S^{1}_{1}\geq A^{2/3}]\leq c_{2}e^{-A^{2/3}}

for some
c_{2}>0
independent of
A
by
[
20
, Chapter VII, Corollary 2-(i)]
.
Also,
[
20
, Chapter VII, Theorem 8]
implies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}

\displaystyle\bigl[\text{$X^{2}$ hits $A^{2/3}-X^{1}_{1}$ before hitting $(-\infty,I^{1}_{1}-X^{1}_{1}]$}\bigm|X^{1}_{1},I^{1}_{1}\bigr]=\frac{W(X_{1}^{1}-I_{1}^{1})}{W(X_{1}^{1}-I_{1}^{1}+A^{2/3}-X_{1}^{1})},

where
W
is the scale function of
X^{1}
introduced in
[
20
, Section VII.2]
.
Moreover, since the Laplace exponent
\psi(\lambda)
of
X^{1}
is proportional to
\lambda^{3/2}
by the scaling property of
X^{1}
and the Laplace transform of
W
is
1/\psi
by definition, we see that
W(x)
is proportional to
x^{1/2}
. It thus follows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl[\text{$X^{2}$ hits $A^{2/3}-X^{1}_{1}$ before hitting $(-\infty,I^{1}_{1}-X^{1}_{1}]$}\bigm|X^{1}_{1},I^{1}_{1}\bigr]

\displaystyle=\frac{(X_{1}^{1}-I_{1}^{1})^{1/2}}{(X_{1}^{1}-I_{1}^{1}+A^{2/3}-X_{1}^{1})^{1/2}}\leq(X_{1}^{1}-I_{1}^{1})^{1/2}A^{-1/3}

a.s. on
\{X_{1}^{1}<A^{2/3}\}
, and hence taking expectations yields

\mathbb{P}[\sigma^{1}_{A}<\tau^{1}]\lesssim A^{-1/3}

in view of the fact that
\mathbb{E}[(X_{1}^{1}-I_{1}^{1})^{1/2}]<\infty

as noted in the proof of Lemma
B.1
.
Combining this with (
B.11
) and (
B.12
) implies that

\sup_{A\in[1,\infty)}\mathbb{E}[X^{1}_{A}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\tau\geq A\}}]<\infty
,
which together with (
B.10
) proves the result.
∎

In fact, we also have an upper bound on
\mathbb{E}[(-I^{1}_{\tau})^{p}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\tau<A\}}]

for
p\in[1,\frac{3}{2})
, which with
p=1
matches the lower bound obtained in Proposition
B.3
, as follows.

Proposition B.5
.

For any
p\in[1,\frac{3}{2})
there exists
c_{p}\in(0,\infty)
depending only on
p
and the law of
X^{1}
such that for any
A\in[2,\infty)
,

(B.13)

\mathbb{E}[(-I^{1}_{\tau})^{p}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\tau<A\}}]\leq\begin{cases}c_{1}\log A&\textrm{if $p=1$,}\\
c_{p}A^{2(p-1)/3}&\textrm{if $p\in(1,\frac{3}{2})$.}\end{cases}

Proof.

Let
p\in[1,\frac{3}{2})
,
A\in[2,\infty)
and set
A^{\prime}:=\min\{n\in\mathbb{N}\mid A\leq 2^{n}\}
. We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(-I^{1}_{\tau})^{p}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\tau<A\}}]\leq\mathbb{E}[(-I^{1}_{\tau})^{p}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\tau<2^{A^{\prime}}\}}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{A^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}\bigl[(-I^{1}_{\tau})^{p}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{2^{k-1}\leq\tau<2^{k}\}}\bigr]

(B.14)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{A^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}\bigl[(-I^{1}_{2^{k}})^{p}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{2^{k-1}\leq\tau<2^{k}\}}\bigr].

Then for each
k\in\mathbb{N}
, since
X^{1}_{t}\geq I^{1}_{1}=I^{1}_{t}
a.s. on
\{\tau^{1}\geq t\}

for each
t\in[1,\infty)
and
I^{1}_{2^{k}}=\inf_{2^{k-1}\leq s\leq 2^{k}}X^{1}_{s}
on
\{2^{k-1}\leq\tau^{1}<2^{k}\}
,
it follows from the independence of
X^{1},X^{2}
, the convexity of the function
\mathbb{R}\ni x\mapsto(x^{+})^{p}
,
the Markov property of
X^{1}
at time
2^{k-1}
and

\mathbb{P}[\tau^{1}\geq 2^{k-1}]=\mathbb{P}[\tau^{2}\geq 2^{k-1}]
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[(-I^{1}_{2^{k}})^{p}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{2^{k-1}\leq\tau<2^{k}\}}\bigr]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigl[(-I^{1}_{2^{k}})^{p}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{2^{k-1}\leq\tau\leq\tau^{1}<2^{k}\}}\bigr]+\mathbb{E}\bigl[(-I^{1}_{2^{k}})^{p}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{2^{k-1}\leq\tau<2^{k}\leq\tau^{1}\}}\bigr]

\displaystyle\leq 2^{p-1}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\bigl(X^{1}_{2^{k-1}}-\inf\nolimits_{2^{k-1}\leq s\leq 2^{k}}X^{1}_{s}\bigr)^{p}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\tau^{1}\geq 2^{k-1}\}}\bigr]\mathbb{P}[\tau^{2}\geq 2^{k-1}]

\displaystyle\qquad+2^{p-1}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\bigl((-X^{1}_{2^{k-1}})^{+}\bigr)^{p}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\tau\geq 2^{k-1}\}}\bigr]+\mathbb{E}[(-I^{1}_{1})^{p}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\tau^{1}\geq 2^{k}\}}]\mathbb{P}[\tau^{2}\geq 2^{k-1}]

(B.15)

\displaystyle\leq 2^{p-1}\mathbb{E}[(-I^{1}_{2^{k-1}})^{p}]\mathbb{P}[\tau^{2}\geq 2^{k-1}]^{2}+(2^{p-1}+1)\mathbb{E}[(-I^{1}_{1})^{p}]\mathbb{P}[\tau^{2}\geq 2^{k-1}].

Now, recalling that for some
c\in(0,\infty)
determined solely by the law of
X^{1}
we have

\mathbb{E}[(-I^{1}_{2^{k-1}})^{p}]=(2^{k-1})^{2p/3}\mathbb{E}[(-I^{1}_{1})^{p}]\leq c(2^{k-1})^{2p/3}

by the scaling property of
X^{1}
and
[
20
, Chapter VIII, Proposition 4]

and
\mathbb{P}[\tau^{2}\geq 2^{k-1}]\leq c2^{-(k-1)/3}
by Lemma
B.1
, we conclude from
(
B.14
), (
B.15
) and
2^{A^{\prime}}<2A
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(-I^{1}_{\tau})^{p}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\tau<A\}}]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{A^{\prime}}\bigl(2^{p-1}c^{3}(2^{k-1})^{2p/3}2^{-2(k-1)/3}+(2^{p-1}+1)c^{2}2^{-(k-1)/3}\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq 2^{p-1}c^{3}\sum_{k=1}^{A^{\prime}}(2^{2(p-1)/3})^{k-1}+\frac{(2^{p-1}+1)c^{2}}{1-2^{-1/3}}\leq\begin{cases}c_{1}\log A&\textrm{if $p=1$,}\\
c_{p}A^{2(p-1)/3}&\textrm{if $p\in(1,\frac{3}{2})$}\end{cases}

for some
c_{p}\in(0,\infty)
explicit in
2^{p-1}
and
c
, completing the proof.
∎

We also need the following propositions in the proof of Proposition
5.2
.

Proposition B.6
.

There exists
c_{1}\in(0,\infty)
such that for any
y\in(0,1]
,

(B.16)

\mathbb{P}\bigl[\tau=\tau^{2}<2,\,X^{1}_{\tau}-I^{1}_{\tau}>4,\,I^{2}_{\tau}>-y\bigr]\geq c_{1}y.

Proof.

Let
x,y\in(0,\infty)
. By (
B.1
) and the independence of
X^{1},X^{2}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}

\displaystyle\bigl[\tau=\tau^{2}<2,\,X^{1}_{\tau}-I^{1}_{\tau}>x,\,I^{2}_{\tau}>-2y\bigr]

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\bigl[\tau^{1}\geq 2,\,\inf\nolimits_{1\leq t\leq 2}(X^{1}_{t}-I^{1}_{t})>x,\,\tau^{2}\leq 2,\,I^{2}_{\tau^{2}}>-2y\bigr]

(B.17)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigl[\tau^{1}\geq 2,\,\inf\nolimits_{1\leq t\leq 2}(X^{1}_{t}-I^{1}_{t})>x\bigr]\,\mathbb{P}\bigl[\tau^{2}\leq 2,\,I^{2}_{\tau^{2}}>-2y\bigr].

For the first term of the product in (
B.17
),
we have
\mathbb{P}[X^{1}_{1}-I^{1}_{1}>2x]>0

by
[
20
, Chapter VI, Proposition 3 and Chapter VII, Corollary 2-(i)]
and

\mathbb{P}[I^{1}_{1}>-x]>0
by
[
20
, Chapter VIII, Proposition 2]
,
and then, since
\{X^{1}_{t+1}-X^{1}_{1}\}_{t\geq 0}
has the same law as
X^{1}

and is independent of
\{X^{1}_{t}\}_{0\leq t\leq 1}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl[\tau^{1}\geq 2,\,\inf\nolimits_{1\leq t\leq 2}(X^{1}_{t}-I^{1}_{t})>x\bigr]

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\bigl[X^{1}_{1}-I^{1}_{1}>2x,\,\inf\nolimits_{0\leq t\leq 1}(X^{1}_{t+1}-X^{1}_{1})>-x\bigr]

(B.18)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}[X^{1}_{1}-I^{1}_{1}>2x]\,\mathbb{P}[I^{1}_{1}>-x]>0.

For the second term of the product in (
B.17
),
setting
\tau_{z}:=\inf\{t\geq 0\mid X^{2}_{t}\leq-z\}
for
z\in(0,\infty)
,
by
\tau^{2}\geq 1
and the Markov property of
X^{2}
at time
1
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl[\tau^{2}\leq 2,\,I^{2}_{\tau^{2}}>-2y\bigr]

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\bigl[I^{2}_{1}>-y,\,X^{2}_{1}\in[1,2],\,\tau_{y}\leq 2,\,X^{2}_{\tau_{y}}>-2y\bigr]

(B.19)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\bigl[I^{2}_{1}>-y,\,X^{2}_{1}\in[1,2]\bigr]\,\inf\nolimits_{z\in[1,2]}\mathbb{P}\bigl[\tau_{z+y}\leq 1,\,X^{2}_{\tau_{z+y}}>-z-2y\bigr].

Since the left-hand side of (
B.16
) is non-decreasing in
y
,
in view of (
B.17
), (
B.18
)
and (
B.19
) it suffices to show that there exist

c_{2},c_{3},c_{4}\in(0,\infty)
with
c_{2}\leq 1
such that for any
y\in(0,c_{2}]
and any
z\in[1,3]
,

(B.20)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl[I^{2}_{1}>-y,\,X^{2}_{1}\in[1,2]\bigr]

\displaystyle\geq c_{3}y^{1/2},

(B.21)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl[\tau_{z}\leq 1,\,X^{2}_{\tau_{z}}>-z-y\bigr]

\displaystyle\geq c_{4}y^{1/2}.

For (
B.20
), set
\sigma_{z}:=\inf\{t\geq 0\mid X^{2}_{t}>z\}

for
z\in[0,\infty)
, so that
\mathbb{P}[\sigma_{z}<\infty]=1

by
[
20
, Chapter VII, Proof of Theorem 1]
, and let
y,u\in(0,\infty)
.
Then by the scaling property of
X^{2}
and
[
20
, Chapter VIII, Proposition 2]
,
for a constant
c_{5}\in[1,\infty)
independent of
y,u
we have

\mathbb{P}\bigl[I^{2}_{u/2}>-y\bigr]=\mathbb{P}\bigl[I^{2}_{1}>-(u/2)^{-2/3}y\bigr]\leq c_{5}u^{-1/3}y^{1/2}
,
which together with the scaling property of
X^{2}
and
[
20
, Chapter VII, Theorem 8]

implies that, with
a:=u^{-2/3}
,

(B.22)

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}\bigl[I^{2}_{\sigma_{a}}>-ay,\,\sigma_{a}<1/2\bigr]&=\mathbb{P}\bigl[I^{2}_{\sigma_{1}}>-y,\,\sigma_{1}<u/2\bigr]\\
&=\mathbb{P}\bigl[I^{2}_{\sigma_{1}}>-y\bigr]-\mathbb{P}\bigl[I^{2}_{\sigma_{1}}>-y,\,\sigma_{1}\geq u/2\bigr]\\
&\geq y^{1/2}/(1+y)^{1/2}-\mathbb{P}\bigl[I^{2}_{u/2}>-y\bigr]\\
&\geq\bigl((1+y)^{-1/2}-c_{5}u^{-1/3}\bigr)y^{1/2}.\end{split}

Choosing
u:=125c_{5}^{3}
and replacing
y
with
a^{-1}y=u^{2/3}y
in
(
B.22
), for any
y\in(0,a]
we obtain

(B.23)

\mathbb{P}\bigl[I^{2}_{\sigma_{a}}>-y,\,\sigma_{a}<1/2\bigr]\geq\bigl(2^{-1/2}-c_{5}u^{-1/3}\bigr)u^{1/3}y^{1/2}>2c_{5}y^{1/2}.

Moreover, for all
x>0
, we let
\mathbb{P}_{x}
denote the law of
X^{2}+x
, and
let
\mathbb{P}_{x}^{+}
be the probability measure on the space of
[0,\infty)
-valued cadlag paths
(equipped with the
\sigma
-algebra generated by the coordinate process
X=\{X_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
) given by

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}^{+}\bigl[X_{t}\in dy\bigr]=\frac{W(y)}{W(x)}\mathbb{P}_{x}\bigl[X_{t}\in dy,\,t<T_{(-\infty,0)}\bigr],

where
W
is as in the proof of Proposition
B.4
and

T_{(-\infty,0)}:=\inf\{t\geq 0\mid X_{t}<0\}
. Then,
by
[
20
, Chapter VII, Proposition 14]

there exists a probability measure
\mathbb{P}_{0}^{+}
such that
\mathbb{P}_{x}^{+}\to\mathbb{P}_{0}^{+}
as
x\downarrow 0

in the sense of finite-dimensional distributions, and
by
[
20
, Chapter VII, Corollary 16]
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{0}^{+}\bigl[X_{t}\in dy\bigr]=\frac{yW(y)}{t}\mathbb{P}\bigl[X^{2}_{t}\in dy\bigr].

It follows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl[X_{1/2}^{2}>3/2-b,\,I_{1/2}^{2}\geq-b\bigr]=\mathbb{P}_{b}\Bigl[X_{1/2}>3/2,\,\inf_{0\leq s\leq 1/2}X_{s}>0\Bigr]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{b}\bigl[X_{1/2}>3/2,\,T_{(-\infty,0)}>1/2\bigr]=W(b)\int_{3/2}^{\infty}\frac{1}{W(z)}\mathbb{P}_{b}^{+}\bigl[X_{1/2}\in dz\bigr]

for any
b>0
, and

\displaystyle\int_{3/2}^{\infty}\frac{1}{W(z)}\mathbb{P}_{b}^{+}\bigl[X_{1/2}\in dz\bigr]

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{b\downarrow 0}\int_{3/2}^{\infty}\frac{1}{W(z)}\mathbb{P}_{0}^{+}\bigl[X_{1/2}\in dz\bigr]=2\int_{3/2}^{\infty}z\mathbb{P}\bigl[X^{2}_{1/2}\in dz\bigr]>0

since
\mathbb{P}[X^{2}_{1/2}>3/2]>0
by the scaling property of

X^{2}
and
[
20
, Chapter VII, Corollary 2-(i)]
.
We see therefore that, if
c_{5}\in[1,\infty)
is large enough so that

a=u^{-2/3}=(5c_{5})^{-2}
is small enough, then

(B.24)

q_{1}:=\mathbb{P}\bigl[\sigma_{3/2-a}<1/2,\,I_{\sigma_{3/2-a}}^{2}\geq-a\bigr]\geq\mathbb{P}\bigl[X_{1/2}^{2}>3/2-a,\,I_{1/2}^{2}\geq-a\bigr]>0.

Noting that
\mathbb{P}\bigl[X^{2}_{\sigma_{z}}=z\bigr]=1
for
z\in(0,\infty)
and that

q_{2}:=\mathbb{P}\bigl[\sup_{0\leq t\leq 1}|X^{2}_{t}|\leq 1/2\bigr]>0

by
[
20
, Chapter VIII, Proposition 3]
, from the strong Markov property of

X^{2}
at times
\sigma_{a},\sigma_{3/2}
(see, e.g.,
[
20
, Chapter I, Proposition 6]
),
(
B.24
) and (
B.23
) we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl[I^{2}_{1}>-y,\,X^{2}_{1}\in[1,2]\bigr]

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\Bigl[I^{2}_{\sigma_{a}}>-y,\,\sigma_{a}<\tfrac{1}{2},\,\inf_{\sigma_{a}\leq t\leq\sigma_{3/2}}X^{2}_{t}\geq 0,\,\sigma_{3/2}-\sigma_{a}<\tfrac{1}{2},\,\sup_{0\leq t\leq 1}\bigl|X^{2}_{t+\sigma_{3/2}}-\tfrac{3}{2}\bigr|\leq\tfrac{1}{2}\Bigr]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigl[I^{2}_{\sigma_{a}}>-y,\,\sigma_{a}<\tfrac{1}{2}\bigr]q_{1}q_{2}\geq(2c_{5}q_{1}q_{2})y^{1/2},

proving (
B.20
).

Next, to see (
B.21
), let
y\in(0,1]
and
z\in[1,3]
.
By
[
20
, Chapter VIII, Exercise 3]
,

(B.25)

\mathbb{P}\bigl[X^{2}_{\tau_{z}}>-z-y\bigr]=\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{0}^{y/(z+y)}x^{-1/2}(1-x)^{-1/2}\,dx\geq\frac{y^{1/2}}{\pi}.

Let
b\in(0,\infty)
, and recall that

\bigl|\mathbb{E}\bigl[e^{\sqrt{-1}\lambda X^{2}_{1}}\bigr]\bigr|=e^{-c_{6}|\lambda|^{3/2}}

for any
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
for some
c_{6}\in(0,\infty)
by
[
20
, Chapter VIII, equation (1)]

and hence that the law of
X^{2}_{1}
has a bounded continuous density

f_{1}\colon\mathbb{R}\to[0,\infty)
by Fourier inversion. Then noting that

\pi^{-1}\int_{0}^{\alpha/(\alpha+\beta)}x^{-1/2}(1-x)^{-1/2}\,dx\leq(\alpha/\beta)^{1/2}

for any
\alpha,\beta\in(0,\infty)
by considering the cases of
\alpha\leq\beta

and
\alpha>\beta
separately, we see from the Markov property of
X^{2}
at time
b
,

[
20
, Chapter VIII, Exercise 3]
and the scaling property of
X^{2}
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl[\tau_{z}>b,\,X^{2}_{\tau_{z}}>-z-y\bigr]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigl[{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\tau_{z}>b\}}\bigl(\mathbb{P}[X^{2}_{\tau_{z+x}}>-z-y-x]|_{x=X^{2}_{b}}\bigr)\bigr]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\biggl[{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\tau_{z}>b\}}\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{0}^{y/(z+y+X^{2}_{b})}x^{-1/2}(1-x)^{-1/2}\,dx\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq y^{1/2}\mathbb{E}\bigl[(z+X^{2}_{b})^{-1/2}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\tau_{z}>b\}}\bigr]=y^{1/2}b^{-1/3}\mathbb{E}\bigl[(w+X^{2}_{1})^{-1/2}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\tau_{w}>1\}}\bigr]

\displaystyle\leq y^{1/2}b^{-1/3}\mathbb{E}\bigl[(w+X^{2}_{1})^{-1/2}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{w+X^{2}_{1}>0\}}\bigr]=y^{1/2}b^{-1/3}\int_{0}^{\infty}x^{-1/2}f_{1}(x-w)\,dx

(B.26)

\displaystyle\leq y^{1/2}b^{-1/3}\biggl(c_{7}\int_{0}^{1}x^{-1/2}\,dx+\int_{1}^{\infty}f_{1}(x-w)\,dx\biggr)\leq y^{1/2}b^{-1/3}(2c_{7}+1),

where
w:=b^{-2/3}z
and
c_{7}:=\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}f_{1}(x)
.
Thus by choosing
b:=\pi^{3}(4c_{7}+2)^{3}
, from (
B.25
)
and (
B.26
) we obtain

(B.27)

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}\bigl[\tau_{z}\leq b,\,X^{2}_{\tau_{z}}>-z-y\bigr]&=\mathbb{P}\bigl[X^{2}_{\tau_{z}}>-z-y\bigr]-\mathbb{P}\bigl[\tau_{z}>b,\,X^{2}_{\tau_{z}}>-z-y\bigr]\\
&\geq(2\pi)^{-1}y^{1/2}.\end{split}

Further, by
[
20
, Chapter VIII, Proposition 4]
and the scaling property
of
X^{2}
there exist
A\in[2,\infty)
and
c_{8}\in(0,\infty)
such that for any

\epsilon\in[\frac{1}{10}b^{-2/3},b^{-2/3}]
, any
x\in[\frac{1}{10}A,10A]
and any
s\in(0,\infty)
,

\mathbb{P}\bigl[I^{2}_{s^{-3/2}}\in[-x/s,-(1-\epsilon)x/s)\bigr]=\mathbb{P}\bigl[I^{2}_{1}\in[-x,-(1-\epsilon)x)\bigr]\geq c_{8},

which in turn, with
\epsilon=\epsilon_{0}:=\frac{1}{6}b^{-2/3}
,

x=x_{z,b}:=(z-\frac{1}{2}b^{-2/3})A
and
s=A
, yields

(B.28)

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\bigl[\tau_{z,b}\leq\tfrac{1}{2},\,X^{2}_{\tau_{z,b}}\in[-z+\tfrac{1}{2}b^{-2/3},-z+b^{-2/3}]\bigr]\\
&\geq\mathbb{P}\bigl[I^{2}_{A^{-3/2}}\in[-z+\tfrac{1}{2}b^{-2/3},-z+b^{-2/3})\bigr]\\
&\geq\mathbb{P}\bigl[I^{2}_{A^{-3/2}}\in[-x_{z,b}/A,-(1-\epsilon_{0})x_{z,b}/A)\bigr]\geq c_{8},\end{split}

where
\tau_{z,b}:=\tau_{z-b^{-2/3}}
. It follows from the strong Markov property
of
X^{2}
at time
\tau_{z,b}
, (
B.28
),
the scaling property of
X^{2}
and (
B.27
)
that, provided
y\in(0,\frac{1}{2}b^{-2/3}]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl[\tau_{z}\leq 1,\,X^{2}_{\tau_{z}}>-z-y\bigr]

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\bigl[\tau_{z,b}\leq\tfrac{1}{2},\,X^{2}_{\tau_{z,b}}\in[-z+\tfrac{1}{2}b^{-2/3},-z+b^{-2/3}],\,\tau_{z}\leq\tau_{z,b}+\tfrac{1}{2},\,X^{2}_{\tau_{z}}>-z-y\bigr]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigl[{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{\{\tau_{z,b}\leq\frac{1}{2},\,X^{2}_{\tau_{z,b}}\in[-z+\frac{1}{2}b^{-2/3},-z+b^{-2/3}]\}}\bigl(\mathbb{P}[\tau_{z+x}\leq\tfrac{1}{2},\,X^{2}_{\tau_{z+x}}>-z-y-x]|_{x=X^{2}_{\tau_{z,b}}}\bigr)\bigr]

\displaystyle\geq c_{8}\inf\nolimits_{x\in[\frac{1}{2}b^{-2/3},b^{-2/3}]}\mathbb{P}\bigl[\tau_{x}\leq\tfrac{1}{2},\,X^{2}_{\tau_{x}}>-x-y\bigr]

\displaystyle=c_{8}\inf\nolimits_{x\in[1,2]}\mathbb{P}\bigl[\tau_{x}\leq\sqrt{2}b,\,X^{2}_{\tau_{x}}>-x-2b^{2/3}y\bigr]\geq c_{8}(2\pi)^{-1}(2b^{2/3})^{1/2}y^{1/2},

which proves (
B.21
) and thereby completes
the proof of (
B.16
).
∎

Proposition B.7
.

Let
X=\{X_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
be a
3/2
-stable Lévy process with only downward jumps and

X_{0}=0
, and set
Z_{t}:=X_{t}-\inf_{0\leq s\leq t}(X_{s}\wedge 0)
for
t\in[0,\infty)
.
Then there exist
c_{1},c_{2}\in(0,\infty)
such that

\mathbb{P}[\sup_{0\leq t\leq 1}Z_{t}\geq x]\leq c_{1}e^{-c_{2}x}

for any
x\in[0,\infty)
.

Proof.

Set
\tau_{0}:=0
, and define sequences
\{\sigma_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty},\{\tau_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}

of stopping times for
X
inductively by
\sigma_{n}:=\inf\{t\in[\tau_{n-1},\infty)\mid Z_{t}\geq 1\}

and
\tau_{n}:=\inf\{t\in[\sigma_{n},\infty)\mid Z_{t}=0\}
for
n\geq 1
, so that

Z_{\sigma_{n}}=1
on
\{\sigma_{n}<\infty\}
by the absence of upward jumps of
X
.
Since
\{Z_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
is strong Markov by
[
20
, Chapter VI, Proposition 1]
,
it easily follows from
[
20
, Chapter VII, Theorem 8]
that

\sigma_{n}<\tau_{n}<\infty
for any
n\geq 1
a.s. and hence that

\{\{Z_{t+\sigma_{n}}\}_{t\in[0,\tau_{n}-\sigma_{n})}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}

is i.i.d. with law given by that of
\{X_{t+\sigma}\}_{t\in[0,\tau-\sigma)}
with

\sigma:=\inf\{t\in[0,\infty)\mid X_{t}\geq 1\}
and
\tau:=\inf\{t\in[\sigma,\infty)\mid X_{t}\leq 0\}
.
Moreover, recalling that
\{X_{t+\sigma}-1\}_{t\geq 0}
has the same law as
X
by
the strong Markov property
[
20
, Chapter I, Proposition 6]
of
X
, we have

q:=\mathbb{P}[\tau-\sigma\leq 1]\in(0,1)
by
[
20
, Chapter VIII, Propositions 2 and 4]

and
c_{3}:=\mathbb{E}[\exp(2(-\log q)\sup_{0\leq t<(\tau-\sigma)\wedge 1}X_{t+\sigma})]<\infty

by
[
20
, Chapter VII, Corollary 2-(i)]
, and the random variable

N:=\min\{n\geq 1\mid\tau_{n}>1\}
satisfies

\mathbb{P}[N\geq n]\leq\mathbb{P}[\bigcap_{j=1}^{n-1}\{\tau_{j}-\sigma_{j}\leq 1\}]=q^{n-1}

for any
n\geq 1
. It thus follows that for any
x\in[1,\infty)
, with

n:=\min(\mathbb{N}\cap[x,\infty))
,
c_{2}:=-\log q
and
c_{4}:=c_{3}c_{2}^{-1}e^{c_{2}-1}+1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}[\sup\nolimits_{0\leq t\leq 1}Z_{t}\geq x]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\Bigl[\bigcup\nolimits_{1\leq j\leq n}\{\sup\nolimits_{0\leq t<(\tau_{n}-\sigma_{n})\wedge 1}Z_{t+\sigma_{j}}\geq x\}\Bigr]+\mathbb{P}[N>n]

\displaystyle\leq n\mathbb{P}[\sup\nolimits_{0\leq t<(\tau-\sigma)\wedge 1}X_{t+\sigma}\geq x]+q^{n}

\displaystyle\leq nc_{3}e^{-2c_{2}x}+e^{-c_{2}n}\leq(x+1)c_{3}e^{-2c_{2}x}+e^{-c_{2}x}\leq c_{4}e^{-c_{2}x},

proving the assertion for
x\in[1,\infty)
. The assertion for
x\in[0,1)
follows by setting
c_{1}:=c_{4}\vee e^{c_{2}}
.
∎
